**This is the standing read.** CURRENT state only. Verified against the working tree **2026-08-11**, with
§5, §6, §10, §11, §13 and §14 re-verified **2026-08-18** (the channels-v2 review fix waves), and §5's mention
rules, §6 in full and §10's channel-tool bullets re-verified **2026-08-22** (the inbound-consent retirement
and the mention/narration fixes), and §3's route-classification bullet, §4, the new **§4A**, §7's live-surface
count, §9 and §12 re-verified **2026-08-23** (the home-channels wave), and §10's `launch_agent`
bullet plus §11's own-channel lanes re-verified **2026-08-25** (the own-machine launch lane, F-320),
and **§4A's guest bullets, §7's RLS/subscription bullets and §12's guest-role entries re-verified and
CORRECTED 2026-08-26** (the guest-lane review wave: the two wrapper families, the fourteen floors,
the public-channel fence, the realtime guest arm, F-324 resolved),
and **§4A's cap bullets, §5's `memberManagement` / Info-tab bullets, §5A's authorship-marker bullet

holds MORE THAN TWO members — the `enforce_link_container_member_cap` trigger, both service capacity
gates and the `LINK_CONTAINER_FULL` code are all deleted; F-306 closed), **and §4A's roster shape, §5A's
authorship-marker bullet and §8's cache-shape bullet re-verified and CORRECTED 2026-08-26 for `peers`**
(the same ruling's second half: `HomeChannel.peers` is a LIST and the row draws a stacked avatar row —
F-307 RESOLVED).
and **§10's `direct_agent` bullets, §11's own-channel lanes and the narration-ring bullets, and §12's
direction-mailbox entries re-verified and CORRECTED 2026-08-31** (Samuel's two post-1.23.0 rulings:
the one-generation launch bound became a per-channel SETTING with a rolling budget behind it, and
the operator's OWN agents may `direct_agent` each other — F-374's loop bounded, F-376 resolved,

the constant was 8000 and prose was silently flattening every newline).
A bullet
carrying its own later date was measured on that date; one carrying none is on the file's date above.
The change-narrative — why a rule exists, what it replaced, what incident bought it — lives in
`docs/ENGINEERING.md`; each section points into it. Never restate history here; never restate a live rule there.

Three conventions this file obeys and you must too:
- **A number carries its measurement date**, or it is not written down.
- **Code references are symbol anchors** (`path › symbol`) or grep patterns. Never a bare line number.
- **Deploy state is a measurement.** This file records the COMMAND, never the answer. The tree holds claims, not deploy facts.

---

## 1. Layout & file size

- **Feature-first.** `src/features/<name>/` owns its UI, hooks, types, `server/`. No cross-feature imports — shared things go to `src/shared/`. Never a top-level `lib/` or `components/` under `src/`.
- **Hard cap 500 lines, lint-enforced at `error`.** `eslint.config.mjs › "max-lines"` = `{max:500, skipBlankLines:false, skipComments:false}` over **three globs: `src/**`, `packages/*/src/**` AND `apps/*/src/**`** — the SPA is capped too, on the same block. **Both `skip*` false — every physical line counts.** "It's mostly comments" is not an argument for an exemption.
- **A file at 500 cannot absorb a COMMENT.** Correcting a stale docblock in an at-cap file is a split, not a doc fix.
- **Exemptions are a closed list in `eslint.config.mjs` carrying "DO NOT ADD TO THIS LIST — split the file instead."**
- **Measured 2026-08-11: exactly FOUR files exceed 500 in `src/`+`packages/`, and the exemption list names exactly those four.** Two are carve-outs (`src/shared/supabase/types.ts` generated; `src/features/knowledge/server/seed-fixtures-data.ts` data table); the real backlog is `billing/components/upgrade-modal.tsx` and `billing/server/webhook-handler.test.ts`. Re-measure, never quote — and use THIS form, which prunes `node_modules`/`dist` and drops `wc`'s `total` row:
  ```
  find src packages \( -name node_modules -o -name dist \) -prune -o \( -name '*.ts' -o -name '*.tsx' \) -print | xargs wc -l | awk '$1>500 && $2!="total"'
  ```
  ⚠ **The naive `find src packages -name '*.ts' -o -name '*.tsx' | xargs wc -l | awk '$1>500'` returns 73 rows, not 4** — the unparenthesized `-o` loses the path restriction, so it sweeps `node_modules` and the committed `dist/`, and `xargs` splits into batches that each emit their own `total`. A verification command that over-reports is worse than none: it trains you to ignore it.
- **Carve-outs (justify at top of file):** generated code, dense type-only files, pure data/config tables, and a single-function switch reducer that is one coherent state machine.
- **`dopl-desktop-app/` and `scripts/` are outside the root config.** The desktop has its own `eslint.config.js`, same rule, same severity, **zero exemptions**. `test/live/**/*.js` is its own block — the `.js` extension is load-bearing, it keeps that tier out of `node --test 'test/**/*.mjs'`.
- **Desktop runs with no headroom, and the NAMES change faster than the fact does.** ⚠ This bullet listed six files measured 2026-08-11 (`ui-sync.js`, `session-reducer.js`, `session-profiles.js`, `session-engine.js` at 500; `mcp-config.js`, `auth-tokens.js` at 499); **re-measured 2026-08-20, five of the six had moved** (397 / 498 / 374 / 477 / 424), which is exactly why a list of names is the wrong thing to write down here. **Re-run, never quote:**
  ```
  find dopl-desktop-app/{main,renderer,test,scripts} -name node_modules -prune -o -type f \( -name '*.js' -o -name '*.mjs' \) -print | xargs wc -l | awk '$1>=490 && $2!="total"' | sort -rn
  ```
  Nothing in the desktop tree is over. ⚠ **BUT "AND NOTHING SITS AT 500 EITHER" WAS TRUE ONLY FOR THE 2026-08-20 MEASUREMENT AND IS NOT NOW** (corrected 2026-08-23): **several `main/` modules sit AT the cap and several more are within two lines of it** — run the command above rather than trusting a list — which is the state this section's own next bullet is about — such a file cannot absorb a comment, so the next correction to any of them is a SPLIT. F-282 and F-283 are the standing entries. **Re-run the command; a list of names here rots faster than the fact does.** `test/eslint-config-coverage.test.mjs` asserts (via ESLint's own `calculateConfigForFile`) that every desktop file resolves to the cap — `main/`, `renderer/`, `test/**/*.mjs` and `test/live/**/*.js` alike, **zero exemptions**.
- **Split by responsibility, layer, or sub-feature** — one file per reason to change. For `op`-dispatched MCP tools the pattern is fixed: the original stays the thin **registrar** (schemas + `op` routing) and delegates to `<tool>-ops-read/-write/-admin.ts`, shared resolvers in `<tool>-shared.ts`/`-render.ts`. Keep the re-export barrel if callers import through it.
- **A split scheduled on a line count waits indefinitely; a split scheduled by a new reason-to-change lands the same day.**

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §1, §2.

## 1A. Loading states — per-page skeletons

**Written and measured 2026-08-28**, and **re-measured in full 2026-08-30** against the restructured
shell (see the GROUND bullet) — the whole section, not bullet-by-bullet, so the file-header date
above does not apply to it.

> ⚠ **LETTERED, NOT NUMBERED**, for the reason §5A states: §2…§14 are cited by number from `CLAUDE.md`
> and from docblocks across three trees. **Do not renumber this file to "fix" it.**

- **A PAGE'S LOADING STATE IS *THAT PAGE'S* SHAPE.** Samuel's ruling 2026-08-28, over the generic ghosts: *"way off"*. NINE surfaces now paint their own geometry — `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/home-skeleton.tsx` (the /home frame + its Knowledge and Agents faces), `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/skeletons/knowledge-skeletons.tsx` (the knowledge ROOT and an OPENED base, one file because both hosts of that surface load through it), `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/overview/overview-skeleton.tsx`, `› pages/channels/channels-skeleton.tsx`, `› pages/agents/agents-skeleton.tsx`, `› pages/members/members-skeleton.tsx`. A ghost that resolves into a different surface than the one it stood for is worse than no ghost: it moves the content the operator was already reading toward. ⚠ **THE COUNT IS SHAPES, NOT FILES** (six files export the nine) — measure the files with `ls apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/*/*-skeleton.tsx apps/desktop-ui/src/components/skeletons/*-skeletons.tsx` and the shapes with the `SHAPES` table in `› components/skeletons/page-skeletons.test.tsx`, which every one of them is registered in.
- 🔑 **THE GROUND IS THE SHELL'S, NOT THE SKELETON'S — and that is what the 2026-08-30 restructure settled.** A workspace page renders inside `src/shared/layout/app-shell/app-shell.module.css › .pageCard`, the WHITE card floating in the one gray panel, and `› .pageCard :global(.page-float)` collapses the page's own float to a plain filling box. **A skeleton wearing `page-float` therefore lands on exactly the ground its page lands on, with no per-skeleton repaint** — which is why the four pre-restructure ghosts needed no re-grounding and why adding one here would be the bug. What DOES follow the levels is anything a skeleton draws INSIDE the card: a section panel is the gray WELL (`SECTION_PANEL_GROUND`, level 3) in both the Agents ghost and the opened-base ghost, and the members detail band is `--surface-invert` because the real header is. Level table: `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md › The frame model ALTERNATES`.
- **`PageLoading` IS NOT DEPRECATED AND MUST NOT BE.** `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/page-states.tsx › PageLoading` remains THE loading state of every page with no shape of its own — settings, boot, onboarding, the two pop-out windows, chats, skills, ontology, and the app shell's boot chain, where ONE steady shape across five sequential pending states is the whole point. **Replace the wrong ghosts; do not multiply them.** A wave that left `PageLoading` unused would have grown a per-page spinner everywhere instead. ⚠ **MEMBERS LEFT THIS LIST 2026-08-30** — it was `variant="two-pane"`, i.e. a fixed 372px avatar list over a centred `max-w-[760px]` document, and the page is a `minmax(380px,42fr)` grid cell over a dark-headed detail pane. **Re-derive the list rather than inheriting it: `grep -rln PageLoading apps/desktop-ui/src/pages`.**
- **DO NOT CREATE A LOADING STATE WHERE NONE EXISTS.** A skeleton goes at a gate that already rendered `PageLoading` or a blank; an HMR-fast path gets nothing.
- **ONE SHIMMER RECIPE, AND IT IS SHARED WITH THE WEB TREE** — `src/shared/ui/skeleton.tsx › Skeleton` (`animate-pulse` over `bg-surface-raised-2`, `aria-hidden`), with `› SkeletonBar` / `› SkeletonLine` / `› SkeletonText` / `› SkeletonRow` / `› TranscriptSkeleton` built on it. **Never a locally hand-rolled pulse** — clones drift to a different surface tint. `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/skeletons/page-skeletons.test.tsx` asserts, per shape, that the `animate-pulse` count equals the `[data-slot="skeleton"]` count.
- **EVERY SKELETON SURFACE ANNOUNCES ITSELF AND DROPS ITS MOTION** — `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/skeletons/skeleton-surface.tsx › SkeletonSurface`: `role="status"` + `aria-busy` + an `sr-only` label (the visual is `aria-hidden` end to end, so without it a reader gets silence), and the `prefers-reduced-motion` opt-out in `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/skeletons/skeletons.module.css` — **static blocks in the same geometry, never nothing**. ⚠ **The opt-out is on the SURFACE, not in the atom**, because that atom is the web tree's too; a skeleton gets it by standing inside the wrapper. ⚠ `SkeletonSurface` **paints no ground** — the page's own recipe (`page-float`, the shell root, `bg-home-panel`) arrives as `className`, the same division `src/shared/ui/section-panel.tsx › SectionPanel` draws.
- 🔑 **GEOMETRY BY REFERENCE, NEVER RESTATED.** A skeleton mounts the REAL page's classes, module and width vars — not numbers copied out of them. Live examples: `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/home.module.css › .kbCards` / `› .kbCell` are what the /home Knowledge ghost renders into (so the 3 columns, the 224px rows, the gap and the 1080px step-down are inherited); the two knowledge ghosts read `src/features/knowledge/components/knowledge-v2/knowledge-v2.module.css › .cardGrid` / `› .baseHead` / `› .rail` / `› .detailPane`; /home sizes its ghost list from `home.module.css › .page`'s `--home-list-w`, the same var the header indents by. ⚠ **A restated number does not render wrong — it silently stops being the page's shape** the first time the real grid is re-tuned, which is why this is pinned rather than reviewed.
- **THE DRIFT PINS ARE IN `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/skeletons/page-skeletons.test.tsx`, and they are SOURCE SCANS.** A CSS-module class is a build artifact and a Tailwind arbitrary value is a string, so neither is comparable any other way. They pin, per surface: the /home Knowledge grid names `home.kbCards` in the page **and** the skeleton; `grid grid-cols-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(196px,1fr))] gap-2.5` is byte-identical across `src/features/agent-templates/components/template-section.tsx › TemplateGrid` and both Agents ghosts; the knowledge ghosts import the v2 module (`.cardGrid`, `.baseHead`, `.rail`, `.detailPane`, `.infoBody`) and contain **no** `232px`/`244px`; the Overview ghost byte-shares three strings with `pages/overview/{index,stat-cards,period-stats}.tsx` and **IMPORTS** its plot height from `components/charts/bar-series.tsx › PLOT_HEIGHT_CLASS` (2026-09-01 — the constant was module-local and unexported in `activity-chart.tsx`, so the ghost byte-shared a literal instead; the plot moved to a module SHARED with /home's Overview face and the ghost now takes the value the way the Channels ghost takes `COMPOSER_BOTTOM`, which is why the pin asserts the IMPORT and the ABSENCE of the literal); the Channels ghost byte-shares eight with `channels-v2/{sidebar,sidebar-rows,bits,message-pane,composer}.tsx` and **imports** `composer-input.tsx › COMPOSER_BOTTOM` rather than retyping `pb-4`; the Members ghost byte-shares the page's grid template and mounts `members-v2/bits.tsx › SECTION_CARD` itself; and each gate names its own skeleton and no longer imports `PageLoading`. ⚠ **A BYTE-SHARE PIN IS BIDIRECTIONAL** — it fails when the SKELETON drifts and when the PAGE does, which is the half that matters, since a re-tuned page is the only way a correct skeleton goes wrong. ⚠ **The scans strip comments first** — these files explain what they replaced, so a raw-text scan would fail on the very docblock recording the decision.
- 🚫 **NO TEXT, NOTHING PRESSABLE.** The label is `sr-only` and is the only string a skeleton may contain — a heading over a shimmering grid asserts a section the read has not confirmed. That is also why the ghost panels are NOT `SectionPanel` itself (it takes a `label` string and paints an `<h2>`); they keep its box and its `data-section-panel` hook, so /home's `home.module.css › .frame` still repaints them. /home's three-face selector ghosts the kit's `.seg-track` holding inert blocks — zero `<button>`, zero `<a>`.
- **A SHARED CORE TAKES THE HOST'S SHAPE THROUGH A SLOT, IT NEVER IMPORTS ONE.** `src/features/agent-templates/components/agent-templates-core.tsx › AgentTemplatesCoreProps.loadingSkeleton`, and since 2026-08-30 `src/features/members/components/members-v2/members-v2-view.tsx › MembersV2View`'s `loadingSkeleton` — those trees are Next-free and router-free so both packages mount them, and neither can reach into `apps/desktop-ui/`. Same idiom as `channels-v2-core.tsx`'s `Link` and `src/shared/ui/skeleton.tsx › TwoPaneListSkeleton`'s `detail`. ⚠ **IT EXISTS BECAUSE THOSE TWO PAGES HAVE TWO GATES EACH** — the workspace resolve in the seam, then the shared tree's own read (templates / roster) — and the second one painting the shared ghost made one page swap skeletons mid-load, which is the flicker `page-states.tsx` argues against arriving inside a single page. **The slot is ADDITIVE:** a host that passes nothing keeps `PageShellSkeleton` / `TwoPaneListSkeleton`, so the web tree is unchanged.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §1, §2; `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` for the tokens and kit classes every shape is built from.

## 2. Data layer — repository / service / handler

- **Three layers.** `server/repository.ts` = pure data access, one function per query, **takes** a `SupabaseClient` (never creates one). `server/service.ts` = business logic, throws domain errors. `server/dto.ts` = `snake_case`→`camelCase`. `server/errors.ts` = error classes.
- **No `snake_case` key leaks past `repository.ts`.**
- **Every workspace-scoped query is filtered by `workspace_id` at the repository.** This holds even where the read runs as service role — **service role bypasses RLS entirely**, so on that path the service IS the fence and RLS is not a backstop.
- **A route handler is ≤80 lines and thin.** Pages never talk to Supabase; an RSC and its API twin read the same service code so they cannot drift.
- **A DTO carrying PII needs an ENTITLEMENT decision, not just a shape decision.** The viewer is a DTO INPUT: `channels/server/dto.ts › mapMemberRow` takes `viewerUserId` and scrubs private fields to the caller's own row.
- **Per-member settings are private BY COLUMN PRIVILEGE — not by policy, not by the DTO.** RLS filters ROWS; the requirement is a COLUMN. `channel_members` has `SELECT` revoked from `anon`/`authenticated` with a per-column `GRANT` of public columns only; `agent_tool_profile` is service_role-only, which binds PostgREST and realtime frames both. **Standing rule for any NEW per-member setting: add it to the DTO scrub AND leave it out of that migration's `GRANT` list. Two edits; the second is the one that binds.** Keep the scrub — it shapes the API response, where the server reads as service role and the column privilege redacts nothing.
  - **The worked example is `favorited_at` (`20260819120000`, §5's favourites bullet).** ⚠ The second edit is an ABSENCE, not a statement: a column added by `ALTER TABLE` inherits nothing from the earlier per-column `GRANT`, so it is service_role-only the moment it exists and the migration adds no grant. An absence is what silently stops being true — so that file **asserts the outcome in a `DO $$` block** (`has_column_privilege('authenticated', …, 'favorited_at', 'SELECT')` must be false) rather than trusting it, because restoring the table-wide grant (the documented ROLLBACK of `20260810120000`) would hand this column out with no error anywhere and take `agent_tool_profile` with it.
- **Errors: throw, don't return tuples.** Convert at the boundary. The three real pieces are `src/shared/lib/http-error.ts › HttpError` (the class, and `toResponseBody()` is the ONE place the envelope shape is decided), `shared/api/parse-json.ts › parseJson`, and the shared catch-block helper `shared/api/http-error-response.ts › toHttpErrorResponse(source, err, mapDomainError?)` — imported by ~53 modules. Four feature wrappers delegate to it with their own domain mapper (`toKnowledgeErrorResponse`, `toChatErrorResponse`, `toSkillErrorResponse`, `channels/server/http-mapping.ts › toChannelErrorResponse`). Never a raw error string or a stack trace.
  - ⚠ **There is NO `withErrorHandler` and no `shared/api/error-handler.ts`** — that pairing has never existed in this repo. It is a docs-only fiction that lived in ENGINEERING.md for months and was copied into this file's first draft; `scripts/check-doc-refs.mjs` now resolves `path › symbol` anchors specifically so this class of claim cannot be written again. Conversion is a **catch block calling `toHttpErrorResponse`**, not a wrapper composed around the handler.
- **A NEW route uses the NESTED envelope — `{ error: { code, message, details? } }`** — because that is what `HttpError.toResponseBody()` returns and what every `toHttpErrorResponse` path emits. **The FLAT `{error, message, workspaces?}` shape in §4 is workspace RESOLUTION only**, surfaced verbatim by `@dopl/client`; it is a legacy surface with clients parsing it, not a second option to pick from. Two shapes exist; only one is choosable.
- **Never send `Cache-Control: no-transform`.** It forbids intermediaries applying a content coding — an instruction NOT to compress, not a stronger `no-cache`. What an SSE route needs is `X-Accel-Buffering: no` (`shared/api/sse-keep-alive.ts`).

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §3, §8, §12.

## 3. Auth wrappers & caller-type gates

**The wrappers that EXIST** (`grep -rn "^export function with" src/shared/auth/`, verified 2026-08-11):

| Symbol | File | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `withUserAuth(handler)` | `shared/auth/with-auth.ts` | OAuth bearer (`dopl_at_*`) OR session cookie; injects `{userId, agentTokenId?, apiKeyWorkspaceId?, params}`; logs 5xx |
| `withMcpAccess(action, handler)` | `shared/auth/with-auth.ts` | Composes `withUserAuth`; paywall-gates MCP callers, logs analytics |
| `withWorkspaceAuth(handler, opts)` | `shared/auth/with-workspace-auth.ts` | Composes `withUserAuth`; resolves workspace fail-closed, verifies membership + `meetsMinRole`. **Default for workspace-scoped content routes.** |
| `isAdmin(userId)` | `shared/auth/with-auth.ts` | Site-admin check vs `ADMIN_USER_ID` |
| `requireCronSecret(request)` | `shared/auth/require-cron-secret.ts` | Bearer gate for `/api/cron/*`; fail-closed 503 when unset |

⚠ **`withExternalAuth` and `withAdminAuth` DO NOT EXIST** — **zero hits in `src/` + `packages/`**, verified 2026-08-11. Not "zero hits repo-wide": four DOC mentions survive, and all four are correct in place — `ENGINEERING.md:500` (**corrected 2026-08-11 in this same batch**; it now names the fiction as a fiction and points here), `docs/CLEANUP.md` (a dated capture, describing the tree as it was), and this line. **ENGINEERING no longer asserts these wrappers exist; do not "fix" it again.** Do not import or "restore" them, and do not invent a `requireUser` either.

- ⚠ **THE RETURNED HANDLER'S SECOND PARAMETER IS REQUIRED, AND IT IS A TYPE CONTRACT WITH NEXT RATHER THAN A STYLE CHOICE (2026-08-25).** It is `shared/auth/with-auth.ts › RouteContextArg` (`{ params: Promise<…> }`, exported for that reason). Next generates, per route it compiles IN DEV, a `.next/dev/types/app/api/**/route.ts` asserting the exported handler's second arg extends its own `RouteContext`, and `tsconfig.json` INCLUDES that tree — so an OPTIONAL parameter (or one given a DEFAULT, which does not make a parameter required in the function type) infers `… | undefined` and reddens every wrapped route the dev server has served. **CI never sees it: a clean checkout has no `.next/`.** The gate was therefore red only on the machine running the mandated always-on dev stack, which is the machine doing the work. Pinned by `shared/auth/route-context-signature.test.ts`, which mirrors the generated shapes and goes red at `tsc` on either regression. `withMcpAccess` / `withWorkspaceAuth` inherit the signature.
- **Service-layer gates:** `features/workspaces/server/authz.ts › requireWorkspaceRole` (the one membership-fetch+role-check helper) and the pure hierarchy in `features/workspaces/member-policy.ts`, shared with the members UI.
- **Two caller-type options, orthogonal to `minRole`. Cookie/session callers authenticate AS the human and are NEVER gated by either — these only constrain OAuth tokens.**
  - **`writeScopeExempt` (default: gate ON).** OAuth bearer on any non-`GET` without `dopl.write` → 403 `WRITE_SCOPE_REQUIRED` + `WWW-Authenticate: insufficient_scope`. **TWO routes carry it, both MCP PLUMBING rather than content writes** (2026-08-11): `user/mcp-status` (the liveness ping) and `mcp/credits/consume` (the §10 credit charge — a READ-ONLY session still costs a credit per tool call, so gating it on `dopl.write` would make read-only agents free). **Neither is `sessionOnly`**: every MCP caller is an OAuth token, so a session gate would refuse the only caller there is. The pinned set is in `write-gate-coverage.test.ts`; adding a third is an edit there.
  - **`sessionOnly: true`.** Rejects ALL OAuth tokens (403 `SESSION_REQUIRED`) regardless of scope — destructive, credential-minting, or permission-mutating routes an agent must never reach. **All FOUR billing writes carry it**, `billing/cancel` included (added 2026-08-11 with the in-app cancel flow): unlike the portal handoff, that one ends the subscription on our own route, with no Stripe-hosted page a human has to click through.
- **THE EXECUTABLE AUTHORITY IS `src/shared/auth/write-gate-coverage.test.ts`, NOT PROSE.** It pins **three** sets: `writeScopeExempt`, method-level `sessionOnly`, and field-level `SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS`. Adding a destructive route without `sessionOnly`, or narrowing any set, fails the suite. **Re-count, never amend by memory:** `grep -rl "sessionOnly: true" src/app/api --include=route.ts | wc -l` → **38 route files, re-measured 2026-09-02** (27 on 2026-08-26, 21 on 2026-08-11; the number moves every wave, which is the argument for the command over the number). ⚠ **The 2026-09-02 jump is ELEVEN in two waves, not a drift**: the two T81 pin routes (R4), then the NINE app-only-deletion routes in the bullet below. ⚠ **This line said 26 UNDER TODAY'S DATE STAMP and the command answered 27** — the unlisted one is `agent-templates/[templateId]/route.ts`, and the pinned array in `write-gate-coverage.test.ts` held all 27 the whole time. A stamped number that was never run is worse than an unstamped one: it buys the next reader's trust. **Run the command.**
  - **The wave's addition is `PUT /api/knowledge/bases/[baseId]/channel-grants`** (⚠ this read "the 26th" and was off by one — see the count above; an ORDINAL into an unordered `grep` was never a durable way to name a route, so it names the route) (2026-08-26, Home Knowledge Panels M1, §9). **The reasoning is `POST /api/home/links`' verbatim — IT HANDS CONTENT TO A PERSON.** A grant at `visible` puts a whole knowledge base in front of every member of a channel, GUESTS INCLUDED (the channel lane reads exactly these rows), and `guest_write` additionally hands that person a pen. A `full`-profile session has Bash, can read the 90-day device token off disk, and would otherwise be one HTTP call from widening its own operator's audience. ⚠ **Per-METHOD, and the GET is the point:** reading which channels a base already reaches decides nothing, so `GET` on that file stays ungated.
  ⚠ **`--include=route.ts` is load-bearing** and was added when `billing/cancel/route.test.ts` began asserting the option it is gated by: without it the same grep answers 22, counting a TEST that names the flag as a route that carries it. The coverage test itself has always filtered to `route.ts`, so the two never disagreed — the prose command did.
- 🔒 **A RULE AN AGENT IS *TOLD* MUST HAVE A FENCE IN THE CODE — THE APP-ONLY-DELETION WAVE (2026-09-02, Samuel's ruling).** Every `_admin` tool in `packages/mcp-server` serves the sentence *"Deletion is app-only … there is no MCP path to it, for any role or token"* (`packages/mcp-server/src/delete-policy.ts › deleteAdminDescription`), and until this wave `packages/mcp-server/src/gating.ts › opRefusal` was the ONLY thing that made it true. ⚠ **THAT GUARDS ONE DOOR.** The MCP server reaches the app over LOOPBACK HTTP, a `full`-profile session has Bash and its own `dopl_at_*` bearer, and every REST `DELETE` the refusal covers was `withWorkspaceAuth(…, { minRole: "member" })` and nothing else — so the agent just told *"no role, scope or argument changes that"* could delete the row with one HTTP call. **A tool description is a PROMPT; `sessionOnly` is a FENCE.** The NINE routes that gained it: `knowledge/bases/[baseId]`, `knowledge/folders/[folderId]`, `knowledge/bases/[baseId]/folders-by-path`, `knowledge/entries/[entryId]`, `skills/[skillSlug]`, `chats/[chatId]`, `chats/folders/[folderId]`, `ontology/objects/[objectId]`, `ontology/clusters/[clusterId]`. (`agent-templates/[templateId]` is the tenth refused op and has carried it since 2026-08-22.)
  - ⚠ **`minRole` IS UNCHANGED, and that is the shape of the fix**: this adds a CALLER-TYPE gate, it does not raise the role floor. A member still deletes — in the app, behind the confirmation the whole rule is built on.
  - ⚠ **Per-METHOD throughout, and the ungated methods are the POINT.** The `GET`s and `PATCH`es stay open because `delete-policy.ts › DELETE_REFUSAL` redirects an agent to exactly those (*"Editing and rewriting are still available to you"*); gating them would close the alternative the refusal advertises.
  - **THE OP→ROUTE MAP IS EXECUTABLE: `src/shared/auth/app-only-delete-gate.test.ts`.** It parses `DELETE_BLOCKED_OPS` out of the MCP package's SOURCE TEXT (the `src` vitest project does not build `packages/`; same technique as `tools/parity-harness.ts`) and fails when a refused op has no REST route carrying the gate — so a NEW `_admin` delete op cannot ship advertising a fence that only `opRefusal` provides. It also drives each route's real `DELETE` export twice: agent token → 403 `SESSION_REQUIRED` with the service never reached, session caller → through.
  - ⚠ **`folders-by-path` IS THE SECOND DOOR ONTO THE SAME TWO ACTS** and the one an id-keyed sweep misses: its `?path=` resolves to a folder OR an entry, so gating only `folders/[folderId]` and `entries/[entryId]` would have left `delete_folder` and `delete_file` reachable by name.
- **Granularity is per-METHOD by default** — the `GET`s on the channels consent and members routes are deliberately ungated; polling and reading decide nothing. ⚠ The channels **trust** route was the third example here and is DELETED (2026-08-22, §6).
- ⚠ **THERE ARE TWO FIELD-LEVEL AXES ON `PATCH /channels/[channelId]` AND READING THEM AS ONE RULE IS THE BUG (2026-08-25).** CALLER-TYPE (`SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS`, below — may an OAuth token do this at all) lives in the ROUTE; ROLE (may this MEMBER do this) lives in the SERVICE, `channels/server/service-writes.ts › updateChannel`. They are orthogonal and neither implies the other.
  - The role split is: the four HEADER fields (`name`, `topic`, `visibility`, `archived`) require `canManageChannel`; **`infoCard` requires MEMBERSHIP only** — the Info tab's curated card is the channel's shared scratch surface, and §4A's ruling that a home channel is *"a relationship, not a tenancy"* (any member may mint its link, not the owner only) is the precedent it is taken from. It changes no visibility, no roster, no lifecycle and no fact (§5).
  - 🔒 **A MIXED PATCH TAKES THE STRICTER GATE, AND THE LOOSE SET IS DERIVED BY SUBTRACTION** from `› MANAGED_CHANNEL_FIELDS` — so a fifth field added to `ChannelUpdateSchema` is MANAGED by default and the compiler carries the choice. **A future loose field is a conscious edit to that constant and to `channels/server/service-info-card.test.ts`**, which drives the real function rather than reading the constant (§14: a pin on a symbol is not a pin).
  - **`infoCard` is deliberately NOT `sessionOnly`.** It writes no credential and reaches nobody; an agent tidying its own channel's card is a capability with a use, and gating it would spend one for nothing.
- **THE RULE: gate the METHOD by default; gate the FIELD when the method carries writes an agent may legitimately make.** The one CALLER-TYPE field gate is `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/route.ts › SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS = ["visibility"]`. **A field gate is invisible to the method-level tripwire**, so the coverage test separately asserts the file set is exactly that route, that the constant names `visibility`, that the handler reads `auth.agentTokenId`, and that it throws `SESSION_REQUIRED` — a constant nothing reads would satisfy a file list while gating nothing. **A second field-gated route is a conscious edit to that test.**
- **`PATCH /channels/[channelId]/members` is `sessionOnly` because the agent tool profile is a CONTAINMENT control, not a preference** — the setting relied on for containment must not be writable by the thing it contains, durably. **Do not generalize to the next route — check what else the method writes.**
  - ⚠ **IT CARRIES TWO FIELDS SINCE 2026-08-19 AND IS STILL METHOD-GATED**, which is that rule being applied rather than skipped: `favorite` (§5) joined `agentToolProfile`, so the two no longer coincide by "sole field" and the question was re-asked. The answer to *is this a write an agent may legitimately make?* is NO for both, for different reasons — containment for one, and for the other that a favourite is the OPERATOR's own sidebar shortcut list, which an agent reordering is not a capability with a use. A field gate would have bought an agent-writable `favorite` and cost a second `SESSION_ONLY_FIELDS` route.
- 🔒 **A ROUTE'S OWN FLOOR IS NOT ITS CONTENT'S FLOOR — CHASE THE ROW UP TO WHAT OWNS IT (2026-08-26).** `GET /api/knowledge/entries/[entryId]` runs at `withWorkspaceAuth`'s viewer default and `knowledge/server/service-entries.ts › getEntry` checked `assertSameWorkspace` and NOTHING ELSE, so **any workspace viewer could read the body of an entry inside a `visibility='private'` base they cannot see** — the service-role route strictly WIDER than the RLS policy behind it, which correctly requires `public OR created_by = auth.uid()`. It bypassed the M-10 arm the container lock lights up (step 1 of §4) and the AUDIENCE CEILING (§10) with it, and `resolveEntryRefs` (`?ids=`, 100 per request) applied `canSeeBase` but not the ceiling. ⚠ **"You need the id" was never the fence**: ontology attributes of `kind:"knowledge"` ship raw entry-id arrays and `dopl_ontology` is an auto-allowed read tool. **Both now compose `getBaseById`** — the foundational lookup that holds visibility AND the ceiling — and the refusal is `EntryNotFoundError`, so "no such entry", "its base is invisible to you" and "its base is outside your audience" are ONE answer. Pinned by `knowledge/server/service-entries-visibility.test.ts`.
  - ⚠ **THE EVIDENCE WAS ALREADY IN THE TREE AND NOBODY GENERALISED IT.** `knowledge/server/export.ts › buildEntryFile` had bolted its own `getBaseById` on beside the comment *"getEntry only checks workspace — gate on base visibility too"* — one caller fixing itself instead of the door, which is why `service-bases.ts`'s docblock claim that *"fencing here fences the surface"* was FALSE at the time it was written. **A gate that one caller has to remember is not a gate**; that docblock now says so.
- 🔒 **THE MCP TRANSPORT BOOTS ON THE CREDENTIAL LOCK, NOT ON THE HEADER (2026-08-26).** `shared/auth/mcp-transport-pin.ts › resolveTransportWorkspaceId` — key lock first, `X-Workspace-Id` second, blank is not a pin on either. `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts` read `headerPin ?? apiKeyWorkspaceId`, correct while the field had no producer and **backwards the moment B1 gave it one**: a client-supplied header outranked the credential's own lock, deciding the workspace directory AND B3's `lockedTo`. ⚠ **A contradicting header is IGNORED here, not 403'd** — this is the transport boot, not a content route; the loopback that follows carries the locked credential, so `withWorkspaceAuth` still answers `API_KEY_WORKSPACE_MISMATCH`. One refusal, in the layer that owns the rows.
- **Rate limiting: ONE budget, both doors.** `withUserAuth`'s bearer branch limits on the same subject the MCP transport uses (`mcp:<tokenId>`), so a token has one budget across `/api/mcp` and every REST route. It runs **before** the sessionOnly/write-scope gates, so requests that would 403 still spend budget. Fail-closed: DB error → 429. Sessions never enter this branch. `/api/oauth/register` and `/token` carry per-IP limits (`shared/auth/oauth-rate-limit.ts`).
- **Bearer Supabase JWTs are SESSION callers.** `withUserAuth` routes by exact prefix: `dopl_at_*` → agent branch; anything else is verified locally as a Supabase JWT with cookie-identical semantics (the bundled SPA authenticates this way). **Never key agent-vs-user behavior off "has an Authorization header" — key off `agentTokenId`.**
- **Middleware verifies locally, never over the network.** `src/proxy.ts` must not call `supabase.auth.getUser()`; use `getClaims()` (local JWKS verification). Two traps, pinned by `proxy.test.ts`/`proxy-claims.test.ts`: `getClaims()` can throw a plain `Error` (not an `AuthError`, so not converted — wrap it, treat a throw as no session), and **cookie refresh depends on calling it with NO argument** (that path runs `getSession()` first, rotating near-expiry cookies onto the response).
- **THE SIGN-IN FORM IS ONE PAGE, `/authenticate`** — `src/app/(auth)/authenticate/page.tsx`, in the `(auth)` route group; `?mode=signup` opens it in sign-up. **Re-verified against the tree 2026-08-25.** `/login` and `/signup` survive as REDIRECTORS that 307 into it preserving their query (`(auth)/forward-query.ts › forwardQuery`), because the world links to them: every bounce destination in this codebase names `/login`, and the landing page shipped `/signup` CTAs for months. All three are named in `shared/auth/auth-routes.ts › AUTH_ENTRY_ROUTES`, and **`public-routes.ts › PUBLIC_ROUTES` SPREADS that list** — so an entry added there is published by the same edit. All three are session-AWARE: a visitor with a session is bounced into the app BEFORE the redirector can hop, honouring a validated `?redirectTo=`.
  - ⚠ **THE Q4 LOOP BREAKER COUNTS `/login` AND `/authenticate` TOGETHER** — `auth-routes.ts › LOOP_COUNTED_AUTH_ROUTES`, consumed only through `› isLoopCountedAuthRoute` (`src/proxy.ts`; the cookie itself is `shared/auth/login-bounce.ts › LOGIN_BOUNCE_COOKIE`). `/login` is the only path server components redirect to, so it is where an outage cycle arrives — but when the breaker trips and SERVES `/login`, that page 307s onward, and a counter that did not follow would let the signed-in bounce restart the cycle with the breaker blind to it. **`/signup` stays uncounted: nothing redirects to it, so it cannot be one end of a cycle — which is why adding a `redirect("/signup")` anywhere puts an uncounted path at one end of a possible one.**
  - `authenticate`, `login` and `signup` are all in both `config/index.ts › RESERVED_WORKSPACE_SLUGS` and `shared/lib/url/website-retirement.ts › RESERVED_TOP_LEVEL`.
  - **This paragraph was REFACTOR-FINDINGS F-209 from 2026-08-18 to 2026-08-25** — it described the page for a week while the rework sat uncommitted in the main checkout, so every worktree measured a tree that disagreed with it. **The doc was ahead of the COMMIT, not of the product.**
- ⚠ **`PUBLIC_ROUTES` AND `SELF_AUTH_ROUTES` NO LONGER LIVE IN `src/proxy.ts` — they are `src/shared/auth/public-routes.ts › PUBLIC_ROUTES` / `› SELF_AUTH_ROUTES` (extracted 2026-08-23).** `proxy.ts` was AT the 500-line cap and a file at the cap cannot absorb a new entry, let alone the paragraph explaining one (§1); the home-channel wave needed two entries and a paragraph. **The split is DATA vs BEHAVIOUR: `proxy.ts` still owns every decision made with the lists**, this module owns only which paths are in which class and why. **Both match with `pathname.startsWith`, so an entry is a PREFIX** — before adding one, check that no gated route's pathname begins with those bytes. The worked hazard is live: `/api/home/link/` (singular) is public and `/api/home/links` (plural) must not be (§4A). Pinned by `proxy.test.ts`.
- **`config.matcher` in `src/proxy.ts` is an auth boundary written as a negative lookahead.** A path may be excluded only if *nothing below the auth call can change what it does*. Two easy-to-miss failures: a public but **session-AWARE** path fails it, and so does one whose only interaction is the **Supabase cookie refresh** (dropping it shortens sessions). Pinned by `proxy.test.ts` section 6, which compiles the real exported matcher and asserts both directions.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §9, §9.1, §9.4.

## 4. Workspace resolution — fail-closed

`features/workspaces/server/service.ts › resolveActiveWorkspace(userId, headerWorkspaceId)` is THE resolver behind `withWorkspaceAuth` and `GET /api/workspaces/me`:

1. 🔒 **CREDENTIAL WORKSPACE LOCK** (in `withWorkspaceAuth`, before the resolver) — a contradicting target → 403 `API_KEY_WORKSPACE_MISMATCH`, and the lock **OVERRIDES** rather than merely validating: a locked caller cannot fall through to step 3's auto-target either. ✅ **THIS STOPPED BEING "DEAD SCAFFOLDING; PRESERVED" ON 2026-08-26 — IT HAS A PRODUCER (Home Knowledge Panels M5, plan §4.4 B1; Samuel's RULING 4).** `mcp_tokens.workspace_id` (migration `mcp_token_workspace_lock`, §12) is read by `shared/auth/mcp-oauth.ts › validateAccessToken` and forwarded as `apiKeyWorkspaceId` by **both** wrapper families — `shared/auth/with-auth.ts` and `shared/auth/with-mcp-transport-auth.ts`. `shared/auth/mcp-container-token.ts › issueContainerToken` mints them: the desktop asks for one when it spawns a session into a `kind='link'` container that has a PEER in it, and revokes it at session end.
   - ⚠ **THE ENFORCEMENT CHAIN WAS INTACT AND THE PRODUCER WAS MISSING AT *THREE* LAYERS, WHICH IS WORSE THAN THIS BULLET USED TO SAY.** The `api_keys` table it was written for was dropped by `20260609000000_drop_api_key_auth.sql`; `with-auth.ts` never wrote the field in ANY of its three auth branches (its own docblock said *"always undefined"*); and `with-mcp-transport-auth.ts` passed a hardcoded `null`. So the 403 above, and every gate downstream, had been unreachable code rather than an unused feature. **Re-derive, never quote:** `grep -rn apiKeyWorkspaceId src/shared/auth`.
   - 🔒 **IT IS THE ONLY PART OF THE AUDIENCE CEILING THAT BINDS A PROCESS'S CHILDREN.** A `full` profile has Bash and the operator's 90-day device token is on disk, so an agent can issue any loopback HTTP the operator could — which is why the MCP directory lock (§10) and the desktop grant gate (§11) are TRIPWIRES. A lock on the TOKEN ROW is different in kind: everything the agent shells out to can only present the credential it was handed, and that credential names one workspace.
   - 🔒 ✅ **IT LIT UP EVERY M-10 GATE AT ONCE AND THAT WAS A DEFECT, NOT THE DESIGN — F-336/F-333, RULED BY SAMUEL AND FIXED 2026-08-27.** This bullet used to say the visibility gates biting was intended: `apiKeyWorkspaceId` non-null made `knowledge/server/service-shared.ts › canSeeBase` refuse a **PRIVATE** base outright — the caller's OWN included — and the mirrors in chats, skills and agent-templates, plus the `fromWorkspaceKey` branches in the three write services, did the same. **The lock answers WHICH WORKSPACE; visibility answers WHICH ROWS INSIDE IT.** Using one for the other made the operator's own private base unreadable by the operator's own agent *even when granted `agent_only`* (the visibility gate 404s before layer A's grant row is read), and made every `containerCopyDraft` template invisible to the agents it was copied for. **The predicates now ask `shared/auth/credential-audience.ts › isSharedCredential`, never the lock.** `mcp_tokens.workspace_lock_kind` (migration `mcp_token_workspace_lock_kind`, §12) says WHAT KIND of lock a row carries; `'container_session'` = one human's session, narrowed — it reads that human's private rows. **Anything else, INCLUDING AN UNSTATED KIND, is a SHARED credential and keeps the original refusal verbatim**, so a workspace-scoped key reintroduced later inherits the narrow rule by default. ⚠ **The 403 in step 1, and every other WORKSPACE gate, is untouched.** Re-derive: `grep -rn 'isSharedCredential\|apiKeyWorkspaceLockKind' src`.
   - 🔒 ✅ **AND THE LOCK IS ENFORCED IN *BOTH* AUTH FAMILIES SINCE 2026-08-26 — IT WAS ENFORCED IN ONE.** Step 1 above is `withWorkspaceAuth`'s. The OTHER family — `withUserAuth` + `workspaces/server/segment.ts › resolveApiWorkspace` / `resolveApiWorkspaceAccess`, i.e. everything under `/api/workspaces/[workspaceSlug]/**` — had **zero references to `apiKeyWorkspaceId`**, so `mcp-container-token.ts`'s claim that *"that credential cannot name another workspace"* was a property of ONE WRAPPER, not of the credential. **The reachable walk:** `POST /api/boot` with NO segment answers `ensureDefaultWorkspace` — the operator's HOME workspace id AND its canonical segment — to any valid credential; that segment is then the argument the 19 `resolveApiWorkspace` route files take (access-matrix, members-with-emails, overview, overview-series, teams, invitations, join-link, my-access, member activity). **The comparison now lives ONCE, in `resolveWorkspaceSegmentForUser` (`› withinKeyLock`), beside the role floor the guest wave put there for exactly the same reason**, and boot's PROVISIONING branch refuses a locked caller outright before it reads anything.
     - ⚠ **IT IS THREADED BY EVERY CALLER, NOT DEFAULTED, AND THAT IS THE TRADE.** `ApiWorkspaceOpts.apiKeyWorkspaceId` has no fail-closed default available to it — the resolver cannot see a credential nobody handed it, and a request-scoped ambient would answer "no lock" on a scope miss, i.e. fail OPEN. So the resolver holds the COMPARISON and `workspaces/server/api-workspace-floor.test.ts` (its caller-scan block) holds the THREADING: every route file in the family must name the field, and every resolve call in them must pass it. **A pin on the fence is not a pin on its callers; here both are pins.**
     - ⚠ **A REFUSAL IS `null` → the caller's existing 404**, the same answer a non-member gets — so the lock cannot be used to learn that a workspace exists, and no route grows a new error shape. Blank-after-trim reads as NO lock on both doors.
   - **A CHILD CREDENTIAL IS NEVER MORE THAN ITS PARENT** — minted for the caller's own user id, under the caller's own proved membership, with the same scopes a device token carries, and then NARROWED. There is no input to the minter that widens anything. `POST/DELETE /api/auth/mcp-container-token` are BOTH `sessionOnly`, and that is the security property rather than a copy of the device route's options: a bearer that could reach the mint would ask for a lock somewhere else, and one that could reach the revoke would kill its own lock and re-run unlocked. Pinned by `shared/auth/mcp-container-token.test.ts`.
2. **`X-Workspace-Id` header — UUID only.** Blank or non-UUID (a slug) → 400 `WORKSPACE_INVALID`. Never coerced to "no header", never a 500. `withWorkspaceAuth({workspaceIdFromQuery:true})` lets `?workspaceId=` slot in at the same priority (header wins if both) — that option exists for header-less `<a download>` export routes.
3. **No header → active STANDARD memberships:** exactly one auto-targets; **0 or 2+ → 400 `WORKSPACE_REQUIRED`**.
   - ⚠ **"STANDARD" is load-bearing since 2026-08-23 (§4A), and RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-25 against the tree after the bound claim landed** — `service.ts › resolveActiveWorkspace` still runs `listWorkspacesWithRoleForUser` through `isStandardWorkspace` BEFORE it counts, and `workspace-kind.test.ts` now also pins the SOLO (one-member) container against every implicit pick. Nothing in this bullet moved. `resolveActiveWorkspace` runs `listWorkspacesWithRoleForUser` through `workspaces/types.ts › isStandardWorkspace` before it counts, so `kind='link'` home-channel containers are **neither auto-target candidates nor entries in the `WORKSPACE_REQUIRED` list**. A user with one standard workspace and N link containers still auto-targets the standard one; a **link-only user gets `WORKSPACE_REQUIRED` with `workspaces: []`**, exactly as a membership-less user does. **A link container is only ever reached by EXPLICIT addressing** — step 2's header, `?workspaceId=`, or MCP `workspace=` — and step 2 stays unfiltered so that door works. Pinned by `workspaces/server/workspace-kind.test.ts`.

- **There is NO silent default-workspace fallback.** `findDefaultWorkspaceForUser` is oldest-OWNED and is **forbidden in auth resolution**. **THREE sanctioned uses** — signup-bootstrap (`ensureDefaultWorkspace`), the Stripe billing-grandfather path, and, since 2026-08-23, the **link-container billing reroute** (`billing/server/credits-service.ts › resolveBillingTarget`, §4A). ⚠ **Since 2026-08-26 the reroute passes it the CONTAINER OWNER's user id, not the caller's** — same sanctioned use, different subject; the owner comes from `workspaces/server/repository.ts › findActiveOwnerUserId`.
  - ⚠ **It now FILTERS to standard workspaces, and that changed its shape.** `workspaces/server/repository.ts › findDefaultWorkspaceForUser` runs both branches through `isStandardWorkspace` (the legacy literal-slug `"default"` hit and the oldest-owned scan), so a link container can never become anyone's default. **The kind filter runs in CODE, not SQL** — it was written while the column was unapplied (it has applied since; §12) and stays in code because one predicate, `isStandardWorkspace`, is the §4A rule — which is why that scan carries **no `.limit(1)`**: the oldest owned row may be a container the pick has to skip. `› countWorkspacesOwnedBy` filters for the same reason: a container must not make an unambiguous legacy-subscription mapping look ambiguous to the webhook.
  - **The SQL mirror is a separate edit, and it is APPLIED** (re-measured 2026-08-24, §12). `supabase/migrations/20260823160000_default_workspace_kind_guard.sql` narrows `ensure_default_workspace`'s existing-workspace SELECT to `kind = 'standard'`. **Two guards now, not one** — this bullet said the app-side filter was the whole guard, and that stopped being true the evening the file ran.
- **404 ordering is load-bearing.** A missing workspace answers before membership is judged, so "not a member" and "does not exist" stay indistinguishable and existence is never an oracle. The two reads run in parallel.
- **Envelope is flat `{error, message, workspaces?}`**, surfaced verbatim by `@dopl/client` and the web `apiRequest`. `WORKSPACE_REQUIRED` lists `{name, slug, role}` per membership.
- **Any UI reading a workspace-scoped route MUST send the header** (`apiRequest({workspaceId})`) or the fetch fails closed for multi-workspace users.
- **A boot endpoint never guesses a workspace.** `POST /api/boot` with `{segment}` resolves THAT segment and nothing else; with no body it takes the documented provisioning path, gated on onboarding, idempotent — **200, never 201**. 🔒 ⚠ **AND THE PROVISIONING PATH IS CLOSED TO A CONTAINER-LOCKED CREDENTIAL (2026-08-26)**: that branch's whole job is to NAME a workspace the caller did not name, which is the one thing a lock exists to stop, and the id + canonical segment it answered were the first step of walking out of a container. A locked caller gets `null` → the route's plain 404. **Refused, not redirected to the locked workspace** — boot is the SPA's launch read and the SPA presents a session cookie, which carries no lock and is untouched.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §9 (Workspace resolution), §7.
