## 4A. Home channels & workspace KINDS (2026-08-23, INVERTED 2026-08-24)

> A **HOME CHANNEL** is an account-level channel carried by a hidden **link container**: a `kind='link'` workspace holding exactly ONE channel and **ONE OR MORE** members. **CREATION IS FIRST-CLASS AND COMES FIRST** — "New channel" mints a SOLO container (one member, one PRIVATE NON-DIRECT channel) which the operator works in with their own agents, and which is a finished thing on its own. **PEERS JOIN AFTER THE FACT**, and by exactly one route: a link **BOUND** to that container (`channel_links.workspace_id`), whose claim inserts the claimer as a member. **THE ROSTER HAS NO CEILING (Samuel's ruling, 2026-08-26)** — it grows ONE PERSON AT A TIME, each by their own single-use link.

- 👥 **THE ROSTER IS A LIST ON THE PAYLOAD, AND `peer` IS ITS HEAD (2026-08-26, the ruling's second half — F-307 RESOLVED).** `home/types.ts › HomeChannel.peers: HomePeer[]` carries EVERY other member, ordered `joined_at ASC, user_id ASC` by `home/server/repository-containers.ts › listContainerPeers`. ⚠ **THE `user_id` TIEBREAKER IS LOAD-BEARING**: `joined_at` alone is not a TOTAL order, and two members admitted in the same millisecond (or legacy rows carrying NULL) would flip between loads — which was the bug. **`peer` SURVIVES as a DERIVED projection of `peers[0]`**, computed in exactly ONE place (`service-reads.ts › hydrateChannels`) and never independently; two fields that can disagree about who is in a room is what F-307 was.
  - 🔒 **KEEPING `peer` IS A §8 DECISION, NOT BACK-COMPAT SENTIMENT.** A cache entry written before this wave HAS `peer` and LACKS `peers`, so a client that knew only the list would fall back to `[]` and paint EVERY channel as solo on the first paint after the upgrade — "Just you", the agent glyph, no faces. **That is a FALSE sentence about who is in the room**, where degrading to one face is merely the old correct answer. The client's fallback is therefore a two-field MERGE, not a plain `?? EMPTY_PEERS`.
  - **ONE READ SITE, AND IT IS PINNED AS AN ABSENCE.** `pages/home/home-rows.ts › channelPeople` is the only thing on the home page that names `.peers`; `› channelTitle` (two names, then `+N`), `› channelSubline` ("Just you" / the address / `N people`) and every component go through it. ⚠ **It is an ACCESSOR, which §8 normally forbids** — the exemption is bought by `home-rows.test.ts`, which reads the directory's SOURCE and fails if any other file names `.peers` outside a comment. **Take the exemption only with the pin.**
  - **THE ROW DRAWS A STACK AT 2+** (`pages/home/relationship-list.tsx` → `shared/ui/avatar-stack.tsx › AvatarStack`, `size="sm"` `max={3}`; the Info-tab header does the same at `md`). `AvatarStack` GAINED a size (default `xs`, so its four older callers are byte-identical) rather than the home page forking a fifth stack. ⚠ **The stack's sizes are `avatar.tsx › SIZE`'s to the pixel**, or a row changes height the moment a second person joins. ⚠ **`+N` counts what is HIDDEN, not the total.**
  - ⚠ **THE INFO TAB'S EMAIL ROW IS A ONE-PERSON ROW AND IS DROPPED ABOVE ONE** (Samuel's call, 2026-08-26). It was already absent on a SOLO channel — a row answering a question nobody asked; with three members it answers about the WRONG person, since the header names two others. **Every member's address is one section down in the roster, beside their face.** Do not "fix" this by stacking N Email rows: the card is curated and capped, and the roster IS that list.
- 🔴 **THE TWO-MEMBER CAP IS RETIRED, AND WHAT REPLACED IT IS THE TOKEN, NOT A BIGGER NUMBER (2026-08-26).** MVP was "0 or 1 peer" and the `enforce_link_container_member_cap` trigger held it; `20260830120000_link_container_multi_member.sql` drops the trigger and the function, `mintContainerLink` lost its `>= 2` → 409 gate, `claimBoundLink` lost its capacity step, and **`LINK_CONTAINER_FULL` no longer exists anywhere in this tree** (F-306 closed by deletion). **What bounds growth now is that a link is SINGLE-USE and at most ONE may be open per container** — one token admits one named person, and adding the next is a fresh mint. **Do not re-derive a cap from a roster length in any layer**: the UI's Add-person affordance persists at every size, and a component that hides it is asserting a limit the server does not have.

- ⚠ **THE 2026-08-23 SHAPE IS STILL LIVE AND IS THE "LEGACY UNBOUND" PATH.** Before the inversion a link was minted with no container and the CLAIM minted a two-member container plus a DIRECT channel. **Measured 2026-08-24 against the live project: open claimable tokens with `workspace_id IS NULL` exist**, so that branch is kept on purpose. **NULL = mint a container, non-NULL = join the named one** — one column, two claim branches. Nothing can PRODUCE an unbound link any more (`schema.ts › HomeLinkMintSchema` requires `workspaceId`), so the branch only ever shrinks; it may not be deleted until that count reaches zero.
- **THE TWO CLAIM BRANCHES ARE TWO FUNCTIONS, AND THE FRONT DOOR IS `home/server/service-writes.ts › claimLink`** (2026-08-25). It judges the TOKEN — unknown 404, dead 410, both properties of the token and neither dependent on shape — then dispatches on `link.workspace_id`: null stays in `› claimUnboundLink`, otherwise `home/server/service-claim-bound.ts › claimBoundLink`. ⚠ **THEIR ROLLBACK STORIES ARE OPPOSITES, WHICH IS WHY THEY ARE NOT ONE FUNCTION WITH A FLAG:** the unbound branch CREATED the workspace and may delete it, the bound branch was HANDED one and must never delete it — the owner's transcript is in there, and a stranger's failed claim may not take it. Collapsing them would put the destructive rollback on the default path. Same sibling-service argument as `channels/server/service-await-workspace.ts`.
  - **The bound claim's order is its correctness argument** and its docblock is the authority: self-claim 400 → dedup on MEMBERSHIP → spend the use → workspace member row → channel member row (via `channels/server/service › addMember`, acting as the container OWNER, because the claimer is not yet a member of anything) → claim row → revoke the link. **The only compensation is `deleteContainerMember`**; there is no workspace rollback on this path. ⚠ **SEVEN STEPS SINCE 2026-08-26, NOT EIGHT** — a CAPACITY step used to sit between the dedup and the spend, and the raise-to-409 translation that backed it (`isContainerFullRaise`) went with it.

- **A LINK CONTAINER IS A REAL WORKSPACE AND A REAL MEMBERSHIP — that is the design, not an implementation leak.** Rows in `workspaces` and `workspace_members`, addressed by the ordinary `{slug}-{publicId}` segment, read by the ordinary channels routes, realtime and MCP. **Every fence in this system is keyed on `workspace_id`** (RLS `is_current_workspace_member`, the realtime subscription filter, the channel-child workspace guard trigger, MCP workspace targeting, credits); a workspace-less channel would need a second, parallel statement of each of them. Why/history: ENGINEERING.md, 2026-08-23 stratum.
- **`workspaces.kind` is `'standard' | 'link'`** (`workspaces/types.ts › WorkspaceKind`). ⚠ **THE COLUMN IS APPLIED** (2026-08-23 evening; the measurement is §12's, not this line's) and is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'standard'`, so live rows carry it. **ABSENT `kind` STILL READS AS STANDARD** — the predicate keeps the default because a narrowed projection, an older server or a test fixture omits it.
  - ⚠ **THE PREDICATE IS POSITIVE: `(kind ?? "standard") === "standard"`, NEVER `!== "link"`** (flipped 2026-08-24 in BOTH copies — `workspaces/types.ts › isStandardWorkspace` and its hand-mirror `packages/dopl-client/src/types.ts › isStandardWorkspace`, F-295). The negative spelling admits every kind added to the union LATER: the next one would be silently standard in the rail, the switcher, `list_workspaces` and default resolution, with no error anywhere. **A listing predicate must let a value IN, not fail to keep one out.** Pinned in both suites (`workspaces/server/workspace-kind.test.ts`, `packages/mcp-server/src/workspace-kind.test.ts`), each asserting an unknown kind is NOT standard.
  - ⚠ **MEMBER COUNT IS NOT PART OF IT.** A SOLO (one-member) container is a link container everywhere — not "really just my workspace". Same suites pin it against the plausible-looking special case.
- 🔒 **`GET /api/workspaces` IS UNFILTERED, BY CONTRACT.** It returns link containers alongside standard ones **on purpose**: `dopl-desktop-app/main/channel-listener.js` fans over that exact list to decide what to watch, and a home channel that is not watched is a home channel whose messages never arrive. **Do not "fix" it by filtering at the route** — that would silently unwatch every relationship, with no error anywhere.
- **SO EVERY UI CONSUMER FILTERS, AND THROUGH ONE PREDICATE: `workspaces/types.ts › isStandardWorkspace`.** Never a hand-rolled `kind === "link"`, never `kind !== undefined`. **Measured 2026-08-23, the consumers are:**

  | Consumer | What it drops the containers out of |
  |---|---|
  | `app-shell.tsx › selectWorkspaces` (SPA) | the workspace rail |
  | `workspace-switcher-core.tsx › switchable` | the switch list — ⚠ `active` stays off the UNFILTERED list, so the header can still name where you are when you ARE inside one |
  | `pages/home/index.tsx › selectStandardWorkspaces` | the home page's own "create/pick a workspace" list |
  | `pricing-content.tsx` | the sole-workspace test — a container must not make a one-workspace user look multi-workspace and skip the billing status read |
  | `workspace-danger-zone-core.tsx` | where a post-delete redirect lands — landing on a container navigates into a workspace with no UI |
  | `packages/mcp-server/src/workspace-directory.ts › getWorkspaceList` | `list_workspaces`, and the `WORKSPACE_REQUIRED` choice list `› noWorkspaceError` prints |
  | `packages/mcp-server/src/factory.ts › listable` | the SOLE-MEMBERSHIP auto-target at boot |
  | `packages/mcp-server/src/server.ts › listableDirectory` | the server instructions block |

  Re-derive, never quote: `grep -rn isStandardWorkspace src packages apps --exclude-dir=dist`.
- ⚠ **`workspace-directory.ts › resolveWorkspaceRef` DELIBERATELY DOES NOT FILTER**, and that asymmetry is the feature: `workspace=<container id or slug>` is how an agent acting in a home channel addresses its container, and the container is absent from every LISTING. **The predicate is NOT an authz check.** Authz is membership, everywhere, unchanged.
- 🔒 **A LINK CONTAINER IS CLOSED TO EVERY WORKSPACE-LEVEL ADD, AT EVERY SIZE.** Invitations, join links and direct member-add all refuse a `kind='link'` target with **`LINK_CONTAINER_CLOSED`** (`workspaces/server/authz.ts › assertMemberAddable`) — anybody added that way would read a private relationship's whole transcript, with no single-use token proving the operator meant to admit *them*. ⚠ **THIS BULLET SAID "CAPPED AT TWO MEMBERS AND CLOSED" UNTIL 2026-08-26, AND ONLY THE SECOND HALF SURVIVED.** The two were always separate rules doing separate jobs: the CAP bounded HOW MANY (retired — see the section head), the CLOSED refusal bounds HOW, and it is untouched. **The link-claim lane is still the only door in.**
  - ⚠ **THIS REVERSES THE 2026-08-23 IMMUTABILITY RULE (Samuel, 2026-08-24), DELIBERATE — do not reinstate.** That rule said a container's roster is *"exactly the pair the claim minted, forever"*, and the error code was `LINK_CONTAINER_IMMUTABLE`. **Its premise was that the roster is COMPLETE AT BIRTH**, which was true when the only way to get a container was a two-person claim. The inversion made a container start with ONE member, so "immutable" became a false description of a thing that is designed to grow exactly once. **The rename is the reversal made visible:** an old name asserting a property the code no longer has is worse than no name.
  - **WHAT SURVIVES IS THE REASON, NOT THE RULE — AND IT HAS NOW SURVIVED TWICE.** 2026-08-24 turned a FREEZE (never any add) into a CAP (at most two), keeping the same refusal on every path. 2026-08-26 retired the CAP too. **The reason that outlived both is that an UNPROVEN add is the harm** — not a headcount — so what is left is the refusal on every path that cannot show a single-use token bound to that container, plus the one admitted exception below.
  - **THE ONE ADMITTED ADD IS THE BOUND CLAIM, AND IT DOES NOT ROUTE THROUGH `assertMemberAddable`.** `home/server/service-claim-bound.ts › claimBoundLink` writes the member row itself, having proved possession of a single-use token bound to that exact container. The workspace-level paths have no such proof and must never become a second door. ⚠ **That bypass is pinned as an ABSENCE** — `workspaces/server/link-container-guard.test.ts` reads the claim module's source and asserts it names no `assertMemberAddable`, because a "tidy-up" routing every member write through the shared guard would silently brick Add-person and no mock could tell.
  - 🔴 **THERE IS NO LONGER ANY DATABASE ENFORCEMENT OF A MEMBER COUNT, and that too is a MEASUREMENT rather than a claim this file can make** (§12's doctrine applied to code): `grep -rn enforce_link_container_member_cap supabase/migrations` now returns **two** files — `20260824120000`, which created the trigger, and `20260830120000_link_container_multi_member.sql`, which DROPS it and the function. **Read the later one; an applied migration is history and is never edited.** Until 2026-08-26 this bullet said the trigger was the fence and the service checks were the friendlier sentence in front of it. **Both are gone.** ⚠ **THE PRECEDING TWO BULLETS ARE NOT WEAKENED BY THAT** — the fence that keeps a stranger out was always `assertMemberAddable`, which never counted anything.
  - **REMOVAL AND DEPARTURE STAY ALLOWED, unchanged by either reversal.** A member who leaves drops the container by one — every size is an ordinary state — and the survivors can mint a fresh link and invite somebody else. That is the intended way a relationship changes shape. ⚠ **DEPARTURE-IS-REMOVAL still carries the `LINK_MINT_FORBIDDEN` floor's whole weight** (`member`+ to mint): with no cap, a surviving GUEST would otherwise be able to admit strangers one after another with nothing counting.
  - **The channel INSIDE it is a SEPARATE question, and the answer changed with the inversion.** ⚠ **A container minted by "New channel" holds a PRIVATE NON-DIRECT channel, so `channels/server/http-mapping.ts › DIRECT_CHANNEL_IMMUTABLE` does NOT apply to it — and that is precisely what makes Add-person possible**, since `addMember` refuses a direct channel outright. **Only a LEGACY unbound-claim container holds a DIRECT channel**, and in those the DM immutability still bites, which is one more reason nothing can mint an unbound link any more. **Owner-only delete is ordinary role policy** in both shapes. Three guards, three reasons, and now two channel shapes; do not collapse them.
- **ROUTES AND THE SINGULAR/PLURAL SPLIT, which is the whole auth boundary.** `shared/auth/public-routes.ts › PUBLIC_ROUTES` carries `/link/` (the claim page) and `/api/home/link/` — **SINGULAR** — whose `…/info` lookup is narrowed server-side to a display name and three booleans (`home/server/service-reads.ts › getLinkPublicInfo`; the claim under the same prefix is still `withUserAuth`). ⚠ **`/api/home/links` — PLURAL, the creator's own mint/list/revoke — is deliberately NOT public** and stays behind the session gate. A one-character prefix edit here makes a private surface public. **`/api/home/channels` (2026-08-24) shares neither prefix** and is likewise not public; `proxy.test.ts` pins the set.
  - ⚠ **`getLinkPublicInfo` DID NOT GROW A CHANNEL NAME WHEN LINKS BECAME BOUND, and must not.** A bound token names a private channel and its holder is unauthenticated by definition — "you are being invited to #q3-fundraise" is a leak wearing a nicety's clothes. Pinned in `home/server/service-reads.test.ts`.
  - **THE THREE WRITE GATES, and they are not the same (Samuel's rulings, 2026-08-24).** `POST /api/home/channels` is **NOT `sessionOnly`** — an agent token may create a home channel, matching `POST /api/workspaces`, because a container the caller is alone in reaches nobody. `POST /api/home/links` **IS `sessionOnly`**: it mints a credential that reaches a PERSON, which is the line. And **ANY MEMBER of a container may mint that link**, not the owner only — a home channel is a relationship, not a tenancy. ⚠ **THIS BULLET USED TO FINISH "so the CAP bounds the outcome rather than the role does", AND THAT CLAUSE IS DEAD (2026-08-26).** With the cap retired, what bounds the outcome is the SINGLE-USE token (one mint, one person) and the `member`+ mint floor below (`home/server/service-writes.ts › mintContainerLink`).
    - 🔒 ⚠ **"ANY MEMBER" MEANS `member`+ SINCE 2026-08-26, AND A GUEST MAY NOT MINT.** That ruling was made when `member` WAS the floor role. `meetsMinRole("guest","guest")` is true, so a guest passed the grant-above-self guard and only the two-member cap refused — and **departure-is-removal** (below) lets a container drop back to ONE member, so a surviving GUEST could hand a third party a link into the operator's transcript. The floor is an explicit `meetsMinRole(minter.role,"member")` → 403 `LINK_MINT_FORBIDDEN`, **separate from** grant-above-self's 403 `GRANT_ABOVE_SELF`: a `member` clears the floor and is still refused an `admin` grant. Pinned by `home/server/service-writes-granted-role.test.ts`. ⚠ **SINCE 2026-08-26 THIS FLOOR IS THE WHOLE CASE, NOT THE BETTER HALF OF TWO.** The cap is retired, so the accident that used to refuse a guest whenever the container happened to be full is gone; without this 403 a guest could admit strangers indefinitely.
    - 🔒 ⚠ **AN OPEN LINK IS REUSED ONLY WHEN ITS `granted_role` MATCHES THE ONE ASKED FOR (2026-08-26).** M3's role picker was a silent no-op on the reuse branch: `mintContainerLink` returned a claimable open link VERBATIM without comparing the grant, and the popover renders "Create another", so reuse is the NORMAL second click. Picking "Member — full channel" over an open GUEST link answered 200 with the guest link and the peer landed as a guest — **and the reverse pointed the same silence at privilege.** A mismatch now takes the existing revoke-and-remint path (the dead-link branch): the operator's explicit choice wins over a URL they may already have pasted.
      - 🔒 ⚠ **"MISMATCH" MEANS AN EXPLICIT PICK THAT DIFFERS — AN ABSENT FIELD IS NOT A PICK (corrected 2026-08-26, second pass).** `HomeLinkMintSchema.grantedRole` carried `.default("guest")`, so the service could not tell *"the operator chose Guest"* from *"the body said nothing"* — and a pre-M2 client (or any body omitting the field) pressing "Add person" against an open **member** link therefore took the mismatch branch: it REVOKED the operator's outstanding invitation, minted a guest one, answered **200**, and signalled nothing. A silent rotation and a silent DOWNGRADE of a URL already in somebody's inbox; before M3 the identical request returned the member link verbatim. **THE SEMANTICS ARE NOW: field ABSENT = "reuse whatever is open" (the pre-M3 behaviour); field PRESENT and different = revoke-and-remint.** The field is `optional()` at the boundary and the fail-closed **fresh-mint** default (`guest`) lives in `home/server/service-writes.ts › mintContainerLink › roleToMint`, where it governs MINTING and never MATCHING. ⚠ The dead-link revoke is untouched by this and still fires on a role-less body — the brick guard is not a grant comparison. Pinned by `home/server/service-writes-granted-role.test.ts` and the schema case in `guest-claim-f319-closure.test.ts`. **`HomePendingLink.grantedRole` is projected by `home/server/dto.ts › mapLinkRow`** so the UI can say what an open invitation grants — it was read into `ChannelLinkRow` at M2 and stopped there, which is why nothing downstream could notice. ⚠ **Stale-cache (§8): absent on a `linkOut` cached before this field existed — fall back to `"guest"`, the DB default.**
  - **`link` is a reserved slug** in `config/index.ts › RESERVED_WORKSPACE_SLUGS`, for the same reason `join` and `invite` are: `/link/{token}` is a top-level route and a workspace slugged `link` would be silently shadowed by it.
- **THE GUEST WEB LANE — `/c/{containerId}` (2026-08-25; spec `docs/specs/guest-web-channel.md`).** A person with NO desktop app claims a link and lands on `src/app/c/[workspaceId]/page.tsx › GuestChannelPage`: ONE channel in a browser, no rail, no sidebar, no workspace surface, no settings. **The segment is the RAW container UUID**, not the `{slug}-{publicId}` segment every other workspace URL uses — the claim response already carries it, `X-Workspace-Id` is UUID-only (§4 step 2), and `findMemberContainer` takes it.
  - 🔒 **THE ORDER OF THREE GATES IS THE CONTRACT:** auth BEFORE any lookup (a signed-out visitor learns nothing about the id in their URL, not even whether it is well-formed) → the UUID guard (garbage never reaches the service) → `home/server/service-reads.ts › getHomeChannel`, whose `null` is the home fence idiom: **not a container, not a link container and not a member all render the SAME `notFound()`**, so the URL is never an existence oracle for container ids. `getHomeChannel` composes `findMemberContainer` + `hydrateOneChannel` and is **not** a filter over `getHomeChannels` (that read is capped, so scanning its page would 404 a channel the caller really has).
  - **Route bookkeeping, all four lists, and one of them is an ABSENCE.** `c` is reserved in `config/index.ts › RESERVED_WORKSPACE_SLUGS` and in `shared/lib/url/website-retirement.ts › RESERVED_TOP_LEVEL` (a KEEP that was accidental before it was named). It is **deliberately NOT in `shared/layout/layout-shell.tsx › NON_WORKSPACE_ROOTS`** — that branch dresses the page in a centred container, wrong for a full-viewport surface, so the page draws its own root and overrides the body paint itself; the omission carries a comment saying so. And it is **NOT in `PUBLIC_ROUTES`: auth-gated BY OMISSION**, which produces the signed-out 307 to `/login?redirectTo=/c/<id>`. Pinned in `src/proxy.test.ts`, `src/proxy-matcher.test.ts` and `src/proxy-retirement-keep.test.ts`.
  - ⚠ **THE CLAIM CARD NOW NAVIGATES THE WEB APP, WHICH REVERSES A DELIBERATE FENCE (owner ruling, 2026-08-25).** `src/app/link/[token]/claim-card.tsx` used to state that *nothing here navigates the web app*, and a test pinned it. A SUCCESSFUL claim now also offers the lane, beside the `dopl://open/home` attempt. **What did NOT change:** the card still binds NO router — the web lane is a plain `<a href>`, so the full page load lands on the route's own fence — and the **dead endings still navigate nowhere and still show no deep link** (arrived dead, or a 410 mid-claim). **The fence became HOW, not WHETHER.** Why/history: ENGINEERING.md, 2026-08-25 stratum.
  - ✅ **THE GUEST IS A REAL CAPABILITY TIER AND THE NARROWING IS SERVER-ENFORCED (guest-role M0–M2, 2026-08-25; F-319 RESOLVED).** A bound claim now inserts the claimer at the LINK's `granted_role` (default `guest`), not a hardcoded `admin` — so the UI narrowing is backed by the server, not cosmetic. Do NOT re-add a reading that the hidden controls are the only fence; the role IS the fence.
  - ⚠ **THE `guest` ROLE, ITS ENFORCEMENT FLOOR, AND THE LINK-CARRIED GRANT ALL EXIST (guest-role M0–M2, 2026-08-25).** `guest` is the FLOOR role (`workspaces/types.ts › Role`, `ROLE_RANK` rank 0, below `viewer`), admitted by the DB (`workspace_members_role_check` + the `is_workspace_member` guest arm, migration `20260825140000_guest_role.sql`). ENFORCEMENT IS INVERTED-DEFAULT: `withWorkspaceAuth` defaults `minRole` to `"viewer"`, so **every workspace-scoped route REJECTS a guest unless it explicitly opts down to `minRole: "guest"`** — Knowledge, Skills, Ontology, Chats, Overview, Members, Billing, channel management (PATCH/DELETE), agent launch/sessions all reject guest by that default, and the DELETE-channel path with them.
    - ⚠ **THERE ARE TWO WRAPPER FAMILIES AND THE FAIL-CLOSED CLAIM ABOVE COVERS ONLY ONE (corrected 2026-08-26).** `withWorkspaceAuth`'s `viewer` default is the first. The second is **`withUserAuth` + `workspaces/server/segment.ts › resolveApiWorkspace` / `resolveApiWorkspaceAccess`** — nineteen route files under `src/app/api/workspaces/**` (re-derive: `grep -rln resolveApiWorkspace src/app/api`) — which proved membership **EXISTENCE** (`status='active'`), READ the role and **never compared it**. Measured guest-reachable before the fix, and every one of them a route the paragraph above names as rejecting guests: `GET /api/workspaces/[slug]/members` (the full roster WITH emails), `/overview`, `/overview-series` (31 days of message/MCP/thread volume across every channel in the container — the unscoped path has no channel fence at all, by design), `GET /api/workspaces/[slug]`, `/my-access` (which ENUMERATES every teams-mode resource id).
      - **THE FIX IS AT THE RESOLVER, NOT AT FIVE ROUTES**: `segment.ts › ApiWorkspaceOpts.minRole` defaults to `"viewer"`, matching the other wrapper, so the family is fail-closed by default and the NEXT route added there inherits it. A refusal is `null` → each caller's existing **404**, never a new 403 — so "not a member", "not enough role" and "does not exist" stay one answer. Pinned by `workspaces/server/api-workspace-floor.test.ts` (drives the resolver) and by `guest-route-floor.test.ts` **set C** (scans the family's callers for a silent opt-down; the allowlist is EMPTY).
      - ⚠ **`PATCH /api/workspaces/[slug]` was ALREADY the exception and still is** — it gates admin inside `renameWorkspace › requireWorkspaceRole('admin')`, a SERVICE gate, not a wrapper one. Do not assume uniformity in either family.
    - **THE GUEST-ALLOWED ROUTE SET IS A NAMED, PINNED CONTRACT** — **nineteen** `(route, method)` floors a guest reaches, in **fifteen files** (four files carry two floored methods each), enumerated and pinned by `src/app/api/channels/guest-route-floor.test.ts`. **FOURTEEN ARE CHANNEL-CONTENT ROUTES**: list/read channels, read+post messages (**this is where an `@`-mention is PARSED — Q2**), the two await polls, list+create threads (Q1), read one thread, read members, the presence heartbeat (Q2), **both mentions verbs**, and **peer agent-session state**. **FOUR ARE THE KNOWLEDGE LANE** (2026-08-26, next bullet). ⚠ **THE NINETEENTH IS NOT A CHANNEL ROUTE AND NOT A CAPABILITY: `POST /api/mcp/credits/consume` (2026-08-26)** — it is a METER, and at the `viewer` default it made guest tool calls FREE rather than refusing them (the registrar fails open on the 403; F-325, and the billing bullet below). It grants no data and no write. That test reads route SOURCE and goes red if any allowed floor is dropped OR any route OUTSIDE the set is set to `guest`. **The workspace floor is a TRIPWIRE; the TRUE gate is the channel-membership fence** (`loadVisibleChannel` hides a private channel from a non-member, `postMessage` / `createTaskFanOut` refuse `!membership`) — unchanged, and not to be weakened.
      - 🔒 **A GUEST REACHES KNOWLEDGE ONLY THROUGH A `(knowledge_base, channel)` GRANT AT `visible`. WORKSPACE KNOWLEDGE ROUTES ARE STILL `viewer`+ AND STILL REFUSE ONE** (Home Knowledge Panels M2, 2026-08-26; plan `docs/specs/home-knowledge-panels.plan.md` §3). Every route under `/api/knowledge/**` keeps the `viewer` default, and lowering one would not even work: `defaultLevelForRole("guest")` is `null`, so `service-shared.ts › assertBaseVisible` (via `teams/server/access.ts › requireEffectiveAccess`) refuses a guest whatever the floor says. The lane is therefore a **separate, channel-scoped set of routes with its own gates**, not a widened workspace route — `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/knowledge/**`, **four `(route, method)` pairs in three files**: `bases` GET (the granted bases), `bases/[baseId]/tree` GET (folders + entry metadata), `entries/[entryId]` GET (one body) and `entries/[entryId]` PUT (§3.4's guest write). Search, export, create, move, delete, star and settings are **omitted BY DECISION**.
        - **FOUR FENCES, AND THE ORDERING IS THE CONTRACT.** (1) `withWorkspaceAuth(..., {minRole:"guest"})` — the tripwire. (2) `channels/server/service-shared.ts › loadVisibleChannel` **with 🔒 `membership !== null` REQUIRED** — `shared/api/channel-knowledge-lane.ts › requireChannelKnowledgeContext`, the one place it is written for all four pairs. (3) the grant row at `level='visible'`. (4) base `deleted_at IS NULL` and same workspace. Fences 3 and 4 are `knowledge/server/service-channel-grants.ts › assertGrantVisible`; the write adds `guest_write === true` in `› assertGrantWritable`.
        - 🔒 **FENCE 2 REFUSES THE PUBLIC ARM OUTRIGHT, AND THAT IS THE LINE THAT WILL REGRESS.** `loadVisibleChannel` **returns successfully with `membership: null`** on a `visibility='public'` channel. A guest does not reach that arm (`mayReadPublicChannels`), but a workspace **`viewer` does** — and F-327 says a public channel can exist inside a container. Without that line a viewer who never joined the channel reads every base granted into it. The other eleven guest-floored `loadVisibleChannel` routes serve the CHANNEL's own content, where the public arm is the intended behaviour; here the payload is a whole knowledge base, so the same arm is a larger leak, not a smaller one.
        - 🔒 **`agent_only` IS A 404 ON THIS LANE, ALWAYS — a DIFFERENT AUDIENCE, not a lower level.** It is filtered out **in SQL** (`repository-channel-grants.ts › listChannelGrantsAtLevel`) so such a row never enters the process, and `assertGrantVisible` gives it the same answer an absent grant gets. ⚠ Every refusal on the lane is that one 404 — not-a-member, no grant, `agent_only`, dead base, another base's entry, another workspace's entry, a malformed uuid. **The single exception is `CHANNEL_GRANT_READ_ONLY` (403)** on a `visible`-but-not-`guest_write` PUT, where the caller has already been shown the row and there is nothing left to conceal.
        - **THE LANE REUSES THE REPOSITORY READERS AND NONE OF THE WORKSPACE GATES.** `service-channel-grants.ts` (gates) and `service-channel-lane.ts` (payloads) import nothing from `service-shared.ts`, and do not go through `getBaseById` / `getBaseTree` / `getEntry` either — those compose the workspace audience. Pinned as an ABSENCE over both files by `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/knowledge/grant-lane.test.ts` (the `link-container-guard` technique), which also drives the real fences through the real route handlers for the other nine properties. ⚠ **The `level` filter needed a REPOSITORY pin**: `grant-lane.test.ts` mocks the repo, so deleting `.eq("level", ...)` left it green while `agent_only` grants entered a guest's list.
        - **THE GUEST WRITE (§3.4) IS TITLE + BODY + `expectedVersion`, `.strict()`, AND NOTHING ELSE** — no create, no folders, no delete, no `position` / `entryType` / `excerpt` (Samuel's ruling 3). Stamps `last_edited_by = ctx.userId` and the **literal** `last_edited_source = 'user'`. **`agent_write_enabled` is NOT consulted** — and that is only safe because `assertGrantWritable` **refuses an agent token on this lane outright** (403 `AGENT_WRITE_DISABLED`); without it this route would be the one place F-10/F-10b could be walked around, since a `full`-profile session has Bash and the 90-day device token is on disk. ⚠ **The service is the fence, not RLS**: every read and write below it runs on the service-role client, so the `knowledge_entries` policies never fire for this caller.
      - ⚠ **THIS BULLET SAID "TWELVE" AND MISLABELLED TWO OF THEM (corrected 2026-08-26).** It read *"presence + `@`-mention (Q2)"*, and the route it meant is not an `@`-mention route at all: `…/[channelId]/mentions` is GET = `listMyChannelMentions` (read MY inbox) / POST = `markMentionsRead`. **A mention is parsed out of message TEXT by `channels/server/service-writes-metadata-mentions.ts`, so Q2 was already delivered by `POST …/messages`.** M1 had also lowered the WRONG verb — POST, not GET — so `useChannelMentions`, which mounts for every guest, 403'd on every guest.
      - **THE TWO ADDITIONS ARE `GET …/mentions` AND `GET …/[channelId]/sessions`, and both come from the same rule: THE SURFACE MUST NOT ISSUE A REQUEST IT WILL GET 403 ON.** `channel-surface-data.ts › useChannelSurfaceData` mounts for every host, guest lane included, and issued three out-of-set reads — two on a POLL LOOP. Mentions and sessions were LOWERED (the mention inbox is the guest's own, `ctx.userId`-scoped inside the service; seeing that the operator's agent is working is the lane's whole proposition, and the payload is the state projection alone). **The consent inbox was NOT lowered — it is NOT MOUNTED**, gated off `capabilities.selfManagement === false`, because an outbound consent row is a draft the viewer's OWN agent wrote and a guest runs none. Pinned by `channels-v2/guest-surface-reads.test.tsx`.
    - 🔒 **A GUEST DOES NOT INHERIT THE `visibility='public'` ARM (2026-08-26).** "Public" means *any workspace member can see and join*, which is a claim about a TENANCY; a guest has none. Nothing stops a PUBLIC channel existing inside a container (`createChannel` never reads `workspace.kind`, `POST /api/channels` is `member`+ which the owner clears, the MCP `open` op exposes `visibility`, no DB constraint — **F-327**), and **fifteen of the eighteen guest-floored CHANNEL-PATH routes compose `loadVisibleChannel`** — so a lowered floor plus an inherited public arm was a cross-channel read. ⚠ **That count has been wrong three times (this bullet said "seven", then "eleven of fourteen"; `service-shared.ts` said "twelve"); measured 2026-08-26 AFTER the knowledge lane's four pairs, the three that do NOT compose it are `channels/route.ts` GET, `channels/await/route.ts` GET and `channels/presence/route.ts` POST, none of which takes a channel ref. Re-derive, never quote:** walk each pair in `guest-route-floor.test.ts › GUEST_ALLOWED` to the service function its route calls, then `grep -rn loadVisibleChannel src/features/channels/server src/shared/api`. ⚠ **The four knowledge-lane pairs reach it through `shared/api/channel-knowledge-lane.ts`, not through a channels service function** — the first callers outside `features/channels/server`, and the reason `loadVisibleChannel` is now on the channels barrel (the only gate that is). **They additionally REQUIRE `membership !== null`**, so the public arm does not reach them at all. The rule has **four statements that must not drift**: `channels/server/service-shared.ts › mayReadPublicChannels` (single-ref, and `null` role fails closed), `repository-visibility.ts › visibleChannelsOr({includePublic})` (the LIST — it answers `null` for "may see nothing", because a no-term PostgREST `or` is a syntax error), `service-reads.ts › revalidateAwaitAccess` (the long-poll's per-tick re-check, which must answer what the entry gate answered), and the DB's own guest arm below. Pinned by `channels/server/guest-public-channel-fence.test.ts`.
    - ✅ **THE LINK CARRIES THE GRANTED ROLE, AND THE CLAIM WRITES IT (guest-role M2, closes F-319).** `channel_links.granted_role` (migration `20260825150000_channel_link_granted_role.sql`; `text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'guest' CHECK (granted_role IN ('guest','viewer','member'))`) is the role a bound claim confers. `home/server/service-claim-bound.ts › claimBoundLink` passes `link.granted_role` into `repository-containers.ts › insertContainerMember` (now a `role: Role` arg), so a link-claimer lands at exactly the granted role — default `guest`. **The CHECK makes `admin`/`owner`-via-link UNREPRESENTABLE** — the ceiling is `member`. ⚠ **THE "DEFAULT `guest` BACKFILLS EVERY OPEN LINK FAIL-CLOSED" CLAIM IS TRUE OF THE ROWS AND EMPTY IN EFFECT (measured 2026-08-26): 3 rows total, only 2 of them open, and BOTH open ones are UNBOUND (`workspace_id IS NULL`)** — the one bound link had already been revoked. The unbound claim path never reads `granted_role` (it keeps its hardcoded `admin`, below), **so the backfill touched zero live claimable links.** Re-measure, never quote: `select workspace_id is null as unbound, granted_role, revoked_at is not null from channel_links;`. `HomeLinkMintSchema.grantedRole` (enum guest/viewer/member, default guest) plus a **grant-above-self guard** in `mintContainerLink` (`meetsMinRole(minterRole, grantedRole)` or 403 `GRANT_ABOVE_SELF`) fence the mint side; in a container the minter is the owner, so the DB CHECK is the operative ceiling. ⚠ **The LEGACY UNBOUND path (`insertLinkContainer` / `claimUnboundLink`) keeps its hardcoded `admin`** — honoring `granted_role` there would silently downgrade links already in the wild. Closure pinned by `home/server/guest-claim-f319-closure.test.ts` (a guest claimer fails `canManageChannel`, cannot delete others' threads, is below the viewer floor) and the claim/mint suites.
    - **THE UI TELL (guest-role M3).** `pages/home/add-person-dialog.tsx` (renamed from `-popover.tsx` 2026-08-27, when it became a `StandardDialog`) mints at a chosen `grantedRole` — a second `SelectMenu` (**default `guest`**, options "Guest — chat only" / "Member — full channel"; `viewer` is schema-valid but deliberately not offered, Samuel's ruling). And the roster shows a **"Guest" pill**: since the channel role is only `owner`/`member` (§5), a link-claimed guest reads `member` there, so `ChannelMember` gained `workspaceRole: Role | null` — hydrated by `service-reads.ts › listChannelMembers` via `workspaces/server/repository.ts › listMemberRolesByUserIds` (bounded to the roster's own ids, §9), mapped `?? null` in `mapMemberRow`, and read `=== "guest"` by `channels-v2/bits.tsx › RolePill`. ⚠ **STALE-CACHE (§8): `workspaceRole` is a new field on the cached members payload** — absent on a payload minted before M3, it reads as `null` → "not a guest", fail-safe. Pinned by a stale-cache render test in `pages/home/person-info-tab.test.tsx` and a `RolePill` `guest=undefined` unit test.
- 🔒 **BILLING — THE OPERATOR PAYS. A CONTAINER'S MCP BURN IS CHARGED TO THE CONTAINER'S OWNER, WHOEVER MADE THE CALL (Samuel, 2026-08-26: "charge MCP calls from a guest to the user"). NO LONGER PROVISIONAL on the question of WHO PAYS.** A link container is a relationship, not a tenant, so **it has no plan**. `billing/server/credits-service.ts › resolveBillingTarget` reroutes an MCP burn aimed at a container onto **the oldest-owned STANDARD workspace of the container's OWNER** — the person who minted the link, resolved from the `workspace_members` row at `role='owner'` (`workspaces/server/repository.ts › findActiveOwnerUserId`). A guest's, a peer's and the owner's own tool calls all land on the same counter. Pinned by `billing/server/credits-link-reroute.test.ts` and, end to end through the real wrapper, `app/api/mcp/credits/consume/route-guest-floor.test.ts`.
  - ⚠ **THIS REPLACED "each side spends their own allowance" (the 2026-08-23 PROVISIONAL wiring), AND THE OLD RULE WAS NEVER REACHED FOR A GUEST.** `POST /api/mcp/credits/consume` sat at `withWorkspaceAuth`'s `viewer` default, so a guest-scoped consume call **403'd** — and `packages/mcp-server/src/registrar.ts › createCreditedRunner › charge` fails OPEN on any throw, so the 403 was not a refusal, it was a FREE tool call plus one `[credits] consume call failed` log line. **The floor and the owner-resolution are ONE fix; either half alone is a different bug.** That was **F-325**, resolved by this change.
  - 🔒 **THE ROUTE'S FLOOR IS `minRole: "guest"`, AND IT IS A METER RATHER THAN A CAPABILITY.** It is the **only non-channel** entry in the guest-allowed set (⚠ this said "the fifteenth and only non-channel" while the same section's contract said the set holds **nineteen** — the ordinal was written when the set was 15 and the four `…/knowledge/**` routes then joined it. **Re-derive the size from `GUEST_ALLOWED` in the pin, never from a sentence**) (§4A's route-floor contract, `app/api/channels/guest-route-floor.test.ts`). The only effect of a successful call is that somebody's counter goes UP; it returns no workspace data and performs no write. ⚠ **Raising this floor back closes nothing — it re-opens the free lane.**
  - **`findActiveOwnerUserId` READS MEMBERSHIP, NOT `workspaces.owner_id`, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** An active-owner ROW is a DB guarantee — `20260720184806_workspace_last_active_owner_guard.sql` (H-5) RAISEs on the delete/demote that would leave a workspace with zero — so `null` means the workspace is gone, never that ownership drifted. **Do not add an `owner_id` fallback:** a second mechanism is a second thing to drift. It answers "who pays", never "who may" — **not an authz read**.
  - ✅ **NO-BILLING-WORKSPACE: FAIL OPEN, AND LOGGED — NEVER SILENT (decided 2026-08-26).** An owner with no standard workspace (and the unreachable no-active-owner case) runs **UNMETERED and ALLOWED** (`› unmetered`), matching `POST /api/mcp/credits/consume`'s existing posture: refusing would brick a relationship on the strength of the OTHER party's billing — a guest doing invited work would read "out of credits" for a plan that is not theirs and that they cannot buy. **The honesty requirement is the log**: `consumeMcpCredits` emits one `console.warn` naming the container, the caller, the payer and a `UnmeteredReason` (`container-owner-has-no-billing-workspace` / `container-has-no-active-owner`) before returning. ⚠ **Its counters come back ZEROED and stamped `degraded: true`** (`CreditsSummary.degraded`) — a statement that nothing was measured, not that nothing was spent.
    - **MEASURED 2026-08-26 against the live project: the branch is UNREACHED, and BOTH ownership representations agree** — 3 link containers and 9 standard workspaces, **every one** carrying an active `role='owner'` membership row whose user equals `workspaces.owner_id`, and **all 3 container owners own a standard workspace**, so `would_run_unmetered = 0`. **This is deploy state, not tree state — re-measure, never quote:** join `workspaces` to a lateral `select user_id from workspace_members where workspace_id = w.id and status='active' and role='owner' order by joined_at limit 1`, then test `exists (select 1 from workspaces o where o.owner_id = <that user> and o.kind='standard')`.
  - 🔒 **THE METER IS NARROWED TO THE PAYER, AND THAT NARROWING IS A FENCE.** `GET /api/billing/status` is at the wrapper's plain `viewer` default, so a `member`-granted link claimer (and a legacy unbound claimer, who lands at `admin`) can address a container with it — and the payload is far more than a credit count: **plan, member count, seat count, object cap and `objectsUsed` all come from whichever workspace the target names.** So `billing/server/status-service.ts` reports the owner's target **only to the payer**; a non-payer falls to the same unmetered/`degraded` posture as an unresolvable target, over the container's own (empty) entitlements. Without it, charging the operator would have printed the operator's private workspace inside the relationship.
  - ⚠ **WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS THE LIMIT, NOT THE PAYER OR THE ATTRIBUTION (Samuel, 2026-08-26: guardrail/limit settings come later).** A guest can spend the operator's allowance, bounded only by the plan's own credit ceiling and `withUserAuth`'s rate limiting. **Per-guest guardrails are unbuilt** — that half of **F-328** stands.
  - **THE ATTRIBUTION HALF OF F-328 IS CLOSED AT THE SCHEMA (2026-09-01): `credit_usage_events`**, a per-burn ledger written BESIDE the counter (`20260901120000_credit_usage_events.sql`, `billing/server/credit-ledger.ts`, fired from `consumeMcpCredits` only when the consume was ALLOWED). It carries `workspace_id` = the PAYER, `origin_workspace_id` = where the call was made (**the "by channel" dimension** — a home container holds exactly one channel), `user_id`, `amount` and the stamped `period_start`. 🔒 **It is NOT the billing counter and must never become one**: `consume_workspace_credits` remains the sole authority on `allowed` and on the meter. ⚠ **The writer is FIRE-AND-FORGET, so the ledger may UNDER-COUNT** — a dropped insert costs an attribution row, never a credit — and every reader treats `SUM(amount)` as a FLOOR. ⚠ **There is no `channel_id` column**: the consume route's auth context carries no channel and resolving one would tax the hottest write path in the product (§12); readers join the container's channel name instead. ⚠ **It starts EMPTY** — there is no history behind the migration, so the /home credit rails and the credit series are sparse until traffic accrues, and the series answers an EMPTY array rather than zeroed bins.
  - ⚠ **THE TRAFFIC PROFILE INVERTED UNDER THIS BULLET IN 2026-08-24 AND THAT IS WHY THE FAIL-OPEN BRANCH MATTERS.** The reroute was written when a container was a rare two-person relationship; a SOLO container is now the operator's **primary agent surface** — created first, worked in daily — so it is the main road for home-channel MCP burn, and an operator with no billable standard workspace runs their *main* agent surface unmetered rather than an occasional DM.

- **`/home` HAS FOUR FACES AND ALL FOUR ARE BUILT — Overview (2026-09-01), Channels, Knowledge (2026-08-26, Home Knowledge Panels M3) and Agents (2026-08-26, Home Agents Tab M2).** The header's `SegmentedControl` is LOCAL state, not a route, and **ONE LAYOUT SERVES ALL FOUR** (Samuel, 2026-08-24): the conversation column and the pane never move between faces — only the pane's contents swap. ⚠ **The Agents face lists template IDENTITIES, and the channel info column has a different tab of the same name listing live SESSIONS** — both names stay by Samuel's ruling (§5A).
  - **THE FACE VOCABULARY LIVES IN `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/home-tabs.ts`** — `HomeTab`, `HOME_TABS`, `HOME_DEFAULT_TAB` and the three pane prefixes — split out of `index.tsx` on 2026-09-01 when the fourth face took that file past the 500-line cap (§1). `paneToken`/`renderPane` stayed in the page: they read the page's `selected` row.
  - **"CHANNELS" WAS "CHAT" UNTIL 2026-09-01 (Samuel), LABEL AND LOCAL KEY.** There is no per-face route on /home, so no URL, no deep link and no persisted value had to migrate. ⚠ Not to be read as licence to rename the `channels` PAGE segment (`apps/desktop-ui/src/routes.tsx › WORKSPACE_PAGES`), which is a real path with a hand copy in `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js`.
  - **OVERVIEW IS LEFTMOST AND CHANNELS IS STILL THE DEFAULT**, which is two decisions and is stated as two: `HOME_DEFAULT_TAB` is not derived from `HOME_TABS[0]`, so re-ordering the row cannot silently move where /home lands.
  - **OVERVIEW IS LEFTMOST *AND* IS THE DEFAULT (Samuel, 2026-09-01)** — the landing moved off Channels, so opening Dopl answers "what needs me / what is happening / what is running" before "what did we say". Still TWO decisions stated as two: `HOME_DEFAULT_TAB` is not derived from `HOME_TABS[0]`, so re-ordering the row cannot silently move where the app lands. LOCAL state only — /home has no per-face route and nothing is persisted, so there was no URL or stored value to migrate.
  - 🔒 **THE FACE IS CROSS-CHANNEL AND THE CHANNEL-SCOPED PANEL IS DELETED (Samuel, 2026-09-01) — THIS WAS A DUPLICATION BUG, NOT A PREFERENCE.** It stacked an ACCOUNT-wide panel over a CHANNEL-scoped one built from the SAME components against `?workspaceId=`; for an operator whose fence holds ONE home channel the two payloads are identical by construction, so **every stat tile, chart and rail rendered twice**. The param, the `scope` field and the second panel are gone from `home/server/service-overview.ts`, both routes and `HomeOverview`. ⚠ **Do not reintroduce the param** — it is not an option, it is the second half of a duplicate render; `src/app/api/home/overview/route.test.ts` asserts a sender that still passes one gets the account-wide payload.
  - 🔒 **THREE PANELS, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM LAYERS THE SAME WAY (Samuel, four passes of corrections, 2026-09-01).** The stack on this page is **page ground → GRAY `SectionPanel` → WHITE `.bento` card(s) holding the content**, and it has no exceptions: **ACTIVITY** is a two-up row of cards (Waiting on you, Recent threads) with the agent board beneath; **USAGE** is ONE white `.bento` card holding the full-width capacity bar on top and the credits histogram below it; **ALL CHANNELS** is four white rail cards. ⚠ **A pass forced `!bg-home-card` onto the Usage `SectionPanel` itself** — painting the panel white and dropping the bar and plot straight onto it, one layer short and the only section that did not match its siblings. **That override is deleted; do not reintroduce it.** The Usage card wears the SAME `.bento` recipe as `overview-rails.tsx › RailCard`, so the panels layer identically by construction rather than by two class strings that happen to agree. ⚠ **Nothing else goes in the Usage panel** — a rail, a note or a total goes in the panel below (*"Usage panel has the usage bar and the histogram. That's it."*). ⚠ Activity rows are ONE truncated line each, capped at 6 with a `+N more` tail; an empty card says so in one line and the agent board renders `null` (its heading folds with it). The row of stat tiles is DELETED along with its five server head-counts.
  - 🔒 **THE HISTOGRAM IS CREDITS AND HAS NO METRIC SWITCHER** (`HOME_OVERVIEW_DEFAULT_METRIC`; Samuel: *"I explicitly said not to do MCP calls but credits. Why is there a MCP option"*). MCP traffic is on the same face as the **Top MCP tools** rail and messages as the **Messages by channel** rail. ⚠ The ROUTE still validates all three metrics — it is a general endpoint with its own contract tests; what is pinned is what this PAGE asks for.
  - 🔒 **`month` IS THE WHOLE CALENDAR MONTH — 28..31 BINS, AND THE COUNT DOES NOT DEPEND ON TODAY'S DATE.** It was month-to-DATE for one pass (`bins = now.getUTCDate()`), which is **1 on the first of the month**: the chart rendered ONE bar stretched across the entire plot. ⚠ **The future bins are zero and that is the point** — the axis is the frame the operator reads the month against. ⚠ **And the credits series ZERO-FILLS rather than answering `[]`**: an empty ledger still draws the full month, because the page must never lose its chart. A zero on a day the ledger covers really is zero; on a day before the ledger existed it is unmeasured, and the axis cannot tell those apart — the trade Samuel took knowingly.
  - 🔒 **THE LEDGER READ IS THE ONE READ ON THIS PAGE THAT DEGRADES INSTEAD OF THROWING** (`repository-overview.ts › scanCreditEvents`). `credit_usage_events` ships as an UNAPPLIED migration, so between deploy and apply the table does not exist and PostgREST answers `42P01`; rethrowing took the WHOLE face down, because that read sits in `getHomeOverview`'s `Promise.all`. It logs and answers empty. ⚠ **Safe here and only here**: an empty ledger is already an expected state this surface renders correctly, which no other read on the page can say.
  - **WAITING ON YOU IS THREE SERVER-SIDE LANES, AND WHAT IT CANNOT SEE IS PART OF THE CONTRACT** (`home/server/repository-attention.ts`, each fenced by the container list **and** by the caller): pending `channel_consent_requests` where `operator_user_id` is the caller (an agent stopped waiting for a Send decision — and the `pending` filter carries an `expires_at` test, because that table's expiry sweep is LAZY); the caller's own non-ended `channel_sessions` at `detail = 'permission'`; and unread @-mentions (`metadata->mentionedUserIds` containment, anti-joined against `channel_mention_reads`). ⚠ **A tool-gate PROMPT never crosses the wire** — its text, tool and choices live in the desktop's gate — so the panel can say THAT a session is blocked and where, never what it is asking. ⚠ **An agent question that tags nobody is invisible**: there is no question KIND on `channel_messages`, so the mention lane is an approximation and no read recovers the rest.
  - **ACTIVE AGENTS IS A BOARD GROUPED BY CHANNEL**, one lane per container that has a non-ended session, ordered by the server's recency (a `Map` preserves insertion order — never re-sorted by name). The lane/card vocabulary is the ontology board's (`features/ontology/components/kanban-board.tsx`) **adapted, not cloned**: same `rounded-[14px] bg-bg-inset` lane and `rounded-[10px]` bordered card, different sizes — that board's `w-72`/`h-[216px]` are arithmetic for an eight-line object card and do not belong here.
  - 🔒 **AN ACTIVITY ROW'S CLICK IS A SELECTION, NOT A ROUTE**, and the model forces it: a home channel lives in a `kind='link'` CONTAINER, containers have no page (the account rail drops them by `isStandardWorkspace`), and `/{segment}/channels/{id}` is a WORKSPACE route. `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/use-activity-jump.ts` performs the three moves — select the row, raise the Channels face, hand `RelationshipRecord` an `initialThreadId` — and **holds the thread KEYED BY THE ROW it was picked for**, so a thread id can never reach a container it does not belong to. A manual pick in the left list clears it. ⚠ The jump target for an agent is `channel_sessions.task_id`, which `20260822150000_channel_sessions_telemetry.sql` classifies **PUBLIC**; a channel-level launch and a MENTION have none (`channel_messages` has no `task_id`), and both degrade to opening the channel.
  - ⚠ **THE OVERVIEW PANE TOKEN CARRIES NO ROW** (`home-tabs.ts › OVERVIEW_PANE`, compared with `===` not `startsWith`). Every other face renders a CHANNEL's contents and must re-key on the selection; this one is cross-channel, so keying it by `selected` would remount and refetch the whole analytics face on every click of the list beside it.
  - **`pages/home/person-info-tab.tsx` CARRIED THE SAME DERIVATION AND LOST IT IN THE SAME CHANGE.** Its header was a PERSON CARD chosen by roster size, over a Channel-info `Email` row that rendered at exactly one peer; the tab therefore became that member's profile the moment somebody joined. The header is the channel's name now and the Email row is gone. ⚠ **`"email"` STAYS IN `features/channels/info-card.ts › INFO_CARD_BUILT_IN_KEYS`** — stored cards carry it in `hidden` for operators who removed the row while it existed, and that file's own rule is that a hidden key for a row nobody renders is INERT rather than wrong.
  - **MEMBERS APPEAR IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE ON THAT TAB: `pages/home/person-members.tsx`**, each with their face, name and address, where every fact can be attributed to the person it belongs to.
  - 🔒 **THE RECORD PANE'S HEADER READS A SECOND, DIFFERENT COUNTERPART DERIVATION, AND /home NOW OPTS OUT OF IT.** `features/channels/lib/channel-display.ts › channelDisplayName` names a channel after its peer whenever `channels.is_direct`; `channels-v2/channel-surface.tsx` asks it, so a container minted before the 2026-08-24 channel-first inversion named the peer at the TOP OF THE PANE under a row and an Info card that both said the channel's. `ChannelSurfaceCapabilities.peerNamedHeader` (default `true`) pins the header to `channel.name`, and `pages/home/relationship-record.tsx` passes `false`. ⚠ **`channel-display.ts` ITSELF IS UNCHANGED AND MUST BE** — it is the ONE derivation for the workspace surfaces (`channels-v2/sidebar.tsx` name + avatar, `channels-v2/channel-manage.tsx`), where a DM must keep naming its peer. Pinned in `channels-v2/channel-surface.test.tsx › the header's identity` (both directions). ⚠ **The DATA is still open — F-402**: no backfill was written, so link containers holding an `is_direct = true` channel still exist and any future surface that asks that module about one re-opens this.
  - ⚠ **REAL DMs ARE UNTOUCHED AND THEIR CODE IS ELSEWHERE** — `channels.is_direct` + `Channel.directPeer`, rendered by the channels feature. A home container holds a PRIVATE, NON-DIRECT channel by construction (`home/server/repository-containers.ts › listContainerChannels`), so nothing in `home-rows.ts` ever touched a DM.
  - ⚠ **SEARCH STILL REACHES MEMBERS** (`home-rows.ts › searchText`) and that survived deliberately: finding a channel by who is in it is a QUERY, not a presentation of identity, and the row that comes back is still titled by the channel.
  - 🔒 **THE PANE TOKEN IS KEYED BY THE ROW FOR EVERY FACE, WITH NO EXCEPTION LEFT.** `pages/home/index.tsx › paneToken` is `knowledge:${rowId}`, `agents:${rowId}`, or the bare row id on Chat. **Keyed by the TAB, a channel switch leaves the token frozen** — `Crossfade` never fires and the pane swaps one channel's knowledge (or templates) for another's under a token asserting nothing changed. `renderPane` reads the row out of `shown`, never out of `selected`, for the reason its docblock already gave Chat: the outgoing view must stay a pure function of the token that is still on screen. ⚠ **The prefixes are SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION AND BY DISJOINTNESS**: row ids are `rel:`/`link:`-prefixed (`home-rows.ts`) so no chat token can wear either, and neither face's prefix is a prefix OF the other, so the `startsWith` branches cannot claim each other's tokens. Pinned in `pages/home/knowledge-panels.test.tsx › the pane token` and `pages/home/agent-panels.test.tsx › the pane token`, each asserting BOTH halves (a fade starts, and the outgoing pane still shows the previous channel's data).
  - **TWO SECTIONS, NO SCOPE PILL** (`pages/home/knowledge-panels.tsx`; Samuel's ruling 2026-08-27, superseding the three-scope shape): **SHARED IN THIS CHANNEL** = the container's bases carrying a `(kb, channel)` grant (`agent_only` ones BADGED, or the operator cannot tell what the peer sees from what only the agent reaches); **PERSONAL** = the caller's HOME SHELF, always — `private` + own **AND `home_scoped`**, in the workspace `POST /api/boot` names. **Each section owns its own create button**, and the two differ in exactly two props (`shelf` / `shareToChannelId`), each silent when wrong.
  - 🔒 **THE PER-CHANNEL PRIVATE SCOPE IS DELETED, AND ITS CONSEQUENCE IS A RULE: A CONTAINER BASE REACHES /home ONLY THROUGH A CHANNEL GRANT.** An ungranted `private` base sitting in a link container is unreachable from this surface — no scope lists it, and that is intended, not an oversight. **No live rows were stranded** (measured 2026-08-26: the operator's containers hold zero bases); the remedy for one that appears later is the base's own sharing section, not a scope that made a private container shelf look like a place to keep things. Pinned in `pages/home/knowledge-panels.test.tsx › shows NO container base that lacks a grant`.
  - ⚠ **"PERSONAL" IS UI COPY. `visibility: 'private'` IS NOT RENAMED** — not in the column, not in `KbScope`, not in `canSeeBase`, not in any predicate. A section label and a stored value; do not conflate them in code or in a grep.
  - 🔒 **SCOPE C IS A SHELF, NOT A WORKSPACE (Samuel's ruling 2026-08-26; `20260831120000_knowledge_base_home_scoped.sql`).** It lists ONLY bases created FROM that pane. **The exclusion runs BOTH WAYS**: the workspace Knowledge page (`apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/knowledge/index.tsx`) sends `?shelf=workspace` and does not show home-shelf bases either — two PLACES over one table, not a saved filter over one list. ⚠ **THE FILTER IS A `WHERE`, NEVER A CLIENT `.filter()`** (`features/knowledge/server/repository-bases.ts › listBasesForWorkspace`), per §11; an OMITTED `?shelf=` means BOTH shelves, so forgetting it WIDENS — which is why both surfaces thread a module constant rather than a literal. MCP `kb_list_bases` and workspace SEARCH deliberately stay unfiltered. ⚠ **THE MARKER IS NEVER PROJECTED** onto `KnowledgeBase` (absent from `server/dto.ts › KNOWLEDGE_BASE_COLS`), so the SDK-mirrored row type does not widen and no client can re-implement the fence; a surface that must SHOW the shelf gets a sibling key. Writes are fenced by `server/service-base-writes.ts › resolveHomeScope` — person-standing credential + `private` + the caller's own default standard workspace — and REFUSE (403 `HOME_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN`) rather than downgrading to the other shelf.
  - 🔒 **THE SHARED SECTION'S CREATE MAKES BOTH WRITES AT ONCE, AND ROLLS BOTH BACK** (2026-08-27). `POST /api/knowledge/bases` takes `shareToChannelId`: the base lands in the CHANNEL'S CONTAINER and a `channel_resource_grants` row lands at `level: 'visible'`, `guestWrite: false`, via the same `service-channel-grants.ts › setChannelKnowledgeGrant` the base's own sharing section calls — **never a forked write path**. The base is HARD-deleted if the grant fails (a tombstone would still own the slug), because a container base that landed ungranted is invisible on the surface that made it, per the rule above. Fences, in order: the ROUTE runs `isChannelVisibleTo` **before** creating anything (404, the same answer an unknown channel gets — and before, so the caller cannot learn by TIMING what the 404 refuses to say); `withWorkspaceAuth` floors the create at **`member`**, so a GUEST peer standing in the channel is refused; then the grant service's own gates. ⚠ **The base is `private` on the workspace axis and the GRANT carries the audience** — so the create dialog hides its "Who can access" picker in this mode rather than asking a second, contradicting question.
  - 🔒 **NEITHER /home CREATE ASKS THE AUDIENCE (Samuel, 2026-08-27).** The SHARED button is picker-less because its grant answers the question (above); the PERSONAL one is picker-less because **the button pressed IS the answer** — the caller's own home shelf — and two of the picker's three options (`public`, `team`) name audiences /home does not have. **Scoped by an explicit prop, not inferred**: `knowledge/components/create-base-dialog.tsx › Props.audienceFixed`, set by `pages/home/knowledge-panels.tsx` (both buttons) and `pages/home/knowledge-base-view.tsx` (threaded through `knowledge-v2/landing-preview-core.tsx`). **The WORKSPACE Knowledge page passes neither prop and keeps the picker** — that page's create button names no audience, so it is the one place the question is still worth asking. ⚠ `pages/home/knowledge-panels.test.tsx › asks the audience question ONCE` asserted the OPPOSITE for the personal branch until this ruling.
  - 🔒 **AN AGENT TOKEN CANNOT SET A CHANNEL GRANT, BY EITHER DOOR** (2026-08-27). `setChannelKnowledgeGrant` refuses `ctx.source === "agent"` outright. It did not need to while its only caller was the `sessionOnly` PUT; the create-and-share branch is a second caller on a route that is **not** `sessionOnly` and must not become one (MCP `kb_create_base` rides it), so the refusal moved to the one place both doors pass through. **The PUT's `sessionOnly` stays** — the route refuses the CREDENTIAL at the door, this refuses the ACT; removing either is a widening.
  - ⚠ **WHAT THIS RULING WAS NOT: a cross-workspace leak.** It was reported as one; measured against production 2026-08-26, every workspace gate held and the rows shown really did live in the caller's own default standard workspace. **The defect was the RANGE, not a gate** — do not re-diagnose it as scoping. Full measurement in ENGINEERING, "The home shelf: a bug report that named the wrong defect".
  - ⚠ **THE HOME WORKSPACE IS ALREADY IN THIS PAGE'S BOOT QUERY** — `POST /api/boot`'s no-segment answer (`identity.data.workspace` / `.segment` / `.role`), so the third scope costs no second identity read. **It is NULL until the caller is onboarded**, and the section says so rather than fetching a workspace that does not exist.
  - **THE CHANNEL-SCOPED BASE LIST IS ITS OWN CACHE ENTRY** — `["knowledge", "bases:<ws>", "channel:<id>"]` (`pages/home/knowledge-panel-cards.tsx › channelBasesQueryKey`), because the detail mount reads the UNSCOPED list on the plain key against the same workspace and one key would let the two fetchers take turns, blanking `channelGrants` (§9). The third element keeps it reachable by every writer's two-element PREFIX (§8).
  - 🔒 **THE KB MOUNT TAKES A NO-OP `urlSync`, BUILT ONCE IN A `useState` INITIALIZER** (`pages/home/knowledge-base-view.tsx`). /home is not under `/:workspaceSegment`, so `knowledge-v2/routing.ts › createHistoryUrlSync` — the DEFAULT — would `pushState` to `/undefined/knowledge/<segment>` and strand Back; and an adapter rebuilt per render re-runs the controller's write effect with the pre-change selection (that file's own header). ⚠ **`canEdit` FALLS OPEN with no `MyAccessProvider` mounted, and one deliberately is not** — a container has no team grants, so the provider could not change the answer, and a guest never reaches a base to open. The server is the gate. **F-330.**

⚠ **FOUR PRODUCT QUESTIONS ARE OPEN AGAINST THIS SECTION AND ARE DEFERRED TO SAMUEL, NOT SETTLED** — REFACTOR-FINDINGS **F-296** (deleting a container blocks nobody), **F-297** (the public claim surface is unthrottled), **F-298** (no mint quota; the pending-links read filters after its limit), **F-299** (a claim reveals both emails with no accept step). **Do not read this section's silence on any of them as a ruling.** The desktop's fan-out cost is **F-300**; the duplicated predicate is **F-295**.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md, 2026-08-23 stratum (Why `kind='link'` containers).

## 5. Channels — core model

> A **CHANNEL** (or DM) holds many **THREADS**.
> A **THREAD** is ONE exchange between two members about one thing — a single message or a long piece of work. It is SHARED: both members see the same thread, title, and status.
> A **SESSION** is ONE member's agent run working a thread, on THAT member's machine. Each side has its own. A session pauses and resumes; a thread does not. You never see the other member's session, only the messages it sends.

Inside a member's own window there is exactly ONE session, so it never needs a qualifier — **never write "agent session" or "channel session" in UI copy.**

- ⚠ **THERE IS EXACTLY ONE CHANNELS SURFACE, AND IT IS THE PORTED ONE (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 12 — the CUTOVER).** `/:workspaceSegment/channels` and `/:workspaceSegment/channels/:channelId` are two rows over ONE page component (`apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/channels/index.tsx`) over `components/channels-v2/channels-v2-core.tsx`. **The temporary `channels-v2` route, its page folder, its nav row and its deep-link key are GONE** — a re-added row would resolve to nothing — and the two-pane page went with them: `channels-view-core.tsx`, `channel-pane.tsx`, `channels-list-pane.tsx`, `rooms-sidebar.tsx`, `channel-transcript.tsx`, `message-composer.tsx`, plus `activity-event-row.tsx`, `address-picker.tsx`, `lib/agent-display.ts` and `hooks/use-channel-agents.ts`, orphaned by their removal.
  - ⚠ **THE DIRECTORY IS STILL CALLED `components/channels-v2/`, AND THAT IS A COMPONENT-FAMILY NAME, NOT A ROUTE.** Every anchor in this file points into it. **Do not read it as an unshipped surface**; renaming it buys a word, and the strings that mattered — the route, the nav label, the deep-link key, `main/shell-mode.js › CHANNELS_PAGE` — carry no `v2` anywhere. **42 files, measured 2026-08-19** (36 earlier the same day, then 40, then 41; the Settings tab and the pop-out's thread-only surface added two components and two suites, the favourites wiring split `› derivations.ts` off the core, and the settings-inlining ruling added `› settings-agent.tsx` while deleting two files OUTSIDE this folder) — earlier drafts of this bullet and of `pages/channels/index.tsx` said ~28 and 27, both undated and both wrong by then. Re-run, never quote: `ls src/features/channels/components/channels-v2 | wc -l`.
  - **CHANNEL MANAGEMENT CARRIED OVER WHOLESALE**, which was the ruling: create, invite, visibility (via the confirm), archive, delete, leave, folders, and the per-channel tool profile are all reachable from the new surface. ⚠ **The TRUST ROWS were the sixth item in this list and are DELETED (2026-08-22, §6)** — `useTrustRules`, the roster it fed and the `/api/channels/trust` read behind it all went with the inbound consent lane. **An unreachable dialog is a deleted feature with a file still in the tree, which `npx knip` does not flag** — `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/channels/index.test.tsx` asserts the entry points instead, and it opens the tab to reach them.
    - ⚠ **THEY MOVED OFF THE PANE HEADER ON 2026-08-19 (Samuel, live review). THE CHANNEL HEADER'S RIGHT SIDE IS THE INFO TOGGLE AND NOTHING ELSE.** The cluster is the right panel's **SETTINGS tab** now — `components/channels-v2/channel-manage.tsx` still owns every dialog, every write hook and the one `gate`, and `› settings-tab.tsx` is the rows it renders into. The KEBAB is what changed shape — `components/channel-actions-menu.tsx` was **deleted** and its four items are explicit rows, still reporting intent into the same two confirm dialogs. The **Sparkles "Ask the assistant" button was DELETED, not rehomed**: it had no handler, and decoration is not a feature to move. The breadcrumb's bookmark stays — title furniture, not an action.
    - ⚠ **AND THE POPOVERS DIED LATER THE SAME DAY (2026-08-19, Samuel, live review — this SETTLES the "separate open ruling" the bullet above used to carry, and that clause is gone with it). EVERY SETTING ON THIS TAB IS INLINE: no popover, no drill-down, no icon-button hiding a value.** `components/channel-settings-popover.tsx` and `components/channel-folder-control.tsx` were **DELETED**, and their controls rebuilt as `components/channels-v2/settings-agent.tsx › ChannelAgentSettingsView` — the durable launch posture (two `SelectMenu`s — the permission ARM until 2026-08-20, §11), the durable tool profile (a real `radiogroup`, all three options visible), and the working folder (label + Change/Use-default buttons). ⚠ It also rebuilt a TRUST ROSTER, deleted 2026-08-22 — see the bullet below. `› ChannelAgentSettings` is the bridge-bound wrapper `channel-manage.tsx` mounts into the tab's `agent` slot; the tab (`› settings-tab.tsx`) is layout plus the Channel action rows and owns no write.
      - ⚠ **AND THE EXPLAINER COPY WENT LATER THE SAME DAY (2026-08-19, Samuel, live review — third ruling of the day, and it SUPERSEDES the "descriptions always visible" and states-its-lifetime clauses of the two above). "WE SHOULD NOT BE EXPLAINING EVERYTHING TO THE USER."** The first inline pass carried a `text-caption` paragraph under nearly every control and read as documentation. **THE RULE NOW: a row is a NAME + a CONTROL, plus at most a few-word secondary line. No paragraph-style explainer block anywhere on this tab.** Cut: the arm's lifetime sentence, the durable-Tools note, the folder note, and trust's two-sentence scope/effect pair. Each tool profile's full sentence became a **≤5-word** line ("Everything, including connected apps" / "Files, web, and Dopl" / "Local files only"), and trust's pair became ONE line, `settings-agent.tsx › TRUST_SCOPE_HINT`.
        - **WHAT CARRIES THE MEANING NOW IS THE HEADINGS, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE MARGIN.** "For the next request you allow" is the ONLY thing left saying the arm is an arm (§6, §11 — the 30-minute expiry line is gone from the UI, the TTL is not), and "For every session on this channel" is the only thing saying Tools is durable. **A heading change here is a containment-copy change, not a wording preference.**
        - **THREE CLAIMS SURVIVED THE CUT BECAUSE THEY ARE NOT DECORATION**: Tools keeps a line per option (it is the containment pick, and the lines still say what each profile GRANTS and rank none as safe); trust keeps its WORKSPACE-WIDE scope, which a per-channel tab cannot leave implicit; the empty roster keeps one line. **The deleted sentences are preserved as DOCBLOCKS in `settings-agent.tsx`** — developer-facing, and a reader must not restore one to the surface to "restore" a rule.
        - **Pinned as a MEASUREMENT, not a copy list:** `channels-v2/settings-tab.test.tsx › minimal copy — the tab is a settings panel, not documentation` bounds every `text-caption` node on the tab at 8 words and refuses a mid-string sentence break, so a new explainer goes red whichever control it is bolted under. Why: ENGINEERING.md § *THE CONTAINMENT UI*.
      - 🔒 **THE TAB'S ONE WARNING IS A `ConfirmDialog` FIRED ON THE TRANSITION — IT IS NOT, AND MAY NOT BECOME, A STANDING BANNER (Home Knowledge Panels M6, 2026-08-26).** `components/channels-v2/posture-warning.tsx` owns the predicate, the copy and the dialog in one file (`go-public-dialog.tsx`'s precedent), and `› channel-manage.tsx` routes BOTH posture writes through it because either axis can be the flip. The combination is three conjuncts — the Sends axis at `auto_both`, the channel tool profile at `full`, and **somebody else on the roster** — and each is defensible alone. ⚠ **THE DIALOG SHAPE IS THE MINIMAL-COPY RULING ABOVE, APPLIED, NOT AN EXCEPTION TO IT:** an explainer always on screen is read once; a confirmation is read every time it fires, which is exactly as often as this combination gets created. **Nobody may "restore the warning" by adding a `text-caption` paragraph under either select** — that goes red on `settings-tab.test.tsx › minimal copy`, and it would be the weaker copy besides. ⚠ **It fires on the transition INTO the combination only** (`› entersPostureWarning`) — never when the channel is already in it, is leaving it, or is changing something unrelated — and **Cancel writes nothing**, because the dialog precedes the write and there is no revert path. ⚠ **It is a HUMAN'S CONFIRMATION, NEVER AN ENFORCEMENT**: containment is the tool profile plus `main/session-profiles.js`, and the audience ceiling is the §10 grant fence. `messageMode: null` (no bridge, so no posture control at all) warns about nothing — UNKNOWN is not EMPTY (§11). Pinned by `channels-v2/posture-warning.test.tsx`. ⚠ **THE LIVE-AGENT STRIP HAS NO EQUIVALENT** — `› agent-posture.tsx › PostureControls` renders the same two axes but writes the LIVE session mode over the bridge (read per call by `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-io.js › grantArgs`), storing nothing and reaching the same combination unwarned. That is **F-341**, filed and deliberately not built.
      - ⚠ **THE WIRING IS UNTOUCHED, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** Same `prefs.toolProfile`, same `prefs.trust` + the `trustBusyIds` double-fire guard, same `useTrustRules` mounted with the tab, same `useChannelPermissionPreset` / `useChannelFolder` bridges, same one `gate`. **No new endpoint and no new fetch path** — a reader must not conclude a write moved because the chrome did.
      - **DESKTOP-ONLY GROUPS STILL VANISH WHOLE** (the no-dead-rows rule below): a plain browser gets neither the arm nor the folder, headings included, and the durable Tools control — a cloud write — is there either way. ⚠ **THE ARM LEFT THIS TAB ON 2026-08-20 AND THE TWO SELECTS NOW WRITE THE DURABLE LAUNCH POSTURE (§11).** They wrote the arm, under `launch-panel.tsx › LAUNCH_SETTINGS_HEADING` imported rather than re-typed — and **the heading was carrying the entire single-use disclosure on its own**, because the minimal-copy ruling above had cut the 30-minute line. It could not: the rows sit among four durable controls, so the operator read them as a fifth, picked `bypass`, and got manual/ask on every session after the first while the control still said Bypass. The tab's heading is now `settings-agent.tsx › LAUNCH_POSTURE_HEADING` (*"When you launch an agent"*), **every group on this tab is durable**. ⚠ **AND THE ARM DID NOT "GO BACK TO THE REQUEST CARD" — IT WAS DELETED HOURS LATER THE SAME DAY (§6, §11; F-233), and this sentence outlived it by one wave.** Its web controls had not rendered since the 2026-08-18 consent rewrite, so there was no surface to return it to. **There is ONE permission record now.** **The CONSENT CARD's own copy was never touched** — `launch-panel.tsx` renders the same words it did.
      - ⚠ **THE TRUST ROSTER LEFT THIS TAB ON 2026-08-22 (Samuel — the inbound consent retirement, §6).** "Always allow \<teammate>" was standing consent for an INBOUND ask, which is the decision that ruling deleted, so it governed nothing and had never fired. Gone in one wave: the section, its WORKSPACE-WIDE scope hint, its empty-roster line, its `Switch` rows, the `trustedIds` / `trustBusyIds` props, `hooks/use-trust-rules.ts`, the `trust` mutation and its optimistic cache pair in `lib/optimistic-cache.ts`, and `client/query-keys.ts`'s `/api/channels/trust` path + key. ⚠ **`settings-agent.tsx`'s `Note` recipe went with it** — its only two callers were trust's hint and the empty roster — so the minimal-copy ruling above is now true BY CONSTRUCTION on this tab: there is no secondary-line component left to hang a third sentence off.
      - **THE MODEL IS A THIRD ROW IN THE LAUNCH-POSTURE GROUP (Samuel, 2026-08-22).** `Default` (writes NO id — the SDK's own default applies), Fable 5, Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Haiku 4.5. It sits under *"When you launch an agent"* rather than in the every-session group below, and the heading is the reason: an inbound-triggered session carries no launch posture at all, so putting the model there would promise it applies to runs it cannot reach.
        - **ONE ID→LABEL MAP, `lib/agent-models.ts`, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE.** Four surfaces name a model (this row, the live selector, the Agents-tab cards, the agent panel header); a second table is the two-readers-one-fact defect with a MODEL NAME as the thing that drifts. ⚠ **It refuses to be a second authority on the IDS** — the roster is the SDK's and moves without this tree shipping, so `› agentModelLabel` renders an UNKNOWN id as itself rather than erasing it.
        - ⚠ **ABSENT IS NOT `null` IS NOT "Default", and all three distinctions are load-bearing.** The model rides the EXISTING `get/setLaunchPosture` pair, so there is no bridge MEMBER to feature-detect — the capability signal is whether the get reply carried the KEY (`lib/permission-modes.ts › hasModelKey`, an own-key test, never a truthiness test). **A desktop that omits the key gets NO ROW**, because such a build DROPS the field on write and a live row would let the operator pick Opus, report success, and launch every agent on the default with nothing saying so. `model: null` is a CURRENT main saying "no model chosen" and renders "Default".
        - ⚠ **THE MODEL IS NOT A THIRD PERMISSION AXIS.** `lib/permission-modes.ts › normalizePermissionPreset` still rejects a half-valid AXIS PAIR whole — but it carries the model through when present and stays valid without it, or every desktop older than the field would render blank permission controls.
        - **A LIVE AGENT CAN BE SWITCHED TOO** (`components/channels-v2/agent-posture.tsx`), gated on its OWN capability (`components/channels-v2/agents-controls.ts › canSetAgentModel`, detecting `sessions.setModel` and nothing else) — "has the two axes, has no model op" is a real build shape and one flag for both would either hide working controls or render one that can only refuse. ⚠ **The strip states no timing for a model change, and says NOTHING on success**: the two axes can promise "from its next decision" because `main/session-io.js › grantArgs` re-reads them at every gate, and a model swap may instead need the SDK query rebuilt. **The FEED is the confirmation** — main stamps `DesktopSessionSummary.model` from `system/init` and from every assistant message, so the control re-renders from main's own value. Only a REFUSAL earns copy. ⚠ **That value is FREE-FORM** (a dated id, a `[1m]` variant) and need not be one of the four `setModel` accepts, so `lib/agent-models.ts › agentModelOptionsFor` appends the current one — a `SelectMenu` whose value matches no option renders BLANK, which is the surface saying nothing where it has an answer.
        - **THE CARDS SHOW THE SESSION'S EFFECTIVE MODEL, NEVER THE CHANNEL'S STORED PICK** (`components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › agentRunningModel`, off an additive `model` on the summary).
