A live agent may have been switched mid-run or spawned before the posture changed — reading the record instead is F-142's defect restated for a different field. ⚠ **Absent renders NOTHING, not "Default"**: a card states what an agent IS RUNNING, and a build that reports no model has said nothing about that. It rides the detail line that already exists rather than earning a fourth pill.
      - **`components/channel-settings-popover.test.tsx` was rewritten DOWN into `components/channels-v2/settings-tab.test.tsx`** (§14's mixed-file rule), which is now the one root suite over both halves; its DOM/bridge twin is `apps/desktop-ui/src/features/channels/settings-agent.test.tsx`, including the two-surfaces-one-arm case. ⚠ `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/channels/index.test.tsx` reaches the settings by the **Tools radiogroup** now, not by a "Channel settings" button that no longer exists.
    - ⚠ **THE FOURTH TAB WAS `Links`, AND IT WAS A DELIBERATE EMPTY STATE.** Nothing was rehomed out of it — there was nothing in it — and where a channel's files and links land stays open (wiring plan, Risk 10). `channel-actions-menu.test.tsx`'s rules were **rewritten down into `components/channels-v2/settings-tab.test.tsx`**, not deleted with it (§14): the DM-has-no-Leave rule (Q2) is a data-loss guard, not a menu detail.
    - ⚠ **THE MANAGE CLUSTER MOUNTS WITH THE TAB**, so `useTrustRules`' `/api/channels/trust` read no longer fires on page load. `pages/channels/index.test.tsx` asserts BOTH halves — zero requests until the tab opens, and the read arriving with it.
  - **The first-run explainer is KEPT** (Samuel: keep for now, redesign later) and rehomed onto the no-channels branch; it is the ONLY reason `ChannelsV2Core` takes a `Link` prop, and nothing else in that tree may take one.
  - **Design furniture stays HARDCODED** — the activity heatmap, Linked threads and the Assistant / Drafts / Saved-items nav rows, each marked `// HARDCODED — no backing data yet` and fed from `channels-v2/fixtures.ts`. **Never render zeros from missing backing data.** Why: ENGINEERING.md § *Furniture may be hardcoded; a claim about a person may not*.
  - ⚠ **FAVORITES LEFT THAT LIST ON 2026-08-19 (Samuel — this ruling supersedes the keep-hardcoded one for THIS section only; the rest of the furniture above still stands).** It is real, and the shape of the wiring is the point:
    - **STORAGE is a COLUMN, `channel_members.favorited_at timestamptz NULL`** (`20260819120000`) — null = not favourited, a timestamp = when. It is a per-member setting, so it paid §2's two-edit rule: scrubbed in `channels/server/dto.ts › mapMemberRow` AND absent from `20260810120000`'s column `GRANT`, which is the edit that binds PostgREST and CDC. ⚠ It went on `channel_members` rather than into its own table BECAUSE the key matched: `20260818140000_channel_mention_reads.sql` could not, its key being `(user_id, message_id)` with no row to hang it on. The migration argues both sides.
    - **NO NEW READ AND NO NEW ENDPOINT.** `service-reads.ts › listChannels` already loads the caller's own membership row per channel, so the favourite rides out as `Channel.myFavoritedAt` beside `role` and `myAgentToolProfile`, and the sidebar's section is a PARTITION of the list it already had. The WRITE is `PATCH /channels/[channelId]/members` with `favorite: boolean` — the same per-member preference route the tool profile uses (see §3 for why that method stays `sessionOnly`).
      - ⚠ **EVERY READ OF THE FIELD IS `!= null`, NEVER `!== null`** (`channels-v2/sidebar.tsx › isFavorite`, and both compares in `channels-v2/channels-v2-core.tsx`). A desktop build can run against an OLDER deployed server whose `listChannels` predates the column, so `myFavoritedAt` arrives **`undefined`** — a strict compare reads that as favourited and puts EVERY channel in Favorites, which under the move rule below empties both home sections. Same ship-order direction as the pair below: a new desktop against an old server is the side that breaks.
    - **A FAVOURITE IS A MOVE, NOT A SHORTCUT (2026-08-19, Samuel — SUPERSEDES the SHORTCUT / Slack-semantics ruling made earlier THE SAME DAY).** A favourited channel LEAVES Channels or Direct messages and renders only in Favorites; un-favouriting returns it to its home section. **One channel is ONE row in that column** — the two-row version was two places to look for one unread dot and two rows to reconcile after every write. Rows are `ChannelBranch`, same anatomy **and the same thread nesting** as the sections below (this section is the channel's home now, so a bare `ChannelRow` here would have dropped the OPEN channel's thread rows out of the sidebar entirely), **ordered by NAME** — a favourites list is used by pointing, and alphabetical is the only order that never reorders under traffic.
      - ⚠ **A HOME SECTION EMPTIED BY THE MOVE keeps its header and says its ordinary "none yet" line** — the same copy as having none at all, and never `SIDEBAR_NO_MATCHES`, which is a claim about a query nobody typed. The guards read the post-move lists (`channels-v2/sidebar.tsx › roomsHome`, `› directHome`), never the raw props; reading `rooms.length` would say "none match" to a reader who searched for nothing.
    - **NO FAVOURITES → NO SECTION, HEADER INCLUDED** — unlike Channels and Direct messages, which always say "none yet". ⚠ **The FILTER is the exception**: a query that empties a section which HAS rows keeps its header and says `SIDEBAR_NO_MATCHES`, because "you have none" and "none match" are opposite claims that look identical as blank space. `channels-v2/sidebar.test.tsx › the sidebar's real Favorites section` pins all four bullets.
    - The header **BOOKMARK is the toggle** (`channels-v2/message-pane.tsx › PaneHeader`), filled + `aria-pressed` when favourited, wording `Bookmark {name}` / `Remove bookmark from {name}` — **verbatim the knowledge card's family** (`knowledge-v2/home/base-card.tsx`), because one save affordance across the app is one sentence. In a THREAD it still favourites the CHANNEL: the crumb names the channel and no per-(user, thread) row exists.
  - **DROPPED RATHER THAN FAKED, and the line is the same one:** emoji reactions and the link attachment card claim a real person acted, so they are absent, not stubbed — as is the requester-side "N of M agents approved" (F-206). A heatmap claims nothing about anybody.
  - **Every disclosure, chevron, filter and tab row on the surface FUNCTIONS** (Samuel's interaction-completeness ruling). Inert chrome is allowed only where it is marked hardcoded above.
  - 📄 **`pages/channels-v2/MAPPING.md` — the port's intent document — was DELETED with the folder, its rulings migrated here and into §6, §7 and §11.** Do not restore it: it described a mock, and a second statement of live behaviour is the drift this file exists to prevent.
- ⚠ **THE SURFACE THAT TESTS THAT RULE NOW EXISTS: the AGENTS TAB and the AGENT VIEW** (`components/channels-v2/agents-tab.tsx`, `› agent-panel.tsx`, 2026-08-18). They are the operator watching their OWN runtime, which is precisely where a qualifier feels natural — the noun on both is the **AGENT**, and `agents-tab.test.tsx` asserts neither phrase appears in the rendered text of either. **A rename that reintroduces one is a copy regression with a test behind it, not a style preference.**
  - ⚠ **BOTH AGENT SURFACES ARE PER-INSTANCE, NOT PER-THREAD (2026-08-22, F-250/F-251).** Multiplayer puts N of ONE operator's agents on one thread, all posting under that one account with `authorKind: "agent"`, so **thread + author identifies a THREAD, never an agent** — and the panel and the window both promise "this ONE agent, from the inside". Two derivations enforce it: **the SENT lane** takes the instance as a fourth argument (`components/channels-v2/agent-panel.tsx › agentSentMessages`) and discriminates on the writer's own `ChannelMessage.clientMsgId`, the `agent-<agentId>-<n>` stamp `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-outbound-tag.js › nextOwnPostId` mints; **the WORK lane** filters pushed frames on the full three-segment session key (`components/channels-v2/use-agent-narration.ts › narrationMatches`, against `main/session-store.js › sessionKey`).
    - ⚠ **BOTH DEGRADE, NEITHER REFUSES.** A main older than `agentId` runs at most one agent per thread, so the thread IS the instance there: the Sent lane falls back to today's three predicates and the Work lane to the `<channel>:<thread>:` PREFIX. **A dead lane is strictly worse than an exact-where-it-can-be one**, and neither absence is an error state to render.
    - ⚠ **THE SENT LANE EXCLUDES ONLY WHAT IS POSITIVELY ATTRIBUTED TO SOMEBODY ELSE**, and REQUIRING the stamp is the mistake to refuse: it would hide every unstamped row — an older main, an agent that supplied its own `client_msg_id`, and the machine's courtesy no-ops (`main/channel-post.js › postResult`, stamped `agent-<channelId>-<seq>`, which is about the MACHINE). **A lane that silently omits an agent's words is the harder failure to notice than one that shows a sibling's.**

- **`task` == `thread` at the storage boundary, deliberately.** DOMAIN name is `thread` everywhere a human or agent reads (`ChannelThread`, `components/channels-v2/threads-tab.tsx`, `useChannelThreads`, the MCP ops). STORAGE name stays `task`: the `channel_tasks` table, the `metadata.taskId`/`taskMode`/`taskCreatedBy`/`taskTitle`/`taskTarget` wire keys, the `task_*` kinds, the `TASK_*` codes, the `/tasks/**` route paths. A boundary comment marks the mapping in each crossing file.
- **`seq` IS NOT PER-CHANNEL.** The identity sequence lives on the TABLE, so one global counter serves every channel in every workspace: globally unique, strictly increasing per channel but **GAPPY**. **A seq RANGE is never a message count**, and **a cursor is only ever a number the server HANDED you** — never guessed.
- **THE TRANSCRIPT IS A KEYSET WINDOW, NEVER AN OFFSET, AND NEVER THE WHOLE CHANNEL (2026-09-01, F-053 resolved).** `schema-reads.ts › MessageReadQuerySchema` carries **both ends of one window** — `since` walks forward (the await/desktop incremental read), `before` walks backward (the UI's scroll-up page) — and `channels/server/repository-messages.ts › listMessages` is ONE builder with three modes. **What varies is which end the `limit` bites**, not which rows qualify: only the forward mode reads ascending, and a `before` page read ascending would return the channel's oldest `limit` rows every time, i.e. the same page forever. Both predicates ride `channel_messages_channel_seq_idx` on `(channel_id, seq)`, so page N costs what page 1 did. **A `before` page moves NO read watermark** (`service-reads.ts › readMessages`) — history cannot advance a monotonic cursor, and skipping the arithmetic says so.
  - 🔒 **THE SCROLL-BACK HISTORY IS NOT A QUERY-CACHE ENTRY, AND MUST NOT BECOME ONE.** `hooks/use-channel-messages.ts` keeps the newest page as the one `useApiQuery` entry (the entry every optimistic write patches, §8) and holds older pages beside it in `lib/message-window.ts`. A `?before=` sibling entry would sit under the SAME prefix key `use-thread-writes-shared.ts › messagesKey` patches — TanStack matches by array PREFIX — so every send would append its pending row into every loaded page of history, at a `nextSeq` derived from that page's own maximum. **The cost of the choice: a cache patch does not reach the window.** The one that matters is the thread DELETE, so `message-window.ts › dropThreadFromWindow` exists and `channel-surface.tsx` calls it on the delete's own exit callback. Anything added to that write family needs the same treatment or it is half-applied.
  - 🔒 **THE NEWEST PAGE CAN OUTRUN THE LOADED WINDOW, AND A `seq` GAP IS NOT EVIDENCE.** The newest page is refetched on every doorbell and always returns the newest page, so its minimum walks forward; land more than a page between two paints (a working agent posting `task_progress` will) and it steps past where history stopped, leaving rows neither half holds. **`seq` is table-wide (bullet above), so consecutive posts in one channel are never consecutive numbers** and no arithmetic over the rows on hand can detect the hole. `message-window.ts › isContiguous` checks a REMEMBERED boundary cursor instead, and the window is **dropped rather than concatenated** — a rendered hole is two adjacent rows minutes apart with nothing saying why.
  - **`constants.ts › CHANNEL_TRANSCRIPT_PAGE_SIZE` (50) is the read size; `MAX_MESSAGE_LIMIT` (200) is only the schema's cap.** The 200-row open was bounded — never the unbounded read §9 forbids — but it was a hard FLOOR under the channel's history, and paging up is what removes it. ⚠ **A SMALLER FIRST PAGE IS A SMALLER DERIVATION WINDOW:** the channel view's thread cards, escalation cards and outbound send-box join are all derived from the messages ON SCREEN, so a thread quieter than a page has no card until the reader scrolls back. The Threads tab is the record of record for that question and has its own read.
- **Serialized append.** All inserts go through the `channel_message_insert` RPC (service-role EXECUTE only), which takes a per-channel `pg_advisory_xact_lock` **before** the insert, so `seq` commits in strict monotonic order — no committed-out-of-order hole a reader could poll past. Every writer uses it. ⚠ This bullet said "the cron sweep included" until 2026-08-18; **the stale-threads cron was deleted at wiring plan Phase 4** (§13) and the clause named a writer that no longer exists.
- **Channels RLS is stricter than the older tables:** INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE REVOKEd from `authenticated`+`anon`, write policies dropped — **every write is service-role-only through the feature service.** Member SELECT remains for reads and feeds the publication.
- **ADDRESSING IS REQUIRED, FULL STOP — an unaddressed ask triggers NOBODY, in any channel, at any member count.** ⚠ **STRENGTHENED 2026-08-18 (wiring plan Phase 3): the IMPLICIT TRIGGER and the DM AUTO-ADDRESS both RETIRED, in the same change.** The rule used to have two holes in it — `classify` fired on a **known-exact `memberCount === 2`** plus explicit membership, and `resolvePostMetadata` stamped a DM peer into `to_user_id` when the caller named nobody — so "unaddressed reaches nobody" was true only at 3+ and only outside a DM. Both are gone: `to_user_id` comes from the validated `toUserId` and nowhere else, and `classify` no longer reads the member count at all. **Fail-closed BY DESIGN, and now without exception. Never add a broadcast trigger and never re-add a count-keyed one** — the first converts the loop brake into a storm, the second makes the ROSTER a behaviour (below).
  - `GROUP_CHANNEL_MIN_MEMBERS = 3` in `features/channels/constants.ts` SURVIVES, deliberately duplicated in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-addressing.ts` (no shared source tree) and pinned together by `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts` — but it is **now a COPY threshold only.** Nothing branches on it; it decides whether the MCP roster line names a count or talks about "the other member". ⚠ **"Nothing branches on it" was FALSE in `src/**` until 2026-08-18 (wave-2 fix pass):** `components/invite-dialog.tsx › GroupChannelRoutingNote` both gated on `memberCount < GROUP_CHANNEL_MIN_MEMBERS - 1` and recited the threshold in its copy. **Its only reader in `src/**` is now the constant's own file.** ⚠ The command this bullet used to print (`grep -rn GROUP_CHANNEL_MIN_MEMBERS src/`) does NOT show that — it returns hits in three files, the extras being docblock prose that names the constant while explaining why nothing branches on it. **A verification command that contradicts the claim above it trains a reader to ignore both.** Count READERS, not mentions: `grep -rn 'GROUP_CHANNEL_MIN_MEMBERS' src/ | grep -v '^\s*\*'`.
  - ⚠ **THIS IS A SHIP-ORDERED PAIR (§13): WEB DEPLOYS BEFORE A DESKTOP BUILD.** An old desktop against a new server keeps firing its implicit trigger on 2-member channels until it updates — noisy and safe. A new desktop against an old server is the direction that breaks.
- **THE REQUEST FAN-OUT: N addressees, N threads, ONE card.** A thread stays one requester + one target, so the "New agent thread" panel's send loops `createTask` per addressee (`channels/server/service-tasks-fanout.ts › createTaskFanOut`; `createTask` itself is unchanged and the fan-out is a CALLER). Two keys carry it and both are load-bearing:
  - **A per-addressee `client_msg_id`, `${base}:${toUserId}`** (`› addresseeClientMsgId`). ⚠ `channel_tasks` has a partial unique index on `(channel_id, client_msg_id)`, so ONE key across N addressees does not fail — rows 2..N converge on row 1 through the idempotency short-circuit, every call returns a thread, and **the request silently reaches one person.** There is no error to catch: the ROW COUNT is the only witness, and `service-tasks-fanout.test.ts` is what counts it.
  - **A DERIVED group id** (`› fanoutGroupId`, a hash of the creator plus the caller's base key), stamped as reserved `metadata.fanoutGroup` on each opening message. Derived rather than minted per attempt because a retry that re-mints splits one request across two cards; the creator is mixed in so no member can reproduce another's group. **An empty addressee list is a 400** (`schema.ts › TaskFanOutSchema`), not an empty success.
- **The web states the rule in exactly two places** (`channels/components/channels-v2/composer-request-panel.tsx › AgentRequestPanel` — "No agent addressed — this thread reaches nobody.", a SEND-time report on the New-agent-thread panel; **`channels/components/invite-dialog.tsx › GroupChannelRoutingNote` — the CHANNELS one**, the standing explanation, shown UNCONDITIONALLY and stating the thread rule the composer never mentions); **the MCP lane in exactly one** (`channel-addressing.ts`). ⚠ The composer half has moved TWICE and this bullet named the first move only: it was `channels/components/message-composer.tsx` until the 2026-08-18 cutover (deleted), then `channels-v2/composer.tsx` until the 2026-08-22 split at the 500-line cap, which is where it lives now. **The doc said `composer.tsx` until 2026-08-26** — a bare-path reference, so `scripts/check-doc-refs.mjs` could not catch it; it is a symbol anchor now. ⚠ **The invite-dialog half was still count-keyed until the 2026-08-18 wave-2 fix pass** — gated below the group threshold and reciting it, so it taught the retired rule and said nothing in a two-member channel; it now takes no props. ⚠ **`invite-dialog.tsx` is an ambiguous basename: `members/components/invite-dialog.tsx` also exists and states nothing about addressing.** Anything else implying an unaddressed channel message reaches an agent is wrong — **"broadcast" is not a shape this product has.**
- **THE LOOP BRAKE is no longer a special case — it is the general rule.** An UNADDRESSED message can never trigger, whoever wrote it (`dopl-desktop-app/main/targeting.js › classify`). It used to be an agent-only branch sitting above the implicit 1:1 trigger, and the retirement of that trigger is what generalized it. Member-count-independent, as it always was.
- **THE `classify` VERDICT SET IS FOUR WORDS, AND ONE OF THEM CHANGED MEANING (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 7).** `trigger` (consent prompt, maybe a spawn) · `task-reply` (a passive notice, and the SUPPRESSION that stops a reply in my own thread looking like a fresh request) · **`fyi` — now "a message that @-TAGGED ME", not "a message I can see"** · `ignore`. Every `fyi` return in `classify` is conjoined with `targeting.js › mentionsMe`, so a post I am a member of and was not tagged in returns `ignore`. **The notification policy is §11; what belongs here is the verdict shape**, because a reader who still believes `fyi` means "visible" will reintroduce the notification storm by "fixing" a branch. `task-reply` is deliberately NOT gated — it is a routing statement, and the tag gates its NOTICE one layer up in `listener-messages.js`'s dispatch. ⚠ **WIDENED 2026-08-20 (the session-window retirement's pair, §11): the suppression now covers EVERY author kind and EVERY mode.** The branch reads only the server-stamped pair (`taskCreatedBy` = me, `taskTarget` = the author) plus the address — the old `authorKind === 'agent'` and `taskMode === 'interactive'` conjuncts existed so the requester's live session window could claim what fell through, and with that lane retired either conjunct failing turned the counterparty's reply IN MY OWN THREAD into a consent card against myself. Anybody OUTSIDE the pair (a third member in my thread, not-my-thread, unaddressed) still classifies as before. Pinned by `test/main-audit-targeting.test.mjs`.
- ⚠ **THE ROSTER IS NO LONGER A BEHAVIOUR, and the departure sweep's REASON changed with it.** A ghost `channel_members` row used to turn a live 1:1 into a "3-party" room and silently disable the implicit trigger — no error, no signal. With the count unread, that failure mode is gone. **The sweep stays and is still required:** a ghost row keeps a departed teammate ADDRESSABLE in the picker and in `op="members"`, keeps every displayed roster count wrong, and leaves a one-member DM that renders as a conversation with yourself. A workspace departure REMOVES the row (`workspaces/server/membership-admin.ts › removeMember` → `channels/server/service-workspace-departure.ts`), best-effort, always AFTER the membership delete.
- **TERMINATION IS A ROW DELETE, NOT A STATUS FLIP.** Nothing writes `workspace_members.status` to anything but `'active'`; no self-serve leave route, no deactivation. "No longer active" and "has no row" are the same statement. **Adding a deactivated state would silently break the departure sweep and its backfill, neither of which fails loudly.**
- **A thread is one requester + one target.** Reads are channel-visible (a third member CAN read a pair's thread; deliberate). Writes → **403 `TASK_FORBIDDEN`** unless the poster is `created_by` or `target_user_id` (`isThreadParticipant`, synchronous — a threaded post costs no participant query). A legacy `task-{channelId}-{seq}` id is validated against its opening message's `{author, to_user_id}` pair, and a non-participant's tag is **SILENTLY STRIPPED, never 403'd** (installed desktops post legacy ids for lifecycle events).
- **A thread may not be addressed to its own creator** — 400 `CHANNEL_TASK_SELF_TARGET`, checked AFTER the addressee-membership check and BEFORE the idempotency short-circuit (behind it, a retry returns the stored dead thread as a success).
- **Reserved metadata is STRIPPED from caller input and re-stamped only from validated fields**, at the single point `channels/server/service-writes-metadata.ts › resolvePostMetadata`. ⚠ **THE LIST LIVES IN CODE — read the `delete metadata.*` run in that function plus `service-writes-metadata-markers.ts` before trusting any prose copy, this one included.** As measured 2026-08-11 it is:
  - **Stripped, then re-stamped from a server or validated source:** `to_user_id`, `summary`, `runtime`, `appVersion`, `session_id`, `intent`, `handoff`, **`fanoutGroup`**, **`mentionedUserIds`**, and the four task keys (`taskMode`/`taskCreatedBy`/`taskTitle`/`taskTarget`). ⚠ **`fanoutGroup` is reserved on `handoff`'s terms and for a sharper reason:** the transcript renders every opening message sharing it as ONE card, so a settable value would draw your own thread inside somebody else's request. Like `handoff`, it stamps ONLY onto a post carrying a thread tag its poster is entitled to.
  - **Stripped and NEVER re-stamped** (no writer left; the strip is what keeps them unforgeable): `to_user_notify`, and the three dead agent-attribution keys `to_agent_id`/`to_agent_ids`/`author_agent_id`. ⚠ **THEIR LAST RENDERER WENT AT THE 2026-08-18 CUTOVER** — `lib/agent-display.ts` and `channel-transcript.tsx` resolved a stored `author_agent_id` to "quartz · Ada's agent" and both were deleted with the two-pane page (F-218). **They stay in the strip list anyway, and that is the safe direction, not an oversight:** removing a key from the strip is a WIDENING that makes it caller-settable, so the day a reader returns the forgery returns with it — and the corollary below says a reader comes back only AFTER the key does.
  - **The markers**, from `service-writes-metadata-markers.ts`: **`CALM_FLAG_KEYS` — SIX, not five**: `declined`, `dropped`, `interrupted`, `capped`, `ended`, **and `session_ended`**. The sixth rides a `task_progress` rather than a `task_failed` and is NOT read by `calmTerminalStatus`, which is exactly why it is easy to leave out of a list and exactly why it is reserved on the same terms — it changes how the peer's card READS.
    - ⚠ **`CLOSE_PROPOSAL_KEYS` (`closeProposed`/`closeOutcome`) AND `REOPEN_MARKER_KEY` (`threadReopened`) LEFT THIS LIST ENTIRELY, 2026-08-18** (wiring plan Phase 4), and the ASYMMETRY with the dead agent keys above is the rule: **a key stays reserved with no writer only while something still RENDERS it.** The dead agent keys do (stored rows carry them). Nothing renders a close prompt or a reopen notice — the component, `readCloseProposal` and `isThreadReopenedMarker` were deleted in the same change — so there is no forgery surface left to defend. **A reader coming back means the key comes back to the strip list FIRST.**
  - `taskId` stays caller-settable by design (validated, and silently dropped when it is not the poster's own exchange).
- **MENTIONS ARE RESOLVED SERVER-SIDE AT INSERT, AND A MENTION IS NOT AN ADDRESS (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 6).** `metadata.mentionedUserIds` is stripped from caller input unconditionally and re-stamped only from the server's own parse of the BODY against this channel's roster (`channels/server/service-writes-metadata-mentions.ts › resolveBodyMentions`, fold 9 of `resolvePostMetadata`). ⚠ Reserved on `fanoutGroup`'s terms and for a sharper reason: **the set decides whose Tags inbox a message lands in, and — since Phase 7 SHIPPED the same day — it is what DECIDES WHETHER THE DESKTOP NOTIFIES AT ALL (§11), so a caller-settable value is a notification-forgery primitive.** The alternative — an explicit MCP argument — is worse on its own terms (§10: an unknown tool argument is refused BY NAME, so a mistyped one narrates success over an invisible delivery failure).
  - **IT DOES NOT ADDRESS ANYBODY AND STARTS NOBODY'S AGENT.** `to_user_id` still comes from the validated `toUserId` and nowhere else, at every member count. An @-tag is an INBOX fact. **Never wire a trigger to this key** — it is the one reserved key a body can produce, so a trigger on it would make an unaddressed post reach somebody after all.
  - ⚠ **NOT gated on a surviving thread tag**, unlike `handoff` / `fanoutGroup`: those are claims ABOUT a thread; this is a claim about the BODY, which stands on its own in a plain channel post. Stamped only when non-empty, so no existing row shape moved.
  - **ONE PARSER, `channels/lib/mentions.ts`** — the server's resolution AND the transcript's tint run it, because a second copy is how the two ends come to disagree about what counts as a tag. **THE MATCH RULE:** a token is `@` + a run of non-whitespace non-`@`, trailing punctuation stripped, compared LOWERCASE-EXACT (never a prefix, never a substring) against roster handles derived from the display name and the email local part — the SLUG, the whole source with whitespace removed, and its first word. ⚠ **AMBIGUITY FAILS CLOSED: a handle two members claim resolves to NOBODY**, or the order a roster read happens to return would decide whose inbox a message lands in.
    - **THE HANDLE CONVENTION IS THE SLUG: lowercase, whitespace runs → `-` (Samuel, 2026-08-27).** "Samuel Wang" is `@samuel-wang`. **ONE SLUGGER, `channels/lib/mentions.ts › mentionSlug`**, used by the roster derivation AND by agent handles — a second `.replace(/\s+/g, "-")` at a call site is how the composer's insert and the transcript's tint come to spell one name two ways. ⚠ **IT IS FIRST IN `handlesOf`, AND ORDER IS WHAT MAKES IT THE CONVENTION**: `insertableHandle` returns the first handle the index resolves back to that member, so the head of the list is what the picker INSERTS. ⚠ **THE OLDER FORMS ARE KEPT, NOT REPLACED** — the token lives in the message BODY as plain text with no id under it, so dropping the squashed form would un-tag every `@samuelwang` ever written. **The set only grows.**
    - **AGENTS ARE A SECOND, SEPARATE NAMESPACE (`channels/lib/agent-mentions.ts`, 2026-08-27), AND IT DECIDES TINT ONLY.** An agent is not a member, the server's resolver correctly answers nobody for it, and **that must not change** — a caller-settable mention set is a notification-forgery primitive. An agent answers to its **slugged custom name** (`main/agent-names.js`) and **always** to `agent-<id>`; the id form is never withdrawn by a rename, because a name is machine-local and mutable while the id is minted once and never recycled. ⚠ **THE ROSTER IS ASKED FIRST AND WINS**, so naming an agent after a colleague cannot repoint a token the server stamped at the colleague. ⚠ **NO VIEWER HIGHLIGHT ON THE AGENT ARM** — the `bg-link/10` wash means "the server says this tagged YOU", which is a fact about a member.
    - 🔑 **THE ROUTING VERDICT IS MAIN'S, AND IT ACCEPTS BOTH SHAPES.** `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-dispatch.js › mentionedAgentIds` matches `@(?:agent-)?<id>` behind a negative lookbehind (which is what stops one mention resolving twice) and INTERSECTS with the ids live on that thread — so `agent-` in front of a guess grants nothing. ⚠ **A convention change that moved only the renderer would have tinted a token this parser ignored**, which is the tint-says-tagged / stamp-says-nobody split F-266 already cost a wave to fix once. Pinned in `test/session-dispatch.test.mjs`.
  - **AN AGENT'S NAME IN THE TRANSCRIPT IS RESOLVED AT RENDER, NEVER STAMPED (Samuel, 2026-08-27).** `AuthorIndex` carries an `agents` map (`view-model.ts › indexAgents`, built from the desktop feed the Agents tab already reads — no new read, no second poll), and `attribution-pill.tsx › attributionName` takes the CURRENT name off it. ⚠ **THE BUG THIS FIXED WAS AN OMISSION, NOT A STALE CACHE**: that function hardcoded `Agent #<id>` and never consulted the store, while `agents-model.ts › agentDisplayName` had preferred the operator's own name since 2026-08-25 — so a rename showed on the CARD and never in the chat area. Two renderers, one fact, one of them not asking. ⚠ **NOTHING IS COPIED INTO A MESSAGE ROW AND NOTHING MAY START BEING** — a name in the payload freezes at send time and puts the transcript back where it was, one row at a time. A rename lands on main's next summary push, with no refetch and no message rewritten.
  - ⚠ **CODE DOES NOT TAG (2026-08-22, Samuel).** A handle inside an inline code span or a fenced block names NOBODY — `lib/mentions-mask.ts › maskCodeRegions`, run inside `mentions.ts › mentionTokensOf`, so both the resolver and the cheap-exit check go through it. ⚠ The MCP side deliberately does NOT mirror this rule — `channel-post-guidance.ts › classifyMentions` counts a backticked handle as one the AUTHOR wrote, so it still reports `tags=0/1`, which is the signal that sends a reader to this cause. **Measured, not theorised:** two agents writing DOCUMENTATION about @-tagging shipped backticked handles and TAGGED BOTH OPERATORS for real (seqs 647 / 653, 2026-08-21). ⚠ **The RENDERER already obeyed it and the SERVER did not**, which is the disagreement the one-parser rule exists to prevent. The two ends reach it by different mechanisms and that is deliberate: `message-markdown.tsx` gets it STRUCTURALLY (a `codespan` / `code` token never reaches `MentionText`), the server from the masker; the RULE is written down once, in `lib/mentions-mask.ts` (SPLIT out of `mentions.ts` on 2026-08-22 at the 500-line cap — §1 — and re-exported through it, so no import moved). ⚠ **Indented four-space blocks are deliberately NOT masked** — `marked` calls them code, so the two ends disagree in that one shape (server tags, transcript does not tint), and what justifies leaving it is the COST of guessing at indentation, NOT the direction. ⚠ **NEITHER DIRECTION IS "THE SAFE ONE" AND THIS BULLET CALLED THE SAME SHAPE BOTH "worse" AND "SAFE" UNTIL 2026-08-22:** an unstamped tint lies to the AUTHOR, a tintless stamp lies to the RECIPIENT, and the incident that bought the rule (seqs 647 / 653) was the second kind. ⚠ The MCP mention scan deliberately does NOT mirror this (see the note above on `channel-post-guidance.ts › classifyMentions`): it asks "did the AUTHOR try", so a backticked-only body still reports `tags=0/1` rather than silently claiming nothing was written.
  - ⚠ **MARKUP IS NOT A HANDLE, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (2026-08-22, F-266 — RESOLVED).** The two ends now agree on every shape anybody measured but one. Delimiters that WRAP a handle come off the token, so `**@diana**`, `*`/`__`/`_`/`~~` and `<b>@diana</b>` all tag; text markdown reads as STRUCTURE is blanked, so an escaped `\@diana`, a link/image DESTINATION and a link reference definition tag nobody. **`*`, `_` and `~` joined `TRAILING_PUNCTUATION`, and the strip is a LOOP to a fixed point** — `>` is already in that class, so one pass in the wrong order turns `@diana</b>` into `@diana</b`.
    - ⚠ **THE BOLD CASE IS WHY THIS IS IN §5 AND NOT A TIDY-UP.** `**@sam** I am blocked` is how an agent writes the escalation the bullet below enables — it TINTED and stamped NOBODY, so the transcript told the author it had reached a human while the Tags inbox stayed empty and `main/targeting.js › mentionsMe` never fired. **The SPA suite pinned the tint and the server suite pinned the stamp, and neither pinned them against each other.**
    - ⚠ **AUTOLINKS STILL TAG, DELIBERATELY.** `<https://…/@diana>` and a bare `https://…/@diana` become `link` tokens whose `tokens` ARE the url text, so the transcript **tints them**. Making a URL's `@` stop tagging would stamp nothing under a visible highlight — a new divergence wearing a fix's clothes. **They already agree; leave them.**
    - **THE GUARD IS THE MEASUREMENT: `lib/mentions-tint-parity.test.ts`** walks `marked` the way `message-markdown.tsx` walks it, collects every string reaching `MentionText`, and asserts tint === stamp over its whole table. It carries a red-proof case and a tripwire that reads that component's source, so a new token arm there fails HERE. ⚠ **Anything claiming the two ends agree must point at that run, not at this bullet.**
  - ⚠ **THE AUTHOR IS DROPPED FOR A HUMAN AND KEPT FOR AN AGENT (2026-08-22, Samuel).** A human self-tag is not an inbox item (and a badge you could inflate by typing your own name). An AGENT posts on its operator's account, so a tag at "the author" IS its escalation path to the one human who can unblock it — dropping it made the Tags inbox unreachable from the machine that most needs it. **The discriminator is the CREDENTIAL, never the body:** `ctx.source === "agent"` (i.e. `auth.agentTokenId`), passed as `resolveBodyMentions`'s `authorIsAgent`. It is still not an ADDRESS — it starts no session on the operator's machine or anyone else's.
  - **THE ROSTER READ IS LAZY AND SHARED** (§12 — do not tax the hot write path). The token scan is a string check and runs FIRST, so a body with no `@` pays nothing; when a read is needed it is memoized on the promise and shared with `resolveDirectPeer`, so a post needing both pays ONE `channel_members` read.
  - **READ-STATE IS A ROW PER READ, `channel_mention_reads`** (`20260818140000`), natural key `(user_id, message_id)`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so a second mark is a no-op that KEEPS the first `read_at`. A cursor was rejected on the model: the inbox marks items read OUT OF ORDER and no monotonic watermark can express that. Landing it in its own table also kept `channel_members` (which IS published) from growing a per-member setting and the §2 two-edit rule that comes with one. **Service-role writes only, RLS narrowed to `user_id = auth.uid()`** — when a teammate read their mentions is nobody else's business.
  - ⚠ **DELIBERATELY OUT OF THE PUBLICATION (§7 + §12).** Every row is written by the viewer's own client about the viewer's own reading, so there is no peer event; a published table with no subscriber costs WAL decode plus a per-subscription RLS evaluation on every write, forever. **New mentions still arrive live — they are `channel_messages` rows, and that doorbell is already paid for.** Replica identity stays DEFAULT: nothing subscribes, and every delete is a cascade from a row that is itself going away.
  - **THE UNREAD BADGE IS CLIENT ARITHMETIC** over the projection (`components/channels-v2/info-tab.tsx`), never a second server derivation — same shape as presence's 90 s window and the sidebar's 24 h one, and the same reason: two derivations of one number are two numbers that disagree later.
- 🔒 **A STRUCTURED ESCALATION IS `kind='message'` PLUS RESERVED METADATA, AND THE KIND IS NOT NEGOTIABLE (Samuel, 2026-08-31).** An agent asks a human a question as STRUCTURE — `metadata.escalation` = `{issue, context, options[2..6] each {label, consequence}, recommendation{index, why}|null}` — and the channels transcript renders it as a CARD WITH OPTION BUTTONS. The shape is stated ONCE, in `src/features/channels/escalation.ts`.
  - ⚠ **THREE INDEPENDENT REASONS IT IS NOT A NEW `kind`, ANY ONE SUFFICIENT.** (1) `dopl-desktop-app/main/targeting.js › classify` returns `ignore` for every `m.kind !== 'message'`, so a card on any other kind **could never notify the human it is asking** — half the feature, silently gone. (2) The six kinds are a column CHECK (`20260725120000_channels.sql`) plus four hand-mirrored unions, and every lane is already owned: `system` is server-only, the three lifecycle kinds are refused from an agent token, `task_progress` is the milestone lane. (3) **The transcript's own card precedent is metadata-keyed, not kind-keyed** — `components/channels-v2/view-model.ts › threadIdOf` is how a thread card is decided.
  - ⚠ **BOTH KEYS ARE RESERVED ON `fanoutGroup`'s EXACT TERMS**, stripped from caller input unconditionally and re-stamped only from validated top-level fields (`server/service-writes-metadata.ts › resolvePostMetadata` folds 10 and 11, split into `service-writes-metadata-escalation.ts` because that fold READS ANOTHER ROW and enforces an authorization). The card renders CONTROLS THAT WRITE BACK AND WAKE AN AGENT, so a caller-settable key would let any member hang a working control — and a wake behind it — off any words at all.
  - ⚠ **THE MESSAGE `body` STILL CARRIES THE WHOLE QUESTION IN PROSE, AND THAT IS THE DEGRADE CONTRACT.** Four live surfaces know nothing about the key — `dopl_channel(op="read")`, a plain browser, the pop-out thread window, and every desktop older than the card. **A card whose absence leaves a blank row is not shippable**; this one leaves the words a prose escalation would have had. Composed by `escalation.ts › escalationBody`, hand-mirrored in the MCP tree and pinned against it (§10).
  - 🔒 **WHO MAY ANSWER: THE MEMBERS IT TAGGED, ELSE THE AUTHOR — and it is the conservative side of a real fork.** `server/service-writes-metadata-escalation.ts › escalationAnswerers` is the fence (403 `CHANNEL_ESCALATION_FORBIDDEN`); `components/channels-v2/view-model-escalation.ts › toEscalationRow` restates the same predicate to decide whether the BUTTONS ARE DRAWN, and it must stay the same one or a member sees a control that can only refuse. **A peer's escalation is answerable by you exactly when it tagged you**, which is what makes another member's card read-only with no second concept.
    - ⚠ **IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT `to_user_id`.** Addressing a member TRIGGERS their listener and starts THEIR agent — precisely wrong for a question that exists because only a person can answer it. An @-tag is an INBOX fact and starts nobody, and it is already server-resolved, caller-unsettable and ambiguity-fails-closed. The AUTHOR fallback is this section's own 2026-08-22 ruling made useful: an agent's tag at its own operator is kept *because it is its escalation path to the one human who can unblock it*.
    - ⚠ **THE ALTERNATIVE — CHANNEL-WIDE ANSWERABILITY — IS FLAGGED, NOT BUILT.** It is defensible, but it lets a bystander steer somebody else's agent with one click. **Building the narrow one first is reversible; building the wide one first is not.**
  - **THE ANSWER IS A CHANNEL MESSAGE, AND ITS PUBLICNESS IS A RULING.** `metadata.escalationAnswer` = `{escalationMessageId, optionIndex, agentId}`, stamped onto the answering member's own post. An escalation is a question about SHARED work asked in a SHARED room; routing the answer down a private lane would leave a visible question with an invisible answer and a card that reads unanswered forever. It is also what keeps this feature independent of the private direct lane.
    - ⚠ **`agentId` IS DERIVED, NEVER ACCEPTED** — off the ESCALATION's own `client_msg_id` stamp (`lib/agent-post-stamp.ts › parseAgentPostStamp`, MOVED there from `agents-model.ts` on 2026-08-31 and re-exported, because a service must not import a `"use client"` module; **one declaration, still**). A caller-supplied id would make this key a wake primitive aimed anywhere. `null` is the ordinary answer for an escalation filed by an EXTERNAL MCP session, and the answer is still delivered to every live agent on the thread.
    - ⚠ **403 HERE, WHERE A FOREIGN THREAD TAG IS SILENTLY STRIPPED, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE RULING.** The strip exists because installed desktops post legacy `task-…` ids; `escalationAnswer` has no installed writers, and a silent strip would let a button report success over an answer that reached nobody — the failure the whole card exists to remove.
    - **ONE ANSWER PER ESCALATION, ENFORCED AT REST** — a partial unique index over `(metadata->'escalationAnswer'->>'escalationMessageId')` (`20260902120000`), surfacing as 23505 → 409 `CHANNEL_ESCALATION_ANSWERED`. ⚠ **NOT converged onto the first answer** the way a `client_msg_id` collision is: an idempotency retry is the SAME caller asking for the SAME thing, and reporting somebody else's decision back as this caller's own is a different act. The transcript applies the same first-wins rule client-side (`view-model-escalation.ts › answersByEscalation`).
  - ⚠ **IT RENDERS IN BOTH ROW PIPELINES, AND THERE ARE TWO.** The channel and thread transcripts share `view-model-rows.ts` → `transcript.tsx` → `authored-row.tsx`; the AGENT STREAM has its own union, builder and dispatch and mounts no `AuthoredRow` (`agent-stream-escalation.tsx`). A card in one and not the other is the *"where did my question go"* report, so it is implemented twice on purpose and the two ends are held against each other by two suites.
  - ⚠ **IT STAYS IN THE CHANNEL VIEW EVEN WHEN THREADED.** A threaded message normally collapses into its thread CARD there; a question waiting on a human is the one row that must not be one click away from being seen. It is also the ONE duplication `channelRows` allows.
  - **BUTTONS ARE ABSENT, NEVER DISABLED**, when the viewer may not answer or the host hands no write callback — the pop-out thread window and the guest lane both land there. An inert button is indistinguishable from a broken one.
- **`authorKind` is CALLER-ASSERTABLE, not derived from the credential.** An explicit body value wins; `ctx.source` is the fallback. Load-bearing — the desktop posts agent results over the operator's cookie session. NOT assertable: `author_user_id` is always `ctx.userId`, and `system` is rejected by the route schema. **Treat `authorKind` as a display/routing claim scoped to one user, never as an authentication fact.**
  - **THE TRANSCRIPTS SPELL THIS OUT IN LAYOUT: side comes from `author_user_id`, the attribution pill's agent NAME comes from `authorKind`, and the two never swap jobs.** `channels-v2/transcript.tsx` (and `view-model-rows.ts › toMessageRow` behind it) hangs a row right when `authorUserId === currentUserId` and names it "Agent" when `authorKind === "agent"` — an agent therefore renders on ITS OPERATOR'S side, wearing ITS OPERATOR'S avatar, never in a third column. Keying the side off `authorKind` would let a caller choose which half of somebody else's screen their words land on. `channels-v2/transcript.test.tsx` pins the peer-claiming-`agent` case specifically. ⚠ **The SIDE is now `items-end` / `items-start` on the row, not `flex-row-reverse`** — the row became a COLUMN when the pill moved above the bodies (below), and the rule it expresses did not change.
    - ⚠ **ATTRIBUTION IS ONE PILL, AND THE GREY `Agent · <id>` CHIP IS DELETED (2026-08-22, Samuel, from two reference screenshots; it SUPERSEDES the chip ruling made earlier the same day).** Every message group — human and agent alike, in the channel view and the thread view — is headed by `components/channels-v2/attribution-pill.tsx › AttributionPill`: a rounded capsule on the kit's `.bento` face carrying the author's AVATAR on the left and TWO STACKED LINES beside it, the NAME over the MESSAGE TIMESTAMP (the transcript's existing time format, in the slot the reference screenshots gave a job subtitle). The message blocks render BELOW it at full column width; the old `w-10` avatar gutter and its baseline name/chip/time row are gone, and so is the continuation SPACER that gutter needed. **ONE header idiom for the whole transcript** — a second shape for agent rows is how two surfaces come to word one exchange differently, and the transcript's own thread CARD (`thread-card-row.tsx › ThreadCardMessage`) renders the same `authored-row.tsx › AuthoredRow` and so wears the same pill. ⚠ **THE SHELL AND THE CARD LEFT `transcript.tsx` ON 2026-08-28**, at the 500-line cap and on the sharing seam: once the card is its own file the shell cannot live inside either consumer without the other importing through it. Both moved VERBATIM.
      - ⚠ **AN AGENT'S PILL IS A BUTTON THAT OPENS THAT AGENT'S VIEW; A HUMAN'S IS NOT (2026-08-28, Samuel, over a channel transcript screenshot of an `Agent #rrr5o70x` pill).** An agent-authored pill renders as a real `<button>` (`aria-label` "Open agent <name>", the app's raised ±1px press as utilities — no local shadow or colour recipe) and fires the SAME open mechanism the Agents tab's card fires: `transcript.tsx › Message` → `message-pane.tsx` → `channel-surface.tsx`'s `onOpenAgent={sel.setOpenAgent}`, the identical function `info-panel.tsx` hands `agents-tab.tsx`. **There is no second pipe**, and the key is the row's `agentId`, which IS `agents-model.ts › agentKey`'s answer for any session that has one. ⚠ **THE GATE IS `AuthorIndex.agents` PLUS THE CALLBACK, and it is host context this transcript already holds** — not a new capability prop. A pill only becomes a button when THIS MACHINE knows the agent it names, which is exactly when `agent-panel.tsx` can resolve `openAgent` back to a session; so the guest web lane and every plain browser (no bridge ⇒ empty map), the pop-out thread window (empty map AND no callback), a PEER's agent (never in the map — the peer cards' "state only, never openable" rule, for free) and an UNSTAMPED post (`agentId` null) all keep the inert `<span>`. **Absent, never disabled** — an inert button is indistinguishable from a broken one. ⚠ **ONE FACE FOR BOTH ELEMENTS**: the `<button>` and the `<span>` carry the same classes, the same `data-agent-id` and the same `data-attribution-pill`, because /home repaints these on that attribute (`pages/home/home.module.css`) and a forked face would silently stop reaching agent rows. Pinned in `components/channels-v2/agent-pill-open.test.tsx`.
      - **THE AGENT-NESS MOVED INTO THE NAME LINE, AND THE `#` IS LITERAL.** `attribution-pill.tsx › attributionName` is the one place it is worded, in three cases and no more: a human row reads the member's name (`view-model-rows.ts › labelFor`, including "You" for the viewer); a STAMPED agent post reads **`Agent #<agentId>`**; an UNSTAMPED one reads plain **`Agent`**. ⚠ **Unstamped is "cannot say", never a blank and never an invented id** — three real classes carry no per-instance stamp (a main older than it, an agent supplying its own idempotency key, and every machine-level courtesy post), and all three must read exactly as they did (§11). ⚠ It is ONE text node, not a name plus a separator: the chip's middot was decoration a screen reader spelled out. ⚠ **AND IT SURVIVES THE AGENT (2026-08-25).** The name is derived from the MESSAGE — `parseAgentPostStamp(clientMsgId)` — and from no local table, so ending an agent, sweeping its history or DELETING it outright leaves every one of its rows reading exactly as it did. **The operator's own display name is not in this path at all** (it renders on the agent's CARD, off the desktop feed), which is why deleting an agent can destroy that label without touching a transcript. `components/channels-v2/agent-delete.test.tsx` pins the before/after render.
      - **ONE PARSE HELPER, `components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › parseAgentPostStamp`, AND THE REGEX IS DECLARED THERE ONCE.** `components/channels-v2/agent-panel.tsx › agentSentMessages` (F-251) reads the same token, and two hand-written charsets for one wire format is how the two come to disagree about what an agent id is. ⚠ **Do not re-declare the pattern** — a duplicate-charset attempt was already caught at review.
      - **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE ANCHORING, NOT A PREFIX.** `main/channel-post.js › postCourtesy` stamps MACHINE-level posts `agent-<channelUUID>-<seq>`, and a channel UUID can BEGIN with eight id-shaped characters — so a `startsWith` would attribute a machine post to an agent that does not exist. Same rule, same reason, as `agentSentMessages`.
      - ⚠ **THE RUN-GROUPING IS THE HALF WITH NO PILL TO WORK WITH, and it survived the redesign unchanged.** A continuation drops the pill entirely — avatar, name line and timestamp — so two of one operator's agents alternating collapsed into ONE unbroken run under one name, which is the report exactly. `components/channels-v2/view-model-rows.ts › isContinuation` therefore breaks on a different agent id (agent rows only: a HUMAN's `client_msg_id` is caller-chosen and must not be able to split their own run). Two unstamped posts still group; `null` never MATCHES a real id, so both directions fail toward showing a name. **F-251's rule; do not let a pill redesign quietly take it, because the pill cannot fix a row that has none.**
      - **THE ACCENT IS A HINT, THE ID IS THE TRUTH — AND IT NOW RIDES THE PILL'S BORDER.** `components/channels-v2/bits.tsx › agentAccent` is a pure function of the id — no counter, no order-of-appearance index — so it survives a refetch reordering the transcript. It has FOUR faces because that is how many non-severity identities the token set holds: ⚠ **the `success`/`caution`/`warning`/`danger` ramp is deliberately excluded**, since keying an identity off it would paint one of an operator's agents alarm-red and rank the rest. Four faces over an 8-char id space COLLIDE, which is why the id TEXT ships beside the accent rather than instead of it. ⚠ **ONE PALETTE, RE-USED, NOT A SECOND ONE.** The triple was built for a chip, so the pill applies it and then neutralises the FILL and the INK with two trailing utilities (`cn` is `tailwind-merge`; later wins within a group) — only the BORDER COLOUR survives, the elevated card face stays the card face and the name stays primary ink. `agent-attribution.test.tsx` pins both halves; a refactor that lets the fill through turns the transcript into four differently-coloured boxes. ⚠ `bits.tsx › AgentChip` itself is NOT deleted — `components/channels-v2/mentions-list.tsx` still wears it in the Tags inbox, which has its own row idiom.
- ⚠ **DM AUTO-ADDRESS IS RETIRED (2026-08-18).** It was fail-QUIET: a post into an `is_direct` channel with no caller `to` was stamped with the other member, resolved live off `channel_members`, and an ambiguous roster silently produced an unaddressed post rather than a 400. **`metadata.to_user_id` now comes from the validated `toUserId` and from nowhere else** (`channels/server/service-writes-metadata.ts › resolvePostMetadata`). A DM is a channel with two members; addressing works there exactly as it does in a room of ten.
  - **`resolveDirectPeer` SURVIVES with ONE reader: thread inheritance.** A plain `message` in a DM that explicitly names the peer may still join the pair's single open thread. `intent === "chat"` skips peer resolution entirely, which is what keeps a chat post out of an open thread; it no longer has an auto-address to suppress, because there is none.
  - **`intent` is now a DECLARATION, not a switch.** `chat` says on the wire that the post is work for nobody — the receiving side reads it — and `chat` + explicit `to` is still a 400 (`CHANNEL_CHAT_ADDRESSED`).
- **DM membership and visibility are IMMUTABLE** (`DirectChannelImmutableError` → 400) — that protects the `direct_key` identity and live peer resolution.
- **DELETES ARE PERMANENT — with one branch.** No trash, no restore, no retention window.
  - **A DM SOFT-deletes** — `deleted_at` on a direct channel is the CLOSE half of close/reopen; either side's next open revives the SAME row with full history. **A tombstoned DM is LIVE PRODUCT STATE. Never hard-delete one; never add `channels` to a tombstone-cleanup migration.**
    - ⚠ **ACCEPTED TRADE, ALREADY FILED — do not re-file it (F-189).** Since `20260810100000_channels_rls_hide_tombstoned.sql` added `AND deleted_at IS NULL` to `channels_member_select`, **closing a DM is no longer a LIVE event.** The close is an UPDATE, `realtime.apply_rls` evaluates the NEW record against that policy, the row is now invisible, and **the frame is dropped**. The peer's sidebar keeps rendering the closed DM until their next refetch (`listChannels` filters `deleted_at IS NULL`, so the next read is correct). **A downgrade from live to next-refetch, not stale-forever. Shipped knowingly; remedy sketched but NOT taken in F-189** — and the remedy's ORDER is the design (touch the peer's `channel_members` row BEFORE stamping the tombstone; after it, the same policy drops that frame too).
  - **Every OTHER channel HARD-deletes** (owner/workspace-admin only) as ONE statement — every child FK into `channels(id)` is `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so it is already atomic. **The COUNT is a measurement and moves** (EIGHT on 2026-08-22, `channel_launch_directives` being the newest; seven on 2026-08-18, `channel_mention_reads` before it); `channels/schema-sql.test.ts` counts them and asserts each one cascades. **A new child that does not cascade is what that test exists to catch — re-blessing the number is not the fix.** **Do not add an RPC to "follow the pattern"**: the pattern is atomicity, and one statement has it.
  - **Three writers of `channels.deleted_at`:** the DM delete branch, `reviveChannel` clearing it, and `removeWorkspaceDepartedMember`. The third is server-to-server, takes no `ChannelContext` on purpose, and **must never be wired to a route or MCP op** — exposed it is an unauthenticated "evict this user from every room" primitive. **Order is crash-safety: close FIRST, delete the membership row SECOND.**
  - **`knowledge_entries.folder_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` — the trap.** Deleting a folder row alone ORPHANS its entries to the base root where they REAPPEAR. Delete the subtree's entries first.
  - **`ontology_memberships.parent_object_id`'s cascade is a LIE about the tree** — the column is on **`ontology_memberships`, NOT on `ontology_objects`** (`20260706120000_ontology.sql`, `REFERENCES ontology_objects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`), so deleting an object cascades away the MEMBERSHIP rows that name it as parent and leaves every child OBJECT behind, parentless. `cascade_hard_delete_object` is the ONLY correct way to delete an object. **The wrong table name is the whole trap here** — looking for the cascade on `ontology_objects` finds nothing and reads as "there is no cascade".
  - **A THREAD hard-deletes too, and its cascade is HAND-WRITTEN rather than FK-driven** — the rule is stated once, below the thread-closing bullet ("A THREAD CANNOT BE CLOSED. IT CAN BE DELETED"). ⚠ Do not read the one-statement argument above as applying to it: `channel_messages` has no FK into `channel_tasks` at all.
  - **Deleting is a cache eviction, not just a write.** The query cache is IndexedDB-persisted (24h `gcTime`) and read hooks prefer `data` over `error`, so an INVALIDATED entry for a deleted row still RENDERS across relaunches. `removeQueries` the subtree; invalidating the parent list is not enough. ⚠ **It bites where the deleted row HAS a subtree of its own.** A THREAD has no per-thread query key — the transcript and the thread list are per-CHANNEL entries that outlive it — so its optimistic patches ARE its eviction and there is nothing to `removeQueries`.
  - 🔒 **A NEW FIELD ON A CACHED PAYLOAD IS OPTIONAL AT EVERY READ, whatever the server now sends (Samuel, 2026-08-25).** The same 24h IndexedDB persistence means the first paint after any upgrade renders rows minted BEFORE the field existed — the API always sending it does not make it present in the cache, and the type saying it is present is the lie that crashes the screen (`Cannot read properties of undefined`, shipped live twice on 2026-08-25: `Channel.infoCard`, and the class before it). So: every reader of a field added to an already-persisted payload type takes an explicit absent-fallback (`?? EMPTY_X`, matching what `{}` parses to server-side), and the change's tests include a STALE-CACHE case — the fixture WITHOUT the new field — or the tests are testing a cache state no user boots into. Measurement: `grep -n "CACHE-SHAPE FALLBACK" apps/desktop-ui/src` for the idiom.
  - **`deleted_at` columns and their read-path `IS NULL` filters STAY.** A NEW read of such a table still carries the guard.
- ⚠ **SESSION CARDS, SESSION PILLS AND THE THREAD POPOVER ARE REMOVED — 2026-08-18 (wiring plan Phase 5). AN AGENT'S RUN STATE IS NOT TRANSCRIPT CONTENT.** The operator watches their own agents in the v2 right panel's **Agents tab**, over LOCAL RUNTIME STATE (`spa-bridge.ts › DesktopSessionSummary`, projected by `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-summary.js`) — never a server table, because context occupancy and token spend are not stored anywhere. Pause, end and DELETE live on the AGENT (§11) — never on the thread, and never on what it said.
  - ⚠ **A CARD CAN BE DELETED, AND WHAT THAT DELETES IS THE CARD (2026-08-25, Samuel's ruling).** Every OWN agent card — running, idle and retained-ended alike — carries a trash icon revealed by the card's own hover, left of its Open button (`components/channels-v2/agent-delete.tsx`, the naked-glyph row idiom of docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md; feature-detected on `sessions.delete`, so a plain browser and an older main show none). **ONE confirmation, and a LIVE agent adds a clause to it rather than a second question** — main ends it inside the same call. **PEER cards have none and must not:** the op reaches only this machine's local stores, so a trash icon on a colleague's agent would be a control over nothing. ⚠ **⚠ IT DELETES A LOCAL VIEW, NEVER A CONVERSATION.** The agent's messages, their side and their attribution are `channel_messages` — the shared record — and are untouched; the id rides the message, so its rows still read `Agent #<id>` afterwards. The teardown is §11.
  - ⚠ **A PEER CARD'S LIVENESS IS THE ROW'S PRESENCE, NEVER ITS AGE (2026-08-22, Samuel — this SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-20 freshness guard, which is DELETED; do not reinstate it).** *"The card STAYS until the session actually goes away."* `components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › peerCardsFor` now filters on THREE things and nothing else — not mine (the LOCAL feed is the richer truth for my own), not `ended` (`› isAgentActive`, the one rule both lists share), and the tab's own thread narrowing — and a row's membership in the fetch IS the liveness signal: the desktop's push REPLACES its whole set, so an ended session leaves by OMISSION, and `main/session-state-push.js › liveForWire` keeps ended rows off the wire besides (§11).
    - ⚠ **`channel_sessions.updated_at` IS NOT A HEARTBEAT AND NOTHING MAY TREAT IT AS ONE.** The push fires on a projection CHANGE only (`session-state-push.js` forbids a timer in its own header), and `channels/server/repository-sessions.ts › sessionRowMatches` compares field by field and deliberately does NOT re-stamp a row whose projection did not move — so an IDLE peer agent, alive and listening, crossed `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS` and its card VANISHED mid-run. **That is the defect the guard caused, and it is worse than the one it fixed:** a card that disappears is unattributable, and the reader cannot tell it from an agent that never existed.
    - **WHAT THE GUARD BOUGHT IS KEPT AS INK.** Rows do outlive a crashed or signed-out desktop (`session-state-push.js`'s own KNOWN GAP), so `agents-model.ts › peerRowStale` still answers "this row has not moved in a while" over the SAME `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS` — a second staleness number would let the roster call a member offline while their card read at full strength — and `agents-tab.tsx` DIMS such a card (`opacity-60`, `data-stale`) and adds ONE clause naming when it last moved. **No "may be offline" copy**: the surface does not know that, and guessing is what the guard was.
    - ⚠ **THE BADGE AND THE LIST STILL RUN ONE DERIVATION**, so a quiet peer row is counted exactly as it is drawn (`› activeAgentCount` over the same `peerCardsFor`). ⚠ **`components/channels-v2/peer-activity.tsx › peerWorkingOn` KEEPS ITS WINDOW and the two now diverge deliberately** — that row makes a PRESENT-TENSE claim a reader waits on ("Diana's agent is working…") and must fail toward silence; a card answers "does this agent exist", which age cannot settle. **They must not be merged back.**
  - **What went, in one change:** `components/session-card.tsx`, `› session-card-status.tsx`, `› session-pills-bar.tsx`, `› thread-panel.tsx`, `› threads-button.tsx`, `› thread-party.tsx` and the whole `lib/group-thread*` family (grouping, draft, markers, render, types) with their tests. **Two reads survived it and were rehomed rather than deleted**: `lib/calm-terminal.ts › calmTerminalStatus` (still written by `main/trigger-outcomes.js` and by every installed desktop) and `lib/legacy-task-id.ts › parseLegacyTaskSeq` (server-side validation of a legacy thread tag, not a render helper). ⚠ `calmTerminalStatus` has **ONE direct consumer**, `lib/message-receipt.ts`, which since the wave-2 fix pass (2026-08-18) has TWO derivations over it — `deriveMessageReceipt` and `lifecycleReceiptStatus` — and is itself read by `components/channels-v2/view-model-rows.ts`. The muted activity dot was `components/activity-event-row.tsx`, deleted with the two-pane page.
  - ⚠ **THE SESSION WINDOW STOPPED POSTING `task_started` / `task_finished` / `task_failed`. THE TRIGGER LANES DID NOT, AND THE SERVER STILL ACCEPTS ALL THREE.** the session window's own echoes are gone — **that is the whole of what Phase 5 removed**, and an earlier draft of this bullet said "the desktop stopped posting" them, which was false about the build in people's hands. **What THIS desktop still posts, enumerated:**
    - `main/trigger-outcomes.js` — `task_failed` + `{declined:true}` ("Request declined"), + `{dropped:true}` ("Reply was not sent."), + `{interrupted:true}` ("Request interrupted");
    - `main/trigger.js` — `task_failed` on an approved reply that did not land, and `task_finished` on one that did;
    - `main/trigger-headless.js` — the FULL set: `task_started` from the spawn's `onStart`, `task_finished` on a clean no-reply finish, `task_failed` on an errored run or a review row that could not be created.
    - **tightening `service-writes-lifecycle.ts` is NOT part of this and cannot be until the desktop floor rises (§13)** — and every INSTALLED build posts all three from the session window as well.
  - **THE READER RENDERS THE TERMINAL KINDS AS A RECEIPT, AND DROPS `task_started`.** `components/channels-v2/view-model-rows.ts › toReceiptRow` (over `lib/message-receipt.ts › lifecycleReceiptStatus`) turns a `task_failed`/`task_finished` carrying a calm flag OR a non-empty body into one slim muted line — **because a calm ending changes how the peer reads the exchange, which is the same argument that put the flag in the metadata in the first place.** ⚠ **It dropped all three until the wave-2 fix pass, so a consent DENY rendered as nothing at all** and the requester saw an unanswered ask. **`task_started` is still dropped unconditionally on every surface** — run state lives in the Agents tab, not in a transcript. A receipt's LABEL comes from the flag, never from the body; only a flagless `task_failed` may wear alarm ink.
  - **The other lifecycle post this desktop makes is the calm `task_progress` `session_ended` note** (`main/trigger-outcomes.js › lifecycleHandlers`, moved there 2026-08-20 when the window factory that used to carry it was deleted — F-228; a WINDOWLESS session raises it exactly as a windowed one did), which is the milestone lane and is what tells a waiting peer that the other side stopped (§11's quit guard depends on it). `task_progress` is NOT a receipt kind — its body is prose and renders as a message.
  - **`CALM_FLAG_KEYS` is untouched and still SIX.** The reserved strip is a write-side property and does not care that a client reader left (see the asymmetry rule above): the note's BODY is still rendered, so a caller able to set `session_ended` could narrate somebody else's session as ended.
- ⚠ **THREAD CLOSING IS REMOVED — 2026-08-18 (wiring plan Phase 4). A THREAD HAS NO FINISHED STATE.** No close, no propose-then-confirm, no reopen, on any surface: **the operator pauses or ends an AGENT, not a thread.** What went, in one change: `service-tasks-lifecycle.ts` (`closeTask`/`reopenTask` + both echo keys), `service-tasks-propose.ts`, the `close`/`propose_close`/`reopen` arms of `schema.ts › TaskUpdateSchema` and of `/tasks/[taskId] › handlePatch`, `ThreadCloseIsHumanOnlyError` (403 `CHANNEL_CLOSE_IS_HUMAN_ONLY`), `repository-tasks.ts › updateTaskIfStatus`, the stale-threads cron (§13), the MCP `propose_close` / `close_thread` ops **and their names in the enum**, and every client control (`threadOpConfig`, `session-card-close.tsx`, the thread panel's row actions, `readCloseProposal`, `isThreadReopenedMarker`).
  - **`channel_tasks.status` and its CHECK CONSTRAINT STAY, LEGACY AND UNBRANCHED-ON.** Rows closed before the removal still carry `closed`; dropping the column is a migration behind a desktop-floor raise (§13). **Nothing writes it and NOTHING BRANCHES ON IT.** ⚠ **It is still READ, and this bullet claimed otherwise until 2026-08-18:** `channels/server/dto.ts` carries `status` in `CHANNEL_TASK_ACTIVITY_COLS` and `mapTaskRow` maps it onto `ChannelThread.status`, so it is selected, mapped and shipped to every client — nothing DECIDES anything on it. What died at wiring plan Phase 5 was the last thing that branched: the thread popover's status label, deleted with `components/thread-panel.tsx`. ⚠ **A new `status === "open"` filter is a bug, not a tightening** — three of them were deleted here (`resolveInheritableTask`, the MCP not-threaded note, `isThreadClosed`) and each one's only remaining effect was to hide a live, postable exchange from its own party.
  - **The `threadClosed` post notice is gone too**, along with `service-writes-metadata-thread.ts › isThreadClosed` and the MCP `closedThreadNote`. Its shape is worth keeping where a post-time notice ever returns: **the post LANDED, so warn, never refuse**, and the notice rides the response ENVELOPE rather than the message, because a READ of the same row could never carry it.
  - ⚠ **THE AGENT-FACING HALF IS THE SHARP ONE, and it is enforced:** a thread's state is exactly what an agent polls and waits on, so ONE surviving sentence sends it looking for a transition that can never arrive. `list_threads` / `get_thread` no longer render status, outcome, `closedAt` or the outcome summary; every re-arm stop rule now says the ABSENCE out loud rather than dropping the clause. **`packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-law.test.ts › REMOVED_VOCABULARY` carries six new entries** (§10) and has no allowlist.
- ⚠ **A THREAD CANNOT BE CLOSED. IT CAN BE DELETED — 2026-08-21 (Samuel). HARD, CASCADING, PERMANENT, AND NOT A FINISHED STATE.** The bullet above is untouched: every thread that EXISTS is live, there is still no `close`, no outcome, no reopen and no `status` write anywhere. What is new is that a thread can stop existing — the mistaken thread, the duplicate, the one opened against the wrong person. **`DELETE /api/channels/[channelId]/tasks/[taskId]` → `channels/server/service-tasks-delete.ts › deleteTask`.** ⚠ **Do not resurrect closing by pointing at this**, and do not let the delete grow a status write; `service-tasks-delete.test.ts › writes no thread STATUS on the way out` is the tripwire.
  - **WHO: the thread's CREATOR, or `service-shared.ts › canManageChannel` (channel owner / workspace admin).** ⚠ **THE ADDRESSEE IS DELIBERATELY REFUSED, and the asymmetry is the rule, not an oversight:** both parties may POST in a thread (`isThreadParticipant`, above) because writing is what a shared exchange is for; DESTROYING the shared record is not symmetric with contributing to it. A public channel's non-member reader — who CAN read a pair's thread — is refused before any thread row loads. Pinned by `service-tasks-delete.test.ts`.
  - ⚠ **OFF THE MCP SURFACE, AND THE ROUTE IS `sessionOnly` (§3).** No `dopl_channel` op reaches it and none may be added — "no destructive ops over MCP", and an agent token able to delete a thread could erase the exchange it was losing. `shared/auth/write-gate-coverage.test.ts` pins the file into the `sessionOnly` set. ⚠ Per-METHOD: the GET and the set-mode PATCH on that route are untouched.
  - ⚠ **THE CASCADE IS HAND-WRITTEN, NOT ONE STATEMENT, because `channel_messages` has NO FK into `channel_tasks`** — the transcript rides on `metadata.taskId` (below), so deleting the thread row cascades nothing. **CHILDREN FIRST, THE `channel_tasks` ROW LAST**, and the order is chosen for what a mid-cascade FAILURE leaves: every prefix is a thread with LESS hanging off it, which re-clicking Delete finishes. **The reverse order is silently destructive** — `channel_sessions.task_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so the row leaving first strands live-looking session rows on no thread and orphans every tagged message on an id that resolves to nothing.
    1. `channel_messages` tagged `metadata->>'taskId'` (indexed by `channel_messages_thread_activity_idx`), returning the seqs it removed. **`channel_mention_reads` rides along inside it** — its `message_id` FK is already `ON DELETE CASCADE` (`20260818140000`), **verified rather than assumed, which is why NO migration was needed.**
    2. `channel_consent_requests` still `pending` against one of those seqs → **EXPIRED, never deleted.** A decided row is the AUDIT of a human decision and a thread deletion is nobody's licence to erase it; a pending row whose trigger message is gone can never be answered honestly. `message_seq` has no FK but IS a clean key — `seq` is a table-wide identity (below), so it is globally unique, and the seqs come from the delete that actually happened rather than a guessed range.
    3. `channel_sessions` targeting the thread, **EVERY member's** — they are projections of a live desktop registry, not owned state, and the statement the row makes is gone whoever wrote it.
    4. `channel_task_participants` — dead code since breakout rooms went, but pre-existing rows are still there. Its FK already cascades; the statement is explicit anyway so the children-first order is not a half-truth.
    5. `channel_tasks`.
  - ⚠ **THE PEER'S RUNNING AGENT IS NOT REACHABLE AND THAT IS ACCEPTED (Samuel).** The server removes the ROW that says a peer's agent is on the thread; the agent itself lives in another operator's main process and stops on its own idle/abandon timer. **The OPERATOR'S OWN agents ARE ended first, client-side** (`components/channels-v2/thread-manage.tsx` → `agents-controls.ts › useAgentControls`), because that is the only side that can — **best-effort, feature-detected, and it must never BLOCK the delete.** ⚠ **Order: STOP, THEN DELETE.** Reversed, the transcript goes out from under a session still posting into it.
  - ⚠ **THE DOORBELL IS `channel_messages`, AND IT WAS NOT FREE — CORRECTED 2026-08-22.** `channel_tasks` stays out of the publication (§7) and so does `channel_sessions`, and the consent step UPDATEs rather than deletes, so step 1's DELETEs on `channel_messages` are the ONLY event this cascade can ring with. **This bullet claimed that already worked — "carries `workspace_id` under the replica identity `20260807150000` set" — and it was FALSE:** that migration named `channel_messages` on its EXEMPTION list ("cascade from `channels` / `workspaces` only", true before a thread could be deleted), so the table sat at `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT` and **every one of those DELETE frames was dropped server-side by both subscribers' `workspace_id` filter** — the counterparty kept rendering a thread that no longer existed until a manual refetch, then 404'd on click, with no error anywhere. **`20260822130000_channel_messages_delete_doorbell.sql` gives the table `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX (workspace_id, id)`**; the cost argument is in that file's header and is unusually one-sided (**the table has NO UPDATE path at all** — replica identity is paid on UPDATE and DELETE, never on INSERT). `ui-sync-replica-identity.test.mjs › DELETE_DOORBELL_TABLES` carries the table now instead of `NO_DELETE_DOORBELL`.
  - **THE SURFACE IS THE THREAD-SCOPED SETTINGS TAB** (§11) — a destructive row behind `ConfirmDialog`, wording parallel to the channel delete's. The other control on that tab is `set_mode`, which is **CREATOR-only because the service is**; a manager who is not the creator sees Delete alone, and a reader entitled to neither gets the empty state (the no-dead-rows rule above).
- **AN AGENT WRITES PROSE AND MILESTONES. NOTHING ELSE.** `task_started`/`task_finished`/`task_failed` state a fact about a RUNTIME and are refused from an agent-token caller at two layers (the MCP tool, fast and teaching; `service-writes-lifecycle.ts`, authoritative). **The seam is the CREDENTIAL — do not "simplify" it to a kind check**: the desktop's own echoes arrive on Supabase cookies and must keep working. `task_progress` stays agent-writable.
- **THE MILESTONE IS AN OP, NOT A KIND** — `op="milestone"` fixes `kind` at the routing seam, so "mark a milestone" and "send a reply" cannot be confused by picking wrongly between adjacent enum values.
- **Status is decided by FLAG, never by string.** A calm ending reads as **strict `=== true`** on a reserved key. Matching body copy regresses the first time somebody improves the wording; a truthy coercion paints real failures calm.
- **`channel_tasks` stays OUT of the publication, and nothing changes it any more.** It never had a subscriber; the close and reopen ECHOES existed to ring the `channel_messages` doorbell for a row change peers could not see, and both went with thread closing. `set_thread_mode` is now the ONLY write to the table after creation, and it is eventually consistent by design. **Do not add the table to the publication for it.**
- **A WRITE THAT ALSO POSTS A MESSAGE HANDS ITS SEQ BACK.** `openingSeq` is the surviving instance (`echoSeq` and `markerSeq` went with close/propose). ⚠ **`null` means look it up, NEVER "one past the last seq you saw"** — a guessed cursor once armed an `await` past a peer's whole deliverable, which was sitting below the guess.
- **THREAD ACTIVITY IS READ OFF `channel_messages`, NEVER `channel_tasks.updated_at`** — that column's only writer is `repository-tasks.ts › updateTask`, reached from `set_mode` alone since close and reopen were removed, so it equals `created_at` for nearly every thread and `set_thread_mode` resets it with zero activity. That was C-1. **A general rule, not the stale sweep's private one (generalized 2026-08-18):**
  - **ONE DEFINITION of activity, AND NOW ONLY ONE STATEMENT OF IT:** the newest message tagged `metadata.taskId` for the thread, falling back to the thread's own `created_at` — the `channel_tasks_activity` view (`20260818120000`). ✅ **F-202 IS RESOLVED BY THE PHASE-4 DELETION (2026-08-18).** The second statement was `channel_tasks_stale` (`20260807160000`), the cron's RPC, carrying a verbatim copy of the same lateral plus a close-proposal exclusion; **the cron is deleted and the RPC has no caller**, so there is no longer a clock that can drift against this one. ⚠ **The RPC itself is still in the database** — dropping it (and it is the last object referencing the close-proposal marker keys) is a migration, filed as **F-207**, and until it runs `supabase db reset` still replays it.
  - **THE THREAD LIST IS ORDERED BY IT.** `repository-tasks.ts › listTasksByChannel` is the ONE read behind both the web's thread overlay and MCP `list_threads`, so the two surfaces cannot disagree about which exchange is live. It is bounded and reports `truncated` (§9). ⚠ **Nothing downstream re-sorts it** — the LIMIT clipped against that order, so a re-sorted page is the wrong rows in a plausible order.
  - **`ChannelThread.lastActivityAt` is ABSENT on a single-thread read, and absent means "this view did not derive it"** — never "no activity", never "same as created". Only the list carries it.
  - **The sidebar's window is CLIENT-SIDE arithmetic over that field** (`constants.ts › SIDEBAR_THREAD_ACTIVE_WINDOW_MS`, Samuel 2026-08-18; the Threads tab lists everything, paged, by activity). Same shape as presence's 90 s window, and the same fail-safe direction: stale data reads INACTIVE. Implemented in `components/channels-v2/view-model-requested.ts › sidebarThreads`.
    - ⚠ **THE "OR REQUESTED" ARM IS DELETED, AND SO IS EVERY SURFACE IT FED (2026-08-22, Samuel — the inbound consent retirement, §6).** `sidebarThreads` takes `(threads, now)` and the 24h window is the ONLY rule left. Gone with it, in one wave: `view-model-requested.ts › requestedThreadIds` / `› pendingRequestIdByThread` / `› pendingAsksByChannel` / `› consentExemptThreadIds`; the sidebar's `Clock`-vs-`Bot` thread glyph and its "— awaiting your approval" accessible name (`sidebar-rows.tsx › ThreadRow` now always renders `Bot`); the per-channel ASK BADGE (`› AskBadge`, and `ChannelRow`'s `askCount`); `bits.tsx › PendingChip`; the transcript card's inline **Decline / Launch agent** pair; `thread-consent.tsx › ThreadAwaitingStrip`; and the Inbox pane's inbound branch (that whole pane is DELETED as of 2026-08-25, §6). **There is no `requested` state anywhere in this tree, derived or otherwise — do not re-derive one.**
    - **THE TRANSCRIPT'S AGENT-THREAD CARD NOW OFFERS TWO ACTIONS, NEITHER OF WHICH IS A DECISION.** **Open thread** (the existing selection nav) and **Launch agent** — the DIRECT launch (`use-agents-panel.ts › launchAgent` over `agents-controls.ts › launchAgentOnThread`), which raises no consent row and answers no request. ⚠ **There is no Decline on any surface.** ⚠ The launch names `ThreadCardRow.openThreadId`, the SAME thread "Open thread" opens — a fan-out card names N threads and `view-model-rows.ts › ownThreadOf` already picked the one this viewer is party to; any other one starts an agent in an exchange the operator cannot write in. ⚠ **Absent, not disabled, without the bridge op** (`canLaunch`), the feature-detection rule the whole bridge family follows. Pinned by `thread-card-actions.test.tsx`.
