    - ⚠ **THE INBOX PANE AND BOTH CONSENT BADGES ARE DELETED (2026-08-25, Samuel — §6).** This bullet said they were "outbound-only": the pane rendered one lane with one verb pair (Cancel / Send), and the channels sidebar's Inbox row badge and the app nav's channels badge both counted `use-consent-inbox.ts › outbound`. **All three are gone**, along with `INBOX_NAV_ROW`, `sidebar.tsx`'s `consentCount` / `inboxOpen` / `onOpenInbox`, `app-sidebar-core.tsx`'s `consentCount`, and the shell's `useConsentInbox` call. The outbound review is `channels-v2/agent-stream.tsx › SentToChannelBox` — inline, in the work stream, reachable on a solo `/home` channel that never had this nav. **A badge is still a claim that something is ACTIONABLE**, which is exactly why none survives: there is no destination left for one to point at.
    - **THE PANE IS STILL THE LAST-RESORT DECISION SURFACE, for the surviving lane.** An outbound row whose `message_seq` is absent — or whose triggering message is below the transcript read's ceiling — cannot be placed on a thread by `view-model-requested.ts › pendingOutboundByThread`, so the thread view's send box never renders it. **A draft nobody can send is an agent that never finishes.**
    - ⚠ **THE NESTED THREAD ROWS COLLAPSE (Samuel, 2026-08-20).** A disclosure on the channel row, DEFAULT EXPANDED and SESSION-LOCAL per channel (`components/channels-v2/sidebar.tsx › threadsCollapsed`, the same absent-means-open `Set` idiom the section headers use). The 24h window above is untouched and is the RIGHT rule — crowding is answered by a disclosure rather than by a narrower window. ⚠ The toggle is a SIBLING of the row, never a child: `ChannelRow` is a `<button>` and nesting a second one is invalid HTML and one target for two actions.
    - ⚠ **THE ADDRESSEE PILLS STILL CARRY NO APPROVAL STATE, and the reason outlived the lane that made it visible.** A consent read is scoped to `(operator, workspace)` with the operator always `ctx.userId` (§6), so a REQUESTER can never see their addressees' state — **"no pending row" would report never-asked as approved.** `bits.tsx › AddresseePill` therefore states the party and nothing else. The missing requester-side projection is REFACTOR-FINDINGS **F-206**; it is no longer reachable from a `requested` chip, because there is none.
  - **Do NOT make the write path pay for this** (§12). No trigger on `channel_messages`, no touch inside `postMessage` — the derivation is read-side for exactly that reason.

- ⚠ **THE INFO TAB'S CURATED CARD IS CURATED, AND THE CURATION IS STORED (Samuel, 2026-08-25).** Every row on it carries a hover-only ×; a discreet ghost row at the end of the list adds a custom `label: value` pair, edited in place on `agent-rename.tsx`'s underline idiom (Enter/blur save, Escape cancels). Live on **/home only** so far (`apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/person-info-tab.tsx`); the workspace channels page's `channels-v2/info-tab.tsx` still renders its fixed list.
  - **ITS HEADING READS "Channel info" ON /home SINCE 2026-08-28 (Samuel, live review)** — it was "Main info", which named a position on the tab rather than a subject. ⚠ **A LABEL ONLY**: `info-card.ts › ChannelInfoCardBuiltInKey` and the `channels.info_card` column are untouched, and `channels-v2/info-tab.tsx` — a different tab — still says "Main info", so the two headings are deliberately not the same string.
  - 🔒 **REMOVING A ROW REMOVES IT FROM THE CARD, NEVER THE FACT.** `hidden` names a BUILT-IN ROW the operator stopped showing — the peer's email is still on their profile, still on the roster, still in the card's own header subline. Nothing is nulled anywhere. A future reader who "finishes the job" turns a display preference into data loss.
  - **STORAGE is a COLUMN, `channels.info_card jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`** (`20260825120000_channel_info_card.sql`, §12), holding `{hidden: [<built-in key>], rows: [{id,label,value}]}`. ⚠ **SHARED, NOT CALLER-RELATIVE** — unlike `myFavoritedAt` above, both sides of a home channel see one card. If it ever needs to be per-person it becomes a `channel_members` column and a `my*` name; do not quietly reinterpret this one.
    - **The SHAPE is stated ONCE, in `src/features/channels/info-card.ts`** — the zod schema, the caps, and the four pure editors every surface builds a next card with. ⚠ **NOT under `server/`**: the renderer's ESLint fence blocks that path, and the SPA needs the same answer as the route (same reasoning as `features/home/schema.ts`).
    - ⚠ **THE DB CHECK IS A FLOOR, AND A SERVER-SIDE BYTE GUARD TIES THE APP TO IT** (corrected 2026-08-25). `channels_info_card_check` asserts a JSON OBJECT whose `octet_length(info_card::text)` is under **4 KiB**. ⚠ **THE PER-FIELD CAPS DO NOT BOUND THE TOTAL**: 12 rows × (64-char id + 40-char label + 200-char value) is ~4.1 KB ASCII / ~9.6 KB CJK once `::text` adds its `": "` / `", "` separators — BOTH over the floor, so a schema-valid card could 500 at the constraint (a PostgREST failure is not an `Error`, so it fell to a generic 500). **`updateChannel` measures the SAME jsonb text form** (`info-card.ts › infoCardTextBytes`, reproducing pg's separators) **and raises a real 413 `INFO_CARD_TOO_LARGE` before the DB sees the row**, capped at `INFO_CARD_MAX_BYTES` (3500, held under the 4096 floor with margin). `info-card.test.ts` measures the worst CJK card the jsonb-text way and proves the guard's ceiling sits under the floor; `service-info-card.test.ts` proves an over-cap card is refused before the write. **Widen `INFO_CARD_MAX_BYTES` and those tests go red — no TypeScript reaches SQL, so the byte guard is the tie.**
    - ⚠ **THE BOUND IS WHY THE COLUMN IS SAFE ON `channels` AT ALL.** That table is read by `repository.ts › listChannels` with `select("*")` (§9's known non-conformer) and UPDATEd on every message post by `› touchChannel` — so an unbounded JSONB here would make a list read size-of-workspace and widen the WAL image of the hottest write in the schema.
  - **NO NEW ENDPOINT.** The write is `PATCH /api/channels/[channelId]` with `infoCard`, the same route that writes `name`/`topic`/`visibility`/`archived`, reconciled off its own answer (`hooks/use-channel-info-card-writes.ts`). The READ is free: the card rides out on `Channel.infoCard` from the list every surface already loads.

The four HEADER fields still require `canManageChannel`; `infoCard` requires MEMBERSHIP only. **A MIXED PATCH TAKES THE MANAGE GATE**, and the loose set is derived by SUBTRACTION from `› MANAGED_CHANNEL_FIELDS`, so a SIXTH field added to `ChannelUpdateSchema` (the five are `name`/`topic`/`visibility`/`archived`/`infoCard`) lands in the managed half without anybody remembering to decide. The person card became write-bearing, and §7/§8 allow ONE gate per live surface: a slot handed a finished node could only mint a second that coordinates with nothing, so the doorbell's refetch would land mid-write and repaint the row just deleted. Same reason `settings-slot.tsx` is passed `gate` beside it.

Add person is the act that works, and it lives on the Info tab. `selfManagement` is about the VIEWER'S OWN STAKE: `false` hides the **Leave channel** row AND the whole `ChannelAgentSettings` block, and only the guest lane passes it (`src/app/c/[workspaceId]/guest-channel.tsx › GuestChannel`; Samuel, rulings R2/R3) — a guest runs no agent, so a tool profile governs a session that does not exist, and leaving is a one-way exit from the only surface they have, since the link that brought them was revoked at claim. ⚠ **ONE FLAG, TWO CONTROLS, ON PURPOSE:** two would let a future host turn one half off and ship the other half's dead control with nothing saying so. `knowledge` is about WHAT THE CHANNEL CARRIES: `true` draws the **Knowledge** tab (`channels-v2/knowledge-tab.tsx` — the knowledge bases granted INTO this channel, read-only unless the grant carries `guest_write`), and **since 2026-08-27 exactly ONE host passes it — the GUEST lane** (`app/c/[workspaceId]/guest-channel.tsx`). ⚠ **`pages/home/relationship-record.tsx` PASSED IT TOO UNTIL THEN, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS NOW THE POINT (Samuel's F-340 ruling).** Five tabs did not fit the 380px info column — the budget is a MEASUREMENT (docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md: the trackless `lg` form's 12px side pad exists because FOUR options with two count badges overflowed at 15px), and "Knowledge" wants ~90px of the ~55px spare. **The DUPLICATE VIEW gave way, not the capability:** the operator already has a full Knowledge FACE one click away on /home's own header (`pages/home/knowledge-panels.tsx`), while `ChannelKnowledgeTab` has ONE consumer and `/api/channels/[channelId]/knowledge/**` has no other client caller — so the tab is the GUEST's only way to read a base granted into the channel, and taking it from them would have removed the capability rather than a second view of it. ⚠ **PINNED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** by `channels-v2/knowledge-tab.test.tsx › the capability, per host`, which reads each host's source (comments stripped, or the /home mount's own explanatory docblock would keep it green): the guest lane HAS the flag, the /home pane does NOT. **Re-adding it to the desktop host re-opens F-340.** ⚠ **THE FIVE-TAB MACHINERY IS NOT DELETED** — `tabsFor`'s `knowledge` arm, the `gap-1` tightening and the `overflow-x-auto` residual all still fire on the guest lane, whose column has the same budget. ⚠ **A CAPABILITY MAY HIDE A ROW, AND SINCE 2026-08-26 MAY ADD A TAB — IT MAY STILL NEVER REMOVE ONE** (Home Knowledge Panels §5.5; the tab row is not a slot, see the `infoTab` docblock). That asymmetry is the whole rule: a host that could delete a tab could ship a surface missing one with nothing saying so, whereas one that adds a tab ships something visibly present. It is also why this flag defaults CLOSED where the other two default open, and why the workspace channel page does not pass it — that page already has the full knowledge surface, and the tab reads a per-channel grant lane. ⚠ **THE TAB'S READS MOUNT WITH THE TAB** (`channels-v2/use-channel-knowledge.ts`), so `false` requests nothing at all, and every one of them is on the guest-floored lane of §4A — pinned by `channels-v2/guest-surface-reads.test.tsx`, which resolves each URL down to its `route.ts` and reads the floor out of it.
    - ⚠ **AND THE TAB IT ADDS IS THE FIFTH, ON A WIDTH BUDGET MEASURED FOR FOUR (F-340, open — Samuel's ruling).** `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` records why `SegmentedControl`'s trackless `lg` form takes a 12px side pad: the info column's FOUR options with two count badges overflowed 15px at the 340px that column then was. It is 380px now, so the four leave ~55px and **"Knowledge" wants ~90.** `channels-v2/info-panel.tsx` does two things about it and neither touches the shared primitive — the row TIGHTENS (`gap-1`, header `px-3`→`px-2`) **only while a fifth tab is present**, and the residual **SCROLLS rather than clipping**. 🔴 **DO NOT RESOLVE IT BY SHORTENING THE LABEL:** "Knowledge" is the product's noun on every other face, and a tab renamed to fit makes the noun disagree with itself. F-340 states the four options and their blast radius; ship the scroll until it is ruled.
  - **THE HOME INFO TAB'S ORDER IS HEADER → MAIN INFO → THREAD ACTIVITY → MEMBERS (+ ADD PERSON)** (Samuel, 2026-08-25 — corrected the same day; the first pass put Members second). Facts, then what has been happening, then who is here — so the tab's one ACTION is the last thing on it. ⚠ **Add person moved UNDER the roster** (it was the tab's foot); the two-state rule is untouched — one section, two states, never both: a pending open link renders the **Link out** panel, anything else renders the button. ⚠ **THE THIRD STATE IS GONE (2026-08-26).** This read "and neither once a peer has arrived (§4A's two-member cap)"; the cap is retired, so **Add person persists at every roster size** and `person-members.tsx` no longer gates on `peer`. What still suppresses it is an invitation already out — one open link per container, not a headcount.
  - 🔒 **THE ROSTER IS `channels-v2/member-roster.tsx › MemberRoster`, AND BOTH SURFACES RENDER IT.** `MemberRow` was module-private in `info-tab.tsx`, so /home grew a home-local approximation — `xs` avatar, name only, no email, `h-9` — which is exactly the drift a shared object exists to prevent (Samuel: *"I don't know why you're making it different"*). The split exported the ROW **and the online/offline partition together**: reusing only the row would have left the two free to disagree about ORDER, which is the half a reader actually reads. **Adapt data wiring at a call site; never the row.**
    - ⚠ **`emptyLine` IS OPT-IN AND /home DOES NOT TAKE IT.** "No members in this channel." is true of a workspace channel and never of a home one — the caller is a member of their own container — so over there the sentence could only ever flash during the roster read's first frame.
    - ⚠ **`channels-v2/info-tab.tsx`'s Members heading carries `Add member` / `Filter members` `IconButton`s WITH NO `onClick`. They are deliberately NOT reproduced on /home** — a container's roster cannot be added to that way at all (§4A) — and copying a dead control onto a second surface doubles it rather than matching it.
      - ⚠ **THIS BULLET SAID "the channels page's" UNTIL 2026-08-30, AND THAT COMPONENT HAS HAD TWO HOSTS SINCE 2026-08-25.** `InfoTab` is `info-panel.tsx`'s DEFAULT — it renders wherever a host supplies no `infoTab` slot — so the **guest web lane** gets it too (`src/app/c/[workspaceId]/guest-channel.tsx` → `channels-v2/channel-surface-standalone.tsx`, which passes no slot). /home is the one host that DOES supply a slot (`pages/home/relationship-record.tsx › infoTab` → `pages/home/person-info-tab.tsx`), which is why it is the exception here rather than the rule. **So the two dead controls are shown to EXTERNAL people, not only to workspace members** — which is the scope §5's interaction-completeness ruling actually cares about. Same correction owed on **F-316**'s Location line, which scopes the fixture strip the same way. Re-derive rather than trusting either sentence: `grep -rn "StandaloneChannelSurface\|infoTab" src apps`.
  - **THREAD ACTIVITY IS THE CHANNELS PAGE'S DENSITY STRIP, WIRED (Samuel, 2026-08-25 — the second correction).** ⚠ The first pass substituted a plain thread LIST, on the grounds that the heatmap is `fixtures.ts › HARDCODED_THREAD_ACTIVITY`. The ruling is to keep the PICTURE and make it true. **The squares and the shade ramp now live in `channels-v2/thread-activity.tsx`** (`ActivityCells` / `ACTIVITY_SHADE`), rendered by both surfaces, so they cannot drift apart while only one is wired.
    - **WHAT FEEDS IT: `GET /api/workspaces/[workspaceSlug]/overview-series?metric=messages&channelId=`** — 31 UTC days, one counted bin each. **No new endpoint**: `channelId` is a narrowing query PARAMETER on the route that already served this shape (§9). It counts MESSAGES, and the strip's `aria-label` says so — `threads` was rejected as a MEASUREMENT, not a taste: it counts threads OPENED per day, which on a two-person relationship is zero almost every day.
    - 🔒 **`channelId` IS A SECOND FENCE, NOT A FILTER.** Those counts run as SERVICE ROLE, so RLS is not a backstop (§2) and workspace membership is not enough — a member of a workspace is not a reader of every PRIVATE channel in it, and an unfenced count reports day by day how busy a room the caller cannot open was. The route checks it through `workspaces/server/service-overview.ts › isChannelVisibleTo` (over `repository-overview.ts › listVisibleChannelRefs`, the channels feature's ONE visibility statement) and answers **404, the same answer an unknown workspace gets**. ⚠ The visibility read runs ONLY when a channel was asked for; the unscoped series must not pay for it.
    - ⚠ **`metric=mcp` + `channelId` IS A 400 (`CHANNEL_SCOPE_UNSUPPORTED`), never a workspace-wide answer under a channel-scoped label.** `mcp_tool_calls` has no `channel_id` column, so the question has no answer in this schema. The service branches per metric rather than dispatching through a shared counter variable, so a third argument cannot be dropped on the floor.
    - ⚠ **QUANTISATION IS RELATIVE TO THE BUSIEST DAY IN THE WINDOW, AND A MEASURED ZERO IS ITS OWN FACE** (`› activityLevels`, pure and pinned). There is no workspace-independent "busy", so an absolute ramp would paint every home channel uniformly pale; and the palest green must not also mean "nothing happened", which is the low-bin-vs-unmeasured-bin confusion the fixture could never avoid. A day with ONE message never rounds down into the empty face. **The strip renders NOTHING while loading or when a stale entry cannot answer** — an empty well is a measured zero, so a full row of them states a month of quiet nobody counted.
    - **THE CHANNELS PAGE IS STILL ON THE FIXTURE, and that is a COST decision, not an oversight:** wiring it means threading the series down through `channel-surface-data.ts` and paying 31 counted bins on **every channel selection** in a workspace. REFACTOR-FINDINGS **F-316**.
  - **Members re-mounts `useChannelMembers` with the SAME arguments `channel-surface-data.ts` uses**, so it shares one TanStack entry and costs no request. Thread activity's series is its own read, keyed by the channel (the id is in the PATH), so a cached strip can never paint under the wrong channel's heading.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §8 (Channels v1.1–v3.1, F-133, F-141…F-147, F-176), and the 2026-08-25 stratum (the info card).

---

## 5A. Agent templates — persistent agent identities

**Written and measured 2026-08-22** against the tree at authoring time — the whole section, not
bullet-by-bullet, so the file-header date above does not apply to it.

> ⚠ **THE SECTION IS LETTERED, NOT NUMBERED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** §6…§14 are cited by number from
> `CLAUDE.md`, from other docs, and from docblocks across three trees; inserting a §6 to renumber nine
> sections would invalidate every one of those citations to buy a digit. **Do not renumber this file to
> "fix" it.**

> A **TEMPLATE** is a named, reusable agent IDENTITY: instructions, a default model, custom fields and
> attached knowledge bases. **A SESSION IS EPHEMERAL; THE TEMPLATE IS THE DURABLE THING** — that
> asymmetry is the whole feature, and it is why templates get a table while sessions get a projection.

- **THREE TABLES, ALL SERVICE-ROLE-WRITE-ONLY** (`supabase/migrations/20260822200000_agent_templates.sql` — ✅ **APPLIED**, name `agent_templates`, history version `20260823091848`; **re-measured 2026-08-26**, and applied is a MEASUREMENT, not a claim: `supabase migration list` (or MCP `list_migrations`) **joined on the NAME**, never on the filename (§12, F-304's re-stamp). ⚠ **This bullet and the two below said "WRITTEN — applied is a measurement" until 2026-08-26** while §12 had already recorded all three as applied on 2026-08-24 — two sections of one file disagreeing about deploy state, which is what F-332's dev mock was still living behind): `agent_templates`, `agent_template_teams`, `agent_template_knowledge_bases`. `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` are REVOKEd from `authenticated`/`anon` and **no write policy exists on any of them**; the migration's trailing `DO $$` block RAISEs rather than trusting it. The service is the fence — and it must be, because the attach gate ("a KB the CALLER can currently read") is a predicate over ANOTHER table's visibility, which a `WITH CHECK` cannot express.
- **VISIBILITY IS ONE COLUMN WITH THREE VALUES — `private` | `team` | `workspace` — AND THIS IS THE ONE FEATURE WHERE THAT IS TRUE.** Skills, chats and KBs store the same three states as a PAIR (`visibility` 'public'|'private' × `access_mode` 'workspace'|'teams') because `access_mode` was added to a live table and its default was the BACKFILL; `src/features/skills/scope.ts › skillScope` is the helper that collapses the pair back. Nothing needed backfilling here, so the collapsed form is what is stored. **Porting a predicate across the boundary requires translating it**: `visibility='team'` here == `visibility='public' AND access_mode='teams'` there.
  - ⚠ **`workspace` IS THE VALUE; "Public" IS THE LABEL.** Never send `'public'` on this feature's wire, never render `'workspace'` to an operator.
- **TEAM LINKAGE IS A JOIN TABLE, MIRRORING WHAT THE SHIPPED FEATURES DO** — `team_resource_access` (`20260611020000_teams.sql`), extended per resource type by `20260707210000_chat_team_sharing.sql` and `20260708150001_skill_team_sharing.sql`. So team visibility is **MANY-teams**, as it is everywhere else; a nullable `team_id` would have been a narrower model than the product already ships.
  - ⚠ **IT IS A DEDICATED JUNCTION (`agent_template_teams`), NOT A FIFTH `resource_type` ON `team_resource_access` — F-277 is the argument.** That table carries a `level` this feature has no concept of, and joining it means widening `src/features/teams/access-levels.ts › TeamResourceType`, which is a CONTRACT with four consumers outside the feature (`teams/server/repository-resources.ts › RESOURCE_TABLES`, `› listTeamsModeResources`, `members/components/member-bits.tsx › RESOURCE_META`, and the hand-copy in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/members-render.ts`). The SHAPE is mirrored; the polymorphic table is not reused.
- **THE VISIBILITY MATRIX IS WRITTEN TWICE AND THE TWO MUST MOVE TOGETHER**: `src/features/agent-templates/server/service-shared.ts › canSeeTemplate` and the `agent_templates_member_select` policy. Same arms, same ORDER. `20260716150000_chats_team_aware_rls.sql` is the record of what a drift costs — RLS stayed permissive after the service tightened, and a team-scoped transcript leaked through PostgREST to every member until somebody compared them.
  - ⚠ **PRIVATE MEANS PRIVATE, ADMINS INCLUDED**, and the arm ORDER is what enforces it: the admin arm sits INSIDE the `team` branch, not above it. An admin administers SHARING; that is not a read of a teammate's private row. This is TIGHTER than `chats_member_select`, which leads with a blanket admin arm. `service-visibility.test.ts` enumerates 3 visibilities × 5 caller kinds so a reordering cannot be green.
  - **A workspace-scoped API key sees ONLY `workspace` templates** (M-10 — such a key may be shared between humans, so it inherits no individual's reach), including ones its own user id created.
- 🔒 **YOU MAY NOT ATTACH A KNOWLEDGE BASE YOU CANNOT READ.** Every id in an attach set is resolved through `service-shared.ts › resolveVisibleKnowledgeBases` — the knowledge feature's `canSeeBase` rule MIRRORED, not imported (§1 forbids the cross-feature import). Without it a template is a laundering channel: attach a teammate's private base by id, flip the template to `workspace`, and every member's spawned agent gets a pointer to it. ⚠ **The refusal is 404-shaped, never a distinguishable 403** — the difference between the two codes would be an existence oracle for other people's private bases.
  - ⚠ **THE SAME PREDICATE FILTERS READS, so the attachment list is VIEWER-FILTERED: two people resolving the SAME template can legitimately get different `knowledgeBases` arrays.** An attachment is a REFERENCE, never a copy — nothing about the base's content is denormalized — so a base that later goes private simply disappears from the payload rather than leaking its name.
- **WRITES ARE CREATOR-OR-WORKSPACE-ADMIN, FOR EVERY FIELD.** There is no "editable by the team it is shared with" level, deliberately: team `visibility` shares the ability to USE an identity, not to rewrite what it says. That is also why the team junction carries no `level` column. **An invisible template 404s BEFORE the write gate can 403**, so a 403 never confirms a row exists.
- **`fields` IS SHAPE-IN-ZOD, SIZE-IN-SQL.** Element shape (`{key, value}` strings, unique keys, both halves charset-bounded as LABELS) is `src/features/agent-templates/schema.ts`; the DB CHECK asserts only `jsonb_typeof(fields) = 'array'` and `octet_length(fields::text) <= 8192`. ⚠ **Zod measures the same BYTES the CHECK measures** (`TextEncoder`, not `.length`) — a character count would pass a CJK payload here and fail it there as an opaque 500.
- **HARD DELETE, NO TOMBSTONE.** No `deleted_at` column, no trash, no restore. Both junctions ride `ON DELETE CASCADE` — **the service writes no hand-rolled cascade**, and `service-writes.test.ts` asserts that ABSENCE, because a hand-written cascade is one that acquires a new child table and forgets it.
- **NOT PUBLISHED TO REALTIME, and the decision is recorded at the table** (§7's rule). A template is edited by one person in a form and read at spawn by a desktop already making an HTTP request: no peer event, no live surface. Same answer `channel_mention_reads` got. It follows that no `workspace_id`-bearing replica identity is needed either — that requirement exists to make DELETE frames deliverable, and there are no frames.
- **THE REST SURFACE IS NOT `sessionOnly`, EXCEPT `DELETE`.** An orchestrator agent LISTING templates is the point of making them persistent, so GET/POST/PATCH stay reachable by an agent token; `DELETE /api/agent-templates/[templateId]` is session-gated because it is permanent and may destroy what a whole team spawns from. ⚠ **Per-METHOD** — `src/shared/auth/write-gate-coverage.test.ts` sees only that the FILE contains `sessionOnly: true`, so `[templateId]/route.test.ts › the per-method gate` is what pins WHICH method carries it.
- **`GET /api/agent-templates/[templateId]/resolve` IS THE LAUNCH CONTRACT** — a FLAT, unwrapped `{name, instructions, model, fields, knowledgeBases, authoredByCaller}`, and nothing else. It composes `getTemplateById`, so it is gated by the same matrix as every other read and is not a second, weaker door. ⚠ **It is separate from the record endpoint on purpose:** the record grows fields as the product does, and the launcher must not re-decide which of them matter every time. A field added to `AgentTemplate` does not appear here unless somebody decides it belongs in a launch. `resolve/route.test.ts` pins the key set EXACTLY.
  - **`authoredByCaller` IS THE SIXTH KEY, AND IT IS A SECURITY GATE RATHER THAN A CONVENIENCE** (2026-08-22). The desktop's ROLE block wears the OPERATOR posture over a template its operator wrote and the `UNTRUSTED_SKILL_BODY_HEADER`-shaped posture over another member's, and that gate cannot be built without it — the same authorship gating `narration.ts › isForeignAuthored` applies for the same reason (`knowledge-shared.ts`: *"noise is how a security header stops being read"*). ⚠ **A COMPUTED BOOLEAN, NEVER `createdBy`**: a raw creator id in a launch payload is ownership information the launcher has no use for, and the boolean discloses nothing the caller cannot already read off the list endpoint. ⚠ **It is AUTHORSHIP, not permission** — a workspace admin resolving someone else's template gets `false`, and so does everyone once the author leaves the workspace (`created_by` is `SET NULL`), which is the fail-closed direction. `server/service-resolve.test.ts` pins the value per caller kind; the route test pins only the shape.
- 🔒 **A TEMPLATE WIDENS PROMPT CONTENT ONLY.** It never supplies, influences or relaxes a containment input: the tool profile, the permission axes, the working folder and the delivery lane are still the MACHINE's, resolved from the machine's own state. `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-launch-op.js` computes the tool profile from `targeting.resolveToolProfile(listener.watchedChannel(...))` BEFORE the template is fetched at all, and `main/template-resolve.js › narrow` is a literal whitelist of the six launch keys — a payload naming `toolProfile`, `startModes` or a `cwd` is DROPPED rather than refused. `prompt-profile-drift.test.mjs` fails any built turn that orders a hard-denied tool, template-built turns included, which is the mechanical enforcement and needs no new test genus.
- **THE TEMPLATE ENTERS THE PROMPT AS A FENCED ROLE BLOCK IN THE USER TURN, NEVER AS A `systemPrompt`** (`dopl-desktop-app/main/prompt-framing-template.js › templateRoleFraming`, spliced into the REQUESTER branch of `main/prompt-framing.js › buildFencedTurn`, last of the framing blocks and adjacent to the goal). ⚠ **A system prompt would sit structurally ABOVE the containment framing** — LANE_EXCLUSIVITY, the SECURITY RULES, the counterparty framing and the identity block all live in the user turn — and for a `team`/`workspace` template that is ANOTHER MEMBER's text, which is exactly backwards. It would also survive `options.resume` uncorrectably. **PRECEDENCE, stated once:** tool-profile deny lists and `canUseTool` (CODE) > the framing's own rules (main's voice, OUTSIDE the fence) > the template's instructions and fields (INSIDE `BEGIN-ROLE-<nonce>`) > the launch goal (INSIDE `BEGIN-REQUEST-<nonce>`). **ROLE FIRST, GOAL LAST**: a template never replaces the goal and a goal never suppresses the template.
  - **BOTH FENCE VOCABULARIES ARE STRIPPED LINE-EXACT** from a template's instructions — the ROLE one so the body cannot close its own container, and the REQUEST one so it cannot forge a goal in main's voice. Same rule and same reason as `main/session-seed.js › frameOperatorTurn`. The name and both halves of every field pass a neutralizer belt at render as well (`main/prompt-sanitize.js › sanitizeText`), even though `SAFE_LABEL_RE` already bounded them at write.
    - ⚠ **THE BELT'S LENGTH BOUND IS THE FIELD'S OWN, AND EVERY REUSE OF A DISPLAY DEFAULT IS A BUG** (2026-08-23, F-287). **The neutralization is the security part** — the whitespace collapse and the fence-token strip, neither of which depends on the bound; **the length is a BUDGET and belongs to the field.** `main/prompt-sanitize.js › sanitizeName` is `sanitizeText(value, 80)` and 80 is correct for exactly one caller: a counterparty `display_name`, which has no server bound of its own. Reused for template text it clipped a field VALUE (bounded 1000 by `schema.ts › TemplateFieldSchema`) to 80 and the template NAME (bounded 120) to 80 in the role line, while every operator-facing surface kept showing the whole thing. The live numbers: **name 120, field key 80, field value 1000**, applied at BOTH the boundary (`main/template-resolve.js › narrow`, per field) and the render (`main/prompt-framing-template.js › fieldLines`). **A BOUNDARY BOUND MUST MATCH THE WRITER'S, NEVER UNDERCUT IT** — a smaller one is a limit the operator can neither see nor satisfy, and the disagreement is silent and always resolves against them. The same parameterization now applies to `main/session-store.js › durableName` and `main/agent-history.js › historyName`, which persist `templateName` at 120.
  - **UNDER `read_only` THE ATTACHED BASES ARE NAMED AND NO TOOL CALL IS ORDERED.** That profile hard-denies `mcp__dopl__dopl_kb`, and a turn that ordered it would fail `prompt-profile-drift.test.mjs`. ⚠ **Named, not dropped** — §11's UNKNOWN-is-not-EMPTY rule: a silently missing section would be the prompt claiming the template has no knowledge attached.
- **A SESSION REPORTS ITS TEMPLATE BY NAME, AS A SNAPSHOT, AND ONLY TO ITS OPERATOR** (2026-08-23, Samuel's OQ-5 ruling; `supabase/migrations/20260823130000_channel_sessions_template_name.sql` — ✅ **APPLIED**, name `channel_sessions_template_name`, history version `20260823092005`, re-measured 2026-08-26 by the command above, joined on the NAME). `channel_sessions.template_name` is an additive nullable TEXT column, and every word of that is load-bearing.
  - **A SESSION KEEPS ITS SPAWN-TIME TEMPLATE CONTENT, AND THAT FALLS OUT OF THE ARCHITECTURE RATHER THAN BEING ENFORCED.** The resolve happens ONCE, in `main/session-launch-op.js`, and the answer is stashed on `context.template`; the role block is built at WAKE from what was captured then (`main/session-seed.js › takeFraming` is a one-shot). A template edited between spawn and wake does not change the session, a template DELETED after spawn does not stop it, and a PARK/RESUME carries it for free because `main/session-park.js › resumeParked` works IN PLACE and never rewrites `s.context`.
    - ⚠ **A CRASH RESUME IS THE OTHER LANE AND IT DID *NOT* CARRY IT — CORRECTED 2026-08-23 (F-288).** This bullet named `resumeParked` as if it covered both, which is exactly the shape of a doc that wins the next argument: `main/session-park.js › startResume` is a full re-`startSession` off a DURABLE RECORD, and the durable projection is a literal whitelist that did not name the template. So after a desktop crash the rebuilt session reported `templateName: null`, `templateName` is in `main/session-telemetry.js › STATE_FIELDS` (so the null BYPASSED the cadence floor), and the next push ERASED `channel_sessions.template_name` under a still-running agent — the value an orchestrator reads in `read_sessions` to tell six agents apart. **THE FIX IS THE NAME, PERSISTED AND REHYDRATED, NOT THE TEMPLATE**: `main/session-io.js › baseRecord` projects it, `main/session-store.js › durableSessionRecord` whitelists it (at the column's own 120, not `durableName`'s 80 display default), and `main/session-park.js › contextFromRecord` rebuilds it as a NAME-ONLY STUB. ⚠ **THE STUB IS SUFFICIENT AND THE BODY IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT**: `instructions` / `fields` / `knowledgeBases` have exactly one consumer, `main/prompt-framing-template.js › templateRoleFraming` through the one-shot `takeFraming`, which a resume never runs (`freshFraming` is false whenever `resumeSdkId` is set) — the SDK's own resume carries the original ROLE block. **A consumer that later reads `template.instructions` off a LIVE session must not assume it is there.**
  - ⚠ **A DENORMALIZED SNAPSHOT, NOT AN FK, AND NOTHING MAY "FIX" THAT LATER.** A session captures its template's content ONCE, at spawn, and keeps it for its whole life — so it must go on reporting what it **ran as** after the template is renamed or deleted. An FK would rename a running agent under its operator, or (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) erase the only record of what it is executing. **A stale name here is correct, not drift.** The mappers resolve nothing: `src/features/channels/server/session-state-service.ts › toUpsert` stores what the desktop reported and `src/features/channels/server/collab-dto.ts › mapOwnSessionStateRow` passes it through.
  - 🔒 **OPERATOR-ONLY, on two independent arguments.** It is operator-authored free text, which is the exact condition `20260822150000_channel_sessions_telemetry.sql` states for a field going PRIVATE rather than joining `detail` on the peer projection; and **a private template's name reaching a peer is an existence oracle** — the same leak the 404-not-403 rule and the deliberate absence of name uniqueness (next bullet) both exist to close. ⚠ **There is deliberately no `hasTemplate` boolean on the peer projection**: a smaller oracle is still one.
  - **THE FENCE IS THE MAPPER; THE COLUMN GRANT IS THE BELT.** `template_name` joins `OPERATOR_ONLY_SESSION_COLUMNS` / `OPERATOR_ONLY_SESSION_FIELDS` (`src/features/channels/server/collab-dto.ts`, parallel by construction, now EIGHT) and is mapped in `mapOwnSessionStateRow` only — `src/features/channels/server/collab-dto.ts › mapPeerSessionStateRow` BUILDS a narrow object, so it fails closed. `src/features/channels/server/session-visibility.test.ts` is a PROPERTY test over those arrays and **covers the new field with no new case, which is that suite's whole point**. The migration excludes the column from the `GRANT SELECT (…)` list for the PostgREST door only; `src/features/channels/schema-sql.test.ts` pins that exclusion.
  - **THE WIRE FIELD IS `templateName`, AND THE SERVER ACCEPTS IT BEFORE ANY DESKTOP SENDS IT.** `src/features/channels/schema-sessions.ts` bounds it `safeLabel(…, 120)` — character for character `agent_templates_name_charset_check`, so a name legal on a template is never refusable into this projection. ⚠ **The order is the contract**: zod validates the ARRAY, so one unknown key would 400 a newer desktop's WHOLE push, `retryable(400)` is false, and that machine's `read_sessions` would answer `[]` forever (§11, §13).
  - **WHERE THE OPERATOR SEES IT:** `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-session-render.ts › telemetryClauses` renders it as the FIRST clause, immediately before the model (`` `Code Auditor` · `opus-5` ``) — ⚠ and the MODEL half of that pair is guaranteed to be ONE token since F-293, so a name carrying a space is a template and never a model — on own-scoped lines only — `read_sessions` and the `sessionBlockLines` block every `await` hold returns. A peer's line is unchanged: a handle and a state.
  - **AND ON THE AGENTS TAB'S OWN-AGENT CARD** (2026-08-22): `components/channels-v2/agents-tab-cards.tsx › AgentCard` prints it in the existing detail line, **before** the model clause and with the same `·` separator the MCP surface uses — WHO the agent is reads ahead of WHAT it runs on. No pill and no `Template:` label (INVARIANTS §5, minimal copy). ⚠ **ABSENT AND `null` BOTH RENDER NOTHING** — a blank agent has no template and an older main omits the field, and neither is a reason to print a word. ⚠ **OPERATOR-ONLY HERE IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A CHECK:** the field is on `spa-bridge-shapes.ts › DesktopSessionSummary` (this machine's own registry) and NOT on `hooks/use-channel-agent-sessions.ts › ChannelPeerSession`, so `› PeerCards` has nothing to render and nothing to be careful with. **Do not plumb it there.**
- ⚠ **TEMPLATES ARE ADDRESSED BY UUID AND HAVE NO SLUG AND NO NAME UNIQUENESS**, which is a MIRROR of `skills` (unique on `(workspace_id, slug)`, nothing on `name`) rather than of `teams` (unique on `(workspace_id, lower(name))`). Uniqueness across a visibility boundary would leak the existence of a private row through a conflict error, and two people may each keep a private "Researcher".
- **AN ORCHESTRATOR NAMES A TEMPLATE BY ID **OR** BY EXACT NAME, IN ONE PARAM, AND AN AMBIGUOUS NAME IS REFUSED WITH THE LIST** (2026-08-23). `dopl_channel(op="launch_agent")` gains `template` (1–120), and the resolution happens SERVER-SIDE in `src/features/channels/server/service-launch.ts › resolveTemplateForDirective`, over `src/features/agent-templates/server/service-resolve-ref.ts › resolveTemplateRef`. ⚠ **ONE PARAM FOR BOTH IS THE TREE'S OWN IDIOM**, not a new convention — `dopl_kb`'s `base` already takes either. The rule, in order: a UUID is an ID (and never falls back to a name lookup, or the two misses answer through each other); otherwise a CASE-INSENSITIVE EXACT name over the CALLER-VISIBLE set; **more than one match ⇒ REFUSE, listing each match's id and visibility; zero ⇒ the same `AGENT_TEMPLATE_NOT_FOUND` shape `/resolve` answers.**
- 🔒 **A MISS MAY NAME THE TENANCY IT MISSED IN, AND ONLY OVER ROWS THE CALLER COULD ALREADY LIST** (2026-09-01, T35). Every template read is keyed `(workspace_id, id)`, so a row in another tenancy is filtered out **before** `canSeeTemplate` runs — "you own it" and "it resolves here" are different questions, and the refusal used to answer only the second. `src/features/agent-templates/server/service-resolve-ref.ts › classifyMissingTemplateRef` is the **ONE** classifier, reached by BOTH doors: `resolveTemplateRef` (the MCP create fence, via a new `elsewhere` arm) and `service-reads.ts › resolveTemplateForLaunch` (the desktop's `/resolve`). ⚠ **IT IS NOT AN EXISTENCE ORACLE, AND THE QUERY IS WHY**: `server/repository-tenancy.ts › findTemplateTenancyRows` matches on TWO arms only — `created_by = <caller>` or `visibility = 'workspace'` — inside workspaces the caller is an **active member** of, so another member's `private`/`team` row matches nothing anywhere and a probed id answers exactly as a nonexistent one does. A **SHARED credential** is classified never (arm 2 of `canSeeTemplate`, restated). ⚠ **`details.elsewhere` IS ABSENT, NOT NULL**, on an ordinary miss (`HttpError.toResponseBody` omits `undefined`) — a key present on one 404 and missing from the other would itself be the distinction. ⚠ Two extra reads **only on a miss**; the hit path is unchanged.
- ⚠ **THE DESKTOP'S HALF STOPS AT THE LOG, AND THE WIRE IS THE REASON.** `main/template-resolve.js › resolveTemplate` reads `details.elsewhere` off the 404 and returns it beside the unchanged `reason`, but `POST .../decide` carries a refusal REASON out of a **closed vocabulary** (`channels/server/service-launch-dto.ts › LAUNCH_REFUSAL_REASONS`, paired with the column's own CHECK) and no free text — so no tenancy crosses that wire. The orchestrator is given the same **RULE** instead of the row, by `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-doctrine.ts › TENANCY_RULE` — interpolated into the `no-template` entry of that file's `REFUSALS` block, and into the two CREATE-time refusals in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-launch.ts › templateNotFound`. ⚠ **THIS ANCHOR POINTED AT A `REFUSAL_SENTENCES` MAP IN `channel-ops-launch.ts` UNTIL 2026-09-01, AND THAT SYMBOL IS GONE** (named without the `›` form on purpose — `check-doc-refs.mjs` resolves every anchor it sees, including one in a sentence explaining that the target was deleted): the P1 verbosity tier (T10) replaced the per-reason paragraph map with `RETRY_ADVICE` (`"once" | "no"`) and moved the sentences into the doctrine, which a result now POINTS at rather than carrying. `check-doc-refs.mjs` caught it on the integration branch. ⚠ Do not widen the enum to "fix" this: the vocabulary and the CHECK move together.
  - 🔒 **IT NEVER PICKS, AND THAT FALLS OUT OF THE BULLET ABOVE.** Names are deliberately not unique, so two visible "Researcher"s is a legitimate state; every natural tie-break ("mine wins", "newest wins") starts an identity the caller did not choose and reports success. ⚠ **THE MATCH LIST IS NOT AN ORACLE** — every row in it already passed `canSeeTemplate` for that caller, so it discloses exactly what `GET /api/agent-templates` would. On the wire it is a 409 `AGENT_TEMPLATE_AMBIGUOUS` with `details.matches`; `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-launch.ts › ambiguousTemplate` renders it. ⚠ **The MCP layer branches on the ERROR CODE, never the status**: one call now has two ways to 404 (no such channel/membership, no such template) and telling an agent its CHANNEL was wrong when its template name was is the mis-narration `channel-errors.ts` exists to stop.
  - ⚠ **THE VISIBILITY MATRIX IS COMPOSED, NOT COPIED — the channels service imports it.** `canSeeTemplate` is already written twice (that function and `agent_templates_member_select`) and documented as having to move together; a third statement inside `channels/` is exactly the shape **F-278** is filed against. INVARIANTS §1 says there are no cross-feature imports and **F-275 records that the tree has never obeyed that rule**; `channels → agent-templates` already existed on the client side. ⚠ **`ChannelContext` GREW `apiKeyWorkspaceId` FOR THIS ONE READER** (2026-08-23) — arm 2 of the matrix (M-10) rides on that field, and building the template context with `null` would let a workspace-scoped key resolve the key owner's PRIVATE templates by name.
- 🔒 **ON THE DIRECTIVE LANE THE TEMPLATE PASSES TWO FENCES AND THEY BELONG TO DIFFERENT PEOPLE.** The **CREATE** fence is the ORCHESTRATOR's credential (above): it cannot NAME what it cannot see. The **RESOLVE** fence is the OPERATOR's, on their desktop, at claim time (§11). Both are required and neither substitutes. The consequences are fail-closed and are written down rather than debugged: a `workspace` template works; a `team` template the orchestrator is in and the operator is not is CREATED and then refused `no-template` on the machine; the orchestrator's own PRIVATE template is unusable over this lane unless the orchestrator IS the operator (the common case, not the only one); and a KB the OPERATOR cannot read is simply OMITTED from their resolve payload, so a shared template cannot launder access to a private base.
- **THE DIRECTIVE ROW STORES `template_id` **AND** A `template_name` SNAPSHOT, AND THE PAIR IS THE POINT** (`supabase/migrations/20260823140000_channel_launch_directives_template.sql` — ✅ **APPLIED**, name `channel_launch_directives_template`, history version `20260823092038`, re-measured 2026-08-26 by the command above, joined on the NAME). The FK is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, mirroring `task_id`, so a template deleted between CREATE and CLAIM leaves the directive standing with a NULL id — **byte-identical, on the id alone, to a directive that named no template**, and those two get OPPOSITE answers (launch blank vs REFUSE). The NAME is what survives the SET NULL and makes a deletion legible. ⚠ **A SNAPSHOT, NEVER AN FK, AND NOTHING MAY ADD ONE LATER** — the staleness IS the signal. ⚠ **AND THE SAME MIGRATION WIDENS `channel_launch_directives_refusal_reason_check` TO SEVEN WORDS**, because it lands in the same wave as the producer that can finally write `no-template`; the TS enums ran one word ahead of the CHECK for a day and four files carried a standing "do not ship a producer into this window" note that this migration clears. ⚠ **`'template-approval'` IS NOT ADDED AND MUST NOT BE** — it is an IPC-only word (§11), and a column that could store it would tell a future reader this lane has an approval gate it does not have.

- **TEMPLATES ARE AGENT-AUTHORABLE OVER MCP SINCE 2026-08-28 — `dopl_agent` (list / get / create / update) + `dopl_agent_admin` (delete, refused).** The HTTP surface was always agent-reachable (this section's own "an orchestrator agent LISTING templates is the point"); what was missing was a TOOL. Full rules in §10; the three that belong here:
  - **SHARING INTO A CONTAINER IS `visibility: "workspace"` ON `op="update"`** — there is no `op="share"`, because a template has no grant table and one write must not have two doors.
  - ⚠ **AND IT IS AUDIENCE-CHANGING, SO IT PREVIEWS FIRST.** Inside a `kind='link'` container with a PEER in it, both that update and a `visibility: "workspace"` create go through §10's confirm class — a dry run plus a single-use token. Same argument `lib/template-draft.ts › containerCopyDraft` was reversed over on 2026-08-27: it publishes the operator's agent identity into the room the peer is standing in.
  - 🔒 **THE `template-approval` ASYMMETRY IS NOW LIVE, AND IS ACCEPTED** (Samuel's ruling Q13, 2026-08-28). An agent can now author and share a template a peer's DIRECTIVE-lane launch will run **with no first-run prompt**, where the BUTTON lane raises one (`main/session-launch-op.js`). The consent for the directive lane is the standing per-machine toggle, and the desktop still wears `main/prompt-framing-template.js › FOREIGN_HEADER`. ⚠ **`'template-approval'` still must NOT enter the `refusal_reason` CHECK** — a column that could store it would tell a future reader this lane has a gate it does not have.

### The surface (measured 2026-08-22)

- **ONE PAGE, ONE ROUTE, ONE NAV ROW: `/:workspaceSegment/agents`.** Seam at `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/agents/index.tsx`, surface at `src/features/agent-templates/components/agent-templates-core.tsx` — Next-free and ROUTER-FREE by construction, like every shared page core. ⚠ **The label and the segment are both `Agents`/`agents`, NOT "agent templates"**: the operator's noun for the thing they author is the AGENT, and §5's noun rule ("the noun on both is the **AGENT**") is a copy rule with tests behind it, not a style preference.
  - ⚠ **NO `agents/:templateId` DETAIL ROW, and the absence is load-bearing.** A template is edited in a MODAL, not at a URL, so the hand copy in `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js › WORKSPACE_PAGES` carries **`agents: false`** — a `true` there hands the renderer a third segment matching no route. **All FOUR places the route table's docblock demands are written**: the route row, the `NavSection` member + `NAV` row in `src/shared/layout/app-shell/app-sidebar-core.tsx`, and that hand copy. `apps/desktop-ui/src/routes.test.tsx › registers the agents page` and `› gives the agents page its sidebar nav row` pin the first three against each other; `dopl-desktop-app/test/deep-link-target.test.mjs › the page table matches the SPA's route table` DERIVES the fourth from `routes.tsx` and fails on any drift — so a future detail row is a two-file change or the desktop suite goes red.
- 🚫 **NOTHING ON THIS PAGE IS PRESSED IN (Samuel's ruling, 2026-08-22).** No `FIELD_WELL`, no `.concave-field`, no `.concave-track`, no `SECTION_BOX_INSET`, no `SectionBox` — RAISED, ELEVATED and FLAT faces only. The three scope panels are the flat `bg-card-surface-subtle` face on a hairline (deliberately **not** `SectionBox`, whose BODY is `bg-bg-inset` plus the concave inset shadow); cards are `.bento`; every text control wears the kit's RAISED well — `shared/ui/wells.ts › RAISED_INPUT`, which is `RAISED_WELL` plus type/placeholder/focus and which **was promoted OUT of this feature on 2026-08-27** (see the standard-dialog bullet below); `components/template-editor-rows.tsx` re-exports it. ⚠ **Pinned as a SOURCE READ, not a render** — `components/template-editor.test.tsx › no concave surfaces` sweeps the feature's UI directories for those class names, because jsdom loads no stylesheet and a rendered assertion would pass against a concave field and prove nothing.
- **THREE PANELS, ONE READ.** `GET /api/agent-templates` answers everything the caller may see and `lib/visibility.ts › groupByVisibility` splits it into Private / Team / Public. The server does not group (see the route's own docblock), and the client does not FILTER — a second "is this mine?" test would either duplicate the visibility matrix above or hide a row the server deliberately sent. ⚠ **A row whose `visibility` this build does not know is DROPPED from the sections rather than filed under one** (§11 — UNKNOWN is not EMPTY, and it is not a guess either).
  - 🚫 **THERE IS NO FIXTURE FALLBACK ON THIS READ, AND ONE MUST NOT COME BACK (2026-08-26, F-332 ✅ RESOLVED).** `hooks/use-agent-templates.ts` returns `query.data ?? []` and the error, full stop: a FAILED read renders the plain error state and nothing else, in every build. A dev-only fallback (`client/mock.ts` + `› isMockFallback`, gated on `process.env.NODE_ENV`) existed from 2026-08-23 while the migration above was unapplied; it is applied, and — the reason this is a rule rather than a cleanup — **the same hook now reads a link CONTAINER, where a 403/404 is an ORDINARY answer** (roster change, stale header, a guest opening the tab), so the branch had become a dev build painting fabricated templates under a channel that has none. UNKNOWN is not EMPTY and it is not a GUESS either (§11). `hooks/use-agent-templates.test.ts` is the rewritten-to-survivors pin.
  - **An empty panel keeps its header and says one quiet line.** A section that vanished when empty would make "you have no team templates" and "this workspace has no teams" the same picture.
  - **A card is name + a muted description + a model chip ONLY when one is set.** `channels/lib/agent-models.ts › agentModelShortLabel` returns `null` for an unset model, and that is a different answer from its `agentModelLabel`'s "Default": a card states what a template CARRIES.
- **ONE CREATE AFFORDANCE, AT PAGE LEVEL.** Not a per-section "+": a template's SCOPE is a field in the editor, so a per-panel plus would pre-decide it from the panel that was clicked and then disagree with the control the operator changes two seconds later.
- 🔑 **THE EDITOR IS THE `StandardDialog` REFERENCE (Samuel, 2026-08-27).** It was a narrow `ModalShell` stated inline; its width, its centered-and-uppercased heading, its `RAISED_INPUT` "pillow" fields, its uppercase field headers and its fully-rounded footer pair are now `shared/ui/standard-dialog.tsx` (`StandardDialog` / `DialogField` / `DialogActions` + `DIALOG_TITLE` / `DIALOG_BTN_PRIMARY` / `DIALOG_BTN_SECONDARY`), and **FOUR dialogs compose it**: this editor, `knowledge/components/create-base-dialog.tsx`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/add-person-dialog.tsx` (a Popover until that day, and renamed off the "-popover" name with it) and `› new-channel-dialog.tsx`. **WIDTH IS NOT A PROP**; a fifth surface that needs its own is not a standard dialog. ⚠ **The heading is uppercased in CSS, never in the string** — `title` is also `ModalShell`'s `aria-label`, so a `.toUpperCase()` would rewrite the dialog's accessible name and every `getByRole("dialog", { name })` in three suites. Pinned by `shared/ui/standard-dialog.test.tsx`.
  - **EVERY DROPDOWN IN A STANDARD DIALOG IS `SelectMenu variant="raised"`** — the kit's white raised button (`.auth-btn-3d-light`), not the flat inset pill. The two faces are alternatives, not layers (`shared/ui/select-menu.tsx › TRIGGER_FACE`); the flat one's `hover:bg-surface-raised-2` would flatten the raised gradient.
  - 🔄 **ADDING A CUSTOM FIELD IS A DIALOG OVER THE EDITOR (2026-08-27), REVERSING THE NO-MODAL-IN-MODAL RULE** `components/template-editor-rows.tsx` carried. The old note argued a second surface puts the operator two Escapes from their draft; what settled it is that a field is about to be more than a key and a value, and a row that grows four controls wide is a form pretending to be a list. **Editing and removing stay INLINE.** ⚠ The dialog's confirm reads **"Add", not "Add field"** — the title already names the thing, and two buttons with one accessible name is ambiguous for the operator and for `getByRole`. ⚠ `cleanFields` is still the save-time backstop, pinned in `lib/template-draft.test.ts`, not in the modal's suite.
- **THE EDITOR IS A NARROW `ModalShell`** underneath — this tree's entity-editing idiom (`knowledge/components/base-settings-modal.tsx` and every create dialog), not a slide-over, which has no precedent here. ONE component for create and edit; the only differences are the heading, the Save verb, and whether Delete exists. **It stays OPEN until the write settles** and closes only on success (`create-channel-dialog.tsx`'s idiom): the writes are optimistic, so a modal that closed on the click would leave a failed save with nowhere to report — the row silently rolls back and the operator's edit is gone with no sentence saying why.
  - **`teamIds` is a MULTI-select, because the server's is a set.** A single-team control would pick one on read and drop the rest on the next save. ⚠ The editor CLEARS it when the scope leaves `team`: the schema REFUSES a `teamIds` key without `visibility: 'team'`, so carrying a stale set is a 400 on the next unrelated edit, not a harmless extra key.
  - **CLEARING SENDS `null`, NOT `""`.** The schema's own split (absent leaves the column alone, `null` CLEARS it), and for `model` it is not a choice at all — that field is a `safeLabel` carrying a `.min(1)`, so `""` would 400 the operator picking Default. `lib/template-draft.ts` is the ONE place a draft becomes a body, and `template-draft.test.ts` pins both bodies away from the modal.
  - **DELETE IS BEHIND `ConfirmDialog` AND THE COPY SAYS PERMANENT**, matching the hard-delete rule above. ⚠ The route is `sessionOnly`; the SPA is always a session, so that 403 is unreachable from here and earns no special copy.
- **NO LAUNCH UI ON THIS PAGE.** Choosing a template AT LAUNCH is a later phase, and `agent-templates-core.test.tsx › what this page deliberately leaves out` refuses one — a beachhead here would be a second surface deciding what starting an agent means, which is what `resolve` exists to keep singular.

### The /home face — templates inside a link container (2026-08-26, `docs/specs/home-agents-tab.plan.md` M2)

- **THE SAME `visibility` COLUMN ANSWERS SHARING INSIDE A CONTAINER, AND NOTHING NEW WAS BUILT TO ASK IT (Samuel's ruling Q1).** A `kind='link'` container has no teams (§4A), so the three values collapse to TWO live ones: `workspace` = **shared with the other person in this relationship**, `private` = the creator's alone. `agent_only` has no template referent (a template has ONE consumer — main, at spawn — where a KB has two, which is why Knowledge shares by GRANT and this shares by VISIBILITY). ⚠ **A `channel_resource_grants` row for templates would be a THIRD copy of the visibility matrix** (this section already keeps two, and they must move together); widening is correct only for a MULTI-CHANNEL standard workspace and is filed as **F-334**, not built.
- **THREE SCOPES, TWO SECTIONS, TWO WORKSPACES** (`apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/agent-panels.tsx`): **A** container + `workspace`; **B** container + `private` + the caller's own; **C** the caller's HOME workspace + `private` + their own. B and C share a `SelectMenu` pill because they are the same shelf at two ranges; A is its own section because it is a different QUESTION — who else can wear this identity.
  - ⚠ **THE SECTION-A HEADING IS "Shared in this channel", NEVER "Public"**, and both container label sets live in `agent-templates/lib/visibility.ts › SECTIONS_CONTAINER` / `› SECTION_PRIVATE_EVERYWHERE` — beside `› SECTIONS`, never hand-typed in a component. **Two arrays in one module cannot drift the way two components can.** `team` is a DEAD value here, so `SECTIONS_CONTAINER` has exactly two entries and a `team` row is **DROPPED** by `› groupByVisibility`, never re-filed under a live scope (§11: UNKNOWN is not a guess).
  - **TWO READS ON ONE PATH, DISTINGUISHED ONLY BY WORKSPACE, AND SCOPE C IS LAZY.** `hooks/use-agent-templates.ts` takes `workspaceId: string | null` (null = nothing to ask) plus an `enabled` option; the home list is not requested until the pill names it, and the pill wears `pendingRow` for that first fetch (§8 rule 8). Scope C's workspace id rides `POST /api/boot`'s `workspace`, which is **NULL until the caller is onboarded** — that scope then reads UNAVAILABLE, never EMPTY. **No new endpoint and no channel-scoped key**: the workspace element already separates the two entries, unlike Knowledge's (§9). This is exactly the surface F-331's entry-key rule exists for (§8).
  - ⚠ **`useAgentTemplates` EXPOSES `resolved`, AND EVERY EMPTY SENTENCE IS GATED ON IT.** `templates.length === 0` cannot tell a disabled or in-flight read from a measured one, and `loading` is useless here because TanStack reports a DISABLED query as pending forever. Four distinct sentences (A, B, C, and "no home workspace"), each against its own resolved read — the container and home reads move independently.
- **THE AUTHORSHIP MARKER IS ON EVERY SECTION-A CARD AND IT IS A SECURITY SIGNAL** (`components/template-picker.tsx › authorMarker`, Samuel's ruling Q5). A member-granted claimer can already create a template in the operator's container (POST is member+), and its instructions are what the operator's own agent would follow — the desktop already wears a different ROLE header for one. **The name costs no request:** `HomeChannel.peer` is already on the payload, so a named author needs no lookup; a nameless one still reads `by another member` rather than losing the marker. ⚠ **THIS SAID `peer` IS "the roster minus the caller" ON THE GROUNDS THAT A CONTAINER HOLDS ONE OR TWO MEMBERS, AND THAT PREMISE DIED 2026-08-26** (the cap is retired, §4A). **The roster minus the caller is `HomeChannel.peers` now — a LIST — and this marker reads ALL of it** (`pages/home/agent-panel-cards.tsx › useContainerAuthorMarker`, via `home-rows.ts › channelPeople`); reading only `peer` would have named one possible author in a four-person channel and degraded the other three to `by another member`. **The marker fails SAFE either way** — an unresolvable author is never rendered as MINE — so that was a legibility bug, not a security one; it is fixed because a security signal nobody can read is one nobody acts on. ⚠ **`peer` still exists and is `peers[0]`; do not read it as "the other party" anywhere new.** Owner-flooring container writes is a route-floor change, filed as a QUESTION for Samuel, not built.
- 🚫 **NO LAUNCH CONTROL ON THIS FACE EITHER, and the reason is stronger here than on the workspace page:** the launch lane is ALREADY wired through /home — relationship record → `StandaloneChannelSurface` → the Chat face's `TemplateLaunchPicker` → `useAgentTemplates(container)` → `sessions:launch`. This tab is the AUTHORING half of that lane, and a second launch surface fights `resolve`'s singularity. Pinned by `pages/home/agent-panels.test.tsx › what this pane deliberately leaves out`.
- **TWO SECTIONS, NO SCOPE PILL — THE /home AGENTS FACE (Samuel's ruling 2026-08-27, converging it on the Knowledge face, §5).** **SHARED IN THIS CHANNEL** = the container's `visibility: 'workspace'` templates; **PERSONAL** = the caller's HOME SHELF, always — `private` + own **AND `home_scoped`** (`20260901120000_agent_template_home_scoped.sql`), in the workspace `POST /api/boot` names. Each section owns its own create button.
  - 🔒 **THE PER-CHANNEL PRIVATE SCOPE IS DELETED, AND ITS CONSEQUENCE IS TWO RULES.** (1) A container template reaches /home only at `visibility: 'workspace'`. (2) **`SECTIONS_CONTAINER` IS ONE OPTION** (`lib/visibility.ts`) — the container editor derives its visibility control from that array, and a container is not navigable (`isStandardWorkspace` keeps it off the rail), so offering `private` there would create rows reachable from NO surface. The container mount therefore also passes `defaultVisibility="workspace"`, because `emptyDraft()` starts at `private`.
  - 🔒 **"USE IN THIS CHANNEL" NOW COPIES AT `workspace`, NOT `private` (2026-08-27), AND THE CONFIRM DIALOG SAYS SO.** `lib/template-draft.ts › containerCopyDraft` forced `private` on the argument that "use" must not silently publish the operator's agent into the room the peer is standing in — correct while a private container section existed for it to land in. It does not, so a private copy would be invisible everywhere. **The audience change is stated to the operator before the button** (`pages/home/agent-copy.tsx › describe`: "everyone here will see it"); if that sentence is softened, the copy semantics are wrong again. Pinned in `lib/template-draft.test.ts › SHARES into the channel`.
  - ⚠ **THE SHELF IS NOT THE VISIBILITY AXIS, and F-333/F-336 are untouched by it.** `canSeeTemplate` (and its RLS twin `agent_templates_member_select`) decide WHO MAY READ; `home_scoped` decides WHICH SURFACE LISTS. The predicate neither reads nor is passed the column, a narrowed read can only ever return a SUBSET of the unfiltered one, and a container-session credential sees exactly what it saw before. Pinned in `server/service-shelf.test.ts › does NOT become a visibility gate`.
  - 🔒 **THE `query` AXIS ON `agentTemplateKeys` IS THE SHELF, AND IT REOPENED F-331's SHAPE.** That key factory's docblock said to revisit both sides in the same change if a `query` variant ever appeared; `?shelf=` is it. `list(shelf)` now BINDS the query into `entry()`, and **`useAgentTemplates` and `useAgentTemplateWrites` must be given the same `shelf`** — a read on `[path, ws, {shelf:…}]` patched by a writer on `[path, ws, undefined]` is a silent no-op whose only symptom is a created row that never appears.
- **THE AUTHORING HALF: CREATE AND EDIT, AND THE CREATE AFFORDANCE FOLLOWS THE SCOPE PILL (2026-08-26, M3).** "in this channel" writes into the CONTAINER; "across all channels" writes into the caller's home workspace — the same RULING 6 the /home Knowledge pane ships, and the button sits beside the pill it obeys so the two cannot be read apart. With no home workspace yet (`POST /api/boot` answered `workspace: null`) the button is DISABLED on that scope rather than quietly writing into the container the pill is not pointing at. **A row is EDITED WHERE IT LIVES**: a scope-C card opens against the HOME workspace and a section-A card against the container **whatever the pill currently says** — reading one workspace's list and writing another's is precisely F-331's shape, one layer up. ⚠ **THE TWO FACES DIVERGED ON 2026-08-26/27 AND WERE RE-CONVERGED ON 2026-08-27 — THEY NOW MEAN THE SAME THING AGAIN, AND THAT IS A FACT TO MAINTAIN, NOT TO ASSUME.** Both are TWO SECTIONS with NO SCOPE PILL, both have a PERSONAL section over a `home_scoped` shelf of the caller's default standard workspace, and both have a SHARED section whose create button writes into the CHANNEL'S CONTAINER. ⚠ **What is shared into a channel is the one thing that still differs, and it is a real difference, not a naming one**: a knowledge base is shared by a `(kb, channel)` GRANT ROW (three states, its own table), a template by `visibility: 'workspace'` (no grant table exists for templates — one consumer per row). So the KB create writes two rows atomically and the template create writes one.
  - 🔑 **TWO EDITOR MOUNTS, NOT ONE COMPONENT WITH A FLAG** (`apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/agent-editor.tsx › ContainerTemplateEditor` and `› HomeWorkspaceTemplateEditor`, over a shared inner mount). The rule "**a container must not fetch teams**" cannot be expressed as a conditional `useTeams(…)` call — hooks do not branch — so it is expressed as the SHAPE OF THE FILE instead, and `pages/home/agent-authoring.test.tsx › offers TWO visibility scopes in a container, and asks for no teams` asserts the ABSENCE OF THE CALL on the wire, not merely an empty list.
  - **THE EDITOR IS REUSED, PARAMETERISED BY ONE PROP.** `components/template-editor.tsx › TemplateEditorProps.sections` names the visibility scopes a mount offers, defaulting to `SECTIONS`; the container mount passes `SECTIONS_CONTAINER`, so the control has **two options and the shared one reads "Shared in this channel"** — still from `lib/visibility.ts`, never hand-typed. The home-workspace mount offers all THREE, because that workspace has live teams, and it fetches them so the Team scope is not a control that can only disable Save. ⚠ **The team-CLEARING branch stays in the editor either way**: it guards the workspace page, where `team` is live, and a container mount simply never reaches it.
  - ⚠ **A TEMPLATE SAVED AS Team OR Public IN THE HOME WORKSPACE LANDS OUTSIDE THIS PANE'S SCOPE C**, which lists `private` + mine only. That is correct rather than a bug — the row is in the operator's own workspace and its surface is that workspace's Agents page — and it is written down because "I created it and it vanished" is otherwise read as a cache bug.
  - **THE ATTACHABLE KNOWLEDGE BASES ARE THE TARGET WORKSPACE'S, OFF THE PLAIN KEY** (`knowledge/client/hooks.ts › useKnowledgeBaseList`), not the channel-scoped entry the Knowledge pane reads. `?channelId=` folds in `channelGrants` and lives in its own cache entry on purpose (§9); the attach picker's question is only "which bases can this caller read in this workspace", so it shares the plain entry the workspace Agents page and `HomeKnowledgeBaseView`'s controller already mount instead of pulling the grant-bearing one out from under the Knowledge pane.
  - **THE EDITOR IS MOUNTED ONLY WHILE OPEN**, so its `session` prop is the constant `1`: the workspace page keeps one editor mounted and bumps that number to reload the draft, and here the MOUNT is what loads it. The trade is the exit animation, which is cheaper than a second copy of the draft-reset rule.
- 🚫 **THE NO-CONCAVE RULING APPLIES ACROSS THE TREE BOUNDARY (Samuel's ruling Q4).** The /home panels REUSE `components/template-section.tsx` (`TemplatePanel` + `TemplateGrid`, flat `bg-card-surface-subtle`) rather than growing a second panel recipe, and `template-editor.test.tsx › no concave surfaces › HOME_FILES` sweeps files under `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/` as SOURCE — `agent-panels.tsx` and `agent-panel-cards.tsx` from M2, `agent-editor.tsx` and `agent-copy.tsx` as M3/M4 added them, and **`knowledge-panels.tsx` since 2026-08-27** (the flat-sections ruling below). **The sweep is /home's now, not the Agents face's alone.** ⚠ **This bullet named only the first two until 2026-08-26**, one milestone behind the array; the code was right and the doc was stale, which is the direction that matters least and rots fastest. **Read the array, not this sentence** — every new /home file that renders a surface goes in it, and `› %s is where the sweep expects it` is what stops a rename from reading as a broken suite instead of an unswept file. ⚠ **The SPA is a separate vitest project, but the sweep is a `readFileSync` from the repo root — no import, no alias, no second config** — so one list of forbidden recipes covers both trees.
- 🔓 **ONE SANCTIONED CONCAVE SURFACE ON /home, AND IT IS AN AMENDMENT RATHER THAN A HOLE (Samuel, 2026-09-01).** The Overview face's credit bar was first built as an APPROXIMATION of the billing surface's — a hand-rolled track with a raised fill — precisely because of the ruling above. Samuel's correction: **a design reference IS the spec; clone it exactly.** The bar is now `shared/ui/usage-meter.tsx › UsageMeter`, the same component `billing/components/billing-usage-pane.tsx › BillingUsagePane` renders for "MCP credits", and that component's whole recipe is a `.concave-track`. ⚠ **The sweep bans `UsageMeter` BY NAME across /home** — importing a component was a way to ship a concave surface past a check that only reads class strings — and records `overview-sections.tsx` as the sole exception, asserted to still USE it so the carve-out cannot outlive the bar. **The pair is excused, not the file:** every other forbidden recipe is still swept there.
  - 🔄 **THE REVERSAL LANDED, AND IT WENT TOWARD FLAT (Samuel, 2026-08-27, over a screenshot).** This bullet said "Knowledge reads INSET (`SectionBox`) and Agents reads FLAT under one selector; a reversal for visual parity is an ENGINEERING.md entry, not a styling choice" — it is now an ENGINEERING.md entry ("One ground for two faces"). **Both /home faces are one flat rectangle**: `shared/ui/section-panel.tsx › SectionPanel` — no border line, no header strip, no inset body, no resize grip — with the heading and the cards directly on the ground. `TemplatePanel` is now that component plus a ground, so neither face forks the shape.
  - 🔑 **THE GROUND IS STATED ONCE, IN CSS, FOR BOTH FACES**: `pages/home/home.module.css › .frame :global([data-section-panel])` paints `var(--home-panel)` — the fill the relationship list stands on — and clears the hairline. **Not a prop**, for two reasons: `SectionPanel` is shared and does not choose grounds, so a `tone="home"` on it would turn a per-mount decision into an enum one autocomplete away from every page; and a per-mount class lets one tab be re-tuned without the other. (⚠ This clause used to read "the /home-only palette". **Superseded 2026-08-30** — the four `home-*` values are the APP FRAME palette and the workspace shell consumes them; `docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md`. The rule is unchanged; only its reason was restated.) Same attribute-hook idiom as `[data-composer-panel]` directly above it in that file. ⚠ **Both halves or neither** — the attribute is inert without the rule and the rule is dead the moment the attribute is renamed, so `shared/ui/section-panel.test.tsx › the /home ground` reads the CSS and asserts the pair.
  - ✅ **THE BASE-DETAIL PANE IS IN NOW, AND THE DIVERGENCE IS CLOSED (Samuel's ruling 2026-08-28, over a screenshot of an OPENED base).** This bullet recorded a live divergence — `knowledge-v2/detail/overview-contents.tsx` rendering a `SectionBox` inside this same record pane — and said not to finish the job without asking. **Asked and answered: the whole opened-base surface was overhauled onto the flat language.** Contents and Details are both `SectionPanel` on `shared/ui/section-panel.tsx › SECTION_PANEL_GROUND`, so /home's one grounding rule reaches them exactly as it reaches the Knowledge face's two sections one level out, and the workspace knowledge page gets the same flat card the workspace Agents page wears. ⚠ **`SectionBox` is not deprecated by this** — it is still the pressed-in choice for a section read as a CONTAINER on a page surface (`docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md › Patterns`); what changed is that nothing inside /home's record pane is pressed in, at either level.
  - 🔑 **AND THE PANE IS ONE PANEL, NOT TWO (same ruling).** `knowledge-v2.tsx` renders `.page-float` — THE full-page surface, one per page — and /home mounted it INSIDE the record pane, so an opened base was a card on a card. **`knowledge-v2.tsx › Props.embedded` is the fence**: false by default (the workspace knowledge page keeps its float), passed by `pages/home/knowledge-base-view.tsx` alone. ⚠ **It is a PROP, not a scoped CSS override, because it is a structural fact**: no amount of repainting from `.frame` makes a nested page surface into one surface. Pinned as a COUNT WITH AN IDENTITY — `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/knowledge-base-view.test.tsx` asserts exactly one `.page-float` and that it is the page's own `<main>`, because a bare absence assertion would also pass the day /home lost its own surface.
- ⚠ **"AGENTS" NAMES TWO DIFFERENT SURFACES AND BOTH NAMES STAY (Samuel's ruling Q6, 2026-08-26).** The /home **Agents** face lists template IDENTITIES; the channel info column's **Agents** tab (`channels-v2/agents-tab.tsx`) lists RUNNING SESSIONS. §5's noun rule ("the noun on both is the AGENT") has tests behind it, so **renaming either needs Samuel's word** — the collision is RECORDED here and in both docblocks rather than resolved. ⚠ **"both docblocks" was a claim about ONE of them until 2026-08-26**: `pages/home/agent-panels.tsx` carried it from M2 and `channels-v2/agents-tab.tsx` said nothing about the other face at all, so the surface a reader is most likely to be sitting in when they hit the ambiguity was the one with no note. Both carry it now. **The two faces DO meet in one place and that is why the collision is confusing rather than harmless:** `agents-tab.tsx` mounts `TemplateLaunchPicker`, which reads the very container template list the /home face authors into.
- 🔒 **A HOME-WORKSPACE TEMPLATE CANNOT LAUNCH INTO A HOME CHANNEL, AND NO GRANT TABLE COULD FIX IT.** `getTemplateById` is workspace-filtered and `/resolve` passes the LAUNCH workspace, so a scope-C id resolved against a container 404s — a same-workspace trigger, not a permission the caller lacks. **Scope C is therefore a SHELF, not a launchable list**, and the section's caption says so. ✅ **THE ANSWER IS A CLIENT-COMPOSED COPY, AND IT IS BUILT (2026-08-26, M4, Samuel's ruling Q2): "Use in this channel" on every scope-C card.** `lib/template-draft.ts › containerCopyDraft` (PURE) + the existing `POST /api/agent-templates` against the CONTAINER's writes hook — **no new route, no new service, no server change**; the surface is `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/agent-copy.tsx`. ⚠ **The scope-C caption ("Yours alone. Use one here to make a copy in this channel.") shipped with M2 naming a control that did not exist yet; as of M4 that sentence is TRUE**, and the caption and the control must move together if either does.
  - 🔒 **THE ATTACHED KNOWLEDGE BASES ARE DROPPED, NOT CARRIED, AND THERE IS NO NAME-MATCH RE-ATTACH.** A home-workspace KB id is not in the container and the attach gate ("a KB the CALLER can currently read") would 404 it, so carrying the ids turns a copy into a failed write. **Re-attaching by NAME would be a second, weaker attach gate** — resolving by string where the server resolves by id and readability — and the first time two bases share a name it silently attaches the wrong one. The drop is stated to the operator in ONE line of the confirm step, and it names the count.
  - **IT IS A SNAPSHOT THAT DIVERGES: no FK, no back-pointer, no sync.** The two rows are strangers the moment the POST returns, and editing the original does not reach the copy. Same culture as `channel_sessions.template_name` — A DENORMALIZED SNAPSHOT, NOT AN FK, AND NOTHING MAY "FIX" THAT LATER; a `copiedFrom` column would make a container row point at a workspace its members cannot read. The confirm step says "snapshot", because *use* sounds like a reference and this is not one.
  - **`visibility` IS FORCED TO `private`, NEVER CARRIED**, and `teamIds` is cleared with it. A home template that happened to be `workspace`-visible would otherwise land SHARED WITH THE PEER on a gesture whose whole word is "use"; publishing into a shared container is a second, deliberate edit.
  - **THE NAME IS CARRIED UNCHANGED — NO "(copy)" SUFFIX.** Templates have **no name uniqueness, deliberately** (no unique index, no 409 on the route), so a suffix would be dodging a constraint that does not exist and renaming the operator's agent to do it.
  - **THE SCOPE PILL FOLLOWS THE COPY HOME.** The new row is a CONTAINER row while the operator is looking at the home shelf, so on success the pane points the pill back at "in this channel" — a successful write that left them on the other shelf reads as nothing having happened. From there the Chat face's `TemplateLaunchPicker` is one selector away, and the launched session's ROLE block names the copy (`channel_sessions.template_name`, rendered by `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-session-render.ts` and `channels-v2/agents-tab-cards.tsx`).
  - ⚠ **A SECOND CONTROL ON A CARD GOES INSIDE THE FACE, NOT OVER IT** (`components/template-section.tsx › TemplateCard`): a `<button>` may not contain a `<button>`, so a row carrying an action renders the face as a `div` whose BODY is the button. An absolutely-positioned control over a card-shaped button is invalid HTML that renders, which is the worst kind.
- 🔒 **A GUEST IS KEPT OUT BY THE SERVER FLOORS ALONE, AND THERE IS DELIBERATELY NO UI GATE (stated 2026-08-26; `docs/specs/home-agents-tab.plan.md`'s opening line claimed both halves and only ever had one).** `HOME_TABS` is unconditional and `pages/home/agent-panels.tsx › HomeAgentPanels` does not take boot's `role` — by design, and its docblock says so. What actually refuses a guest is `withWorkspaceAuth`: all three `/api/agent-templates` route files leave their GETs at the **`viewer` default** (`shared/auth/with-workspace-auth.ts › minRole`) and floor their writes at `member`, `guest` ranks below `viewer` (`workspaces/types.ts › ROLE_RANK`), and `guest-route-floor.test.ts › GUEST_ALLOWED` names none of them (Q3's 19, unmoved — re-count it, don't quote it). **A client gate here would be a second, weaker copy of a server rule**, which is the same argument this section makes for leaving the section-A editor open on a peer's row.
- 🔒 ✅ **THE AUDIENCE CEILING REACHES TEMPLATES, AND A "USE IN THIS CHANNEL" COPY IS READABLE BY THE OPERATOR'S OWN AGENTS — F-333 RESOLVED 2026-08-27 (Samuel: "option B", one change with F-336).** `server/service-shared.ts › canSeeTemplate` answers `workspace`-visibility rows to anyone, then — arm 2 — returns FALSE for everything else when the caller is a **SHARED credential** (`shared/auth/credential-audience.ts › isSharedCredential`), which is a credential with no single human behind it. **A container-locked SESSION is not that**: it carries the operator's user id, so arm 3 (creator) answers for it exactly as it answers for the operator at the keyboard, and `lib/template-draft.ts › containerCopyDraft`'s FORCED `private` (correct — "use" must not publish into a room the peer is standing in) stops being a wall the copy's own agent cannot see over.
  - ⚠ **IT DID NOT WORK BETWEEN B1 SHIPPING (2026-08-26) AND THAT RULING.** Arm 2 read `ctx.apiKeyWorkspaceId`, a field with **no producer** until §10's B1, so the locked arm had never fired anywhere; the moment every desktop-spawned session in a container WITH A PEER started running on such a credential, **every copy and every scope-B template became unlistable, unnameable and unresolvable by the agent it was made for.** The mechanism is the F-336 one exactly: a WORKSPACE fence read as a VISIBILITY fence.
  - 🔒 **NO PEER EXPOSURE WAS OPENED, AND THE WIDENING IS PER-PERSON.** The peer — and the peer's own container session — carries the PEER's user id, so arm 3 misses and arm 4 (`private` → nobody else, ADMINS INCLUDED) refuses.
