Pinned as a 3 × 7 grid in `server/service-visibility.test.ts`, whose two new caller rows are the operator's session and the peer's.
  - 🔒 **THERE IS NO GUEST ARM TO WEIGH, AND THAT IS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Every `agent-templates` route sits at `withWorkspaceAuth`'s default `viewer` floor (or `member` for writes) and so does `POST /api/channels/launch-directives`; `guest` ranks BELOW `viewer`, so a guest reaches no template surface at all.
  - 🔒 **THE OPERATOR'S OWN LAUNCH WAS NEVER AFFECTED, AND THE REASON IS THE TRANSPORT.** `main/template-resolve.js` rides `main/api.js › apiFetch`, which is **COOKIE-authed**, not MCP-token-authed — so `apiKeyWorkspaceId` is null on that lane and arm 2 is never reached. The AGENT lane is the one that moved: `channels/server/service-launch.ts › resolveTemplateForDirective` now carries the lock's KIND as well as the lock, or `POST /api/channels/launch-directives` still answers `AGENT_TEMPLATE_NOT_FOUND` for a private template.
- 🔒 **EVERY /home FACE RENDERED THROUGH `Crossfade` IS KEYED BY ITS TOKEN, AND THE TOKEN IS NOT THE KEY (2026-08-26, F-338/F-339 — §4A carries the paneToken half).** `shared/ui/crossfade.tsx › Crossfade` renders `{children(shownToken)}` **unkeyed**, so a face returned at the same position for every token is ONE React instance across a channel switch: props move, `useState` does not. On this face `scope`, `editing` and `copying` survived — and because the editor and the copy dialog take the target workspace as a PROP, a dialog held across the switch retargets at the NEW container and its **create SUCCEEDS there** (both containers are the caller's), while edit/delete 404 only AFTER an optimistic `upsertRow` has appended the foreign row. ⚠ **The switch need not be a click**: `selected` falls back to `visible[0]` whenever the selected row leaves `visible`. **A token is about the ANIMATION; a key is about the INSTANCE, and a pane holding per-channel state owes itself both.**
  - ⚠ **PIN IT ON SURVIVING STATE, NEVER ON RENDERED DATA.** A component's identity cannot be read off the DOM, and the existing data-level pane-token test stayed green through the whole defect. State can only survive if the instance did, so a reset pill and a torn-down dialog ARE the identity assertion.
  - ⚠ **AND `resolved` IS NOT "SETTLED".** `hooks/use-agent-templates.ts › resolved` is `data !== undefined`, so a FAILED read is unresolved forever. **Error is a THIRD state and must be modelled**, or it inherits the pending branch — and a pending branch is allowed to disable controls, which is how a missing error state becomes a TRAP rather than a blank: the scope-C failure painted nothing and held the scope pill `pointer-events-none`, so the operator could not leave the scope that had just failed. `agent-panel-cards.tsx › PrivateAgentSection` now has a `failure` state that OUTRANKS `pending`, prints the server's own wording, and offers a retry.

Why/history: none yet — this feature has no incident behind it. Record one in ENGINEERING.md when it earns it.

---

## 6. Consent — outbound review

> ⚠ **INBOUND CONSENT IS RETIRED (2026-08-22, Samuel: "remove all the stuff about declining and approving of threads").** Consent used to be symmetric. It is not any more, and the two halves must not be read as one rule.
> - **OUTBOUND SURVIVES UNCHANGED and is what this section is about:** the operator's own agent drafts a reply, and a human presses Send before it leaves the machine — unless the channel's durable AUTO-SEND setting is on.
> - **INBOUND IS GONE.** A peer's ask NOTIFIES the operator; the operator launches a session or does not. There is no row to decide, so there is nothing to Allow and nothing to Deny. `ConsentCreateSchema` accepts only `kind:"outbound"` (the union's inbound arm is DELETED, so an inbound create is an ordinary 400); the desktop's dialog, watcher and store are deleted; **`agent_trust_rules` is DROPPED** with the two `/api/channels/trust` routes, `trust-service.ts`, its repository reads and `revalidateAutoAllow` (`supabase/migrations/20260822140000_retire_inbound_consent_and_trust.sql`, WRITTEN — applied is a measurement, §12).
> - **STORED ROWS SPLIT TWO WAYS, deliberately.** DECIDED inbound rows are **KEPT** — they are the audit trail of decisions real humans made, `listConsentRequests(status='decided'|'all')` still returns them, and `requester_user_id` is still hydrated for them (a column something RENDERS is not dead). **PENDING inbound rows are EXPIRED by the migration**, because nothing can decide them any more and they would sit forever as prompts with no surface.
> - **`kind:"inbound"` and `status:"auto_allowed"` stay in the TypeScript unions as READ-ONLY history.** Readable, unwritable. Deleting either value would not delete the rows; it would make them fail to type.

> ⚠ **THE REVIEW SURFACE IS THE WORK-STREAM CARD, AND THE CONSENT INBOX IS DELETED (2026-08-25, Samuel).** *"The transcript card for a pending outbound post must say PENDING, not 'Posted to channel', and carry ONE button: Post."*
> - **THE GATE IS UNTOUCHED.** A held post is still a `pending` `channel_consent_requests` row, still needs a human, still CASes on `status='pending'`, still expires at `CONSENT_TTL_MS`. **Only WHERE the human answers moved.** A future reader must not conclude consent was relaxed — this is the same ruling shape as 2026-08-18's, one surface later.
> - **WHAT IS DELETED, not disarmed:** `channels-v2/inbox-pane.tsx` whole (`ChannelsV2InboxPane` + its `InboxRow`), the sidebar's Inbox nav row and its badge (`INBOX_NAV_ROW` in `channels-v2/fixtures.ts`, and `sidebar.tsx`'s `consentCount` / `inboxOpen` / `onOpenInbox` props), the selection state behind it (`inboxOpen` / `openInbox` in `use-channels-v2-selection.ts`), and the app nav's Channels badge with the read that fed it (`consentCount` in `shared/layout/app-shell/app-sidebar-core.tsx`, and the `useConsentInbox` call in `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/app-shell/app-shell.tsx`). ⚠ **THE SHELL NO LONGER READS CONSENT AT ALL**, so it no longer mounts a workspace-wide realtime subscription on every page of the app.
> - ⚠ **WHY: A SOLO `/home` CHANNEL COULD NEVER REACH THE INBOX.** `/home`'s relationship record mounts `channel-surface-standalone.tsx`, which has no channel tree and no app-shell nav — so a draft its agent held had **no surface anywhere** that could post it, and sat pending until the TTL. The card is inside the work stream both hosts already render, which is why it fixes both surfaces at once rather than adding a second one.
> - **THE CARD'S THREE FACES** (`channels-v2/agent-stream.tsx › SentToChannelBox`): **"Pending" + a Post button** while a live row matches · **"Not sent"** with NOTHING to press when that row is past its own TTL · **"Posted to channel" / "Sent to <thread>"** once the words are IN the channel. ⚠ **THE OLD BOX HAD BEEN LYING SINCE IT SHIPPED** — the `post` narration frame is pushed when the agent CALLS the tool, before the consent row is even created, so a held draft was painted as words the counterparty already had. The file's own docblock said a pending face was dropped because "this surface only ever renders posts that ALREADY EXIST in the transcript"; that was never true.
> - 🔒 **"NOT SENT" IS THE NARROWEST FACE, NOT THE FALLBACK — AND THIS BULLET SAID THE OPPOSITE FOR ONE DAY (corrected 2026-08-25, Samuel saw it over a delivered post).** It read *"'Not sent' once no row does"*, so ABSENCE was rendered as failure — and absence is also what the seconds between a Post and delivery look like, and what a row decided on another surface looks like. **An operator told "Not sent" about a post that went out sends it again**, which is the one direction this gate exists to prevent. The rule now: **failure needs a DEAD ROW** (`expiresAt` elapsed); everything else this card cannot explain is `Pending` with no button, because *"I do not know yet"* and *"it failed"* are different facts.
>   - ⚠ **THE EXPIRY ARITHMETIC IS THE CLIENT'S BECAUSE THE SERVER DOES NOT SWEEP.** `listConsentRequests` is deliberately unswept (below), so an elapsed row still returns `status:"pending"` — trusting that status puts a live Post button on a row `PATCH /consent/[id]` will refuse.
> - 🔒 **THE LANDING CHECK READS THE WHOLE CHANNEL TRANSCRIPT, NEVER THE AGENT-SCOPED LANE.** `buildAgentStream` takes `sent` (agent-scoped, F-251 — what RENDERS as a posted box) and `delivered` (unfiltered — what proves a draft went out) as **two separate inputs, and collapsing them reintroduces the 2026-08-25 defect.** `agent-panel.tsx › agentSentMessages` requires `metadata.taskId`, and **a channel-level post carries none** (measured: `{intent, runtime, summary, session_id}`), so on a solo `/home` container the agent-scoped lane can never see the delivered row — REFACTOR-FINDINGS **F-311**. A frame whose words landed but which that lane cannot attribute renders as the ordinary POSTED face rather than being dropped, or the operator watches the post they just authorized vanish.
> - **NO LOCAL STATE ANYWHERE IN THE CARD'S LIFECYCLE, and that is a rule rather than an outcome.** A first cut tracked pressed cards in a `useState` Set keyed on the stream row; it could not survive a remount and could not see a Post pressed on the OTHER agent surface. **Every input is a server fact** — the transcript, the consent row, the frame — so the same facts always paint the same card, whichever surface approved and however many times the component has remounted.
> - **ONE VERB, AND IT IS `Post`.** There is no Deny on this card and one must not be added — the only other exit is the row's 24h expiry, which the "Not sent" face reports. ⚠ **A `deny` DECISION IS STILL A SERVER CAPABILITY** and `PATCH /consent/[id]` still accepts it: `thread-consent.tsx › ThreadSendBox` (the thread view's send box) is the surviving Cancel, and the desktop cancels its own row when a park closes the tool call (`session-windowless.js › watchRow`). **The endpoint is deliberately wider than the card.**
> - ⚠ **THE JOIN IS ON THE DRAFT'S BODY, BECAUSE THERE IS NO ID TO JOIN ON.** A consent row carries `(channel, message_seq)` and nothing naming an AGENT; the narration frame carries the text and no row id (the row is created later, by `bridgeOutbound`, long after the frame was pushed). `channels-v2/agent-stream-model.ts › postEcho` normalizes both sides the way `main/session-narration.js`'s `POST_CAP` line already normalized the frame. **The frame is already agent-scoped** (the ring belongs to one session), so the only ambiguity is between two identical drafts — where either row approves the same bytes. Filed as REFACTOR-FINDINGS F-310.
> - **THE GATE NOTE IS NOT A SECOND SURFACE.** `main/session-narration.js › entryFor` emits **NO** `Waiting for permission` status line for an outbound POST gate (`payload.type === 'outbound_gate'`) — the `post` frame beside it already carries `pending` and the card says so in the operator's own words. ⚠ **THE RING IS APPEND-ONLY, so a transient line written into it is permanent**: that note sat under a delivered post until the session ended. `agent-stream-model.ts › dropSettledGateNotes` retires the ones already written, guarded so a main that does not stamp gates at all keeps its note — on such a build it is the only thing explaining the silence. **The DOCK's tool gate still emits it**; it has no card.
>   - ⚠ **A CREATE_THREAD GATE IS THE EXCEPTION, BECAUSE IT HAS NO POST FRAME** (2026-08-25, F-321-adjacent). An own-channel `create_thread` joined the outbound consent shape (§4A / Samuel's 2026-08-24 ruling) so it raises the same `outbound_gate`, but `sdkRenderEvents` renders it as a plain `tool_use` — there is no `outbound_post` frame to carry `pending`. On a windowless session that left a `dopl_channel` tool row and then SILENCE to the 24h TTL, over a consent row `bridgeOutbound` really created. So `session-io.js` stamps `threadOpen: true` on exactly the create_thread gate, and `entryFor` mints the pending sent-lane frame the post gets for free — `text` is `input.body` on both arms, so it reconciles against the consent row through `postEcho` like any post. A POST gate carries no `threadOpen` and still emits no line. Pinned in `test/session-io-grant.test.mjs` and `test/session-narration.test.mjs`.
> - **`pending` IS A NEW FIELD ON THE `post` FRAME** (`main/session-narration.js › entryFor`, `shared/lib/spa-bridge-shapes.ts › DesktopNarrationEntry.pending`), stamped from `session-io.js`'s `willGatePost`. ⚠ **ABSENT MEANS "NOT GATED", NOT "UNKNOWN"** — an older main emits a `post` frame only for a post it was free to send. ⚠ **IT NEVER CLEARS**: main writes the ring entry once, so the SERVER's transcript row is what retires a pending card, and pressing Post keeps the card on "Pending" (button gone) until that row arrives rather than flashing "Not sent" through the gap.

- **Consent is a server-side ROW, not an in-process promise**, so the gate survives the machine that raised it. The same request is answerable on **either** the web surface or the desktop notification — **first answer wins**.
- ⚠ **THE DESKTOP HALF OF THE RETIREMENT, IN FULL (2026-08-22).** THREE main-process modules are DELETED, not disarmed — `dopl-desktop-app/main/consent-watcher.js` (the off-loop poll that watched an inbound row and dispatched the spawn / decline / expiry resolvers), `› consent-store.js` (its electron-store durability: the watched map and the settled-key TTL) and `› consent-cadence.js` (its adaptive scan ladder). `dopl-desktop-app/test/removed-vocabulary.test.mjs` refuses all three by name, and `RETIRED_STORE_KEYS` there refuses a READER for `pendingConsent` / `channelWatched` / `channelSettled`, which are still on shipped machines' disks. **`channelSettled` matters most:** a reader would suppress asks whose "settlement" was recorded by a lane that no longer exists.
  - **WHAT REPLACES IT IS A NOTIFICATION WITH TWO VERBS AND NO DECISION** (`main/trigger.js › notifyAsk`): **Launch agent** spawns the responder directly with the triggering message (byte-for-byte the old approved path, minus the row), the BODY CLICK opens the thread, and **Dismiss decides nothing**. It always decided nothing — `close` fires on system auto-dismiss, banner style and Focus mode alike — but it used to leave a row pending on another surface. **The blast-radius line survived the deletion** (where the agent will run, under which tool profile, plus `tool-profiles.js › profileHint`) and is the reason `notifyInbound`'s copy was moved rather than dropped.
  - **THE TRAY "Pending: N" ITEM IS GONE** with `tray.js › setPendingCount` and `index.js`'s `onPending` handler: the count's ONE writer was the watcher, so it had no producer left. **`main/notify-action.js` is a new §2 split** holding the banner SHAPE (one action button, a navigating body click, a Dismiss that decides nothing) — shared by `consent.js › notifyOutbound`, which is about a row, and `trigger.js`, which is not.
  - ⚠ **NOTHING ON THE DESKTOP READS `auto_allowed` ANY MORE.** the deleted watcher's `isHumanAllow` (`String(status) === 'allowed'`) was the one statement of `allowed` vs `auto_allowed` and went with its module; the only surviving read of that status is `session-windowless.js › watchRow`, on an **outbound** row, where any terminal that is not an allow is a deny.
- ⚠ **THE SURFACE CHANGED; THE MODEL DID NOT (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 8).** `components/consent-card.tsx` is DELETED and `components/launch-panel.tsx › LaunchPanel` renders the decision. ⚠ **IT HAS EXACTLY ONE CONSUMER, `components/channels-v2/thread-consent.tsx › ThreadSendBox`** (re-measured 2026-08-20: `grep -rn LaunchPanel src/` returns the DEFINITION and that ONE import — **there is no `launch-panel.test.tsx`; an earlier draft of this parenthesis counted a third hit as "the test" and no such file has ever existed in this tree.** The arrival pop-up that briefly made two consumers is deleted, and the Inbox — which DOES decide, §11 — renders its own buttons rather than this panel). This bullet said "both places that render one (the shipping `channel-pane.tsx` and …)" until then — **`channel-pane.tsx` was deleted at the Phase 12 cutover** (§5), so the sentence named a second surface that does not exist and implied a parity constraint there is nothing to keep parity with. The addressee now reads **Launch agent**, not Allow, and a first click expands the panel into the launch settings before a second decides. **Everything in the rest of this section is untouched by that change** — the same row, the same 24h TTL, the same CAS on `status = 'pending'`, the same `(operator, channel, kind, message_seq)` de-dupe key, the same `sessionOnly` gate on `PATCH /consent/[id]`. (⚠ It also said "the same `revalidateAutoAllow` on the way out" — that function is DELETED 2026-08-22 with the inbound lane; see the block at the top of this section.) **A future reader must not conclude consent was replaced, relaxed, or moved into the client: only the word on the button and the placement of two desktop-only controls changed.**
- ⚠ **THE LAUNCH SETTINGS ARE GONE, AND SO IS THE ARM THEY SET (2026-08-20, Samuel's ruling; F-233).** `LaunchPanel` expanded into the two permission axes plus the working folder, chosen before Allow. **That disclosure had not rendered since this section's own rewrite on 2026-08-18** — the panel's one consumer is the OUTBOUND send box, so its `isInbound` branch was unreachable and nobody noticed for two days. `LaunchSettings`, `LAUNCH_SETTINGS_HEADING`, `permission-preset-row.tsx › RequestPermissionRow` and the whole of `request-folder-row.tsx` are deleted; `LaunchPanel` is now what it always painted — an outbound send box, and nothing else. **The DURABLE launch posture and the per-channel working folder both survive on the Settings tab** (`channels-v2/settings-agent.tsx`) and are a different record with a different consumer — §11. **No third permission axis exists**; `bypass` is a VALUE on the tools axis.
- ⚠ **TRUST IS DELETED, NOT ON HOLD (2026-08-22).** This bullet used to say "auto-launch with saved settings for this person" was the shape trust would take and was **ON HOLD** by Samuel's ruling, with `POST` / `DELETE /trust` left `sessionOnly` and unreferenced — "the seam is left open and empty on purpose". The seam is now CLOSED: standing consent only ever auto-allowed an INBOUND request, and there is no inbound request. ⚠ **It never fired** — measured zero `auto_allowed` rows in `channel_consent_requests` history before the drop (re-measure, never quote: `SELECT count(*) FROM channel_consent_requests WHERE status='auto_allowed';`). ⚠ **`write-gate-coverage.test.ts`'s `sessionOnly` set lost `channels/trust/route.ts`, and NARROWING THAT SET IS EXACTLY WHAT THAT FILE CATCHES** — it is safe only because the route is GONE rather than ungated, and the test says so in place.
- **APPROVE-OUT, AND ONLY APPROVE-OUT.** `outbound` = the operator's own agent drafted a reply; a human must Send **before it leaves the machine** — ⚠ **UNLESS the channel's durable AUTO-SEND setting is on (2026-08-20, Samuel):** `main/channel-prefs.js › getAutoSend` (default OFF) maps the session's message axis to `auto_both`, and the reply posts itself. Auto-send widens MESSAGES only, never tools (`session-profiles.js › grantDecision` returns off Axis B before Axis A is consulted). ⚠ This bullet read **"Approve-in AND approve-out"** and led with `inbound` = a teammate's agent addressed the operator, who had to Allow/Deny before their machine spawned. That half is retired — see the block at the top of this section.
- 🔒 **A CONSENT READ IS SCOPED BY `(operator, workspace)`. OPERATOR ALONE IS NOT ENOUGH.** A row carries `operator_user_id` and nothing else identifying whose workspace raised it, so an operator-only filter returns rows from EVERY workspace they belong to. **Both columns, every read, no exceptions.** **RLS is not a backstop here**: the read runs as service role.
  - The type-level half is NARROWER than it sounds: `workspaceId` is a required field on `ConsentListOpts`, which is a **file-local, non-exported interface in `channels/server/repository-collab.ts`**. It binds callers of that one repository function — it is not a project-wide type that makes every consent read fail to compile. **The rule is the discipline; the type only fences the one door it sits on.**
- ⚠ **`revalidateAutoAllow` IS DELETED (2026-08-22), AND IT IS NOT A RELAXATION.** It re-derived the standing trust rule on every consume-time read and CAS'd a revoked row to `expired`, so old desktops failed closed — the whole reason it lived on the server read path. `auto_allowed` had exactly ONE writer (the trust branch of `createConsentRequest`) and trust was INBOUND-ONLY, so with `agent_trust_rules` dropped **no row of that status can be born and none exists**. A guard kept alive over a shape with no producer teaches the next reader that the producer is still there.
  - **`listConsentRequests` IS STILL DELIBERATELY UNSWEPT** and is now the whole audit surface: it is the only way to read back the decided inbound rows the retirement preserved. Sweeping it would rewrite settled history.
  - ⚠ **THE HTTP-LAYER-ONLY WARNING SURVIVES IN SPIRIT:** realtime and PostgREST hand out the RAW row, and both current consumers are invalidation-only by contract. **A future optimization that reads the subscription payload instead of refetching is still the thing not to do.**

- **Decisions are compare-and-swap on `status = 'pending'`.** Explicitly multi-writer: the pre-read is authorization-only, and a losing writer gets 409 re-read so the error names the status that won. **A human's Deny must never be clobbered by a late Allow.** The desktop treats its own 409 as settled, not a failure — no retry.
- **De-dupe key is `(operator, channel, kind, message_seq)` at ANY status**, deliberately: a denied trigger must never be re-raisable. `operator_user_id` is IN the key because every recipient of one message raises their OWN request.
  - ⚠ **The unique index is PARTIAL — `WHERE message_seq IS NOT NULL`** (`channel_consent_requests_trigger_key`, `20260726140000_channels_consent_hardening.sql`). **A row with a NULL `message_seq` is UNCONSTRAINED**: the database will happily store two, and the "denied cannot be re-raised" guarantee simply does not cover them. Deliberate — a request raised without a triggering seq has no trigger identity to de-dupe ON — but it means **any new consent path that can raise a seq-less request is raising one the DB will not protect.** Give it a seq, or de-dupe it yourself.
- ⚠ **LAUNCH-OVER-MCP HAS ITS OWN CONSENT, AND IT IS NOT A CONSENT ROW (2026-08-22, Samuel: approved, with a LOCAL DESKTOP TOGGLE as the consent).** `dopl_channel(op="launch_agent")` files a `channel_launch_directives` row; the operator's desktop claims it and decides. **The gate is a setting on the machine that would run the agent**, and a machine with it off refuses with the word `no-bridge`. The MCP result states it as a DECISION, never as a fault or as something to work around.
  - ⚠ **THE TRADE IS STATED RATHER THAN BURIED: THE SERVER CANNOT VERIFY THE TOGGLE, SO THE SERVER IS NOT THE GATE.** The desktop is, and it is the only party that can be — there is no server-side proof that a given machine's owner consented. What the server enforces instead is SCOPE: `operator_user_id` is always `ctx.userId`, so an agent can only ever ask its OWN operator's machine.
  - 🔒 **`POST /api/channels/launch-directives/claim` AND `/decide` ARE DELIBERATELY **NOT** `sessionOnly`, AND THE REASON IS THE INVERSE OF `PATCH /consent/[id]`'s.** The caller of those two IS a device token — the desktop main process, which has no cookie and no browser — so gating them to a session would not harden the lane, it would delete it. There is no human decision to bypass: the operator's consent is the toggle, expressed by the machine refusing. **The bound is SCOPE, in the SQL predicate**: every claim and decide matches `operator_user_id = ctx.userId`, so the worst a misused device token can do is claim its own operator's directives. It cannot enumerate (a foreign id answers 404, indistinguishable from absent) and cannot start anything (claiming is a status flip; the launch happens on the machine). **Do not "fix" these onto the `sessionOnly` list** — `write-gate-coverage.test.ts` would then be pinning a set that breaks the feature.
  - **THE CLAIM IS A CAS, and it is the multi-machine correctness mechanism, not an optimization.** One operator may be signed in on several desktops; all see the same INSERT frame and all try. `UPDATE … WHERE id AND status='pending' AND operator_user_id RETURNING` means exactly one wins; the losers get zero rows and **must read that as "stand down", never as an error**. A DECISION is a CAS too (`status IN ('pending','claimed')`), so a retried decide cannot flip a `launched` into a `refused` after the requester has read the first outcome.
  - ⚠ **THE MAILBOX CARRIES THREE VERBS SINCE 2026-09-01 AND ONLY ONE IS BEHIND THE TOGGLE (Samuel: *"I need you to build out dopl mcp being able to end agents. Dopl MCP need to be able to do all that stuff"*).** `channel_launch_directives` gained a `kind` column (`launch` | `end` | `rename`, DEFAULT `launch`) plus `target_agent_id` and `target_name`, so `dopl_channel(op="end_agent")` and `op="rename_agent"` ride the SAME lane an external agent already uses to launch. **`launch` stays gated by `getOrchestratorLaunch`; `end` and `rename` are NOT gated at all**, and the argument is `main/agent-self-ops.js`'s own, already load-bearing for the in-process twins of those two verbs: a STOP verb and a DISPLAY verb widen nothing — neither can start a query, wake a shell, grant a tool or post — and **the toggle exists to gate LOCAL COMPUTE BEING SPENT**, which neither spends. ⚠ **THE OWN-OPERATOR BOUND IS UNCHANGED AND IS STILL THE WHOLE FENCE**: `operator_user_id` is `ctx.userId`, so only the caller's own machines ever claim one. ⚠ **THE VISIBLE CONSEQUENCE, so nobody re-derives it as a bug: `main/launch-directives.js › refresh` now binds realtime on `armed` rather than on the toggle**, because a machine that saw nothing could not answer the two kinds that need no consent. That widened a READ of the operator's own rows and nothing this machine DOES — a launch with the toggle off is still claimed by nobody and spawns nothing, pinned in `test/launch-directive-agent-ops.test.mjs`.
    - ⚠ **THE REFUSAL VOCABULARY WENT TO NINE IN THE SAME WAVE** — `no-session` (no live session of this operator's carries that agent id; **the ORDINARY answer on an end, and usually an agent that already finished** rather than a fault) and `bad-name` (the machine's own `sanitizeName` refused the string). Both have a producer in `main/directive-agent-ops.js` and **the column CHECK lands in the same migration**, which is the 2026-08-22 sequencing lesson applied rather than repeated.
    - ⚠ **THE STATUS VOCABULARY GAINED `done`, AND `launched` IS DELIBERATELY NOT REUSED.** The row is read back by the orchestrator that filed it and rendered into an agent-facing sentence; "launched" on the record of an agent being STOPPED is the one kind of wrong nothing downstream can detect. The CHECK pairs `launched` with `kind='launch'` and `done` with the other two, so no reader has to ask which meaning it is looking at.
    - 🔒 **A SECOND, WEAKER, EARLIER REFUSAL EXISTS AND MUST NOT BE MISTAKEN FOR THE FENCE.** `server/repository-agent-owner.ts › agentInstanceOwner` refuses a target `channel_sessions` reports as ANOTHER member's (403 `CHANNEL_AGENT_FOREIGN` — the one 403 on this lane, argued in the error's docblock: the caller already proved channel membership, inside which the roster and the live agent set are readable anyway). ⚠ **IT REFUSES ONLY ON A POSITIVE FACT AND PASSES ON SILENCE**, because that table is a PROJECTION the desktop pushes: an absent row means nobody reported, never that no such agent exists, and an agent launched seconds ago is routinely in exactly that state. **Hardening it into a refusal-on-absence would break every end of an unreported agent to enforce a check that was never the fence.**
  - **EXPIRY IS LAZY AND THERE IS NO CRON** (`LAUNCH_DIRECTIVE_TTL_MS` = 120s). A non-terminal row past its TTL is REPORTED as `expired` by `service-launch.ts › toDirective`, so the stored `status` and the reported one may disagree — deliberately, because a sweep would be a scheduled job whose only output is cosmetic. ⚠ **An EXPIRED directive may still be DECIDED**: if a machine really started an agent, `launched` is the truthful record however late, and refusing the write would leave a running agent no directive accounts for.
- **Human-only gate (`sessionOnly`): `PATCH /consent/[id]`, and it is now the only one.** The desktop passes a 90-day device token to every spawned agent and it has Bash — ungated, a session could read that token off disk and approve its own outbound review, sending its reply with no human. **`GET` stays open: polling a status decides nothing.** ⚠ `POST /trust` and `DELETE /trust` stood beside it (standing consent retires a gate permanently, so it was worse than any single Allow; DELETE was gated for the mirror reason) and are **deleted with the routes**.

- **The operator is ALWAYS `ctx.userId`, never read from the body.** `requireOperatorRow` collapses missing / foreign-operator / cross-workspace into ONE 404 so ids cannot be probed.
- **Constants** (`features/channels/constants.ts`, verified 2026-08-11): `CONSENT_TTL_MS` = 24h, `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS` = 90s, `UNRESOLVED_TOOL_PROFILE` = `"read_only"`. Expiry is lazy — no cron, no realtime event.
- **A null/unresolved tool profile falls back to `read_only`, matching the desktop.** Do not "fix" it to `full` because the column defaults to `full`: null means *this DTO does not know*, and "Full access" over a session the machine will run `read_only` is a lie in the direction that makes an operator relax.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §8 (v1.2, v1.4; F-174, F-188).

---

## 7. Realtime

- **The publication list is an INVARIANT, not a description.** A published table with no subscriber costs WAL decode plus a per-subscription RLS evaluation on every write, forever. A subscriber on an unpublished table is WORSE than an error — Realtime accepts the binding, reports SUBSCRIBED, and delivers nothing, so the surface silently stops updating.
- 🔒 **THE SECOND-PERSON TEST — "a doorbell only where a SECOND PERSON watches the same screen" (Samuel, 2026-08-28).** This is the rule the cost invariant above serves, and it decides every new subscription: **subscribe when another party can change what this viewer is looking at while they are looking at it; REFETCH otherwise.** A surface where every write is the viewer's own has no peer event to deliver, so a feed there buys WAL decode and a per-subscription RLS evaluation per write and delivers a signal nothing is waiting for — the mutation the client already awaited is the notification.
  - ⚠ **WRITTEN DOWN HERE ON 2026-08-30, AND IT HAD NEVER BEEN WRITTEN DOWN ANYWHERE.** `grep -rn -i "second person\|same screen\|doorbell only" docs/` returned nothing on this subject; the rule existed only as a session ruling, while this section carried it as a worked EXAMPLE (`channel_mention_reads`: *"every row is written by the viewer's own client about the viewer's own reading, so there is no peer event to deliver"*) and as the cost sentence. An example is not a rule, and the four surfaces below are what a missing rule cost.
  - **WHERE IT SAYS YES, measured 2026-08-30:** the channels doorbell (`channels-v2/live.ts › useChannelsV2Live`), `agent_presence` (`channels/client/realtime.ts › usePresenceRealtime`), the consent inbox (`channels/hooks/use-consent-inbox.ts › useConsentInbox`, cross-PROCESS rather than cross-person), the desktop's counterparty delivery (`dopl-desktop-app/main/realtime.js › addChannel`) and the ref-counted shared registry that carries them (`src/shared/realtime/shared-channel-registry.ts › subscribeSharedWorkspaceTables`).
  - **WHERE IT SAYS NO AND A SUBSCRIPTION EXISTS ANYWAY:** `skills/client/realtime.ts › useSkillsRealtime`, `ontology/client/realtime.ts › useOntologyRealtime` and `chats/client/realtime.ts › useChatsRealtime` — single-author surfaces with no peer story, no presence and no conflict UI. `knowledge/client/realtime.ts › useKnowledgeRealtime` is the split case (a KB can have two editors) and is an ASK, not a port. **12 of `SYNC_TABLES`' 17 entries exist only for those four hooks**, so the answer decides the inventory below as well as the client.
  - **THE CHEAP ANSWER, when a surface wants liveness and fails the test: ride a doorbell that is ALREADY PAID FOR.** `channel_sessions` stayed unpublished and its peer cards were bought off `channel_messages` (below) — zero new publication cost, zero new subscriber. Reach for that before reaching for a binding.
  - Re-derive rather than quoting the lists: `grep -rn "subscribeSharedWorkspaceTables\|postgres_changes" src apps packages dopl-desktop-app`.
- ⚠ **19 SINCE 2026-08-31 — `channel_agent_directions` JOINED, on the launch mailbox's exact terms** (`20260903120000`): INSERT **and** UPDATE published, `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX (workspace_id, id)`, no DELETE path so no DELETE doorbell, and **NOT** in `ui-sync-core.js › SYNC_TABLES` — the web has no direction surface and its one subscriber is `main/agent-directions.js` on its own binding. ⚠ Its flag and handler live in `main/realtime-mailboxes.js` (the §1 split), whose flags are PER-MAILBOX because the two lanes have separate operator consents.
- ⚠ **18 SINCE 2026-08-22 — `channel_launch_directives` JOINED, and it is the FIRST channels child published for its UPDATE frames as well as its INSERTs.** The desktop needs both: several of one operator's machines may be signed in, and the losers of the claim CAS must SEE the row leave `pending` instead of spending a round trip to discover it. Replica identity is `USING INDEX (workspace_id, id)` (`20260822160000`, per `20260822130000`'s precedent) — under DEFAULT the subscribers' `workspace_id=eq.<id>` filter drops every UPDATE frame. **No DELETE path, so no DELETE doorbell.** ⚠ **It is NOT in `ui-sync-core.js › SYNC_TABLES`, deliberately**: the web has no launch surface, and its subscriber is `dopl-desktop-app/main/launch-directives.js` on its own binding. **So the two counts below are about different things** — 17 is the SYNC_TABLES inventory, 18 is the publication.
- **17 tables, measured 2026-08-11** against `dopl-desktop-app/main/ui-sync-core.js › SYNC_TABLES`: `knowledge_bases`, `knowledge_folders`, `knowledge_entries`, `skills`, `skill_versions`, `ontology_clusters`, `ontology_objects`, `ontology_memberships`, `ontology_relationships`, `chats`, `chat_messages`, `chat_folders`, `channel_consent_requests`, `channels`, `channel_members`, `channel_messages`, `agent_presence`. **The DB side is deploy state — verify with `supabase db reset` or `grep -rn "ALTER PUBLICATION" supabase/migrations/`.**
- **`test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` pins BOTH directions** against `supabase/migrations` and `SYNC_TABLES`. **Publication changes and subscriber changes MUST ship in the same release.**
- ⚠ **`channel_links` AND `channel_link_claims` ARE DELIBERATELY UNPUBLISHED AND UNBOUND** (home channels, §4A). Both migrations assert their own absence from `supabase_realtime` in a `DO $$` block, and `test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` pins the never-published pair from the other side. **The home surface REFETCHES on write**: the pending-invite chip rides `GET /api/home/channels` as each channel's `linkOut`, and every mutation that can move it — mint, revoke, claim — is a request the client already awaits, so it invalidates the read it just changed. A feed would buy WAL decode plus a per-subscription RLS evaluation per write and deliver a signal nothing waits for. ⚠ **`channel_links.workspace_id` is NULLABLE by design** (NULL = a legacy unbound link), so the row could not satisfy this feed's workspace-scoping contract even if it were published.
- ⚠ **`channel_sessions` STAYS UNPUBLISHED, AND 2026-08-20 IS WHEN THAT WAS RE-DECIDED RATHER THAN INHERITED.** The Agents tab's PEER cards read it over HTTP on `use-agents-panel.ts › PEER_SESSIONS_POLL_MS` (30 s), which looked like an obvious candidate for a doorbell. It is not: the row is rewritten on **every projection move** (`session-state-push.js` subscribes to `session-summary.js › subscribe`, which fires per state change — many per turn, per session), so publishing it buys WAL decode plus a per-subscription RLS evaluation on each of those, for every member of the workspace. That is the cost the first bullet of this section refuses, and the table's own creating migration (`20260805120000_channel_sessions.sql`) already said so.
  - **The liveness was bought from a doorbell that was ALREADY PAID FOR instead.** A peer agent that does anything visible POSTS, so `channel-surface-data.ts › refetchAll` now also calls `agentsPanel.refetch()` — the peer cards ride the `channel_messages` doorbell, and the 30 s poll drops back to the idle backstop. **Zero new publication cost, zero new subscriber.** ⚠ `channel_sessions` is at `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT` and would need an identity carrying `workspace_id` before it could ever be published (the second half of the publication invariant, below) — another reason the cheap answer was the right one.
- ⚠ **A DOORBELL INVALIDATES A PREFIX; IT DOES NOT REFETCH ONE OBSERVER.** `refetchAll`'s channels-list entry calls `queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: channelKeys.list().all })`, not `refetchChannels()`. `query.refetch()` revalidates only the key-variant that is MOUNTED, so any other variant of the same resource (`include=archived` is the one the channel list has) stays stale behind a doorbell that fired for it — `client/query-keys.ts` designs the WRITE path around prefix invalidation for exactly this reason and the realtime path had not followed. Corrected 2026-08-20; the other four entries are per-channel keys with one variant each, so they stay `refetch`.
- 🔒 **A LAUNCH DIRECTIVE IS NOT A MESSAGE, AND IT STAYS OFF `channel_messages` FOR TWO INDEPENDENT REASONS (2026-08-22).** `channel_launch_directives` is the mailbox `dopl_channel(op="launch_agent")` writes and the operator's desktop reads. It LOOKS like a message — author, channel, a body-ish `goal`, a recipient who acts on it — and it is deliberately not one:
  1. **THE LOOP BRAKE.** An AGENT-authored message addressed to a member TRIGGERS that member's listener. A launch request is agent-authored and its whole purpose is to start an agent, so as a message it would be a self-feeding cycle with a channel post at every hop and a consent decision per turn. **The brake exists precisely to stop an agent waking another by talking, and a launch payload is the one thing that must not be able to route through that door.** A separate table cannot trigger a listener, cannot be awaited, and cannot fan out.
  2. **TRANSCRIPT PURITY.** `channel_messages` is what BOTH members read and is the record of what was said between two people. A directive is not addressed to the counterparty, is refused or expired more often than not, and would leak the operator's ORCHESTRATION — how many agents they tried to start, on what model, how often their machine said no — into a room the counterparty can read. The table is OWNER-ONLY (`operator_user_id = auth.uid()`) for that reason.
  - ⚠ **THE CONSEQUENCE, so nobody re-derives it as a bug: a directive has NO `seq` and can never end an `await`.** The requesting agent holds on the ROW (bounded, in the MCP op) or finds the result in `read_sessions` later. That is the intended shape, and it is why `launch_agent` polls instead of telling the agent to arm a wait.
  - **PUBLISHED FOR INSERT *AND* UPDATE**, unlike every other channels child: the desktop needs the INSERT (a directive to decide) and the UPDATE frames too, because several of one operator's machines may be signed in and the losers of the claim CAS must see the row leave `pending` rather than spend a round trip discovering it. Replica identity `USING INDEX (workspace_id, id)` per `20260822130000`'s precedent. **No DELETE path and therefore no DELETE doorbell** — rows are kept as the record of what was asked and what the machine said.
- **A NEW CHANNELS TABLE IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A PUBLISHED ONE, and the decision is written down where the table is created.** `channel_mention_reads` (`20260818140000`, the Tags inbox's read-state) is the worked example of the NO answer: every row is written by the viewer's own client about the viewer's own reading, so there is no peer event to deliver, and the content that DOES arrive from a peer (the mention itself) is a `channel_messages` row riding a doorbell that is already paid for. Same shape as `channel_tasks` staying out. **The test to satisfy is the pinned inventory, so publishing a table you have no subscriber for fails on purpose.**
- ⚠ **THE BUNDLED SPA IS NOT REALTIME-DARK — it rides the ui-sync DOORBELL, and every feature hook lights up unchanged.** `shared/realtime/shared-channel-registry.ts › subscribeSharedWorkspaceTables` takes `› subscribeViaBridge` whenever the bridge exposes `onSyncEvent` + `syncWatch`; `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/app-preload.js` exposes both (pinned by `test/preload-parity.test.mjs › APP_OPS`). **The branch is CAPABILITY-KEYED, never a truthiness check on `window.dopl`** — the legacy wrapper's partial global must keep the WEB path, and a truthiness check there once killed realtime for every wrapper user. The ONLY dark case is an older main missing those two ops, which degrades to a no-op unsubscribe rather than opening a socket the renderer cannot reach (CSP `connect-src 'none'`). **Channels included:** `channels`, `channel_members`, `channel_messages`, `channel_consent_requests` and `agent_presence` are all in `SYNC_TABLES` — verified 2026-08-18 against `dopl-desktop-app/main/ui-sync-core.js › SYNC_TABLES` and `dopl-desktop-app/test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs`, which now names those five individually. Three SPA/desktop comments claimed the opposite for fifteen days (F-199, F-200); **this bullet, not a page docblock, is the statement of record.**
- 🔒 ⚠ **A SUBSCRIBED TABLE'S SELECT POLICY MUST ADMIT EVERY ROLE THAT SUBSCRIBES — AND FOR TWO WEEKS ONE DID NOT (2026-08-26).** The web client's `postgres_changes` binding is a USER client (`shared/realtime/shared-channel-registry.ts` → `getSupabaseBrowser()`), so **RLS filters the feed** — unlike every channel READ, which runs service-role and bypasses RLS entirely. All the channel-family SELECT policies gated on `is_current_workspace_member(workspace_id,'viewer')`, and the guest-role wave put a real role BELOW that floor: the guest web lane subscribed, reported SUBSCRIBED, and received **zero** events. **A guest never saw a reply without reloading.** This is §7's own first bullet in its second form — *a subscriber the policy filters out is worse than an error, because it looks like coverage* — and it is the same class of failure as an unpublished table.
  - **THE FIX IS AN ARM, NOT A SURFACE HACK**: `20260826120000_guest_channel_realtime_rls.sql` adds `OR (is_current_workspace_member(workspace_id,'guest') AND <real channel membership>)` to `channels`, `channel_members`, `channel_messages`, and swaps the literal on `agent_presence`. **`agent_presence` gets a LITERAL, not a second disjunct, and that is this section's cost rule applied**: it is the highest-churn published table and its policy is re-evaluated per subscriber per write, so an OR-arm would roughly double it. **The guest arm deliberately DROPS the `visibility='public'` disjunct**, mirroring §4A's service-side fence.
  - **THE DURABLE PIN IS THE SUBSCRIPTION LIST, NOT THE FOUR TABLES.** `channels/client/guest-realtime-rls.test.ts` asserts *every table in `CHANNEL_TABLES` + `PRESENCE_TABLES` has a guest arm, keeps its viewer arm, and requires real channel membership* — so a FIFTH entry added to either constant without an arm goes red. Same both-directions shape as `test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs`.
  - ⚠ **`channel_consent_requests` HAS NO GUEST ARM AND MUST NOT GET ONE.** The guest surface does not mount `useConsentInbox` at all (§4A) — an outbound consent row is the OPERATOR's own draft queue.
- **AN EVENT IS A DOORBELL, NEVER CONTENT.** Subscribers trigger a filtered service refetch — never payload merging — so RLS and service filters stay authoritative. `ui-sync.js` states this in its own trust-model header.
- **REPLICA IDENTITY is the second half of the publication invariant, and it is what makes DELETEs deliverable at all.** Both subscribers bind `filter: workspace_id=eq.<id>`; on DELETE the deployed `realtime.apply_rls` tests that filter against `old_columns`, which is *the replica identity* — so under `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT` (primary key only) `workspace_id` is absent, the filter can never match, and **the frame is silently dropped.**
  - Fix is `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX` on a unique `(workspace_id, id)` — **not FULL**: FULL logs the entire old row on every UPDATE and would detoast large bodies on every autosave.
  - `ui-sync-replica-identity.test.mjs` pins the INVARIANT (identity must contain `workspace_id`), not the mechanism.
  - **`old_record` is separately redacted to the primary key on any RLS-enabled table**, so a DELETE frame arrives bare even now — the doorbell pattern is not optional, there is no payload to merge.
  - Some tables stay DEFAULT on purpose, and `ui-sync-replica-identity.test.mjs › NO_DELETE_DOORBELL` **states a reason per table rather than merely listing them** — cascade-only children (`chat_messages`), never-deleted rows (`agent_presence`), and `skill_versions`, whose retention trim is the highest-frequency delete in the schema.
  - ⚠ **`channel_messages` LEFT THAT LIST ON 2026-08-22, AND HOW IT LEFT IS THE POINT.** Its reason read "cascade from `channels` / `workspaces` only" — accurate when written, and it stopped being true the day a THREAD became deletable (§5, 2026-08-21): the hand-written cascade's first step is a real `DELETE FROM channel_messages`, and **nothing else in that cascade is published**, so under DEFAULT the counterparty never learned the thread died. `20260822130000` sets `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX (workspace_id, id)`. **A stated reason is what made this findable at all** — the entry named the condition it depended on, so a later feature could be checked against it. The UPDATE cost this section usually weighs is ZERO here: `channel_messages` has **no UPDATE path in the tree** (grep `repository-messages.ts` — select, one insert RPC, one delete), and replica identity is never paid on INSERT. The cost that is real, and is accepted in the migration header: a CHANNEL delete now emits one frame per message where it emitted none, bounded by both subscribers' doorbell coalescing.
  - ⚠ **`channels` is on that list, but NOT for the reason it used to be.** "It still soft-deletes" was retired 2026-08-08 (C-16 / F-173) — **a non-DM channel HARD-deletes now**. The exemption survives on a different reason, and the difference matters: **`channels`' OWN DELETE frames ARE dropped** (it is `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT`, so the WAL record is the primary key alone and both subscribers' `workspace_id=eq.<id>` filter can never match) — **the doorbell that actually rings is the CASCADE**. The hard delete cascades real DELETEs onto `channel_members`, which DOES carry `workspace_id` in its replica identity (`20260807150000`) and rides the same refetch signal in both subscribers. Adding an identity to `channels` would widen `touchChannel` — one UPDATE per message posted — to buy a frame that already arrives. A DM delete is still an UPDATE, and §5 records what that costs (F-189).
- ⚠ **Do NOT verify replica-identity behaviour against upstream `walrus`** — its master returns `coalesce(..., true)` here and says the opposite of what is deployed.
- **A tombstone-by-UPDATE on a published table whose SELECT policy hides tombstones has silently traded its delete doorbell for a refetch** — `apply_rls` evaluates the NEW record, finds it invisible, drops the frame. **If you add `deleted_at IS NULL` to a published table's policy, you are making that trade. Decide it, do not inherit it.** The remedy shape is to touch a SIBLING published row that stays visible BEFORE stamping the tombstone.
- **Never construct a raw `supabase.channel()` for `postgres_changes` in feature code.** Go through `useWorkspaceTablesRealtime`; all mounts sharing a `(topicPrefix, workspaceId, tables)` key ride ONE ref-counted channel via `shared/realtime/shared-channel-registry.ts`, which owns reconnect backoff, a StrictMode-proof teardown grace window, and **generation-unique topic names** (realtime-js dedupes by name and forgets removed channels only after the leave push settles, so topic reuse resurrects corpse channels).
- **Any new live surface MUST use `shared/realtime/refetch-coordinator.ts`** or it will clobber in-flight typing.
- **FOUR registered live surfaces in channels, measured 2026-08-23:** `components/channels-v2/channels-v2-core.tsx`, the three-column shell that IS the `channels` page (§5); `› thread-window.tsx`, the POP-OUT window's thread-only surface (added 2026-08-19 — §11); `› agent-window.tsx`, the AGENT window (registered 2026-08-20); and `› channel-surface-standalone.tsx › StandaloneChannelSurface`, the per-channel surface pinned to one container and mounted with no app shell around it — **the HOME page's relationship record is its first host** (added 2026-08-23, §4A).
  - ⚠ **THE FOURTH DID NOT ADD A FOURTH REGISTRATION, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN OF THE EXTRACTION.** The reads, the derivations and — the part that cannot be forgotten — the ONE `useChannelsV2Live` call are `› channel-surface-data.ts › useChannelSurfaceData`, which **both** `channels-v2-core.tsx` and `channel-surface-standalone.tsx` call. **There is no third code path for the live loop.** What the core keeps for itself is the channels-LIST invalidation, handed down as the host's `onDoorbell` prop, because that list belongs to the TREE and a pinned host has no tree. Why/history: ENGINEERING.md, 2026-08-23 stratum.
  - ⚠ **THE 2026-08-20 SURFACE SHIPPED UNREGISTERED AND THE FAILURE HAD NO ERROR SHAPE — that is the reason this bullet counts rather than describes.** Two of that window's three lanes (narration, posture) are bridge-PUSHED and stayed live on their own, so the window looked healthy while its SENT lane — `useChannelMessages`, its only server read — quietly stopped updating after mount. **A new window that renders a server read owes a registration, and "it looks fine" is not evidence it has one.** Its diet is MESSAGES ONLY: it holds no roster and no presence dot, so its `refetchMembers` is a deliberate no-op, and it takes no `gate` because it makes no server WRITE (its composer reaches `sessions.message` over the bridge, which posts nothing). `agent-window.test.tsx › the window is a LIVE surface` asserts the registration, not the rendering. **All four reach the SAME `useChannelsV2Live` hook** — two of them (the core and the standalone surface) through `channel-surface-data.ts`, the two windows directly — which is the whole reason a new surface is one line rather than a second wiring: a pop-out is a registered app window and `main/ui-sync.js › sendToWindows` fans the doorbell out to it, but only a surface that SUBSCRIBED hears it, and a transcript that quietly stops updating is the failure with no error shape. `thread-window.test.tsx` asserts the registration rather than the rendering. It takes `useChannelsRealtime` through `useRefetchGate`'s coordinator and `usePresenceRealtime` on a trailing `PRESENCE_REFETCH_DEBOUNCE_MS` debounce that refetches the ROSTER only; the consent inbox rides `useConsentRealtime` inside `use-consent-inbox.ts` plus the `CONSENT_INBOX_POLL_MS` backstop. **There were TWO at the cutover** — the retired `channels-view-core.tsx` had been one of them, and it is deleted. **FIVE write families hand their `settleWith` to that ONE gate:** the composer's message / request fan-out (Phase 3), the Tags inbox's mark-read (Phase 6), the outbound consent decision (Phase 8; its Inbox pane deleted 2026-08-25 — the writer is now the work stream's card, `channels-v2/agent-stream.tsx`), the channel-management writes the cutover rehomed (`channels-v2/channel-manage.tsx` — lifecycle, tool profile, trust) and the header bookmark's FAVOURITE (2026-08-19, §5 — `channels/hooks/use-channel-preference-writes.ts › useChannelPreferenceWrites`, the same hook family as the tool profile). ⚠ The favourite is the viewer's OWN write, so optimistic + settle is the whole of its own delivery; a SECOND window learns of it through the `channel_members` doorbell, which that table's publication membership already pays for — the UPDATE's filter is evaluated against the NEW record, so the replica identity is not on its path, and the frame arrives even though `favorited_at` is redacted out of it (an event is a doorbell, never content). The coordinator was registered before any of them existed, which is the point: a coordinator retrofitted after the first write is a coordinator that was missing for exactly the window it was needed.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §7 (Realtime & new-workspace seeding).

## 8. Write layer — client mutations

Three files: `shared/api/query-keys.ts`, `shared/hooks/use-api-mutation.ts`, `shared/ui/pending.ts`.

- **Never retype a key array at a call site.** `apiQueryKey(path, {workspaceId, query})` is the exact tuple the read hook registers; `apiPathKey(path)` is the one-element PREFIX; `apiResource(path)` hands back both. **Writes patch by the prefix**, because TanStack matches per array ELEMENT and a writer does not know which query-param variants a reader mounted. **A key that differs by one element is a silent no-op** — the write lands in an entry no observer is subscribed to; nothing renders, nothing fails.
  - 🔴 **BUT A SURFACE THAT MOUNTS TWO WORKSPACES OF ONE PATH PATCHES THE `entry({workspaceId})` KEY, NOT THE PREFIX (2026-08-26, F-331).** The rule above is about the PARAM axis — the writer cannot enumerate `?include=archived` and friends, so the prefix is the honest reach. The prefix ALSO spans the WORKSPACE element, and that is a different question: those variants are not the writer's to touch. On a one-workspace page the difference is unobservable, which is why the prefix reads as house style; the moment one surface mounts a channel CONTAINER's list beside the home workspace's (the /home Agents tab, §4A/§5A), a create in one appears under the other. ⚠ **An `upsertRow`-shaped reconcile is the sharp end: it APPENDS when the id is absent**, so the leak is a whole foreign row rather than a stale field, and an UPDATE does it twice (optimistic + reconcile). ⚠ **A delete that filters by id looks safe and is only safe BY ACCIDENT** (ids do not repeat across workspaces) — do not read its green test as coverage. ⚠ **`coldKeys` INVERTS THE SAME WAY**: over a prefix it asks "does ANY variant hold data", so one warm workspace answers for a cold one and the cold list never refetches what was just created in it. **Before switching to the entry key, VERIFY the tuple is reproducible** — it is exactly `[path, workspaceId, query]`, so a path with live `query` variants needs both axes and the prefix is still the answer for the param half. Worked example, both sides: `agent-templates/hooks/use-agent-template-writes.ts` (no `query` on this path, verified at the read), pinned by the two-workspace render in `agent-templates/hooks/use-agent-template-writes.test.ts`.
- **Patches operate on the RAW response body** — the read hook stores what the endpoint returned and applies `select` on read.

**The eight rules, none negotiable:**

1. **Invalidation is EXPLICIT.** Auto-invalidating every patched key re-downloads the cache the mutation just reconciled. List only the caches this write could NOT compute — ordering, `lastMessageAt`, a server-written lifecycle echo.
   **The ONE exception is the COLD CACHE.** `optimistic` and `reconcile` both decline on an entry with no data, so on a cold start the write lands server-side and never reaches the screen, invisibly. A write whose reconcile is its only path to the screen names its keys through **`coldKeys(client, keys)` — exported from `use-api-mutation.ts`, ONE implementation; the old feature-local copies are deleted.** It runs in `onSettled` (after `reconcile`), so "still empty" is the decline itself; warm caches re-download nothing.
   **Rule 4 is not a substitute.** Whether a write names its keys conditionally is decided by whether its OWN response can reconcile the surface — never by symmetry with the write next to it.
   **Worked example, both halves in one config** (`channels/hooks/use-thread-writes-fanout.ts › fanOutThreadsConfig`): the transcript is named UNCONDITIONALLY, because the N server-written opening messages carry seqs and ids the response does not return and no reconcile can invent; the THREAD LIST is named through `coldKeys`, because the optimistic patch and the reconcile do cover it whenever it has data, and only a channel whose thread list never loaded would leave the card with no thread to render.
   **A per-item key cannot be reached by a prefix**: a write changing what a server-resolved per-item pane says must NAME each id, captured at submit.
2. **Cancel BEFORE patching, and only queries that HAVE data.** Cancelling second restores the very data the write just replaced; cancelling a first-load query strands the surface empty with nothing scheduled to fill it.
3. **`settleWith` + `useRefetchGate`, never a hand-rolled busy counter.** The gate owns the in-flight count and releases the deferred refetch in `onSettled`, so a write that THROWS cannot strand it.
4. **Key every optimistic write by the id captured AT SUBMIT.** Per-channel reads use `keepPreviousData`, so a switch renders the previous channel's data. **AND a read that FEEDS a write's payload must expose `isPlaceholderData`** — capturing the id protects the KEY and does nothing for a field the draft derives from placeholder data. The gate belongs in the pure decision layer, so it disables Send and says why rather than no-opping.
5. **MERGE, NEVER REPLACE, when the response is narrower than the cache.** Assigning the server's row over the cached one silently drops every field the write endpoint does not return. **A `reconcile` is not automatically safe just because the server answered — the question is always what it answered WITH.**
6. **A feature's READS must be on `useApiQuery` before its WRITES adopt this layer.** There is nothing to patch otherwise, and the failure is silent: the surface keeps rendering from whatever `useState` copy it held. Converting writes first produces a feature that looks converted and behaves exactly as before.
7. **`CREATE_RESOLVE` is the pattern for a server-minted id with instant render** — render a provisional row under a client-side id, resolve it when the response lands. **Ontology uses a reducer for this deliberately** (`graphReducer`, pinned by `features/ontology/graph-state.test.ts`): the board renders from a reducer, not a cache, so there is no entry to patch. **While a row is provisional it is PENDING for real** — its id is not addressable and the realtime write gate is held.
8. **`pendingRow` on a CONTROL closes toggle races.** On a row it is cosmetic; on a toggle, segmented control or role select it is the thing that stops the second click and the two conflicting PATCHes.

- **Idempotency is the client's job — WHERE IT IS DONE AT ALL, WHICH IS ONE TREE.** A minted `client_msg_id` makes a retry after a rolled-back failure return the FIRST attempt's stored row instead of double-posting. ⚠ **Measured 2026-08-11: NINE feature hook files register optimistic writes and exactly ONE mints the key** — `channels/hooks/use-thread-writes.ts`, which **split on 2026-08-18** into itself plus `use-thread-writes-fanout.ts` (the request fan-out's own shape) over `use-thread-writes-shared.ts` (the deps and the two per-channel key builders, shared so a second copy of a key cannot exist — a key that differs by one element is a silent no-op). **The fan-out mints ONE BASE key at submit and the SERVER derives the rest** (`${base}:${toUserId}` per addressee, plus the group id) — so the client still mints exactly one thing per gesture. The other eight (`members/hooks/use-team-writes.ts`, `use-access-writes.ts`, `use-join-requests.ts`, `use-member-writes.ts`, `use-invitation-writes.ts`, `chats/hooks/use-chat-writes.ts`, `channels/hooks/use-channel-preference-writes.ts`, `channels/hooks/use-channel-lifecycle-writes.ts`) carry **no idempotency key at all**. **Do not read this bullet as coverage.** Most of those are PATCH-shaped and idempotent by nature, which is why the gap has been survivable — but it is a gap, not a design. **Extending the key beyond thread writes is OPEN and UNFILED** (the `client_msg_id` entries in the findings log are about SERVER-side `create_thread` dedup, a different problem). Whoever needs it should file it before building it.
- 🔒 **A NEW FIELD ON A CACHED PAYLOAD IS ABSENT ON THE FIRST PAINT AFTER AN UPGRADE — GIVE IT `?? EMPTY_X` AT EVERY READ, AND A FIXTURE WITHOUT IT (standing rule, 2026-08-25).** The query cache is **IndexedDB-persisted with a 24h `gcTime`**, so an entry written by the PREVIOUS bundle survives the update that added the field. The wire type says non-optional and is right; the cache is a different moment. **The failure is not cosmetic**: an object field read straight through (`channel.infoCard.hidden`) THROWS and blanks the pane, for a field that is usually decoration over facts still on screen.
  - **Spell it INLINE at the read, not behind an accessor.** `Channel.infoCard` briefly had an `infoCardOf()` helper and it was deleted: the wire type is non-optional, so the helper is the only place the optionality is visible, and a rule living inside a function nobody has to call is a rule the next read forgets. `?? EMPTY_INFO_CARD` is in the reviewer's line of sight.
  - 🔒 **THE ONE SANCTIONED ACCESSOR, AND WHAT IT COST (2026-08-26).** `pages/home/home-rows.ts › channelPeople` DOES hide the optionality behind a function, against the bullet above. It is allowed because what it centralises is not a plain fallback but a **two-field MERGE** — `peers` if the key is there (EMPTY included, a real solo channel), else the legacy single `peer`, else `EMPTY_PEERS` — and two copies of THAT rule which drift is a worse bug than one forgotten `??`. ⚠ **The "a helper nobody must call is a rule the next read forgets" objection is answered by ENFORCEMENT, not by argument:** `home-rows.test.ts` reads the home directory's SOURCE and fails if any other file names `.peers` outside a comment. **Do not take this exemption without shipping that pin**, and do not read it as softening the inline rule for a plain `?? EMPTY_X` — those stay inline.
  - **The TEST is a fixture with the key DELETED — not `null`, not `{}`.** A stale entry does not carry the key at all, and both of those spellings pass a `?? ` that a missing key would also pass while proving nothing about the shape that actually ships. Worked example: `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/person-info-tab.test.tsx › a cache entry written before the info card existed`.
  - 🔒 **`?.` ON THE CONTAINER IS NOT A FALLBACK ON THE KEY, AND IT READS EXACTLY LIKE ONE (2026-08-26).** `list?.ownerNames[id]`, `list?.baseStats[id]`, `list?.starredBaseIds.includes(id)` guard `list` being ABSENT — a read still in flight — and do NOTHING about `list` being a live stale object whose KEY is `undefined`. The `.includes()` one THROWS. **Every sibling key on a cached payload needs its own `?? EMPTY_X`, spelled per key.** Worked example: `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/home/knowledge-panel-cards.tsx › BaseCell`.
  - 🔒 **A ROUTE-LEVEL FIXTURE CANNOT REACH THE CACHE FALLBACK — IT PINS THE WIRE ONE.** `knowledge/client/api.ts › fetchBaseList` normalises every sibling key on arrival, so a mocked response with a key deleted exercises a PRE-DEPLOY SERVER and nothing else; a payload cached by an older BUNDLE never passes through today's fetcher at all. **The two fallbacks are different code and need different tests** — seed the component (or the QueryClient) with the key-deleted object directly. `pages/home/knowledge-panels.test.tsx`'s §8 block and `pages/home/knowledge-panel-cards.test.tsx` hold the two halves and each says which it is.
  - **The renderer-build buster does NOT cover this.** It re-keys on the packaged app's VERSION (`apps/desktop-ui/vite.config.ts`), so a dev run, a hot reload, or any path where the version has not moved still serves the old entry.
- **An unspecified query option must be OMITTED, never passed as `undefined`.** TanStack merges by spread, so a key present with `undefined` DELETES the client default underneath it instead of falling back to it. Route caller-supplied options through `definedOnly()`. **`0` is not "unset"** — test `!== undefined`, never truthiness.
- **Known gap:** the layer cannot express a PREDICATE invalidation — `invalidate` returns KEYS, while TanStack's `invalidateQueries` accepts a `predicate` this layer cannot pass.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §7 (Writes: `useApiMutation`).
