  - **THE TWO CAPABILITIES THEMSELVES ARE UNCHANGED and are now stated in the doctrine:** (1) the sparse MAIN-ROOM post, with the bar keyed on the agent's OWN run ("you have now posted to this channel in this run, so the next one needs a reason a human would name out loud"); (2) the @-tag — `@` + a handle in the BODY, resolved server-side (§5) — for a decision only a person can make, a summary worth their minutes, and "I am blocked".
- **FOUR NARRATION DEFECTS FIXED 2026-08-22 (Samuel's post-incident rulings), each one a line that told an agent something the code does not do.**
  - **A `chat` POST NOW READS ITS THREAD TAG.** (⚠ HISTORY — the note this fixed was retired on 2026-09-02 with T12; the fact survives as the post result's `intent=chat` beside `landed=thread`, built by `channel-post-linkage.ts › threadFacts`.) `channel-post-notes.ts`'s then-private `addressingNoteLines`'s `intent === "chat"` branch returned EARLY and never looked at `landedThread`, so a chat post threaded into a live exchange was told "no agent was put in front of it" — false by `channel-addressing.ts`'s own fact 3, since `feedLiveSession` hands a first-class (uuid) tag straight into the counterparty's running turn without reading addressing at all. **`intent` governs ADDRESSING; a thread tag is a different route into the same session.** Both branches now use the same `routesToASession` predicate, so a LEGACY `task-…` tag still falls to the plain chat line — it groups on a card and wakes nobody.
  - **A THREAD-SCOPED `read` PRINTS NO SEQ AT ALL.** It used to print `Highest seq shown: <n>` and then spend four sentences forbidding its use as a cursor; **a footgun wrapped in prose is still a footgun, and the number is what survives a skim.** The alternative — return an explicitly safe `nextSince` — is not available: the only safe value is the caller's own prior channel-wide cursor and the op cannot see it. Each message line still carries its own `**#seq**`; what is withheld is the SUMMARY line that reads like a cursor. The channel-wide read is unchanged and still offers its own.
  - **LONG BODIES CLIP ON A MULTI-MESSAGE PAGE** at `channel-render.ts › BODY_CLIP_CHARS` (2000), with a marker naming the characters dropped. ⚠ **A ONE-MESSAGE PAGE IS NEVER CLIPPED, and that is a CONTRACT rather than an optimization:** the marker's remedy is `op="read"` with `since=<seq-1>, limit=1`, which returns exactly that message, so clipping a single-message page would make the remedy hand back another clipped copy. **The marker may NOT point at `op="get_thread"` — that op renders no message bodies at all.** Measured cause: a 128,000-character body in a live `read`.
  - **A SESSION END IS ITS OWN CLAUSE.** Session death rides `kind='task_progress'` with `metadata.session_ended` (non-terminal on purpose — one member's window closing is not the thread failing), so "a step landed" and "the agent working this is gone" rendered identically. The tag now REPLACES the kind tag with `· SESSION ENDED`; a literal `true` only. It matters because `await`'s stop rule is keyed on whether the addressed member showed activity, and a session end reads as activity while meaning the opposite.
  - **A SESSION TAG NAMES THE AGENT SEGMENT.** `metadata.session_id` is the desktop slot key `<channelId>:<taskId>:<agentId>`; the render sliced after the FIRST colon — correct for the pre-multiplayer two-segment key, and against a three-segment one it printed `<thread>:<agent>`, which is not a session identity and repeats the thread tag two clauses away. It now reads from the END and falls back through empty segments, so both key shapes render.
  - **THE MCP HALF MAY STATE THE TAGS INBOX AND NEVER A NOTIFICATION.** Mention-gated notification is the desktop's (§11, wiring plan Phase 7) and ships in a separate build, so this copy states what a tag DOES today plus the product's direction, and no delivery guarantee it cannot see.
  - **THE LAW BLOCK GAINED NO NINTH BULLET — two existing bullets were EXTENDED.** "Reply where you were asked" carries the main-room grant, because that bullet unqualified reads as a BAN and the ban stopped being true; "blocked and need a person" carries the tag, because escalation is exactly what a tag is for. Re-measure the budget rather than quoting it — `channel-law.test.ts` slices the law between `THE LAW OF THIS ROOM` and `THE MODEL` and enforces ≤8 bullets, ≤2200 chars total, ≤900 per bullet:
    ```
    node -e "const s=require('fs').readFileSync('packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-description.ts','utf8');const l=s.slice(s.indexOf('THE LAW OF THIS ROOM'),s.indexOf('THE MODEL'));console.log(l.length, l.split('\n').filter(x=>x.trim().startsWith('- ')).length)"
    ```
  - ⚠ **THE TAG RULE TAUGHT TO AN AGENT IS THE RESOLVER'S RULE, NOT A FRIENDLY PARAPHRASE.** Lowercase EXACT match against handles derived from the display name and the email local part, whole-squashed or first word, trailing punctuation stripped and leading punctuation not, ambiguity failing closed. A prompt that taught a prefix match would produce tags that resolve to nobody — **and a tag that resolves to nobody reports nothing, so the failure is silent.**
- **THE ORCHESTRATOR SURFACE — WHAT AN AGENT MAY NOW LEARN AND DO ABOUT OTHER AGENTS (2026-08-22, Samuel's wave). Read the POSTURE first, because every line of copy in it depends on the same three facts:**
  1. **A SESSION ROW IS A REPORT, NOT AN OBSERVATION.** Nothing on the server watches a machine; `channel_sessions` is a projection the desktop PUSHES, on state change and never on a timer. So a quiet row means NOBODY HAS SAID ANYTHING — which includes the case the desktop crashed.
  2. **THE SERVER STARTS NOTHING AND CANNOT.** `launch_agent` files a request; a machine decides. "launched" means a machine SAID so, and there is no third party to check it against.
  3. **`null` IS UNKNOWN, NEVER ZERO**, on every telemetry field. A render that prints `0 tokens` for an absent number asserts a measurement nobody took.
  - **`read_sessions` IS A TABLE, AND ONLY A TABLE (2026-09-02, T13).** `channel-session-table.ts › SESSION_TABLE_HEAD` + `sessionRow` — handle, state, thread, channel, template, model, tool, idle — SPLIT OUT of `channel-session-render.ts` at the §1 cap, along the seam that was already there: that file owns a session's vocabulary, its staleness window and the legend; the table owns the grid, and the dependency runs ONE way. **`sessionBlockLines` moved with it**, so `read_sessions` and the `await` sessions block still render from ONE function and cannot describe a session differently (`channel-session-liveness.test.ts` holds them byte-for-byte). ⚠ **THE LEGEND STAYED AND THE THREE PARAGRAPHS LEFT.** The legend decodes THIS page's own cells and is conditional on the page containing a hedged row; `SESSION_HANDLE_NOTE` (~1.1k) and `SESSION_TELEMETRY_NOTE` (~800) were standing doctrine rendered on EVERY call of an op an orchestrator polls in a loop, and are DELETED — their text is in `channel-doctrine.ts`. ⚠ **A STALE ROW STILL MAY NOT ASSERT A PRESENT TENSE** — the state cell reads `last reported <state>`, hedge FIRST, because `working (stale)` skims as `working`. ⚠ **`—` IS "NOT REPORTED", NEVER ZERO**, and a grid begs to be filled. ⚠ **POSTURE AND CHAIN ARE NOT COLUMNS YET** — the projection does not carry them; they land with T24/T25, and a column that is `—` on every row for every caller reads as "no posture".
  - **`read_sessions` NO LONGER ASSERTS A LIVE STATE FOR A QUIET ROW.** `updatedAt` already crossed the wire and was DROPPED at render, so a crashed desktop read as `working` forever. A row quiet longer than `channel-session-render.ts › SESSION_STALE_WINDOW_MS` now renders **"last reported <state> · stale, Xm ago — its desktop may be offline"**, and the hedge REPLACES the state clause rather than annotating it (`working · stale` still reads as `working` to a skimming model). ⚠ **It is a HEDGE and never a claim the agent stopped**: the stamp is not a heartbeat, so an alive-but-idle agent crosses the line too — the same conclusion the web reached from the other direction when a wall-clock filter made live cards VANISH (§5, `agents-model.ts › peerRowStale` decides INK, not membership). ⚠ `SESSION_STALE_WINDOW_MS` is a DELIBERATE DUPLICATE of `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS`, pinned across trees by `channel-session-staleness.test.ts` — a second staleness number would let one surface call a machine offline while another reported its agent busily working.
    - ⚠ **AND THE HEDGE WAS A LIE ABOUT THE COMMON CASE UNTIL 2026-08-23 (F-294): AN IDLE-BUT-ALIVE AGENT READ AS "its desktop may be offline" WITHIN ~2 MINUTES.** A quiet row cannot tell idle-alive from dead, but `agent_presence` can — it beats unconditionally (`main/presence.js`, ~120/hr) where the session push does not. The own-scoped read now JOINS the caller's own presence row (`server/session-state-service.ts › listSessionStates` → `repository-collab.ts › presenceForUser`, the PK lookup rather than the workspace scan) and both surfaces carry it as **`operatorOnline`**: a quiet row under a LIVE heartbeat renders **"<state> · quiet Xm — your desktop is online, so this is UNCHANGED, not unknown"** and the legend branches with it; a quiet row under a quiet machine keeps the offline hedge. ⚠ **THREE STATES, AND ABSENT IS THE THIRD** — an older server sends no key and that takes the OFFLINE branch, because a fact nobody reported is not evidence of life. ⚠ **IT IS A BOOLEAN ON THE WIRE, DERIVED SERVER-SIDE** against `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS`: shipping the stamp would invite a client to re-derive freshness against a second number, which is the drift the duplicate-plus-pin above exists to prevent. ⚠ **AN UNREADABLE `updatedAt` KEEPS THE OLD HEDGE EVEN UNDER A LIVE HEARTBEAT** — presence licenses "this report is still current", never "this report is from N minutes ago". ⚠ **THE PUSH SIDE WAS NOT TOUCHED AND MUST NOT BE:** a keepalive re-report was weighed and refused, with the argument recorded in `main/session-state-push.js`'s header beside the no-timer rule it would have amended. `channel-session-liveness.test.ts` pins the whole branch.
    - ⚠ **A MODEL ID IS ONE TOKEN, ALWAYS (2026-08-23, F-293).** The bundled CLI ships explicit long-context ids spelled `claude-opus-5[1m]`, and `narration.ts › neutralizeInline` blanks `[`/`]` to SPACES because they are markdown structure — so the model clause rendered `` `opus-5 1m` ``, putting a fragment shaped exactly like `coarseAge`'s output into the segment `SESSION_TELEMETRY_NOTE` promises holds bare NAMES, one clause from `started 12m ago`. `channel-session-render.ts › shortModelLabel` now JOINS with a hyphen whatever the neutralizer would blank (`opus-5-1m`), so no desktop-supplied id can split the model slot into two bare names; the note states the promise so it is checkable. ⚠ **THE SUFFIX IS KEPT, NOT DROPPED** — it is the 1M variant of a 200k model and erasing it renders two different runs under one name.
  - 🔒 **SESSION TELEMETRY IS OPERATOR-ONLY; PEERS KEEP COARSE.** `channel_sessions` gained eight nullable columns (`20260822150000`). `detail` is the ONLY one a peer may read, and **only because its vocabulary is CLOSED** — six fixed keys from `main/session-detail.js`, narrowed on the way out by `collab-dto.ts › narrowSessionDetail`, so free-form prose in that column can never reach a peer. The other seven (model, tool label, context used/window, tokens, started/last-activity) reach the owner alone. **THE FENCE IS `collab-dto.ts › mapPeerSessionStateRow`, which BUILDS the narrow object rather than scrubbing a wide one** — construction fails CLOSED when a column is added, an omit-list fails OPEN — plus two mappers with two return types and NO default audience argument, so a peer surface that assigned the rich shape would not compile. ⚠ **The column GRANT in that migration is the BELT, not the fence:** every session read runs on the admin client, and `service_role` keeps every grant and is not subject to RLS. `server/session-visibility.test.ts` pins it as a PROPERTY over `OPERATOR_ONLY_SESSION_FIELDS` (names AND values, so a renamed leak still fails), not as a list of examples.
  - **`await` NOW RETURNS THE CALLER'S OWN SESSIONS WITH ITS MESSAGES** — `{messages, timedOut}` widened to `{messages, timedOut, sessions?, operatorOnline?}` (the fourth key 2026-08-23, F-294), ADDITIVE (an older client ignores them). ⚠ **`sessions` AND `operatorOnline` RIDE OR STAY TOGETHER** — one own-scoped read produces both, and emitting `operatorOnline: false` from a read that FAILED would report the operator's machine as gone on the strength of our own outage. ⚠ **READ AT RETURN TIME ONLY, NEVER INSIDE `service-await.ts`'s TICK LOOP**, which is the feature's one structurally growing egress consumer: one read per RETURNED hold, zero during the wait. ⚠ **FAIL-SOFT AND `undefined` ≠ `[]`** — an omitted key is "not reported" (an older server, or a failed read behind an earned payload), `[]` is "the server looked and this machine reports nothing", and collapsing them tells an orchestrator it has no agents whenever it talks to an older deployment.
  - 🔒 **`channel_messages.seq` IS A TABLE-WIDE IDENTITY, NOT MERELY WORKSPACE-GLOBAL, AND THIS BULLET SAID THE NARROWER THING UNTIL 2026-09-01 (F-412).** `supabase/migrations/20260725120000_channels.sql` declares `seq BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` — **one sequence per TABLE** — so a seq is unique and monotonic across every workspace AND every `kind='link'` container at once. The old wording was never false (table-global implies workspace-global) but read as an upper bound it denied the fact the ACCOUNT-WIDE read rests on. ⚠ **The workspace-await's own copy is deliberately left at the narrower statement**, because it describes a hold that IS workspace-scoped and there the narrower claim is the accurate one for what that function does.
  - **`await` WITH NO `channel` HOLDS ACROSS THE WHOLE WORKSPACE.** `since` stays required on both forms — `seq` is (at least) workspace-global and gappy, which is exactly what makes ONE cursor legal across every channel at once. ⚠ **A SIBLING SERVICE, NOT A MODE** (`service-await-workspace.ts`): the two holds have different fences and collapsing them would put two authorization stories behind one signature. ⚠ **THE FENCE IS THE MEMBERSHIP SET, AND IT IS LITERALLY THE `WHERE channel_id IN (…)`** of every query the hold issues — a private channel the caller is not in is never NAMED, not merely filtered out afterwards. ⚠ **M2 IS PRESERVED AND THE WORKSPACE HALF IS EXTRA:** on an existence hit the hold re-proves membership before reading rows (unless the periodic recheck already proved it EARLIER IN THE SAME TICK), **and the fetch uses the FRESH set** — re-proving is pointless if the read still names channels the caller has since left. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NARROWER THAN `op="read"`**, which admits a non-member to a PUBLIC channel: an await is a WAKE primitive, and being woken by every public room nobody invited you into is noise. Fewer rows is never a leak. The result STATES the scope, and `channelCount: 0` is reported rather than rendered as silence.
  - **THE `await` HOLD IS TWO DEFAULTS, AND THE SELF-ECHO FILTER IS SESSION-SCOPED WITH NO ACCOUNT FALLBACK (2026-09-01, F-405/F-406).** ⚠ **DEFAULT HOLD DEPENDS ON THE CALLER'S RUNTIME STAMP** — `channel-await-budget.ts › awaitHoldMs` is the ONE resolver both lanes call: a `desktop-session` request keeps the wake-length hold, anything else gets `EXTERNAL_CLIENT_ABORT_MS - AWAIT_HOLD_EXTERNAL_MARGIN_MS`. One default could not serve both, and it failed the external half SILENTLY: an external MCP client aborts a pending call around 60s, so the long default returned a raw transport error carrying no cursor, no session block and none of the re-arm teaching — the one outcome the whole deadline chain exists to prevent. **An explicit `timeout_ms` is honoured EXACTLY** (clamped only by `AWAIT_HOLD_CAP_MS` and the `DOPL_AWAIT_HOLD_MS` incident lever), or the parameter is a suggestion. ⚠ **`runtime` IS NOW LOAD-BEARING FOR THE HOLD'S LENGTH, NOT ONLY ITS PROSE** — still an observation that GATES nothing (`shared/auth/runtime-header.ts`), and `identity.ts › isDesktopRuntime` is its one comparison, shared with `channel-wake-guidance.ts` so length and claims cannot disagree about one request. 🔒 **AND NO AUTHOR FILTER IS EVER SENT.** `excludeAuthor` keyed the suppression on the ACCOUNT, but every post is stamped `author_user_id = ctx.userId` while one operator runs many concurrent sessions — so an orchestrator and its worker were the same author, and the row was dropped from BOTH the page and the existence probe: the hold did not spin, it held silently to its deadline while the answer sat in the table, and `op="read"` showed what the await swore was not there. Own lines are dropped from the page in hand on `metadata.session_id`, or not at all. ⚠ **THE ACCOUNT FILTER MAY NOT COME BACK AS A "FALLBACK" FOR UNSTAMPED CALLERS** — that is every external client, i.e. the exact population that reported the outage, and it is the shape the first fix shipped with. ⚠ **The TIMED-OUT result is the COMPRESSED one, cursor-first**; the full re-arm rule is taught where it is NEW information (the hold that returns, and the one that fails), never re-read on every empty hold of a poll loop. Every clause this section requires of a re-arm instruction still rides it, including the ABSENCE of a finished state to wait for.
  - **`launch_agent` IS THE 14th OP, AND A DIRECTIVE IS NOT A MESSAGE (§5).** `channel` required; `thread` / `goal` / `model` / `template` / `wait_ms` optional (default 15s, cap 30s). ⚠ **`template` TAKES AN ID **OR** AN EXACT NAME and is resolved SERVER-SIDE under the caller's own template visibility before any row is written (§5A); an ambiguous name is REFUSED with every match listed, and the op branches on the ERROR CODE so a bad template never narrates as a bad channel.** ⚠ **A TIMEOUT IS NOT A FAILURE** — the directive stays pending and the result forbids re-issuing in the strongest terms available, because a second call starts a SECOND agent on the same work and nothing can tell them apart afterwards. ⚠ **THE REFUSAL CONTRACT, SEVEN WORDS SINCE 2026-08-22** — `cap` | `busy` | `no-sdk` | `auth-hold` | `no-bridge` | `no-counterparty` | `no-template` — is a KEY on the wire and a SENTENCE written by the reader, so a reword needs no desktop release and no desktop-authored prose is rendered into an agent-facing result. `no-bridge` is the operator's own toggle and its sentence says so. ⚠ **`no-template` IS THE OPERATOR'S MACHINE FAILING TO RESOLVE THE TEMPLATE — a DIFFERENT fence from the create-time one**, and its sentence says whose visibility failed, because an orchestrator that does not know that re-issues forever. ⚠ **AND THE `model` PARAM'S DESCRIPTION WAS CORRECTED IN THE SAME WAVE (2026-08-23)**: it said an unrecognised id *"is the DESKTOP's to refuse, not this tool's"*, and the desktop does not refuse — it FALLS BACK, silently, through `session-model.js`. The doc-vs-code disagreement was flagged in `docs/AGENT-TEMPLATES-SPEC.md` §3b and the sentence now states the fall-back. `cap` says the machine is FULL and sends the agent to `read_sessions` rather than to a retry. ⚠ **PRESENCE IS A PRE-CHECK, NOT A VERDICT**: `agent_presence` is per-(user, workspace), so an OFFLINE reading short-circuits (no row is filed) and the result names what the check cannot tell — which machine, whether the launching one is up, whether launching is enabled. `launch_agent` is in `gating.ts › WRITE_OPS.dopl_channel`.
  - **`end_agent` AND `rename_agent` ARE THE 19th AND 20th OPS, AND THEY ARE `launch_agent`'s LANE WITH A DIFFERENT `kind` (2026-09-01, Samuel: *"dopl mcp being able to end agents. Dopl MCP need to be able to do all that stuff"*).** Both require `channel` + `agent_id`; `rename_agent` also requires `name` and `wait_ms` is optional on both. They file a `channel_launch_directives` row of kind `end` / `rename` (§6), which the operator's own desktop claims and answers — so **a refusal is normal, a timeout is PENDING not failed, and "ended" means a machine SAID so**, exactly as on a launch. Both are in `gating.ts › WRITE_OPS.dopl_channel`: neither changes a row a read could see, and both are refused to a read-only token anyway because an end mutates a live process.
    - ⚠ **`channel` IS REQUIRED THOUGH `agent_id` ADDRESSES THE TARGET ON ITS OWN, AND THAT IS THE FENCE RATHER THAN ERGONOMICS**: the create proves a MEMBERSHIP ROW there, without which this is a bare "end agent `abcdefgh`" primitive with no room the caller had to be in first. `agent_id` accepts `@agent-<id>` or the bare id through the parser `direct_agent` uses (`channel-agent-id.ts › bareAgentId`, extracted in this wave to stop a third copy).
    - ⚠ **THE RESULT COPY IS A SEPARATE MAP OVER THE SAME NINE-WORD ENUM, AND THE DUPLICATION IS THE POINT.** The wire word is shared; what it means to DO NEXT is not. `cap` on a launch says "wait for a running agent to finish" — advice that contradicts a request to END one — and **`no-bridge` on a launch says the operator's toggle is off and to ask for it to be turned on, which on these two verbs would send an orchestrator to request a permission that does not gate them.** Both are pinned as negative assertions in `channel-ops-agent.test.ts`.
    - ⚠ **`rename_agent` IS DISPLAY-ONLY AND `read_sessions` WILL NEVER SHOW THE NAME** — it lives in `main/agent-names.js` on ONE machine, reaches no server and is invisible to every other member. The result says so explicitly, because an agent expecting the listing to change re-issues forever, and because a name that looked addressable would resurrect the retired `@name` routing model.
    - 🔒 **THE WORD `rename_agent` WAS A BANNED ONE UNTIL THIS WAVE, AND UN-BANNING IT IS A RULING.** It was one of seven ops of the retired NAMED-AGENT surface (channels rollback §1, 2026-08-05), where an agent was a named channel participant and a rename changed its ADDRESS. **SIX of the seven stay banned**; this one came back as a different verb — the same one the in-process `mcp__dopl_agents__rename_agent` has carried since 2026-08-31 — because keeping the ban would have forced TWO names for one verb depending on whether the caller was inside a session or outside it. ⚠ **THE GUARD DID NOT WEAKEN, IT MOVED FROM THE SPELLING TO THE MEANING**: `channel-law.test.ts` now drives the shipped copy for "a LABEL, never an ADDRESS" and `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts` asserts the revival brought back no addressing param (`agent` and `as_agent` are still absent). The banned table and the source-wide scan moved to `law-removed-vocabulary.ts` / `law-scan.test.ts` at the 500-line cap — ⚠ **neither may take a `channel-` prefix**, because the scan globs `channel-*.ts` and the table holds the banned words as regexes.
  - **MILESTONES ARE TAUGHT AS THE ORCHESTRATION CONVENTION, OUTSIDE THE LAW BLOCK.** A launch `goal` should instruct the agent to post `op="milestone"` at each work item's start and finish; awaits deliver them attributed per-agent, which is what makes several agents' progress visible without asking any of them. ⚠ The LAW block gained NO bullet — re-measure rather than quoting (`channel-law.test.ts` enforces ≤8 bullets / ≤2200 chars / ≤900 per bullet; it measured 7 bullets / 1957 chars on 2026-08-22).
- **THE AGENT HANDLE IS PUBLISHED, AND THE RULE FOR SPENDING IT TRAVELS WITH IT (2026-08-31, from the live repro in ENGINEERING).** `channel-session-handle.ts` — its own file at the §2 cap, and a different reason to change: `channel-session-render.ts` answers "what STATE is this session in", this one answers "what may I DO about it", and the second moves when the WAKE RULES do.
  - **NO SCHEMA CHANGE WAS NEEDED, AND THAT IS WORTH KNOWING BEFORE ANYONE ADDS ONE.** `channel_sessions.name` is filled by `main/session-summary.js › nameOf`, which answers `s.agentId` and nothing else — so the row's `name` **IS** the addressable id on any current desktop. What was missing was never the datum; it was that nothing said the datum was an ADDRESS, and nothing named the `@agent-` form the app writes and tints. ⚠ `addressableHandle` is therefore a RECOGNISER, not a validator: the column's own CHECK is the wider `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,30}$` (it predates the multiplayer wave, when the field held pool handles like `flint`), an older desktop is a supported peer (§13), and a name outside the id charset prints **nothing** rather than an `@agent-flint` that reaches nobody.
  - **ONE FORM, PREFIXED.** The desktop parser takes both `@<id>` and `@agent-<id>` (F-350's regex), so this is a CONVENTION question, not a correctness one — and publishing the bare form while the renderer tints the prefixed one is the tint-says-tagged / stamp-says-nobody split (F-266) one namespace over.
  - 🔒 **OWN ROWS ONLY, VIA ITS OWN `handle` FLAG — NOT `telemetry`.** An agent id is a WAKE TOKEN (tier 1 is "at any roster size", and a peer HUMAN who knows one can wake my agent with it), so which handles a result publishes is an AUDIENCE decision, not a verbosity one. Overloading the telemetry flag would tie them together and the next compact own-row page would withdraw the handle silently. Both production call sites (`read_sessions`, `sessionBlockLines`) are own-scoped and pass it explicitly.
  - **`SESSION_HANDLE_NOTE` STATES THE GOAL-FIRST RULE AND THE THREE LIMITS**, in that order: a launch that carried a `goal` is already working (waking is for REDIRECTING); then the wake must NAME an agent, reaches only the caller's OWN operator's agents, and is **not observable from here** because it is decided on a desktop no server sees.
- **THE POST RESULT REPORTS WHAT EACH `@` WAS, NOT ONLY HOW MANY RESOLVED (2026-08-31).** `channel-post-guidance.ts › classifyMentions` / `mentionBreakdownLine`, spliced by `postGuidanceLines`. ⚠ **THE COUNT ALONE MIS-NARRATES THE ORDINARY ORCHESTRATION POST**, which carries both kinds of token at once (`@samuel please look, @agent-x2sz1ztt carry on`): `metadata.mentionedUserIds` is over the HUMAN half, and printed alone it reads as a verdict on the whole body — which is exactly how a live orchestrator concluded its AGENT handle was misspelled.
  - **THE GRAMMAR IS PUBLIC, SO THE ID FORM IS DECIDABLE SERVER-SIDE, EXACTLY.** `AGENT_HANDLE_RE` is `session-dispatch.js › mentionedAgentIds`'s id door, hand-copied minus the lookbehind (unnecessary against one already-extracted token). ⚠ **WHETHER AN AGENT HANDLE ARRIVED IS NOT DECIDABLE ANYWHERE ON THE SERVER** — the resolver is a desktop, over ids no server holds — so the line says what a token IS and what the LAW does with it, never that it landed.
  - ⚠ **A PARTIAL HUMAN RESOLVE SAYS "WHICH ONE IS NOT KNOWABLE FROM HERE".** The stamp is a set of READER IDS and the tokens are strings; there is no join, and inventing one by position or count would name the wrong token as the failure — actionable and wrong, which is worse than silent.
  - ⚠ **`tagOutcomeNote`'s FIVE-CAUSE PARAGRAPH IS NOW GATED ON THERE BEING A MEMBER HANDLE.** Over a body whose every token was an agent handle it answered a question the caller did not ask, in the voice of a defect, about the one thing they did right. A MIXED body gets both lines, breakdown first. ⚠ Cause (5)'s second sentence was corrected in the same pass and has been wrong in BOTH directions — see `channel-zero-tag.test.ts`'s history note.
- ⚠ **THE LAW BLOCK IS AT 8/8 BULLETS AND WITHIN ~40 CHARS OF ITS 2200 BUDGET (2158 chars, measured 2026-08-31 off `dist/` after the same-account exception bullet landed).** The next rule that has to be stated there REPLACES one; it cannot be added. Re-measure rather than quoting — the command is in the FOUR NARRATION DEFECTS bullet above.

- 🔒 **`direct_agent` / `read_directions` — THE PRIVATE DIRECT LANE (Samuel's ruling, 2026-08-31).** An operator's EXTERNAL agent may send a private message into one of that operator's OWN running desktop sessions, and read that turn's final text back. `direct_agent` is in `gating.ts › WRITE_OPS.dopl_channel`; `read_directions` is a read.
  - 🔒 **IT REACHES THE CALLER'S OWN OPERATOR AND NOBODY ELSE, AND THE ABSENCE OF AN ARGUMENT IS THE WHOLE AUTHORIZATION STORY.** No schema on this path accepts an operator id and none may ever get one — the server stamps `ctx.userId`, written LAST in `repository-directions.ts › insertAgentDirection` so no payload key can shadow it, and every read and write carries it as a SQL predicate on the admin client. A peer can neither be directed nor direct you, and there is no spelling of the API that says otherwise. Asserted, not reviewed: `server/service-directions.test.ts`.
  - ⚠ **`agent_id`, NOT `agent`.** `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts` bans a param literally named `agent` — it was the retired named-agent ADDRESSING surface. This one names an INSTANCE ID on the caller's own machine and addresses nobody in the channel, which is exactly the distinction that guard keeps. It accepts the `@agent-<id>` handle `read_sessions` prints as well as the bare id, because refusing the printed form would be a 400 for doing what the neighbouring op taught.
  - ⚠ **REQUIRED, WITH NO OLDEST-AGENT FALLBACK.** Every other op in the family resolves to the oldest live agent on a thread when none is named; on a lane that reaches a PRIVATE TURN that would steer one the orchestrator did not address, with nothing reporting the swap.
  - ⚠ **A DIRECTION IS NOT A MESSAGE** (§5) — no `seq`, so it can never end an `await`; the op holds on the ROW, `launch_agent`'s shape. **A TIMEOUT IS NOT A FAILURE and the result forbids re-issuing in the strongest terms available**, and the cost here is worse than a duplicate launch: a second direction says the same thing to a LIVE agent, which answers twice with nothing able to tell the two apart.
  - ⚠ **THE REPLY IS ONE TURN'S FINAL TEXT AND NOTHING ELSE**, and `null` means NOT REPORTED — never "the agent said nothing". An empty final text and a desktop older than the capture are both honest deliveries, and the render says which it cannot tell.
  - **THE CALLER MAY NOW BE ONE OF THE OPERATOR'S OWN DESKTOP SESSIONS, NOT ONLY AN EXTERNAL ORCHESTRATOR (2026-08-31, Samuel's same-owner ruling).** Nothing on this tool surface changed for it: the server fence compares OPERATOR IDS and a spawned session already presents the operator's own credential, so the whole change is a DESKTOP allow lane (§11). The op's copy, its five refusal words and its hold are unchanged.
  - ⚠ **`senderAgentId` IS A CAPTION, AND AN UNVERIFIED ONE (F-376a, 2026-08-31).** The row carries which of the operator's own agents FILED it, derived SERVER-SIDE from the third segment of `X-Dopl-Session-Id` — **never from a request field**, so the "no schema on this path accepts an identity" rule is intact. 🔒 **NOTHING MAY GATE, ROUTE, FILTER OR AUTHORIZE ON IT**: that header is a documented NON-authorization signal and anything holding the operator's device token can set it, which makes this exactly as trustworthy as `metadata.session_id`. It is safe to DISCLOSE because sender and recipient are the same operator's agents by construction. **`null` is the ORDINARY case** — an external orchestrator sends no session stamp — and renders as the sentence the surface already showed, never as a raw id.
  - ⚠ **`read_directions` IS A SIBLING OP, NOT A MODE**, and it exists because a direction has no second surface: `launch_agent`'s answer to "what happened to my pending row" is *find it in `read_sessions`*, and a direction's answer IS the row. ⚠ **CLAIM AND DECIDE ARE NOT OPS AND MUST NEVER BECOME ONE** — they are how a MACHINE reports what it did, and an external agent does not get to say a direction was delivered, still less to write the `reply` an operator reads as their own agent's words. They are unbound on `@dopl/client` for that reason (`client-surface.test.ts › PUBLIC_SURFACE`).
- 🔒 **`ping` / `pings` — THE "NEEDS YOU" SIGNAL (2026-09-01, `docs/specs/needs-you-ping.md`).** An agent says ONE of three things — `done`, `question`, `blocked` — to EXACTLY ONE recipient, out of band. It exists because an agent that FINISHED had no instrument at all: an UNADDRESSED agent-authored post starts nobody (the loop brake, §5) and an ADDRESSED one fans out to a room and triggers a machine. `ping` is in `gating.ts › WRITE_OPS.dopl_channel`; `pings` is a read.
  - 🔒 **THREE RECIPIENT FORMS, AND THE ABSENCE OF AN ARGUMENT IS AGAIN THE WHOLE LOOP BRAKE.** `to_desktop=true` (the caller's own operator's external session) and `agent_id` (one of that operator's own running agents) both STAMP `ctx.userId` as the recipient server-side; there is no operator field on any schema on this path and none may ever get one. **An agent can never ping another member's agent, because there is nothing to say it with.** `to=<member>` is the one form naming somebody else, and it is fenced like a post: sender must hold a MEMBERSHIP ROW (not mere readability — a public channel widens it by zero callers) and so must the recipient. Asserted, not reviewed: `server/service-pings.test.ts`, `tools/channel-ping.test.ts`.
  - ⚠ **THE SPEC ASKED FOR `to_agent` AND IT CANNOT EXIST.** `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts` asserts `CHANNEL_INPUT_SHAPE` has no such property and `channel-law.test.ts › REMOVED_VOCABULARY` bans the STRING `/to_agents?\b/` from every shipped literal in every non-test `channel-*.ts` — both residue-fences from the named-agent rollback. The capability rides the existing `agent_id`, which already means "one of my own operator's agents". ⚠ **`kind` WAS NOT WIDENED EITHER**: it is a closed enum of MESSAGE kinds, and adding `done` to it would let a post claim to be one. The ping's kind is its own param, `ping_kind`.
  - ⚠ **A PING IS NOT A MESSAGE AND HAS ITS OWN CURSOR.** It is a `channel_pings` row, not a `channel_messages` one, so it does not fan out, it is in no transcript, and — having no message `seq` — **it can never end an `await`**. Its own `seq` is a SEPARATE cursor space: crossing the two reads a plausible WRONG page rather than erroring, which is why both results say so. Nothing replies to a ping.
  - ⚠ **THREE DELIVERY PATHS, ONE ROW.** (a) `GET /api/pings/await?since=` — ONE held request an external session keeps open instead of polling N channels; its fence is BOTH halves of `channel_pings_party_select` — `recipient_user_id = ctx.userId` AND the caller's PROVEN channel-membership set, literally the `WHERE channel_id IN (…)` of every query it issues (**R1, 2026-09-02**) — so it runs the SAME `AWAIT_REVALIDATE_EVERY_TICKS` re-proof cadence as both message holds, proves before it fetches, and reads with the FRESH set (`server/service-pings-await.ts` states why it is a sibling and not a mode). ⚠ **THE DOCS AND THE CODE PREVIOUSLY DISAGREED HERE AND THE CODE WAS WIDER:** the REST lane ran on the admin client with the party clause only, so a member removed from a room kept receiving pings about it while the client lane refused them. Party-only is not the rule and never was. (b) The desktop writes **`ping <chan8> seq <N> to=<recipient8> kind=<kind>`** to `userData/listener.log` for every ping addressed to this operator — the zero-token wake a local watcher arms on, pinned in `dopl-desktop-app/test/ping-delivery.test.mjs`. (c) `op="pings"` is the catch-up read for a client that missed a hold.
  - 🔒 **THE LOCAL WAKE IS THE EXISTING BELT, NOT A SECOND ONE.** On the `agent` form the desktop resolves the `(channelId, taskId, agentId)` slot and calls `sessionEngine.feedInbound` with `wake: true` and an `addressing` naming that agent — where the `@agent-<id>` fan-out door already terminates. ⚠ **NO LIVE SESSION IS NOT A FAILURE**: the row stands in the inbox and the log line is already written, and the MCP result says so rather than promising a wake.
  - ⚠ **NO ACK, NO EXPIRY, NO CRON, NO DELETE, AND NO CONSENT TOGGLE (v1, deliberate).** Rows are the record. The mailbox binds UNCONDITIONALLY in `main/realtime-mailboxes.js`, unlike the other two, and therefore needs no rejoin: a ping buys an external agent no COMPUTE and opens no PRIVATE TURN, which is what those two consents are for.
- **`escalate` — A STRUCTURED QUESTION TO A HUMAN, AND IT IS A POST (Samuel's ruling, 2026-08-31).** Four validated fields — `issue` (one line), `context` (bounded prose), `options` (2–6, each a `label` plus a one-line `consequence`), `recommendation` (`{index, why}`) — that the channels surface renders as a CARD WITH OPTION BUTTONS. In `gating.ts › WRITE_OPS.dopl_channel`, because it writes a real message row.
  - ⚠ **IT DELEGATES TO `opPost` AND THE `kind` IS FIXED AT THE ROUTING SEAM** (`channel.ts`), which is `milestone`'s precedent with a sharper reason: **`dopl-desktop-app/main/targeting.js › classify` returns `ignore` for every `kind !== 'message'`, so a card on any other kind could never notify the human it is asking.** The payload rides RESERVED `metadata.escalation` (§5), never the caller's own metadata.
  - ⚠ **THE BODY CARRIES THE WHOLE QUESTION IN PROSE, AND THAT IS THE DEGRADE CONTRACT.** `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-escalate-render.ts › escalationBody` composes it; four live surfaces know nothing about the metadata key — `op="read"`, a plain browser, the pop-out thread window, and every desktop older than the card — and a stub body would render an empty row on all four. It is a HAND COPY of `src/features/channels/escalation.ts › escalationBody`, paired the way `main/agent-handles.js` is paired with `lib/mentions.ts`: **one fixture table (`ESCALATION_BODY_PARITY_CASES`), driven from both sides**, so either tree changing the render alone fails a suite.
  - ⚠ **`to` IS NOT ROUTED THROUGH, DELIBERATELY.** Addressing a member TRIGGERS their listener and starts THEIR agent (§5) — precisely wrong for a question that exists because only a person can answer it. The @-tag in the body is the inbox mechanism and it starts nobody, so the op's prose and its result both send the agent to one.
  - ⚠ **THE BOUNDS REFUSE IN THE DIRECTION THAT SAYS WHAT TO DO.** The schema enforces 2–6; the handler's sentences distinguish them, because the two have opposite remedies — one option means DO IT (and report with `op="milestone"`), seven means COLLAPSE THEM — and an opaque -32602 gets an agent to pad the list. An out-of-range `recommendation.index` is REFUSED, never dropped: a dropped one posts a card that recommends nothing over an agent that believes it recommended something.
  - ⚠ **THE ANSWER IS PUBLIC, AND THE RESULT SAYS SO.** It arrives as an ordinary message in the same channel, so an agent told nothing would poll a private surface that will never have anything. §5 carries the answer lane and its fence.
- **`create_thread` NOW RUNS FROM A WINDOWLESS DESKTOP SESSION, UNDER THE OPERATOR'S OUTBOUND POSTURE (2026-08-24, Samuel's ruling).** It rides AXIS B's own-channel OUTBOUND lane exactly like `post` and `milestone` — §11 carries the rule and the argument. **What this section owes the reader is the CONSEQUENCE FOR THE TOOL SURFACE:** the op the `THE PROTOCOL` paragraph tells an agent to open with was previously auto-refused on every windowless session in every posture, so an agent taught to start with it could not, and the refusal named a permission prompt rather than anything it could act on. It is now the operator's auto-send setting that decides — auto-send ON sends it, ask-first raises a consent row with the title, the body and the addressee on it. ⚠ **A slug-addressed `create_thread` still gates** (the ID scope, §11), which is why `prompt-framing.js › channelScopeFraming` interpolates the channel UUID.
- **`launch_agent` NOW RUNS FROM A WINDOWLESS DESKTOP SESSION TOO, UNDER BOTH POSTURES AND A RECURSION BOUND (2026-08-25, Samuel's ruling; F-320).** §11 carries the rule, the two-axis conjunction, the depth bound and the refusal vocabulary. **What THIS section owes the reader is the CONSEQUENCE FOR THE TOOL SURFACE:** an agent running on an operator's own machine can now ASK that machine for another agent — the op's whole point, and previously unreachable from a desktop session in every posture — while everything the op's own result copy says is unchanged, because **nothing about the DIRECTIVE lane moved**. The seven refusal words, the local launch-over-MCP toggle that answers them, and *"launched means a machine SAID so"* all still describe what happens AFTER the ask; what changed is only whether a desktop-run agent may make the ask at all. ⚠ **A capped or under-postured session is refused BEFORE the op runs**, by its own machine's permission gate and not by this tool — so that refusal is a permission message, never one of the seven words, and an agent must not read it as `no-bridge`.
  - ⚠ **AND THE `title` DESCRIBE WAS SOFTENED IN THE SAME CHANGE, because the ruling moved what runs first.** It promised the 200-char bound was checked *"here, before the call is made"*; a desktop session decides the call at its own gate before zod ever runs, so a held or refused open answered a permission question where the copy promised a title one. It now says the bound is checked **before anything goes on the wire, once the call is permitted to run** — which is true under both orderings. `channel-schema-caps.test.ts` pins it, with the two other describes that made claims the schema cannot enforce.
- **FOUR TEACHING DEFECTS FIXED 2026-08-24, from a live two-agent adversarial run on v1.19.0.** Every refusal fence held; these are classification and copy. **(1) THE ZERO-TAG DIAGNOSTIC GAINED A FIFTH CAUSE — "YOU TAGGED AN AGENT ID"** (then `channel-post-guidance.ts`'s `tagOutcomeNote`, pinned in the new `channel-zero-tag.test.ts`; ⚠ that renderer was retired on 2026-09-02 with T12 and all five causes now live in `channel-doctrine.ts`, reached from the result's `tags=0/1` verdict), the one cause **both** agents hit independently: mentions resolve against the channel's HUMAN roster, so an agent id can never resolve, while `@<agentid>` in a body is the real, working WAKE the `launch_agent` bullet teaches. The four-cause copy reported a correct action as a probable misspelling and sent the agent to `op="members"` for a name that could not be listed there — ⚠ **so cause (5) deliberately carries NO roster remedy; the remedy sentence still enumerates (2), (3) and (4) by number.** **(2) `get_thread` SAYS IT RETURNS METADATA ONLY** — title, mode, the two parties, timestamps, **no message bodies** — and names `op="read"` with `thread=<id>` as the transcript call, in the op bullet AND on the `thread` param. **(3) `client_msg_id` NAMES ITS TWO DIFFERENT SCOPES:** `post` dedupes PER-AUTHOR (`channel_messages` is unique on `(channel_id, client_msg_id, author_user_id)` since `20260822120000`), `create_thread` dedupes PER-CHANNEL whoever sent it (`channel_tasks` is still `(channel_id, client_msg_id)`, `20260729032037`) — so a colliding key on a thread open hands back **another member's thread with your body posted nowhere** (`service-tasks.ts › convergeOnThread` posts nothing for a non-creator). ⚠ **One sentence covering both would have to be the weaker claim, and the weaker one is wrong in the direction that costs a duplicate.** **(4) THE `since` PARAM CROSS-REFERENCES THE SCOPED-READ NO-CURSOR RULE**, so the two halves meet where an agent looks: the rule lived only in the read RESULT, which is read after the poisoned page is already in context. `channel-thread-scope.test.ts` joins the two ends.
- **Keep the `channel-` filename prefix on any further split** — the parity split-scan auto-discovers `channel-*.ts`, and a handler in an unprefixed file is invisible to the drift guards.
- **MCP CREDITS ARE CHARGED AT THE REGISTRAR, EXACTLY ONCE PER TOOL CALL.** The seam is `registrar.ts › createCreditedRunner`'s `runWithCredits`, called at the SAME two terminal paths `runWithEntitlementGuard` was (the `workspace=`-arg branch and the session-default branch) — it was WIDENED rather than joined by a sibling so no future path can charge twice or not at all. Order is fixed: **after `gates.opRefusal`** (the delete refusal stays first and unconditional — a refused delete costs zero round trips), **after workspace resolution** (credits are per-workspace), **before the handler**. The charge is a loopback `POST /api/mcp/credits/consume` through `@dopl/client › consumeCredits`, +1 awaited request per tool call. **Never move it into `withWorkspaceAuth` beside `logMcpToolCall`** — that fires per loopback request, so one tool call would decrement 0..N times. **FAIL OPEN:** a consume call that throws ALLOWS the tool call and logs, and so does a 200 whose body does not parse into the expected shape — **ONLY an explicit `allowed === false` refuses** (`tools/respond.ts › creditsExhausted`, rendered with the server-supplied upgrade URL). A truthiness test on `outcome.allowed` reads a missing key as a refusal, i.e. fails open on the rare path and closed on the likely one; a body that does not say "no" is not a no. **Meta-tools are exempt by DECISION, not by F-146's accident** — `current_workspace`/`list_workspaces` are how a lost agent finds out where it is, and are user-scoped, not workspace-scoped; metering them means calling the charge EXPLICITLY on both paths, as `opRefusal` is. Numbers live in ONE place, `src/features/billing/credits.ts`; pinned by `packages/mcp-server/src/credits.test.ts`.
  - 🔒 ⚠ **THE FAIL-OPEN IS FOR TRANSPORT FAILURES, AND AN AUTH REFUSAL IS NOT ONE — that distinction cost a whole caller class (F-325, fixed 2026-08-26).** Until the consume route's floor came down to `guest` (§4A), every guest-scoped charge 403'd, this `catch` swallowed it, and **guest tool calls ran free with one log line each.** A 403 must never be the normal path for anybody: **when this layer logs, the charge did not happen for a reason it did not understand.** An unbillable container answers **200 `degraded: true`** instead — the SERVICE decides that and logs it server-side, and the registrar must treat it as an ordinary allowed answer, logging nothing. Pinned in `credits.test.ts` ("a degraded-but-ALLOWED 200 proceeds and logs NOTHING here").
- **THE CREDIT WINDOW IS DECIDED BY THE ENTITLEMENT VERDICT, AND A FREE VERDICT IGNORES THE SUBSCRIPTION ANCHOR.** `credits.ts › resolveCreditPeriod` takes the verdict (`server/entitlements.ts › entitledPlanFor`, the same `paidEntitlement` logic `getWorkspaceEntitlements` runs) and reads it FIRST: paid → the workspace's own billing-date window; **free → always the UTC calendar month, whatever anchor the row carries.** That is the self-heal for a cancellation — a row canceled mid-period keeps an anchor ending in the FUTURE, and honouring it would charge the first free-plan call to the key the paid plan had been spending against (used already past 500), locking the workspace out of MCP until the dead period expired. The webhook also nulls both anchors on **every** cancel path, checkout included; the two halves are belt and braces, because the rule heals rows no webhook reaches.
- **ENFORCEMENT AND THE SETTINGS METER PASS THE SAME VERDICT** (`credits-service.ts › consumeMcpCredits` and `summarizeCredits`, both through `creditPeriodFor(billing, plan)`). A meter reading a different window than the gate charged shows a used/limit pair that does not explain the refusal.
- **THE CONSUME PATH HAS A QUERY BUDGET: THREE ROUND TRIPS** — the billing row and the member count (concurrent), then the RPC. It runs once per tool call, so **do not call `getWorkspaceEntitlements` from it**: that fans out to a `COUNT(*)` over `ontology_objects` for a cap this path never consults, and re-reading `workspace_billing` for the period made it five. `entitledPlanFor` is the lean verdict; ONE billing row feeds both the verdict and the period. Pinned by mock CALL COUNTS in `server/credits-service.test.ts`.
- **An `X-MCP-Tool` name starting with `_` is an INTERNAL call and is NOT logged to `mcp_tool_calls`** (`shared/auth/with-workspace-auth.ts`). `_mcp_credits_consume` fires on every tool call; logging it would add an analytics insert per call and put a synthetic tool at the top of every usage query. The header is still sent.
- 🔒 **THE AGENT AUDIENCE CEILING — ONE FENCE, TWO TRIPWIRES, AND THE DIFFERENCE MAY NEVER BE DRESSED AWAY (Home Knowledge Panels M5, 2026-08-26; plan `docs/specs/home-knowledge-panels.plan.md` §4).** An agent acting inside a `kind='link'` container that has a PEER in it reaches only the knowledge granted into that container's channels. **The threat model is the whole design:** a spawned agent holds a 90-day device token on disk and a `full` profile has Bash, so it can issue any HTTP the operator could. Everything narrowing a PATH is therefore a tripwire; only what refuses inside the server, or rides the credential, is a fence.
  - ⚠ **AND SINCE 2026-09-01 IT FENCES THE CREATE, NOT ONLY THE READS (F-323's authoring half, closed).** `knowledge/server/service-base-writes.ts › assertCreatorCanReadItBack` runs `resolveAgentAudience` BEFORE the insert and refuses the `granted` branch. Until then `createBase` composed no ceiling at all while EVERY read composed one, and a new base carries no grant by construction — so an agent in a shared container wrote rows that were invisible to their own creator from the very next call, behind a SUCCESS string. **Refusal is the only available answer:** granting the base into the container's channel is what would make it reachable, and `setChannelKnowledgeGrant` refuses an agent outright because that grant decides what the PEER may read. ⚠ It narrows only the `granted` branch — any human, any standard workspace and any SOLO container are untouched, so it reaches exactly the population for which the write was already useless.

  | Layer | Where | Kind |
  |---|---|---|
  | **A** — the grant fence | `knowledge/server/service-audience.ts › resolveAgentAudience`, applied at `service-bases.ts`'s foundational lookups | 🔒 **FENCE** — every input is a DB fact on the service client |
  | **B1** — the credential lock | `mcp_tokens.workspace_id` → `apiKeyWorkspaceId` → `withWorkspaceAuth`'s 403 (§4) | 🔒 **FENCE** — rides the credential, so it binds the process AND its children. ⚠ **WORKSPACE AXIS ONLY** — the row's `workspace_lock_kind` is a separate field on a separate axis and is read by `credential-audience.ts › isSharedCredential`, never by a workspace gate (F-336) |
  | **B3** — the directory lock | `factory.ts › bootServer` → `workspace-directory.ts`'s `lockedTo` | ⚠ **TRIPWIRE** — one MCP connection's view |
  | **B2** — the desktop belt | `main/session-audience.js`, `grantDecision` step 1.5 (§11) | ⚠ **TRIPWIRE** — one machine's permission gate |

  - **LAYER A'S DECISION TABLE, AND THREE OF ITS FOUR BRANCHES CHANGE NOTHING.** `source !== "agent"` → unrestricted (humans unaffected); `workspace.kind !== 'link'` → unrestricted; **active members ≤ 1 → unrestricted (SOLO, today's behaviour)**; else reachable **iff** a grant row at EITHER level (`agent_only` OR `visible`) on one of the container's channels, and everything else is the same 404 an invisible base throws. **It can only ever CLOSE.** ⚠ The SOLO carve-out is deliberate at every layer: a one-member container is the operator's primary agent surface (§4A's traffic-profile bullet), and there is no second audience in it to bound. ⚠ **CONSEQUENCE, WHICH IS RULING 2 AND NOT AN ACCIDENT:** a scope-B base (private, in the container, ungranted) is INVISIBLE to the operator's own agent there.
  - 🔒 ✅ **AND THE REMEDY RULING 2 NAMES WORKS — F-336 RESOLVED 2026-08-27 (Samuel: "option B"). A scope-B base is invisible to the operator's own agent UNTIL IT IS GRANTED `agent_only` (or `visible`) ON ONE OF THIS CONTAINER'S CHANNELS**, and then it is readable. ⚠ **IT DID NOT WORK BETWEEN 2026-08-26 AND THAT RULING, AND THE MECHANISM IS WORTH KEEPING:** `service-bases.ts › getBaseById` runs `assertBaseVisible` **BEFORE** `assertWithinAudience`, and `canSeeBase` answered false for ANY non-public base once `apiKeyWorkspaceId` was set — so the grant row was never consulted and the `agent_only` level was decoration. **THE FIX WAS NOT A FENCE WIDENING.** B1 was trespassing on layer A's axis: a locked credential is a WORKSPACE fence, and `canSeeBase` was reading it as a VISIBILITY fence. The predicate now asks `shared/auth/credential-audience.ts › isSharedCredential` — a container-SESSION credential is one human's session and reads that human's private rows; a credential that may be shared between humans still reads none, and an unstated lock kind counts as shared. **Three refusals, three different fences, one 404:** ungranted → layer A; another workspace → B1's 403 / the service's workspace check; a PEER's private base → `canSeeBase` itself. Pinned by `knowledge/server/service-container-credential.test.ts`.
  - 🔒 **F-327-PROOFING, AND THE SENTENCE THAT LICENSES IT.** The ceiling takes the **SET** of the container's channels, because nothing enforces one-channel-per-container. `X-Dopl-Session-Id` parsed `<channelId>:<tail>` may then NARROW that set to one channel **iff the id is already in it**; anything else is ignored ENTIRELY. **A forgeable input used only to narrow inside an already-fenced set is safe** — the set is computed from DB facts BEFORE the header is read, every id the header can select was already in it, and selecting one only REMOVES grants. The worst a forged value achieves is fencing the forger out. ⚠ **Do not "improve" it into a lookup**: resolving the named channel against the database would make the header an ADDRESSING input, which is the one power it must not have.
  - **B3, PLAINLY: `getWorkspaceList()` answers `[container]` and `resolveWorkspaceRef` answers `null` for every other id and slug**, and the INSTRUCTIONS table is empty (a locked session already has its one workspace as its resolved pin, and §4A forbids advertising a container anywhere). ⚠ **Bash can open a SECOND, unpinned MCP connection or hit the loopback directly; neither passes through that object.** Pinned by `packages/mcp-server/src/container-lock.test.ts`, whose header says the same thing so a green run cannot be read as containment.
  - 🔒 ⚠ **`memberCount` IS NEW ON `WorkspaceListItem` AND FAILS CLOSED — §8's STALE-FIELD RULE, INVERTED, IN THREE PLACES.** `?? 0`, and **ZERO IS NOT SOLO**, in `factory.ts › bootServer`, `main/session-credential.js › shouldLockSession` and `main/session-audience.js › audienceFor`. The reflex fallback (unknown ⇒ the permissive case) would silently unlock every container for exactly the release window in which a client runs against a server predating the field. ⚠ It is **HAND-MIRRORED** between `src/features/workspaces/types.ts › WorkspaceWithRole` and `packages/dopl-client/src/types.ts › WorkspaceListItem` (plus the tracked `dist/`), and since 2026-08-26 `scripts/check-role-drift.ts › checkWorkspaceMirror` guards that pair — a SECOND family in the same CI step, carrying one recorded pre-existing asymmetry (`iconUrl`, **F-335**) whose allow-list **may only ever shrink**.
  - **THE RESIDUAL, STATED HONESTLY — AND IT IS THREE THINGS, NOT TWO (corrected 2026-08-26).** (1) A session whose B1 mint FAILED (the desktop fails OPEN and logs — a credential endpoint being down must not stop the operator's agent). (2) Anything using the operator's device token OUTSIDE a desktop-spawned session, which the ceiling never claimed to cover. Layer A still refuses the knowledge read in both. (3) 🔴 **AN UNLOCKED CREDENTIAL REACHABLE FROM INSIDE THE LOCKED SESSION.** `mcp-config.js` wrote the operator's unlocked 90-day device token in PLAINTEXT to `userData/mcp-spawn.json`, and `runtime/claude/loader.js › buildSecretPathDenyRules` fences that path for `Read`/`Grep`/`Glob` only — **`Bash` is not on that list and cannot be**, because Claude Code `Bash(...)` rules match COMMAND patterns, not path globs. One `cat` voids B1 for the session, and layer A with it (the ceiling only fires on `apiKeyWorkspaceId`). ⚠ **The real mitigation is that `Bash` is an ESCALATION tool** (§11), so the read gates at every posture except `bypass` — **B1 is therefore a STRONG TRIPWIRE, not the fence F-329 declared it.** ✅ The file is no longer written and is deleted on the next signed-in launch; the encrypted store under the same directory is not, so the CLASS is open. **F-329 is REOPENED with this as residual #3.**
- **`packages/*/dist` is what the app loads at runtime.** After editing either package's `src/`, run `npm run build:packages` or the old surface ships.
- **MCP tool output carries NO web URLs (2026-08-11).** The knowledge/skill file-write results dropped their `webUrl` field and the `View in Dopl: <url>` trailer — the website those URLs named is retired and 302s to `/get-started`. `src/shared/lib/url/resource-url.ts` is deleted; the onboarding bootstrap prompt no longer teaches the trailer or a `{{URL}}` card slot. (Shipped pre-1.10.3 bundles still carry the old prompt text until they auto-update — a cosmetic mismatch, not a contract.)
- **THE PLAYGROUND IS A SECOND DOOR TO THE SAME MCP TRANSPORT, NEVER A SECOND SERVER (2026-08-17).** The public `/playground` page demos a REAL guest workspace: `POST /api/playground/session` (anonymous, per-IP limited) provisions a throwaway `auth.users` row stamped `user_metadata.playground_guest: true`, a workspace through `features/workspaces/server/service.ts › createWorkspaceForUser` (so the starter corpus seeds it), and a 24h full-scope bearer under the reserved `dopl_client_playground` client (`features/playground/server/token.ts`, hash/rand/prefix primitives imported from `shared/auth/mcp-oauth.ts` so the row stays `validateAccessToken`-compatible). `app/api/playground/mcp/[token]/route.ts` exists because desktop MCP clients accept a URL and no headers — it moves the path token into `Authorization` and DELEGATES to `/api/mcp`'s exported handlers, so auth/gating/credits cannot drift; keep its function config identical to `/api/mcp` (nodejs, 300s). The page's panes poll ordinary workspace GETs with the same bearer (`features/playground/session.tsx`). Guests bill as the FREE plan — the credit allowance is the abuse cap, no playground carve-out. Deletion is the reaper cron (`app/api/cron/playground-reaper`, vercel.json): it double-gates on the playground client id AND the guest metadata marker, and skips+logs on any mismatch — never delete on one signal. Both routes are reasoned EXEMPT entries in `shared/auth/write-gate-coverage.test.ts`; `/api/playground` is in `SELF_AUTH_ROUTES` and the `/playground` page in `PUBLIC_ROUTES` (`src/shared/auth/public-routes.ts` — §3, not `proxy.ts`, since 2026-08-23).

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §7 (MCP surface hardening), §8 (MCP narration, MCP identity, F-100/F-104/F-145).

## 11. Desktop session rules

### 11.0 The runtime seam (2026-08-31 — the adapter port, behaviour-preserving half)

- 🔒 **"BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING" IS THE CLAIM. "BYTE-IDENTICAL" IS NOT, AND WAS RETRACTED 2026-09-01.** An independent review diffed the four moved sites against `git show HEAD:` and found the Claude lane observably different from HEAD. **The correct sentence for this whole section is: behaviour-preserving, with SEVEN declared observable differences and THREE narration changes** — enumerated in 11.0g, which is the list to read before assuming an existing Claude session behaves as it did. ⚠ **THE PHRASE MATTERED BECAUSE IT WAS LOAD-BEARING**: "byte-identical" is the reason a reviewer skips re-testing the Claude lane, and it was asserted in a file header (`runtime/claude/launch-spec.js`) that is itself one of the drifted files. ⚠ **WHAT REALLY WAS PRESERVED IS MOST OF THE SURFACE AND IT WAS VERIFIED**: `grantDecision`'s order, verdicts, reason codes and all five Axis-B lanes; `buildSessionToolConfig`'s three profile branches; `main/session-outbound-tag.js` byte-unchanged (`git diff` empty); `triageOptions`' whole body; the `result`-then-`context` ordering; the subagent skip; and the delta clamps. **The drift is in the SEAMS AROUND the moved code, plus two features that rode inside a change described as a move.**
- 🔒 **CORE MAY NOT NAME A RUNTIME. `dopl-desktop-app/main/runtime/` IS THE ONLY PLACE AN ADAPTER IS NAMED, AND `main/runtime/index.js` IS THE ONLY DOOR.** Core asks it for a frozen `descriptor` (pure data) or for one of the sixteen methods in `main/runtime/contract.js › RUNTIME_METHODS`, and nothing else. ⚠ **This is `main/`'s FIRST SUBDIRECTORY** — **143** flat modules beside it @ 2026-09-01 (`ls dopl-desktop-app/main/*.js | wc -l`), against **137** at the last commit (`git show HEAD:dopl-desktop-app/main | grep -c '\.js$'`). ⚠ **THIS SAID "it said 139 before" AND THAT NUMBER NEVER EXISTED IN A COMMITTED STATE** (corrected 2026-09-01): the wave is uncommitted, so the only two measurable states are 137 at HEAD and 143 in the working tree. **Re-measure both; a delta quoted from memory is how a count becomes fiction.** Core keeps the flat convention. **Every file under `main/runtime/**` must be `.js`**: the 500-line cap's glob is `main/**/*.js`, so a `.mjs`/`.ts` file there would match NO eslint block and be UNCAPPED, in the one tree whose stated invariant is that the cap has no exemptions (`test/eslint-config-coverage.test.mjs` walks it).
- **What MOVED to the adapter, and the one test that keeps it there.** `test/core-vocabulary.test.mjs` scans core's CODE LINES (comments are allowed — this repo's comments carry the argument) for `claude` / `anthropic` / `codex` / Cursor's vendor spellings / `sdk.query` / `canUseTool` / `permissionMode` / `app-server`. It carries a DATED `DEFERRED` census naming the files whose extraction belongs to a later step, and **every row must still have a hit**, so the list shrinks and can never grow quietly.
  | Was | Is | Why it could not stay |
  |---|---|---|
  | the SDK loader, deleted as a core module | `main/runtime/claude/loader.js` | the only module that touches the platform SDK. ⚠ **NOT "no behaviour change" — corrected 2026-09-01**: `› buildMcpServers` gained `X-Dopl-Vendor` (11.0g item 1) |
  | `session-query.js › buildSdkOptions` | `runtime/claude/launch-spec.js › buildOptions` | one platform's option vocabulary. `session-query.js` keeps the LIFECYCLE (supersede-before-relaunch, loop tagging, the launch watchdog) and hands back an OPAQUE spec |
  | `session-io.js › handleSdkMessage` / `› sdkRenderEvents` | `runtime/claude/normalize.js` | a platform's message schema. See 11.0a |
  | `session-model.js › observe` | the same `normalize` | it was a SECOND normalizer, and a boundary that owned only the render mapping would have left it in core |
  | `session-io.js › makeCanUseTool` | SPLIT: `main/session-gate-bridge.js` (verdict plumbing, diag line, card payloads, resolver parking — platform-FREE) + `runtime/claude/axis-b.js` (the held-callback wiring and the reply vocabulary) | |
  | `session-profiles.js`'s Axis-A tail — `buildSessionToolConfig`, `TOOL_MODES`, `normalizeToolMode`, `toolModeAllows`, `AUTO_TOOLS`, `BYPASS_TOOLS`, `BYPASS_READS`, `EDIT_TOOLS`, `ESCALATION_TOOLS`, `floorWindowlessTool` | `runtime/claude/tools.js` | a vocabulary of ONE runtime's BUILT-IN tool names. See 11.0b |
  | `session-triage.js`'s option assembly | `runtime/claude/triage.js › triageOptions` | the SECOND spawn shape on the platform, and every field in it is a fence |
  | `agent-self-ops.js › makeAgentOpsServer` | `runtime/claude/axis-b.js` | built with the platform's own MCP-server helpers. Its PURE half (the rename rule, the end-verdict table, the two result shapes) and `AGENT_OPS_TOOL_NAMES` stayed core |
- **What STAYED, and it is most of the system.** `grantDecision`'s ORDER, the four verdicts, the 23 reason codes, `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY`, every Axis-B lane, `floorWindowlessMessage`, the private-turn withdrawal, the grant keys, the reducer, the state machine, the effect table, the timers, the store, targeting, the wake tiers, the narration ring and the whole listener spine. **`mcp__dopl__*` names are runtime-independent** and live in `main/session-dopl-tools.js` (§2 split, so both sides of the seam read them without a cycle); each adapter's `tools.js` COMPOSES them rather than re-deriving them.
- ⚠ **`main/runtime/index.js` IS ELECTRON-FREE AT LOAD, BY CONTRACT** — and **the mechanism is `claude/index.js`'s THREE LAZY REQUIRES, not a discipline the leaf files keep.** `session-profiles.js` is a PURE module two suites slice and evaluate standalone, and it asks the registry for every gate decision, so `runtime/claude/index.js` defers `./loader`, `./launch-spec` and `./normalize` behind arrow functions (`const platform = () => require('./loader')`, and the two beside it). **The leaves themselves require core at TOP LEVEL**: `claude/normalize.js` requires `../../session-io`, `claude/launch-spec.js` requires `../../session-auth`. ⚠ **THIS SAID THE OPPOSITE UNTIL 2026-09-01 — "`normalize.js` / `launch-spec.js` / `triage.js` / `axis-b.js` lazy-require anything that reaches back through `session-io` or `session-auth`" — and TWO OF THE FOUR DO THE REVERSE.** The OUTCOME was true; the stated mechanism was not, and that is the dangerous kind of wrong doc: **an edit making `claude/index.js` eager would break the invariant while every file still satisfied the old sentence.** (`claude/triage.js` really is lazy — `const sessionAuth = () => require('../../session-auth')` — which is presumably where the generalisation came from.) **A top-level require of `runtime/claude/index.js`'s deferred three closes a cycle and hands the gate a half-initialised module at exactly the moment it asks for a deny list.**
- **A session is STAMPED with its runtime at spawn (`s.runtimeId`, `session-engine.js › startSession`) and never re-reads it.** The conversation handle, the tool vocabulary and the Axis-A modes all belong to ONE runtime, so a park or crash resume must not land on another. **An unknown id resolves to the DEFAULT rather than refusing** — a different kind of fail-closed from a gate's: an unknown id is a record written by a build that knew a runtime this one does not, and refusing would strand the session with no way to end it. Nothing is GRANTED by it; every posture and profile is re-derived per call.
- 🔒 **FOUR DECLARATIONS REFUSE INSTEAD OF HIDING** (`main/runtime/contract.js › LAUNCH_BLOCKING`, driven by `test/runtime-contract.test.mjs` + `test/session-engine-slot.test.mjs`, which assert BOTH that the shipped descriptors satisfy each and that one which does not is really rejected). Everywhere else `null` means HIDE the control, never gray it. ⚠ **AND THE FOUR REFUSE FOUR DIFFERENT-SIZED THINGS, EACH ENFORCED WHERE THAT THING HAPPENS** — read the verb in each bullet, because "launch-blocking" is the family name, not the mechanism.
  - **`axisB.enforcementPoint: null` ⇒ the adapter does not register.** Axis B needs an in-process tool boundary OR a held callback and there is nothing else: the Dopl MCP server is remote HTTP (`runtime/claude/loader.js › buildMcpServers`), so main is not in the call path of a channel call, and **no posture field crosses the wire**, so the server cannot refuse the post either. With neither, an agent posts with **no outbound consent card at all** — the gate coming off, not a capability degraded.
  - **`containment.profiles.<p>.denyList: null` ⇒ THE ADAPTER DOES NOT REGISTER.** ⚠ **THIS SAID "that profile is refused at LAUNCH" UNTIL 2026-09-01 AND IT IS WRONG — confirmed by mutation.** The check is in `contract.js › descriptorProblems`, which `sealAdapter` THROWS on at module load, so `capability.js › canLaunchProfile` is unreachable for an adapter that registered at all; the launch-time predicate is the readable-refusal path for a descriptor built by hand in a test, not the enforcement. (§11.0e states it correctly; `contract.js`'s own comment made the same error and was corrected in the same change.) `grantDecision` step 1 reads the list as the gate's OWN first check — the verdict no task grant and no widest mode can open — so a restricted profile with no list in that runtime's vocabulary has NO enforcement at all. A native sandbox bounds the filesystem; it does not deny the delegation, exfil and persistence built-ins those lists exist for. **This is the one place absent does not mean hide, because the control here IS containment.**
    - 🔒 **AN EMPTY LIST IS REFUSED THE SAME WAY, ON EVERY PROFILE BUT `full` (2026-09-01).** `denyList: []` SAYS "this profile denies nothing", which is the identical condition — the gate's first check with nothing to check — and both checks asked only `Array.isArray`, so it registered cleanly. `full` is exempt because its supervision IS Axis A rather than a list (`contract.js › UNRESTRICTED_PROFILE`); every other profile exists *because* it denies something.
    - 🔒 **AND `sealAdapter` NOW READS THE ENFORCED STRUCTURE, NOT ONLY THE DECLARATION** (`contract.js › mirrorProblems`). `grantDecision` step 1 has always read `runtime.toolConfigFor(p).disallowedTools` — the runtime METHOD — and NEVER `descriptor.containment.profiles.<p>.denyList`. **The descriptor field is a MIRROR**, so every rule above validated a mirror of the thing it claims to protect: an adapter hand-writing a plausible descriptor over a `toolConfigFor` that returns `[]` sealed cleanly, `canLaunchProfile` called the profile enforced, the UI rendered containment, and the gate denied nothing. **MEASURED: hardcoding `denyList: []` across every Claude profile failed 0 of 2786 tests; emptying the ENFORCEMENT list fired 16 pins.** All three shipped adapters DERIVE the mirror (`index.js › profileEntry`: `denyList: cfg.disallowedTools.slice()`) — the seal now asserts that derivation as a SET comparison rather than trusting it.
  - **`session.usageResetsOnResume: 'unverified'` ⇒ RESUME is refused; a cold launch is unaffected.** `session-park.js › resumeParked` zeroes both delta baselines; a runtime that CONTINUES the cumulative total makes every delta negative, `session-io.js › applyCoreEvents` clamps it to 0, and `session-state.js › costCapReached` is then never reached — **the budget control silently stops existing, with no error and no symptom until a bill arrives.**
  - 🔒 **`toolMode.windowlessFloor: null` ⇒ THE WINDOWLESS LAUNCH IS REFUSED (added 2026-09-01).** A windowless session has NO GATE SURFACE — `session-windowless.js › claimGate` answers a permission request with a DENY — so Axis A must be floored to a mode that allows the reads the prompt ORDERS the agent to make. With no floor there is nothing to raise to and the session runs at its STORED mode, which STARTS at the narrowest member and RESETS to it on park: **every tool call denied, silently, with the agent reporting it cannot read files it was told to read.** ⚠ **THE RULE WAS WRITTEN IN `capability.js › floorWindowlessTool`'S HEADER FROM THE START AND NOTHING REFUSED IT** — the predicate returned the session's own un-floored mode instead. It now answers `null`, `› windowlessFloorRefusal` carries the sentence, and `session-launch.js › launch` refuses AFTER `acquireRuntime` and BEFORE `startSession`, so nothing is registered and there is no rollback. ⚠ **A FLOOR NAMED BUT ABSENT FROM `toolMode.options` REFUSES IDENTICALLY** — neither is orderable, and picking for either is the guess this refuses. ⚠ **THE REFUSAL RIDES `{skipped:'disabled'}`, NOT AN EIGHTH WIRE WORD**: `launch-directive-wire.js › REFUSAL_REASONS` is the same seven words as `schema-launch.ts`, `service-launch.ts` and a **deployed column CHECK**, so minting one here would be a refusal the database refuses to record. The specific sentence rides the diag, which is the only surface allowed to name a runtime.
- ⚠ **AND ONE DECLARATION *WARNS* RATHER THAN REFUSING: `axisB.opScoped` (2026-09-01).** `false` or `'unverified'` collapses Axis B from op-scoped to whole-tool — every channel call gates, READS INCLUDED. **It is a WARNING because the direction of failure is CLOSED and that was traced end to end:** input the gate cannot read fails `› postFieldsOk`, `grantDecision` answers `'gate'`, and a windowless gate DENIES. The agent is broken; the boundary is not. `capability.js › axisBOpScopedWarning` is the sentence and `session-launch.js` logs it at the spawn. ⚠ **THE PREDICATE HAD ZERO CONSUMERS UNTIL THIS** — declared, documented in the strongest terms, and read by nothing in `main/` or `src/`, so the operator got no warning and the launch was not refused. **Codex declares `'unverified'` today, so this fires on the shipped tree.**
- **`meter.cost: null` HIDES the cost cap rather than rendering one that cannot fire** — the `null`-means-unmeasured rule applied to a control instead of a number.

#### 11.0a The normalizer is the seam, and it owns ALL THREE raw-message consumers

- The consume loop called **three** things on every platform message and each read the platform's schema directly: the auth-sentinel hold (which SHORT-CIRCUITS the loop), the render mapping, and the per-message usage watcher. It now calls **one**: `rt.normalize(msg, ctx)` → `CoreEvent[]`, applied by `session-io.js › applyCoreEvents`. **A boundary owning only the render mapping would have left two platform-shaped parsers in core and made the golden fixtures cover a third of the surface.**
- ⚠ **`normalize` IS PURE — no I/O, no dispatch, no session mutation.** That is what makes a later adapter testable from a recorded transcript with nothing installed, which is the only honest answer to "no live installs". So the state it used to write travels on events and CORE writes it: the conversation handle and the durable record on `launched`; the **cumulative** cost/token totals on `result`, with the DELTA arithmetic staying in core beside the resume baselines it is the twin of; and the meter's last reading as a `context` event per assistant message, which core remembers and dispatches when the turn ends. **`result` still dispatches BEFORE that turn's `context`** — the order the two consumers ran in, and observable.
- **The event vocabulary is the reducer's, unchanged** (`main/runtime/events.js`). Not one of its names is a vendor's; the reducer was platform-neutral all along and only looked coupled because the thing producing the events read SDK messages directly.
- A REJECTION goes through the same normalizer as a message, as a synthetic `{type:'error', text}`: **the runtime decides what is auth-shaped**, because a rejection string is as platform-specific as a message. The hold BOOKKEEPING (`session-auth.js › holdIfAuthFailure`, `resumeAfterSignIn`, the release path) is core. ⚠ The sentinel REGEXES themselves are still in `main/session-auth-detect.js` beside the operator copy they belong to — a deliberate deviation, so the port's credential step moves the banner copy, the five vendor-named modules and the IPC channel together rather than leaving a second copy of a pattern the headless path pins against.

#### 11.0b Axis A is the runtime's; Axis B is Dopl's — and the split IS the argument

- **Axis A's modes and lists are one runtime's built-in tool vocabulary.** A runtime storing Axis A as its own words would resolve every call to the most restrictive mode → `false` → `gate` → and, on a windowless session, **DENY**. So `descriptor.toolMode.options` declares the modes **narrowest FIRST** — `[0]` is where every unknown value fail-closes and the LAST entry is the widest, which is how the windowless floor stays widen-only and how "is this tool classified at all" is asked without core holding a copy of any list (`session-profiles.js › isClassifiedTool`).
- **Axis B did NOT move.** `ask | auto_inbound | auto_outbound | auto_both` names no platform tool and no platform mode. **So the two windowless floors are now in different files, and that asymmetry is the point:** `floorWindowlessMessage` stays in `session-profiles.js` (it moves between members of an enum every runtime shares) while `floorWindowlessTool` is `descriptor.toolMode.windowlessFloor`, applied by `main/runtime/capability.js › floorWindowlessTool`. **Each half still carries the other's argument in prose** — pinned by `test/session-tool-floor.test.mjs`, which asserts the cross-reference rather than the old adjacency.
- **`grantDecision` gained ONE argument and it decides nothing.** `a.runtime` names WHICH vocabulary steps 1 and 4 are asked in; the order, the verdicts and every Axis-B lane are identical on every runtime, and absent resolves to the default.
- **The SPA/desktop posture parity pin now has TWO winners.** `settings-agent-posture.test.tsx` reads Axis A off `runtime/claude/index.js › descriptor.toolMode` and Axis B off `session-profiles.js`, and derives the declaring files **per axis**. The invariant is unchanged and is not about literals: **the SPA must never offer a mode the desktop would coerce away**, because such a mode is written, acknowledged, re-rendered as the operator's choice, and applied as the most restrictive value on every launch.

#### 11.0c The vendor is a SECOND header dimension, never a fourth runtime value

- 🔒 **`X-Dopl-Runtime` IS CUSTODY; `X-Dopl-Vendor` IS VENDOR.** A Dopl-driven Codex or Cursor session is still a session the desktop app spawned, so it stamps `desktop-session` like every other. ⚠ **Three live sites read the custody word by strict equality or array membership** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/identity.ts › runtimeWord`, `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-wake-guidance.ts`, `dopl-desktop-app/main/targeting.js › DESKTOP_RUNTIMES` — so **adding a vendor word there would not widen the set; it would drop every non-Claude session OUT of it**, silently changing the wake guidance those sessions are taught and whether a requester window opens.
- `src/shared/auth/runtime-header.ts` owns both readers (`readVendorHeader` / `narrowVendor`, recognized values only, exact match). **Absent stays absent** — an older desktop build stamps custody and no vendor, and reading `desktop-session` as "therefore Claude" is the conflation the second header exists to prevent. `dopl-desktop-app/test/runtime-stamp-literals.test.mjs` pins both literals against the server's constants across a join with no shared module, and asserts the custody enum did not grow a vendor word; `channel-wake-runtime.test.ts` carries the regression that a non-Claude desktop session still lands in the desktop branch.

#### 11.0d The SECOND adapter (2026-08-31) — `main/runtime/codex/`, and what it declares it cannot do

- **It cost one `register(require('./codex'))` line in `main/runtime/index.js` and a directory.** No core module gained a branch, and every case in `test/runtime-contract.test.mjs` applied to it at registration. **`registry.ids()` → `['claude','codex','cursor']` @ 2026-09-01** (⚠ this read `['claude','codex']` until then — the THIRD adapter landed in the same wave; §11.0e carries the measurement command). **Registration ORDER is load-bearing** — `DEFAULT_ID` is the first registered, so a session record carrying no runtime id still resolves to the runtime it ran on.
- **The target is `codex app-server` over stdio, not the SDK and not `codex exec`.** Both of those fix policy at LAUNCH and have no mid-turn approval handshake, so on either one Dopl's gate would be a pre-flight list and the outbound consent card would have no mechanism. **`main/runtime/codex/client.js` is the only module that frames JSON-RPC** — ⚠ **it is NOT the only one that spawns a child, corrected 2026-09-01**: `codex/credential.js` runs `execFile('codex', ['login','status'])` as its credential probe. The JSON-RPC half of the old sentence was the load-bearing half and is unchanged; the spawn half was simply false, and "one module spawns children" is exactly the kind of claim a reviewer relies on when auditing where a binary can be invoked (`grep -rn "execFile\|spawn(" dopl-desktop-app/main/runtime/codex/`).
