- 🔒 **AXIS A'S FOUR MODES ARE `approval_policy`'S OWN VALUES, ORDERED NARROWEST FIRST: `untrusted` / `granular` / `on-request` / `never`.** ⚠ **`granular` sits SECOND, not last, and the design's §1.4 table was wrong about this.** That array is an ORDERING — `[0]` is the fail-closed member and the LAST entry is the widest — so listing `granular` last would declare it the widest mode and make a tightly-configured granular session immune to the windowless floor. Dopl's own gate treats `granular` as no wider than `untrusted`, because its five categories are configured on Codex's side and this process cannot read them. `on-failure` is not offered: documented as deprecated.
- **The gate is asked about CODEX'S OWN WORDS, and there are only three kinds.** `commandExecution` and `fileChange` (the app-server's approval-request item names), the five `granular` category names, and MCP tool names. `main/runtime/codex/approval.js › toolNameFor` is the ONE place a raw request becomes one of them; an unrecognised method answers its own raw string, so a shape a later CLI adds GATES rather than inheriting a granted one.
- 🔒 **A RESTRICTED PROFILE SHIPS BOTH LAYERS: the native pair AND a deny list.** `read_only` / `dopl_only` pin `sandbox_mode='read-only'` + `approval_policy='untrusted'` (the operator's Axis-A pick does not move them) **and** deny `commandExecution`, `fileChange`, `sandbox_approval`, `request_permissions`, `skill_approval` plus the Dopl surface. `full` is `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` and pins no native pair. **Nothing is pre-approved on any profile** — Codex raises no approval request for a read, so there is no name to pre-approve and listing one would only create a shadow past the gate.
  - ⚠ **TWO OF CLAUDE'S FIVE HARM GROUPS CANNOT BE DENIED HERE AND THAT IS RECORDED, NOT PAPERED OVER.** Delegation (subagents: `/agent`, `/subagents`, the `SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop` hooks) and persistence/scheduling have **no documented name to deny and no documented switch** — the research marks subagents unverified for adapter purposes. **So a Codex session at `read_only` or `dopl_only` is not PROVEN unable to spawn a subagent that does not inherit its bound.** Smoke items C25/C26; it is Samuel's open question 2 in concrete form.
  - ⚠ **`dopl_only` IS STRICTLY NARROWER HERE THAN ON CLAUDE**: it reaches the web there (`WEB_TOOLS`) and cannot here, because network is a property of the sandbox rather than a named tool and the escalation that would open it is on this profile's deny list.
- 🔒 **AXIS B'S PIN IS PER-MCP-TOOL AND INDEPENDENT OF AXIS A.** `tools.dopl_channel.approval_mode = 'prompt'` (with `default_tools_approval_mode = 'writes'`) is what guarantees a channel post reaches the gate however wide the operator's `approval_policy` is — because **no tool posture can send a message**, on any runtime. ⚠ Which of `prompt` / `approve` is the unconditional ask is smoke item C24, and Axis B's whole enforcement point rides on it.
- ⚠ **THE HELD CALLBACK ANSWERS IN CORE'S VERDICT VOCABULARY (`{behavior, message}`), AND THE WIRE TRANSLATION HAPPENS IN `launch-spec.js › makeApprovalHandler`.** `session-permissions.js` (the operator's own click), `session-outbound-tag.js` and `session-outbound.js › wrapGate` all mint or read that shape, so an adapter answering `{decision:'accept'}` upstream of `wrapGate` would leave every auto-allowed post's inline card on screen forever. **That contract is undeclared — F-382.**
- **The forced thread tag travels the `PreToolUse` hook, verdict-free.** One place decides (the approval callback), one place stamps (`axis-b.js › preToolUseStamp`, which returns `{updatedInput}` and NEVER a `decision` key). ⚠ **NO HOOK CONFIGURATION IS WRITTEN IN v1**: `~/.codex/hooks.json` is a shared file and non-managed hooks must be trusted through a TUI command. Smoke items C6 (does a decision-less return pass through), C17 (trust), C18 (a per-launch transport).
- **The bearer NEVER touches argv.** `-c` overrides appear in the child's command line, so the token rides the child's ENV and only its variable NAME is passed: `bearer_token_env_var` + `env_http_headers` for the workspace and session pins. No token → no `mcp_servers.dopl` entry at all, and the session still launches.
- **`--ignore-user-config` is the fence**, first in argv. The operator's `~/.codex/config.toml` cannot widen a session Dopl launched. ⚠ It is documented under CLI config layering, not on the `app-server` subcommand (C9) — an unknown flag fails the spawn LOUDLY, which is the safe failure.
- 🔒 **FOUR HONEST REFUSALS, EACH ONE A DECLARATION RATHER THAN A GAP.**
  - **`meter.cost: null`** — nothing in the research says Codex reports a USD cost; `total_cost_usd` is Claude's field. **The cost cap is HIDDEN, not zeroed** (C11). Samuel's open question 4.
  - **`session.usageResetsOnResume: 'unverified'`** — `thread/resume`'s usage semantics are unmeasured (C8), so **RESUME IS REFUSED**; `launch-spec.js › resume` asks `capability.js › canResume` rather than restating the rule. Cold launch unaffected.
  - **`triage: null`** — two of the four triage fences (`maxTurns: 1`, "offer no tools at all") have no documented analogue (C5), and `turnBound: null` is launch-blocking. **Codex does not run wake tier 3**; `session-triage.js › claimOne` reads an unavailable runtime as a PASS, so the room stays asleep rather than hanging. Every other wake tier is unaffected.
  - **`credential.interactiveSignIn: null`** — `codex login` opens a browser and `--device-auth` needs a terminal; neither is driveable in-window. `signIn()` answers `null` and the UI shows a pointer, not a button that opens nothing (C20).
- **`packaging.delivery: 'path'`** (decided 2026-08-31; design §2.4 amendment, flagged for Samuel's end review). The `codex` binary is the OPERATOR'S, found on PATH at acquire time, so `available()` is a real probe with an operator-readable refusal and `versionPin` is `null` — a pin would be a claim about a binary this release does not ship. Bundling would add a second notarisation lane to the release and cannot be chosen honestly before C16 is measured.
- ⚠ **`mcp.toolNamePrefix` IS `null` AND IT IS THE HIGHEST-STAKE UNKNOWN.** The research never prints a Codex MCP tool name. `main/mcp-tool-names.js` handles the bare form and `mcp__<server>__`; **a third shape misses every list in the gate at once — Axis B, the pre-approvals, both Axis-A lists and hard-deny — which is F-139 reproduced**, and F-139 presented as "the posture did not stick". Smoke item C22: capture a real name from an `item/started` frame.
- **Fixtures, not installs.** `test/codex-normalize.test.mjs` (11 cases) drives the pure normalizer from synthetic frames whose shapes are cited, per fixture, to a section of the Codex platform research (which lives OUTSIDE this repo, so it is named in prose rather than cited as a path), including a garbage payload that must still render a card; `test/codex-gate.test.mjs` (27 cases) drives the v2.9 invariant BOTH ways against this adapter, the containment table, the windowless floor resolving to a Codex mode, the no-`acceptForSession` refusal, and the stamp.
- ⚠ **THREE CONFORMANCE CASES WERE REPAIRED BY THE SECOND ADAPTER, NOT WORKED AROUND — each was correct at one adapter and wrong at two.** (1) `runtime-contract.test.mjs`'s resume case asserted "a runtime that declares `resume` MUST be able to resume", which is the OPPOSITE of the field's design; it now asserts a resume is never SILENTLY allowed over an unmeasured baseline, and that a refusal is readable. (2) Its unknown-id case listed `"codex"` among the junk values, so it began asserting that a REAL runtime resolves to the default; the junk is now words no runtime will ever be, plus the inverse assertion that every registered id resolves to itself. (3) `adapter-parity.test.mjs`'s "declared absent somewhere" case ran over EVERY field, including ones that can never be null (`entryFile` is refused as a non-string; `axisB.enforcementPoint` refuses registration) — it now runs over the NULLABLE subset, with a dated `PENDING_UNTIL_CURSOR` census for the six fields that cannot vary until the third adapter, which has its own staleness case and empties itself at three.

#### 11.0e The THIRD adapter and the INTEGRATION (2026-08-31, port wave D) — core actually reaches the adapters

- **`registry.ids()` → `['claude','codex','cursor']` @ 2026-08-31** (`cd dopl-desktop-app && node -e "console.log(require('./main/runtime').ids())"`). The third cost one `register(require('./cursor'))` line and a directory, exactly as the second did. ⚠ **IT REGISTERS AND IT DOES NOT SHIP.** `descriptor.session.interrupt` is `'unverified'` — the SDK documents no interrupt — and Dopl may not own a session it cannot stop. Registration is what makes the conformance suite MEASURE it; the ship gate is Samuel's (smoke item X0).
- 🔒 **A RUNTIME PICK IS A CHOICE BETWEEN ENFORCED ALTERNATIVES, NOT A CONTAINMENT INPUT — WHICH IS WHY IT MAY TRAVEL WHERE A TOOL PROFILE MAY NOT.** `main/runtime/contract.js › sealAdapter` REFUSES to register an adapter that declares no Axis-B enforcement point, or a profile with no deny list in that runtime's own vocabulary — so "registered" already means "this build proved its gate". Every adapter re-derives its own deny lists (`runtime.toolConfigFor`) and its own Axis-A vocabulary (`runtime.axisAAllows`), and the four `grantDecision` steps AHEAD of Axis A (hard-deny, the audience belt, the Axis-B branch, the profile's deny list) are core's on all three. **There is no runtime whose selection opens something another runtime closed.** `main/channel-runtime.js` is the one spelling of this argument.
- **THE PICK IS THE `model`'S SHAPE, NOT THE POSTURE'S — durable per channel, and it has FOUR readers on purpose.** `main/channel-runtime.js › getChannelRuntime` (`electron-store`, key `channelRuntime`, local-only, never POSTed). Read by the three LAUNCH lanes — `session-launch-op.js › launchFromButton` (the operator's button, where the launch sheet's own pick outranks it), `trigger.js › launchResponderSession` (the peer-triggered responder) and `launch-directives.js › spawn` (the orchestrator lane) — **plus `channel-dir-ipc.js`, which serves the pick back to the SPA's posture row** (`grep -rn getChannelRuntime dopl-desktop-app/main/`: 4 files, 5 call sites @ 2026-09-01). ⚠ **THIS SAID "THREE" UNTIL 2026-09-01 AND THE MISCOUNT BLUNTED THE POINT IT WAS MAKING.** The sentence's whole force is a CONSUMER-COUNT CONTRAST with the durable permission pair, **whose one consumer IS `channel-dir-ipc.js`** — so like for like the contrast is **4-vs-1**, not 3-vs-1, and the omitted reader was the very file the other side of the comparison names. **THE CONTRAST ITSELF IS UNCHANGED AND IS WHAT KEEPS H2 CLOSED** — a posture may only reach a launch a human is attending. A runtime may reach every lane, because a channel whose operator chose one would otherwise answer its peers on a different vendor's model against a different credential, which is the surprise the port exists to remove.
- **`''` IS THE ONLY SPELLING OF "NO PICK", AND AN UNKNOWN ID CLEARS RATHER THAN PARKS.** `› normalizeRuntimeId` validates against `runtime/index.js › ids()` — never a second list — and answers `''` for anything else; `› setChannelRuntime` DELETES the key rather than storing a value this build cannot resolve. Reading NEVER repairs: a stored id from a build that knew a runtime this one does not is left alone (re-upgrade and the pick is still there) while the launch lands on the default. An unreadable store is the DEFAULT runtime, **not** a refusal — the opposite direction from `orchestrator-consent.js`, and the difference is what the record means: that one is a consent, this one is a choice between alternatives this build has already proved.
- 🔒 **THE SESSION'S RUNTIME IS PERSISTED, OR A CRASH RESUME LANDS ON THE WRONG VENDOR.** `session-io.js › baseRecord` and `session-store.js › durableSessionRecord` both carry `runtimeId`. `session-park.js › startResume` rebuilds the whole session from that record and hands the persisted `sdkSessionId` to whatever runtime it acquires — so without the field it resumed onto the DEFAULT adapter, asking it to continue ANOTHER PLATFORM's conversation id in another platform's tool vocabulary against another credential. **Coerced by CHARSET inline (`/^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,30}$/`), not against the registry**, because that projection is evaluated standalone by the extraction tests; `runtime/index.js › resolve` is what turns an unknown id into the default. `null` = a record written before the field, which reads as the runtime it really ran on.
- 🔒 **RESUME IS A DECLARED CAPABILITY AND AN UNVERIFIED USAGE METER REFUSES IT — COLD LAUNCH IS UNAFFECTED.** Both resume shapes (`session-park.js › resumeParked`, `› startResume`) ask `runtime/capability.js › resumeRefusal` before they rebuild anything, and refuse IN PLACE with the descriptor's own sentence: nothing is torn down, the session stays parked, and the next wake retries the moment the answer lands. **What is at stake is the COST CAP, not the resume.** `resumeParked` zeroes both delta baselines on the assumption that the runtime restarts its cumulative total; a runtime that CONTINUES it makes every later delta negative, `session-io.js` clamps that to zero, and `session-state.js › costCapReached` — fed by that one number — is never reached. The budget control stops existing with no error and no symptom until a bill arrives. ⚠ **TWO OF THE THREE ADAPTERS ANSWER `'unverified'` TODAY** (smoke items C8, X4), so this is the shipped answer for both native runtimes and not a hypothetical branch.
- **`'no-sdk'` IS STILL THE ONE REFUSAL WORD FOR "THIS MACHINE HAS NO AGENT RUNTIME", ON EVERY RUNTIME.** `session-launch.js › launch` forwards `a.runtime` to `acquireRuntime` and invents nothing; a lane that passes nothing lands on the default, which is what every pre-port lane did. **Do not widen the skip vocabulary for a second adapter** — `trigger.js` and the directive lane both branch on the word.
- **THE PICK AND THE DESCRIPTOR TABLE RIDE THE EXISTING POSTURE PAIR, NOT A FOURTH IPC OP.** `channels:getLaunchPosture` answers `{tools, messages, model, runtime, runtimes, defaultRuntime}` and `channels:setLaunchPosture` accepts a `runtime` key (own-key: omitting it leaves the pick alone). ⚠ **THIS IS THE `model` FIELD'S DOCUMENTED IDIOM** (`src/features/channels/lib/permission-modes.ts › hasModelKey`: the model rides the existing pair "because there is no new op to feature-detect on, which is what the rest of this family does") **AND THERE IS A HARD CONSTRAINT BEHIND IT**: `renderer/app-preload.js` is AT the 500-line §1 cap and `test/preload-parity.test.mjs` asserts that no preload requires anything but `electron`, so it has no split seam and cannot grow a bridge NAMESPACE without changing that security invariant (F-387). The descriptors survive the structured-clone hop because `contract.js › sealAdapter` deep-freezes them and REFUSES one carrying a function.
- ⚠ **THE RUNTIME PICK IS **NOT** FANNED OUT TO LIVE SESSIONS, and the asymmetry with `applyPostureToLive` is the rule.** That fan-out widens SUPERVISION on a running agent; a session's runtime is stamped at spawn and never read live, because re-pointing one would hand one platform's conversation id to another platform's adapter.
- ⚠ **NINE DESKTOP FILES SIT AT EXACTLY 500 LINES @ 2026-08-31, SEVEN OF THEM CORE SESSION MODULES** (`cd dopl-desktop-app && find main renderer test -type f \( -name '*.js' -o -name '*.mjs' \) | xargs wc -l | awk '$1==500 && $2!="total" {print $2}'`). `max-lines: 500` fails at 501, so all nine are GREEN and invisible to the lint gate — and **a file at the cap does not stop growing, it stops being CORRECTABLE**. This is not a coincidence: a hard cap with no headroom band converges files onto its own boundary. **RUN the command; do not reason about it** — F-388's own first draft asserted "two" and was wrong by seven. `main/session-ipc-ops.js` is on the list and is where the port's step 6 (`claude:signIn` → `runtime:signIn`) lands, so the next wave is already blocked the same way. F-387 (`renderer/app-preload.js`, the one with NO split seam) and F-388 carry it.
- **A DECLARED `null` IS NOT A FAILED CALL.** `runtime.triageSpec()` answers `null` on the two native adapters by design (no `maxTurns` analogue, no "offer no tools" option — smoke items C5, X6), and `session-triage.js › claimOne` now says so as itself. It used to dereference the `null` and report `"triage: call failed — Cannot read properties of null (reading 'start') (reads as PASS)"` — a TypeError an operator reads as a platform fault, for a call never made, once per dormant candidate per guest message, forever. **The VERDICT was right and is unchanged (a runtime that cannot triage does not claim); the SENTENCE was not.**

#### 11.0f The SPA half of wave D (2026-08-31) — the UI consumes the descriptor

- 🔒 **ONE MODULE ON THE WEB SIDE MAY INTERPRET A DESCRIPTOR'S `null`, AND IT IS `src/features/channels/lib/runtime-capability.ts`** — the mirror of `dopl-desktop-app/main/runtime/capability.js`, rule for rule. **No component may write `descriptor.session.interrupt == null`.** ABSENT hides a control almost everywhere and REFUSES in three places (interrupt, resume, a profile with no deny list), so an inlined check gets the common meaning and is silently wrong at exactly the three that matter — here that is a Stop button that vanished with no reason. ⚠ **NO HOOK, NO BRIDGE, NO REACT**, the rule `permission-modes.ts` states and `agent-models.ts` follows: its ONE reason to change is that `capability.js` changed. Pinned by `src/features/channels/lib/runtime-capability.test.ts`, which drives the REAL adapters via `› runtime-descriptors-harness.ts` (`createRequire` over `dopl-desktop-app/main/runtime`) rather than fixtures — a fixture that mirrors an adapter is a copy that drifts with it.
- **`runtime-capability.ts › hasRuntimeKey` IS THE CAPABILITY PROBE AND IT IS THE TWIN OF `permission-modes.ts › hasModelKey`, NOT A NEW IDEA.** An OWN-KEY test over the raw `getLaunchPosture` reply: `runtime: ''` is a current desktop saying "no pick, the DEFAULT adapter applies"; NO KEY is a desktop with no runtime concept, and it renders **NO runtime row at all** — such a build DROPS the field on write, so a live row would let the operator pick Codex and launch every agent on Claude with nothing saying so (§11's UNKNOWN-is-not-EMPTY rule). `hooks/use-channel-launch-posture.ts` latches it to true, exactly as it latches `modelSupported`.
- **THE WRITE'S OWN-KEY RULE IS THE SAME RULE AT THE OTHER END, AND BOTH ENDS IMPLEMENT IT.** `use-channel-launch-posture.ts › update` branches on `hasOwnProperty(patch,'runtime')`: a patch with NO key sends none (main leaves the pick alone), and `runtime: ''` is a REAL write that sets the channel back to the default adapter. ⚠ **A posture write that moved only tools/messages/model must not carry the key** — sending `''` there would silently clear the operator's pick. `renderer/app-preload.js` spreads it conditionally for the same reason, and `main/channel-dir-ipc.js` reads it with the same `hasOwnProperty`.
- ⚠ **`''` MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS ON THE TWO SURFACES AND THE LABELS SAY SO.** On the DURABLE Settings row it is "Default" — the default ADAPTER. On the composer's per-spawn row it is "Channel default" — no per-spawn preference, so `main/session-launch-op.js`'s chain (`payload.runtime > the channel's pick > the default`) falls through. `use-agent-launch.ts` therefore OMITS the key rather than sending `''` on a launch.
- **AXIS A IS RENDERED FROM `descriptor.toolMode.options`, IN THE PLATFORM'S OWN WORDS AND ITS DECLARED ORDER** (`components/channels-v2/settings-agent-launch-rows.tsx`). The stored value is coerced through `› normalizeToolMode` first — a channel written before a runtime was picked holds `manual`, which Codex does not speak — so the row never names a mode the runtime is never asked for. ⚠ **NO DESCRIPTOR ⇒ DOPL'S OWN FOUR** (`permission-preset-row.tsx › TOOL_OPTIONS`), byte-identical to the pre-port lane. ⚠ **THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING**: index 0 is fail-closed and the last entry is the widest, so `granular` renders SECOND on Codex and `allowlist` FIRST on Cursor — the shipped orders, not the design table's.
- 🔒 **HIDE, NEVER GRAY — AND A REFUSAL GETS A SENTENCE.** Claude declares no `toolMode.secondaryAxis`, so the sandbox row **does not exist** there: no placeholder, no disabled control, no copy explaining a sandbox Claude does not have. Codex and Cursor each render their own. `approval.categories` renders only under the platform's own category-granularity mode; `toolMode.freeform` is `null` on all three, so that branch renders nothing and is written as data anyway (`transport` must be SHOWN if it ever ships). The three REFUSALS render their sentence instead: `interruptRefusal` on the Stop control **and** on the launch surface, `resumeRefusal`, `profileRefusal`. Pinned in `channels-v2/settings-agent-runtime.test.tsx` and `› runtime-refusals.test.tsx`, both driven over the real descriptors.
- ⚠ **`session.interrupt: 'unverified'` DISABLES PAUSE AND END *AND* SAYS WHY** (`channels-v2/agent-panel-controls.tsx`). Cursor today. The sentence is a STANDING note, not the six-second refusal notice beside it — it is a fact about the runtime, not the outcome of a click. **A build with no runtime concept keeps both verbs**: a descriptor nobody sent cannot refuse anything.
- ⚠ **THE STOP CONTROL ASKS THE CHANNEL, BECAUSE `DesktopSessionSummary` CARRIES NO RUNTIME** (`grep -n 'runtime' src/shared/lib/spa-bridge-shapes.ts` → no hits @ 2026-08-31). Correct for every agent launched from that channel with no per-spawn override; **F-393** records the case it cannot see, and the same gap is why `channels-v2/agent-posture.tsx` — the LIVE per-agent Axis-A pick, the one lane whose coercion main already takes a runtime for — still renders Dopl's hardcoded four.
- ⚠ **THE RUNTIME PICK PERSISTS; THE RUNTIME'S OWN AXIS-A VALUE DOES NOT, AND NEITHER DOES THE SECONDARY AXIS (F-390).** `main/channel-prefs.js › normalizePreset` validates the TOOLS axis against a frozen `['manual','accept_edits','auto','bypass']` — the DEFAULT runtime's vocabulary — and rejects the whole write on anything else, and it stores no sandbox mode and no per-category approval at all. So the Permissions row RENDERS every runtime's vocabulary and its write is refused off the default runtime (the hook reverts; nothing lies), while the sandbox and category rows are rendered as **DATA**, at the runtime's own declared default, and are deliberately not controls. `main/session-profiles.js › TOOL_MODES` calls the fix "a step-5 change, not a step-3 one".
- **NO CACHED PAYLOAD GREW, SO §8's STALE-CACHE RULE DOES NOT APPLY HERE** (verified 2026-08-31). The runtime family rides `window.dopl.channels.getLaunchPosture` — a live IPC read on every mount — and never the IndexedDB-persisted query cache §8 is about. The equivalent protection is the own-key probe above, and its "older desktop" case is a real test (`hooks/use-channel-launch-posture.test.tsx`, `channels-v2/settings-agent-runtime.test.tsx`).

#### 11.0g What the port CHANGED in the Claude lane (2026-09-01 — the retraction, and the list)

🔒 **THE CLAIM IS "BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING WITH SEVEN DECLARED OBSERVABLE DIFFERENCES", NOT "BYTE-IDENTICAL".** An independent review diffed the four moved sites against `git show HEAD:` and disproved the stronger phrase; **two of the differences were regressions and are FIXED, and the rest are declared here rather than discovered later.** ⚠ **THIS SECTION EXISTS BECAUSE THE OLD PHRASE WAS AN INSTRUCTION NOT TO LOOK.** A reviewer who reads "byte-identical" skips re-testing the Claude lane, and the phrase was asserted in a header (`runtime/claude/launch-spec.js`) that was itself one of the drifted files.

**FIXED — both converted a swallowed condition into a terminal one:**

1. 🔒 **THE CONTEXT DISPATCH IS SWALLOWED AGAIN** (`session-io.js › applyCoreEvents`, the `result` branch). HEAD wrapped the turn-end context dispatch in `try/catch` inside `session-model.js › observe` and swallowed to one diag line; the move dropped the `try/catch` and left a bare `if (context) dispatch(s, context)`. **`applyCoreEvents` runs inside `session-query.js › consume`'s `for await`, so a throw there is read as a query error and dispatched as `crash` — settle + destroy + `task_failed{interrupted}`.** A reducer bug on a COSMETIC gauge row would therefore tear a session down mid-turn and report it to the waiting peer as an interruption. **The meter may not kill the session.** ⚠ **THE SWALLOW IS FOR THE METER ALONE** — every other dispatch in that loop is a state transition whose failure must still reach `crash`, and `session-context-meter.test.mjs` pins the PAIR, because a single "it does not throw" case would pass over a blanket try/catch around the whole loop. ⚠ **`log` IS INJECTED, NOT REQUIRED**: `diag.js` pulls electron and `session-io.js` may not (a dozen suites require it in plain Node; `session-outbound-tag.test.mjs` pins the absence), so `consume` passes `diag` as `applyCoreEvents`' fifth argument, on the `session-gate-bridge.js › makeCanUseTool(s, dispatch, log)` precedent. **HEAD's line is kept VERBATIM (`session-model: context dispatch failed`) so an existing `listener.log` grep still finds it.**
2. 🔒 **THE AUTH SENTINEL'S ANSWER IS THE STOP CONDITION, NOT THE FACT THAT IT WAS ASKED** (`session-query.js › consume`). HEAD read `if (sessionAuth.holdIfAuthMessage(s, msg)) return;`. **`holdIfAuthFailure` answers FALSE without acting in three cases — no bound deps, no session, and `s.settled`** — and the port discarded the return value and returned unconditionally, so a SETTLED session emitting an auth-shaped message stopped draining a stream HEAD kept reading. Settling does not end the child: `holdIfAuthFailure` is the thing that aborts and closes, and it declines on a settled session precisely because the teardown already ran, so returning there abandons a live iterator whose only other exit (`s.query !== q`) a settled-but-not-superseded session never trips. **It reads `if (hold && sessionAuth.holdIfAuthFailure(s, hold.text)) return;` again — the same shape the catch below it never lost.**

**DECLARED, AND STILL PRESENT — the list to check before assuming an existing Claude session behaves as it did:**

| # | Site | HEAD | Now |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `claude/loader.js › buildMcpServers` | one custody header, `X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-session` | **adds `X-Dopl-Vendor: 'claude'`**. Every existing session's MCP requests carry a header they did not, and the server's prose branches on it (§11.0c) |
| 2 | `claude/launch-spec.js › buildOptions` | `allowedTools: cfg.preApproved` | the two agent-ops verbs are pre-approved on every profile, **and a new in-process `dopl_agents` MCP server is mounted**. `main/agent-self-ops.js` DOES NOT EXIST AT HEAD — a feature inside a move. ⚠ **The SHADOW is unchanged; WHERE IT IS DECLARED moved 2026-09-01 — see the rule below** |
| 3 | `runtime/events.js › result` | `Number(msg.total_cost_usd) \|\| 0`, so `Infinity` survived into `s.lastTotalCost` | `Number.isFinite ? total : null` → `0`; non-finite `sessionTokens` clamped where HEAD passed through |
| 4 | `runtime/events.js › launched` | `if (typeof msg.model === 'string' && msg.model)` | no string check — **a truthy non-string model now lands in `s.liveModel`** |
| 5 | `session-io.js › applyCoreEvents` (`launched`) | dispatch `launched`, **then** assign `s.liveModel` | assigns `s.liveModel` **before** the synchronous dispatch |
| 6 | `session-gate-reason.js › toolReason` | `(d.BYPASS_TOOLS \|\| []).indexOf(...)` — absent-tolerant, degraded to `'unclassified-tool'` | `d.isClassifiedTool(...)` — **throws** if the injected dep is missing |
| 7 | `claude/triage.js › triageSpec.start` | `arg.sdk` used directly | `arg.runtime` **skips `available()`**, so an injected runtime reaching `peekSdk()` gets a **`TypeError` on `null`** — a latent NPE the old shape could not have |

**NARRATION — THREE lines changed** (all in `listener.log`; a fourth, `session-model: context dispatch failed`, was deleted with its `try/catch` and is RESTORED by fix 1 above): `'triage: no SDK —'` → `'triage: no agent runtime —'`; `'no Claude credential on this machine'` → `'no agent-runtime credential on this machine'`; `consume`'s catch now logs the raw error object when there is no `.message`.

- ⚠ **AGENT MANAGEMENT HAS TWO DOORS SINCE 2026-09-01, AND THEY ARE THE SAME TWO VERBS ON THE SAME SUBJECT (Samuel: *"I need you to build out dopl mcp being able to end agents. Dopl MCP need to be able to do all that stuff"*).** `main/agent-self-ops.js` is the IN-PROCESS door — mounted into every spawned session as `mcp__dopl_agents__{rename,end}_agent`. The EXTERNAL door is `dopl_channel(op="end_agent"|"rename_agent")` (§10), which files a `channel_launch_directives` row of kind `end` / `rename` (§6) that this machine claims and dispatches through `main/directive-agent-ops.js`.
  - 🔒 **THE EXTERNAL DOOR IMPLEMENTS NEITHER VERB — IT ROUTES TO THE EXISTING ONE, and that is the constraint on the file rather than a description of it.** `end` → `agent-self-ops.js › endVerdict` then `session-engine.js › controlByTask({action:'end'})`, **the same two calls in the same order** `runtime/claude/axis-b.js`'s in-process `end_agent` makes, bottoming out in the reducer event `sessions:end` and `sessions:delete` also dispatch. `rename` → `agent-self-ops.js › applyRenameTo`, extracted in this wave as the ONE rename write and now shared by all three callers (this lane, `sessions:rename`, the in-process tool). **A second stop path is a second set of teardown bugs** — the sentence `session-reopen.js › controlByTask` already writes about the Agents tab. Pinned at source level in `test/launch-directive-agent-ops.test.mjs`.
  - ⚠ **NEITHER EXTERNAL VERB IS BEHIND THE LAUNCH TOGGLE, AND THAT IS A RULING RECORDED FOR OVERRULING.** §6 carries the argument (a STOP verb and a DISPLAY verb widen nothing; the toggle gates LOCAL COMPUTE BEING SPENT). **TWO LINES IMPLEMENT IT**: the `d.kind === KIND_LAUNCH` test in `launch-directives.js › handle`, and `› refresh` binding realtime on `armed` rather than on the toggle. **The own-operator bound is unchanged and is still the whole fence.**
  - ⚠ **SELF-END STAYS REFUSED IN-PROCESS AND DOES NOT ARISE EXTERNALLY.** `agent-self-ops.js` refuses it because the dispatch would abort the CALLING turn mid-tool-call, so the result could never be delivered and the call would read as a hang. An EXTERNAL caller is not a desktop agent, holds no instance id, and is not mid-turn on this machine — so `directive-agent-ops.js` hands `endVerdict` an empty caller id, which makes the `self` branch UNREACHABLE rather than absent. **One verdict table, not two.**
  - ⚠ **A RENAME CONSULTS NO REGISTRY, ON EITHER DOOR.** `agent-names.js` is keyed by the INSTANCE ADDRESS and outlives the session object on purpose (an idle park, a lazy resume and a crash resume all replace it). Requiring a live session would make a name un-settable at exactly the moments a session is being rebuilt.
- 🔒 **A SHADOW MUST BE DECLARED WHERE THE DENY LISTS ARE (2026-09-01).** `allowedTools` is `cfg.preApproved` and **nothing may be added to it downstream of `runtime/claude/tools.js › buildSessionToolConfig`.** The two agent-ops verbs (`mcp__dopl_agents__rename_agent` / `__end_agent`) rode a `.concat()` in `launch-spec.js` — past `canUseTool` on **every** profile, `read_only` included — where no profile declared them, no profile could refuse one, they were absent from `descriptor.containment.profiles.<p>.allowList`, invisible to the deepEqual pins in `session-profiles.test.mjs`, and unseen by `grantDecision`'s step-2 `preApproved` read. **They are now in the profile table with the argument written beside the deny lists**, so the gate answers `preapproved` (after hard-deny and the audience belt, both of which run first) instead of `gate`. ⚠ **THE SHADOW ITSELF IS STILL RIGHT AND IS NOT THE DEFECT**: an unclassified name gates in EVERY Axis-A mode and a windowless gate DENIES, so routing these two through the gate would delete the feature for exactly the channel-launched sessions the ruling is about. ⚠ **THE `read_only` ANSWER IS ABOUT THESE TWO VERBS AND DOES NOT GENERALISE** — a profile bounds REACH, and rename is display-only against a LOCAL store (nothing resolves an agent by that string) while end is a stop verb that widens nothing. **A third verb on that server that WRITES, ADDRESSES or SENDS belongs on the gate.**

- **Containment is FOUR LAYERS, applied together** — `--tools` (a POSITIVE bound over built-ins; does NOT bound MCP tools), `--settings` (whose `permissions.deny` outranks every allow), `--disallowedTools` (the same names at the CLI layer, because in `-p` mode an invalid settings file is SILENTLY ignored), and `--strict-mcp-config`. **`--allowedTools` is ADDITIVE — it pre-approves, it does NOT bound**; the operator's global settings keep applying.
- **MCP allow/deny entries are EXACT-MATCH, not string-prefix.** The bare server prefix is valid ONLY in a deny list.
- **Profile tables** (`main/tool-profiles.js`, re-measured 2026-08-28): `READ_BUILTINS` = `Read, Grep, Glob, LS, TodoWrite`; `DOPL_SAFE_TOOLS` = **11**; `DOPL_ADMIN_TOOLS` = **5**; `RETIRED_DOPL_TOOLS` = **4**; **`UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` = admins + retired = 9 names.** ⚠ 11/5/9 after MCP surface v2 waves A and B: `dopl_agent` and `dopl_home` joined the SAFE list, `dopl_agent_admin` the ADMIN list. It was 9/4/8 from 2026-08-11. ⚠ **THE DENY FLOOR MOVED FOR A NEW ADMIN, NOT A RETIREMENT** — `RETIRED_DOPL_TOOLS` is unchanged, and every older sentence about that number is about the 2026-08-11 retirement. `test/tool-profiles.test.mjs` holds the desktop lists against the SERVER's live registrations, parsed out of `packages/mcp-server/src` — the count here is prose, that test is the enforcement.
  - ⚠ **`dopl_home` IS ON THE SAFE LIST AND IT WRITES**, by the same rule `dopl_kb` / `dopl_skill` / `dopl_agent` already sit there under: it authors rows a restricted spawn could author anyway and POSTS NOTHING. Its one write makes a room the caller is ALONE in and it cannot invite anybody — minting the link that reaches a person is `sessionOnly` and unreachable over MCP for every role and token. The exfil surface is `dopl_channel`, which stays out of the allow list AND denied.
- **`full` MEANS FULL — and the two spawn lanes are IDENTICAL ONLY IN THE HARD-DENY SET.** `main/runtime/claude/tools.js › SESSION_HARD_DENY` **is** `UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY.slice()` — not a copy, not a superset — so under `full` both lanes hard-deny exactly those nine names (2026-08-28; eight until `dopl_agent_admin` landed) and **everything else is live-gated** by the operator's permission preset. ⚠ **THAT IS WHERE THE SAMENESS STOPS. `tool-profiles.js`'s own header says "the two lanes answer `full` identically"; it is describing the DENY FLOOR, and reading it as the whole spawn shape is the mistake this bullet exists to stop** (the first draft of this file made exactly that copy). **This is a containment claim — verify it per lane before relying on it.** Measured 2026-08-11:

  | Under `full` | **Headless CLI lane** (`session-spawner.js` → `tool-profiles.js › buildRestrictionArgs`) | **SDK lane** (`session-query.js › buildSdkOptions`) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Hard deny | `--disallowedTools UNIVERSAL_HARD_DENY` (9) | `SESSION_HARD_DENY` (same 9) **+ `buildSecretPathDenyRules()`** |
  | Positive bound on built-ins | none (no `--tools`) | ⚠ **SINCE 2026-09-02 (A5): `runtime/claude/tools.js › FULL_BUILTIN_BOUND`**, so `options.tools` is ALWAYS set — the CLI lane is unchanged |
  | Pre-approval | none (no `--allowedTools`) | **`allowedTools: READ_BUILTINS`** |
  | Operator's global settings | **APPLY** (no `--settings`) | **NEVER** — `settingSources: []`, unconditionally, on every profile |
  | Operator's global MCP servers | **LOAD** (no `--strict-mcp-config`) | `mcpServers` is `buildMcpServers(…)`, the single `dopl` HTTP entry — but see the connector-lane bullet below: **the SDK lane loaded NINE MORE until 2026-08-22** |

  **So "`full` grants the operator's REAL tools, global MCP servers included — nothing narrows it" is TRUE OF THE CLI LANE ONLY.** On that lane `buildRestrictionArgs` returns exactly one flag and that is deliberate — **do not add `--tools`, `--allowedTools`, `--settings` or `--strict-mcp-config` there.** The SDK lane is narrowed by `settingSources: []` and a single-entry `mcpServers`, both load-bearing (`// ALWAYS — the global allow-list can never shadow a gate`), and **removing either to "restore parity" would hand a channel session the operator's whole global config.** The SDK lane is also where `permissionMode: 'default'` is pinned, so `canUseTool` cannot be short-circuited.
  - ⚠ **"NARROWED BY CONSTRUCTION" WAS THE OVERSTATEMENT, AND THE SDK-LANE MCP CELL SAID "DO NOT LOAD" WHILE NINE SERVERS LOADED (2026-08-22, F-268, measured).** Both claims enumerate the options `buildSdkOptions` SETS, and **the lane that leaked is one no option covers.** When the session's OAuth credential carries the `user:mcp_servers` scope the CLI fetches `GET /v1/mcp_servers` with that Bearer and connects every **claude.ai ACCOUNT CONNECTOR** as `mcp__claude_ai_<Name>__*`. Measured against the bundled binary (claude 2.1.220 / claude-agent-sdk 0.3.220): the init message's `mcp_servers` held **12 entries, 9 of them connectors** — Slack, Gmail, Google Calendar, Google Drive, Figma, Granola, Notion, Attio, Dopl. **A construction argument is only as wide as the constructor, and this one enumerated our own option set as if it were the child's whole world.**
    - ⚠ **`settingSources: []` IS WHAT REMOVED THE OFF SWITCH.** The CLI's own kill switch is the `disableClaudeAiConnectors` **setting**, and our isolation is precisely what stops it being read. **Tightening the sandbox deleted the control.** The surviving lever is the process env, set at the one assembly point: `runtime/claude/loader.js › buildScrubbedEnv` writes `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS='0'` **last and unconditionally**, so an inherited value cannot re-admit the lane. ⚠ **THE NAME IS INVERTED AND EMPTY IS A NO-OP** — the binary tests "explicitly set falsy" (`['0','false','no','off']`), so `''`, `'1'` and unset all leave it ON. **Do not "clear" this var.** Measured the same day: unset → 9 connectors, `''` → 9, `'0'` → 0, `'false'` → 0, with the `dopl` entry untouched in every case.
    - ⚠ **`--strict-mcp-config` IS NOT THE FIX AND MUST NOT BE ADDED HERE.** It hard-errors on machines carrying an enterprise `managed-mcp.json`, trading a leak for a launch failure on the fleet least able to debug it. `test/claudeai-connector-lane.test.mjs` pins its absence along with the env var, the `settingSources` join, and the framing belt.
    - **CONTAINMENT ALWAYS HELD — this was OFFERED SURFACE, not execution.** Every connector tool is unclassified, so `grantDecision` gates it and a windowless session denies it (all 12 profile × mode cells, `test/session-tool-name-prefix.test.mjs`). What it cost was an **inventory of the operator's connected accounts inside a prompt that can be auto-sent**, and per-turn tokens across every live session. **`prompt-framing-text.js › LANE_EXCLUSIVITY`** is the belt for the day the env var stops working: it tells the agent `mcp__dopl__dopl_channel` is the only path off the machine and that no other `mcp__` server is its lane. ⚠ **Deny-on-unclassified (R3b) was considered and DEFERRED** — the gate already holds, and turning an allow-list into a deny-list is a change to make deliberately, not as a side effect of this one.
  - **`AUTO_TOOLS`/`BYPASS_TOOLS` are POSITIVE allow-lists** — an unclassified name gates in every mode, `bypass` included. ⚠ **SCOPE: "no released built-in is on either or in `preApproved`" is true ONLY of the built-ins F-177 RELEASED** (`Task`, `Agent`, `Artifact`, `SendMessage`, `Cron*`, `Skill`, `ToolSearch`, `EnterWorktree`, …), which is the claim `tool-profiles.js` actually makes. **Read as a universal it is plainly false:** `AUTO_TOOLS` = `READ_BUILTINS` + `EDIT_TOOLS` + `MultiEdit` + `DOPL_READ_TOOLS`, `BYPASS_TOOLS` adds `ESCALATION_TOOLS` (`Bash`, `BashOutput`, `KillShell`, `WebFetch`, `WebSearch`) + `DOPL_WRITE_TOOLS` + `BYPASS_READS`, and `preApproved` is `READ_BUILTINS` on **every** profile. **Do not "finish the job" by pre-approving any of the released set** — a shadowed tool never reaches `canUseTool` at all.
  - 🔒 **`full` IS BOUNDED ON THE OFFER, AND THE BOUND IS DERIVED FROM AXIS A — NEVER HAND-WRITTEN (2026-09-02, A5).** `runtime/claude/tools.js › FULL_BUILTIN_BOUND` is `BYPASS_TOOLS` minus the `mcp__dopl__*` names (`options.tools` bounds BUILT-INS only), i.e. **exactly the set some Axis-A mode can allow** — measured 17 names on 2026-09-02, asserted BY NAME on the wire in `test/session-builtin-bound.test.mjs`, not by comparing the constant to itself. ⚠ **IT IS OFFERED SURFACE, NOT A FENCE, AND READING IT AS A RE-NARROWING OF `full` IS THE MISTAKE THIS BULLET EXISTS TO STOP.** The hard-deny is still `SESSION_HARD_DENY` and nothing else, every built-in F-177 released still resolves `gate` rather than `deny`, and nothing new is pre-approved. **What it removes is CONTEXT**: `builtinTools: []` meant NO bound, so `full` carried every built-in the CLI ships — measured 29 tools / **87,402 chars of tool schema per turn**, `Workflow` alone 21,332 — of which only the classified ones could ever run, because an unclassified name gates in every mode and a windowless gate DENIES. ⚠ **DERIVING IT IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART**: a fourth hand-maintained list would drift from the three above it, and the drift's face is a tool the mode picker says runs and the model never sees. ⚠ **AND `Agent` / `Skill` COME OFF WITH IT — a POSTURE DEFAULT RECORDED FOR OVERRULING**: they are what injects the OPERATOR'S PERSONAL Claude Code agent and skill catalogue into every Dopl agent turn (8,322 ch measured 2026-09-02, the F-268 class, and it breaks cross-machine prompt-cache identity), and Dopl's sanctioned delegation path is `dopl_channel(op="launch_agent")`. **One line re-offers them; making them RUNNABLE is a second, separate decision.**
  - **`read_only` and `dopl_only` did NOT change** — they still hard-deny the whole of `DENIED_BUILTINS` plus admins and retired tools. **A5 did not touch either one's `builtinTools`** (`READ_BUILTINS`, and `+ WEB_TOOLS` respectively).
  - **A `dopl_kb` READ IS CLASSIFIED BY ITS `op`, EXACTLY AS `dopl_channel` ALWAYS HAS BEEN (2026-08-22, the agent-templates OQ-1 ruling).** Tool permission is per-TOOL, and `dopl_kb` carries SEVEN write ops, so it sits in `DOPL_WRITE_TOOLS` — correctly — and therefore missed `AUTO_TOOLS`. **A miss in a WINDOWLESS session is a DENY, not a question**, so before this an agent template's attached knowledge bases were unreadable under every posture but `bypass`. `main/session-profiles.js › grantDecision` now has a fifth step: a call whose `op` is on `main/knowledge-ops.js › KNOWLEDGE_READ_OPS` resolves wherever a `DOPL_READ_TOOL` already resolves. ⚠ **A POSITIVE ALLOW-LIST OF FIVE READS, never "everything that is not a write"** — the same rule the two axes follow, so a write op the server adds later is UNKNOWN here and gates. ⚠ **THE LIST IS DERIVED FROM THE SERVER IN A TEST, NOT AT RUNTIME**: `test/knowledge-read-ops.test.mjs` parses `packages/mcp-server/src/gating.ts › WRITE_OPS.dopl_kb` and the tool's own `op` enum out of the SOURCE and asserts the desktop list is exactly `enum − WRITE_OPS`, so a new READ op fails as a GAP and a new WRITE op fails as a STALE LIST — neither can fail as a silent grant. ⚠ **IT IS LAST, AFTER Axis A** (so `bypass`, which really does cover the whole tool, is still Axis A's answer), and **unreachable under `read_only`**, which hard-denies the tool at step 1. ⚠ **ONE TOOL, DELIBERATELY** — `dopl_skill` / `dopl_ontology` / `dopl_chats` are the other three write tools and are NOT op-scoped: nothing points a windowless agent at them the way a template's attached bases point it at `dopl_kb`, and a classification with no consumer is a widening bought for nothing.
  - **Dropping a name from a deny list makes it UNCLASSIFIED, which resolves to `gate`** — one click from running. Containment must not depend on the server's current tool list.
- **A SPAWNED SESSION IS TOLD THE SAME TWO CAPABILITIES, IN THE VOCABULARY BLOCK (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 11).** `main/prompt-framing-text.js › VOCABULARY` — which opens BOTH sides' first turn (`prompt-framing.js › buildFencedTurn`) — now grants the sparse main-CHANNEL post and the @-tag, with the resolver's real match rule (§10). **They sit in VOCABULARY and not in the delivery section on purpose:** both are about what the agent may CHOOSE to do, while the delivery section is written for the send that is already decided. Pinned on both sides by `test/prompt-framing.test.mjs`; the §H-13 no-em-dash scan and the `task=`-argument ban apply to the block like everything else.
- 🔒 **MAIN RESOLVES AN AGENT TEMPLATE; THE RENDERER SUPPLIES AN ID AND NEVER CONTENT (2026-08-22).** `sessions:launch` takes `templateId` and `main/session-launch-op.js` fetches `GET /api/agent-templates/{id}/resolve` itself, through `main/api.js › apiFetch` (COOKIE auth, the shared 401 repair, `TEMPLATE_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000` — **not** `launch-directives.js`'s 15 s, which is far too long to hold a button click). ⚠ **A renderer-supplied `{name, instructions}` would be renderer-authored text landing in a prompt, and main cannot tell a real template from a fabricated one — F-267 repeated with PROMPT TEXT as the forged input.** Resolving in main also keeps the knowledge-base viewer filter on the OPERATOR's credential, which is structurally guaranteed here and merely true-today in the SPA. The SPA may still render the name optimistically from its own cache: **the optimistic render is a LABEL, the authoritative resolve is the PROMPT.**
  - **REFUSE, NEVER DEGRADE TO BLANK.** A 404 (deleted, or invisible to this operator — ONE answer, because the endpoint is 404-never-403 and the difference must stay unobservable) is `{ok:false, reason:'no-template'}`; a timeout, a 5xx and every other non-2xx are the EXISTING word `busy`. A blank agent silently wearing no identity is worse than a refusal, because the operator will not notice for several turns. ⚠ **An unknown MODEL is the opposite call and stays fail-soft**: it falls through to the next link of the chain, never refuses, because refusing would make a template unusable on an older desktop build — the common case, not the rare one.
  - **THE MODEL PRECEDENCE CHAIN IS COMPUTED IN MAIN AND ONLY IN MAIN:** `sessions:setModel` live override (post-spawn) > the launch sheet's `overrides.model` > `template.model` > `channelPrefs.getLaunchModel` > the SDK's own pick. Each link is consulted in turn and the FIRST that yields a model this build knows wins, so an unrecognised template model does not throw away the operator's own channel default. Every value still passes `session-model.js`, and `buildSdkOptions` re-coerces at the last step before argv.
  - **THE LAUNCH SHEET'S OVERRIDES ARE RE-VALIDATED HERE, AND MAIN IS THE ONLY REAL VALIDATOR (F-281).** `@/shared/lib/safe-label` imports zod, so no renderer surface may hold `SAFE_LABEL_RE`; the SPA enforces only the numbers. `main/template-resolve.js › narrowOverrides` applies the charset rule through this tree's ONE copy of it (`main/session-telemetry.js › UNSAFE_LABEL_RE`) and **DROPS** a row that fails, rather than refusing a launch the operator cannot fix from the sheet they are in. `overrides.fields` REPLACES the template's set and is never merged.
  - **FIRST-USE APPROVAL FOR ANOTHER MEMBER'S TEMPLATE IS MACHINE-LOCAL AND NEVER SERVER-REACHABLE** (`main/channel-prefs.js › isTemplateApproved` / `approveTemplate`, `sessions:approveTemplate`, `appWindowOnly`). A FOREIGN template's first launch on a machine answers `{ok:false, reason:'template-approval'}` with the name and instructions main resolved, for the SPA to show verbatim. ⚠ **The store may never move to the server**, for the reason `orchestratorLaunchEnabled` gives beside it: a spawned session has `Bash` and the operator's credential is on disk, so a server-stored approval lets a credential-holding agent pre-approve itself across the whole fleet. ⚠ **`template-approval` IS AN IPC WORD ONLY** and is not a member of the directive refusal vocabulary: the directive lane has no human at the keyboard and the launch-over-MCP toggle stands in for the click there, so a directive can never produce it. ⚠ **Approving grants nothing but the prompt** — same tool profile, same axes, same working folder, same hard-deny floor.
- **`main/runtime/claude/loader.js`'s dopl MCP entry carries `alwaysLoad: true`. Do not remove it** — pinned by `test/prompt-profile-drift.test.mjs`. Every MCP tool is deferred behind `ToolSearch` once tool search is on (it defaults on), so without `alwaysLoad` the session's own delivery path sits behind a call the prompt forbids by name.
- **The per-server `tools` policy is NEVER SENT.** `doplToolsPolicy` stays in `buildMcpServers`' signature and the profile table as **a record of intent that is deletion-only, never a live input.** The CLI validates `--mcp-config` PER ENTRY and a failed parse **DROPS THE WHOLE SERVER ENTRY** rather than stripping the field; `disallowedTools` already carries the identical bound. **The reusable lesson: a config field is not verified by testing the function that computes it — assert the object that crosses the process boundary, or read back what the runtime says it loaded.**
- **AN UNKNOWN TOOL PROFILE FAILS CLOSED AND SAYS SO.** `normalizeProfile` resolves anything unrecognized to `read_only` through an injected sink. **An explicit `'full'` is untouched.**
- **`mcp__<server>__` IS THE CLIENT'S PREFIX, NEVER OURS TO ASSUME.** Other clients use different segments, so **every tool name reaching the gate is normalized ONCE at the top of `grantDecision`** (`main/mcp-tool-names.js`) and hard-deny, pre-approval and both lookups use that one value. **Never add a fourth matcher comparing a raw name against an `mcp__dopl__…` literal** — its worst face is the deny direction. **The GRANT KEY is the deliberate exception and stays on the RAW name**: a grant is scoped to the shape the operator was shown.
- **AXIS B governs the own channel in BOTH directions.** **The OUTBOUND half is FOUR ops, not one — believe the constants:** `main/session-own-outbound.js › OWN_CHANNEL_OUTBOUND_OPS` (`milestone` + `create_thread` + **`escalate`**, 2026-08-31) beside `session-profiles.js › isOwnChannelPost`. ⚠ This sentence said THREE and listed two until 2026-08-31 — it was counting the POST as the third and reading as if the constant held three, which is the ambiguity a count in prose always develops. **Re-read the constant.** Own-channel READ-ONLY ops follow the INBOUND half, because a read brings the peer's words into this agent's context. **The set is SIX ops — believe the constant:** `main/session-profiles.js › OWN_CHANNEL_READ_OPS = ['read','await','list_threads','get_thread','members','read_sessions']` (re-measured 2026-08-22). ⚠ This bullet said FIVE and listed five until 2026-08-22, while the sub-bullet below already recorded `read_sessions` joining — **the doc disagreed with itself and with the code**, which is the shape a hand-maintained count always ends up in. Re-read the constant, never this sentence. Everything else gates in every posture, including any post, read or thread open naming another channel (**a slug counts as another channel — the safe failure**) and `read_sessions`, whose `channel` is an optional filter.
  - ⚠ **`create_thread` JOINED THE OUTBOUND HALF ON 2026-08-24 (SAMUEL'S RULING), AND IT IS THE FIRST OP ADMITTED ON A DIFFERENT ARGUMENT.** A `milestone` earned the lane by SAYING LESS than the post beside it; a thread open says more — it is a post with a TITLE on it. It earns the lane on the other argument: **outbound CONTENT into this session's own channel, addressed to a member of that same channel**, which is exactly what the outbound half consents to. **WHAT IT COST TO LEAVE IT OFF, live-observed on v1.19.0:** unclassified, it fell to the AXIS-A gate, and a windowless session answers a gate with `deny` (§11's windowless executor bullet) — so the op the tool's own protocol says to start with was auto-refused *("this session has no surface to show one on")* in **every** posture, with nothing an operator could set. **The bar it still clears is the one that keeps `close_thread` out: a thread open settles no shared state, because a thread has none.** `open` / `invite` / `set_thread_mode` / `list` did NOT move. Same channel-by-ID scope, same slug failure, same private-turn withdrawal (`privateTurnMessageMode` transforms the AXIS, so the op inherited it with no call-site change), and its own diag ALLOW code `auto-outbound-thread-open`. **The §2 SPLIT that carries the argument is `main/session-own-outbound.js`** — `session-profiles.js` measured 496 of the 500-line cap. `test/session-channel-read.test.mjs` drives the truth table; `test/session-windowless-deny.test.mjs § 3` drives the consent row a held one raises.
  - ⚠ **`escalate` JOINED THE OUTBOUND HALF ON 2026-08-31 (SAMUEL'S RULING), ON `create_thread`'s ARGUMENT AND NOT ON `milestone`'s.** A milestone earned the lane by SAYING LESS than the post beside it; an escalation says MORE — it is a post with a question and a set of answers on it. It earns the lane on the other argument: **outbound CONTENT into this session's own channel, addressed to a member of that same channel.** The bar that keeps `close_thread` out is cleared the way a thread open clears it — **an escalation settles no shared state, it ASKS.** Same channel-by-ID scope (**a slug is another channel and gates**), same private-turn withdrawal inherited with no call-site change, and its own diag ALLOW code **`auto-outbound-escalate`**.
    - **WHAT LEAVING IT OFF WOULD HAVE COST is F-320's defect class, and it would have shipped the feature broken for its PRIMARY producer.** The agent that most needs to escalate is a BLOCKED one, which is almost always a windowless session on the operator's own machine; unclassified, it falls to the AXIS-A gate, and **a windowless session answers a gate with `deny`** — so the op the tool's own protocol tells a stuck agent to reach for would be auto-refused in *every* posture, with nothing an operator could set.
    - 🔒 **AND IT HAD TO JOIN `session-outbound-tag.js › outboundConsentShape` IN THE SAME CHANGE, OR IT WOULD HAVE BEEN F-321 INSTEAD.** That predicate decides which gated `dopl_channel` call raises the OUTBOUND payload rather than the dock's `permission_request`, and `session-windowless.js › claimGate` DENIES the latter outright. An escalation is a question a human must answer, so the session asking one is windowless far more often than not — without this, a gated escalate would be told *"this session has no surface to show one on"* about the one call whose entire purpose is to reach a surface.
    - ⚠ **`escalationAnswer` IS NOT ON THIS LANE AND MUST NEVER BE.** An agent answering an escalation is an agent deciding a question a HUMAN was asked, which is the whole thing the card exists to prevent. It rides an ordinary `post` and meets `isOwnChannelPost`'s gate like any other; the SPA is its only producer. `test/escalation-answer-wake.test.mjs` pins the absence.

- 🔒 **THE PRIVATE DIRECT LANE — AN OPERATOR'S EXTERNAL AGENT MAY STEER THEIR OWN RUNNING ONE (Samuel's ruling, 2026-08-31).** `channel_agent_directions` is a MAILBOX the operator's own desktop claims (`main/agent-directions.js`, `main/agent-direction-wire.js`); the server delivers nothing and cannot. It rides the EXISTING `sessions:message` op — `session-reopen.js › messageByTask` — so there is one resolution, one `steer`, one private-turn open and one set of lifecycle bugs.
  - 🔒 **THE FRAMING IS THE LOAD-BEARING RULING, AND IT IS THE INVERSE OF THE OPERATOR'S.** `session-seed.js › frameDirectedTurn` fences a direction as DATA and says in as many words that it does NOT carry the operator's authority; `frameOperatorTurn` is deliberately NOT fenced, because the operator is the one voice the framing tells a session to weigh. **Reusing the operator framing would hand the highest authority in the system to the lane with the weakest human in it** — the 2026-08-01 mislabel incident's exact shape. Pinned AT THE CALL SITE (`test/session-direction-lane.test.mjs`); asserting the two framers merely differ is not that pin.
  - **THE CONSENT IS A SECOND LOCAL TOGGLE, NOT A REUSE OF THE LAUNCH ONE.** `main/orchestrator-consent.js › getOrchestratorDirect` (the §1 split out of `channel-prefs.js`, which now carries both) — machine-local, default OFF, read at decision time. **Launching buys COMPUTE; directing reaches a RUNNING agent's private lane**, and an operator may want one and not the other, which one flag cannot say. ⚠ OFF MEANS SILENT — no claim, no decide, no server write — because a refusal from a machine that has not opted in would itself admit the machine is listening.
  - 🔒 **THE REPLY IS THE ONE PLACE PRIVATE-LANE TEXT LEAVES A MACHINE, AND THE RULE MAY NOT BE GENERALISED.** A direction that arrived from off-machine gets an answer that goes back off-machine; NOTHING ELSE does — not the narration ring, not `thinking` frames, not tool calls, not any other turn, and never anything the OPERATOR typed. `main/session-directed.js` owns the capture, the bound and the charset strip. ⚠ **A TURN THAT IS NOT THE DIRECTED ONE MUST LEAVE NO TEXT BEHIND** — the `+2` covers a turn already in flight, and without clearing it a directed turn that ends on tool output would report the PREVIOUS turn's final text (F-371's sibling, found in the same review).
  - 🔒 **THERE IS AN OWN-CHANNEL LANE SINCE 2026-08-31 (SAMUEL'S RULING) — THE OPERATOR'S OWN AGENTS MAY DIRECT EACH OTHER; A PEER'S NEVER MAY.** ⚠ **THIS BULLET SAID THE OPPOSITE, AND THE SENTENCE IT REPLACES — "there is no own-channel lane, and that is what closes the in-machine loop" — WAS TRUE AND IS NOW TRADED AWAY DELIBERATELY, FOR THE BOUND BELOW.**
    - ⚠ **MEASURE THE FENCE BEFORE REASONING ABOUT IT: `operator_user_id` NEVER REFUSED A LAUNCHED AGENT AND COULD NOT HAVE.** A spawned session calls the MCP server with the OPERATOR'S OWN credential — the device token (`mcp-config.js › deviceTokenForSpawn`) or a container-locked child of it (`session-credential.js › sessionBearer`) — and both are `mcp_tokens` rows whose `user_id` IS the operator's. **The fence compares OPERATOR IDS, not credential kinds**, so it already admitted this caller; `agentTokenId` is the only per-caller signal and is read for truthiness, never for identity. **This ruling therefore needed NO server change.** What refused it was THIS MACHINE'S Axis-A gate — the op was on no allow list, so it fell through, and a windowless session answers a gate with an auto-deny.
    - **THE LANE IS `main/session-own-direct.js`, AND IT TAKES THE LAUNCH LANE'S CONJUNCTION**: Axis B's outbound half (the ask LEAVES this machine as a row carrying a body this session wrote) AND Axis A `bypass` (what it buys is a TURN on a local process — no message posture may buy work). ⚠ **A SEPARATE LIST FROM `OWN_MACHINE_LAUNCH_OPS`, THOUGH THE CONJUNCTION IS IDENTICAL**: that one carries the launch-depth bound, and folding this in would make private directions depend on the agent-chaining setting — two rulings answering through each other. ⚠ **`read_directions` IS NOT ON IT** — it starts no turn and sends nothing, so it joined `OWN_CHANNEL_READ_OPS` beside `read_sessions`, whose shape it shares exactly.
    - 🔒 **THE PEER FENCE IS UNTOUCHED, AND IT IS NOT THIS LANE'S.** A peer's agent holds a credential whose `user_id` is the PEER's, so the row it writes is stamped with the PEER's `operator_user_id` and only the PEER's desktop can claim it — a peer cannot address this Mac in any posture, and no schema on the path accepts an operator id. The in-process twin, `session-reopen.js › messageByTask`'s F-373 check, still refuses a mismatch and still fails closed on an unstamped session. **The ruling widens WHO MAY ASK on the operator's own machine, never WHOSE MACHINE MAY BE ASKED.**
    - 🔒 ⚠ **THE LOOP F-374 ACCEPTED IS NOW SAME-MACHINE, SO IT IS BOUNDED — `main/direction-rate.js`, A PER-TARGET-SESSION INBOUND RATE LIMIT** at the one claim funnel (`agent-directions.js › handle`), refusing with the existing `busy` word. ⚠ **AT THE DELIVERY END ON PURPOSE:** every hop of every loop, same-machine or cross-machine, must land a TURN on some session, so bounding what a session RECEIVES bounds the loop from both sides **and closes the cross-machine case F-374 wished for**. ⚠ It cannot reach the operator's own composer, structurally: that path never calls this module. ⚠ **AFTER THE CLAIM, NOT BEFORE IT** — standing down silently would leave the caller waiting out a 10-minute TTL for an answer the machine already has.
  - 🔒 **A DIRECTION REACHES ONLY A SESSION THIS OPERATOR STARTED (F-373).** The engine's registry is process-lifetime and a SIGN-OUT DOES NOT CLEAR IT, so operator A's live agent survives B signing in on the same Mac — and the lane's own owner check compares the row to the CURRENTLY signed-in user, which is B. `startSession` therefore stamps `s.operatorUserId` once and never rewrites it (`session-state-push.js › trackOrigin`'s guard, one module over), and `messageByTask` refuses a mismatch, failing CLOSED on an unstamped session.
  - ⚠ **THE WINDOW IS OPENED AFTER THE DISPATCH, AND THE ORDER IS A FIX (F-372, and it is OLDER than this lane).** A `steer` at a PARKED session makes the reducer emit `resumeQuery` before `pushTurn`, and `resumeParked` resets the private depth — so a window opened first was wiped by the wake that same message triggered, and the turn ran with AXIS B's outbound widening INTACT. `openPrivateTurn` now takes the pre-dispatch `wasInFlight`; absent, it falls back to reading the state, so no other caller moved.
  - ⚠ **THE OPERATOR MUST BE ABLE TO TELL THE TWO VOICES APART.** A direction is tagged `directed` / `lane: 'directed'` and its answer `directed-reply` (`session-narration.js`), so it can never render as words the operator typed. **THE SPA FACE IS BUILT (2026-08-31, Samuel's ruling — F-366's operator half):** `components/channels-v2/agent-stream-directed.tsx › DirectedBox`, a box in `› agent-stream-sent-box.tsx`'s family — full stream width, radius 12, banner over body, timestamp in the banner's trailing tag — **deliberately NOT its dark `--surface-cta` banner**, which on this surface means one thing only (these words left the machine). The geometry says "an exchange"; the weight says whether anyone off this machine can see it. ⚠ **THE WIRE'S ONE LANE BECOMES TWO HERE**, split on the `kind` in `› agent-stream-lanes.ts › frameLane` (the §1 split of `› agent-stream-model.ts`, which stays the import path of record and re-exports it — that file moves when the DESKTOP's vocabulary moves, the model when the stream's own arithmetic does) — inbound reads `Directed by …`, outbound `Reply to …` — because the operator's question is which VOICE spoke, where the wire's `lane` answers who could SEE it. An unrecognised kind on that lane reads as the INBOUND face, never a `note`. ⚠ **AND THE BANNER NAMES THE RELATION, NOT AN AGENT — `your agent` — BECAUSE NO LAYER HOLDS A SENDER IDENTITY (F-376):** `channel_agent_directions.agent_id` is the ADDRESSEE and the row has no sender column, `schema-direction.ts › DirectionCreateSchema` has no such field, so `main/agent-direction-wire.js › directionFrom` has nothing to carry and the frame is `{at, kind, lane, text}`. The relation is what the lane guarantees (`operator_user_id = ctx.userId` on every read and write), so it is the true claim; `DirectedBox`'s `agent` prop takes a resolved DISPLAY NAME the day one exists and never a raw id (§11's rule, `› agent-id-visibility.test.ts`).
  - **THE STREAM'S MESSAGE FACES RENDER MARKDOWN (Samuel, 2026-08-31), THROUGH THE TRANSCRIPT'S OWN RENDERER.** `components/channels-v2/agent-stream-prose.tsx › StreamProse` is a thin adapter over `› message-markdown.tsx` — **reuse, never a fork**, so the untrusted-body discipline (lexed to tokens, mapped to React elements, no HTML string on the path) is stated once. It serves **every message face**: the agent's private answer, the operator's own turn, both directed boxes, and the SENT box's body (2026-08-31 — the ruling extends there, and the §6 seam is untouched: the banner, the four faces, the Post button and the expiry rule are what that card owns and none of them moved). ⚠ **THE SENT BODY IS THE SAME STRING THE TRANSCRIPT RENDERS ONE PANE OVER**, so leaving it raw was one message wearing two faces. ⚠ **THE LOG LANE IS DELIBERATELY NOT A CALLER:** `› agent-stream-log.tsx` bounds its rows by SLICING characters (`COLLAPSED_CHARS` closed, `EXPANDED_CHARS` open) and `line-clamp` is a `-webkit-box` rule that cannot clamp a container of sibling blocks — so markdown there would render wreckage AND lose the bound that keeps a megabyte of tool JSON out of a 380px column. ⚠ **THE ROSTER IS EMPTY AND NO @-HANDLE TINTS**, because neither agent host holds one (`› agent-panel.tsx` already builds `indexMembers([], …)` and says why); a tint asserts the roster resolved somebody and this surface did not ask. ⚠ **WHAT REACHES AN OPERATOR TODAY IS INLINE MARKDOWN ONLY — F-376:** `main/session-narration.js › line` collapses `/\s+/` on every frame, prose included, so lists, headings and fences have lost the newlines they are made of before the SPA is handed the string.
- 🔒 **THE ONE-GENERATION LAUNCH BOUND IS A CHANNEL SETTING, DEFAULT OFF (2026-08-31, SAMUEL'S RULING; the post-1.23.0 field run).** A launched agent's five worker-launch attempts were all refused with *"agents launching agents is limited to ONE generation"*; the operator wanted an orchestrator that staffs supervisors that staff workers, in the one room they run orchestrators in. **`main/channel-prefs.js › getAgentChain` — per channel, LOCAL (`electron-store`), default FALSE, and FALSE is the bound that shipped**, so a machine that has never seen the key behaves exactly as before.
  - **IT LIFTS A DEPTH BOUND AND GRANTS NOTHING ELSE.** `session-own-launch.js › launchLaneVerdict` asks the setting BEFORE the depth and only to SKIP that question; a chained session still clears the same two-axis conjunction the first generation does, plus the machine-wide `orchestratorLaunchEnabled` consent, the channel's tool profile, `SESSION_HARD_DENY` and a free slot. ⚠ **`MAX_LAUNCH_DEPTH` IS STILL 1** — the setting skips the question rather than raising the number, because a bigger number would be arithmetic over a depth that cannot cross the wire.
  - ⚠ **IT IS A SPAWN-TIME STAMP AND IS **NOT** LIVE-APPLIED, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE POSTURE AND FOR THE POSTURE'S OWN REASON.** `channel-dir-ipc.js › applyPostureToLive` fans a changed posture out to running sessions on an argument that names its own limit — *it widens SUPERVISION, never CONTAINMENT*. This is containment, so it takes `launchDepth`'s discipline instead: **exactly one lane reads the setting (`launch-directives.js › spawn`, per directive, never cached), every other spawn shape passes nothing, and absent reads FALSE** — so a peer wake, a resume and a crash recreate all keep the bound. Pinned in `test/launch-chain.test.mjs`.
  - 🔒 ⚠ **WITH IT ON THERE IS NO GENERATION BOUND LEFT, AND TWO REAL CEILINGS STAND IN ITS PLACE. Neither is a generation count and neither may be described as one.** `session-windowless.js › MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS` (instantaneous, already enforced at `session-launch.js › launch` as `cap`), and **`main/launch-budget.js` — a ROLLING PER-CHANNEL BUDGET spent ONLY by a chained spawn**, which is the bound over TIME the concurrency ceiling cannot be: sessions settle and free their slots, so an unbounded chain under a fixed ceiling is a fork bomb that has learned patience. It answers the SAME `cap` word (the seven-word directive vocabulary is closed and `cap`'s sentence is true of both). **The real generation cap needs a `launch_depth` column and is FILED — F-378, not guessed at.**
  - ⚠ **THE REFUSAL SENTENCE CHANGED IN THE SAME WAVE AND THE OLD ONE IS NOW FALSE.** `session-permissions.js › LAUNCH_DEPTH_DENY_MESSAGE` said *"no setting will widen this"*; there is now exactly one that does, and a refusal that denies its own remedy is the defect class that sentence exists to remove. **It names the switch, names whose it is, and STILL forbids re-issuing** — the flag is a spawn-time stamp, so flipping it cannot unblock the session reading the sentence.
  - **THE SURFACE IS THE CHANNEL SETTINGS TAB'S PER-CHANNEL GROUP** (`channels-v2/settings-desktop-rows.tsx › AgentChainRows`, over `hooks/use-channel-agent-chain.ts` and the `channels:get/setAgentChain` `appWindowOnly` pair). ⚠ **NOT the machine-scoped group** — that one governs "on this Mac", this governs "in this room", and one heading cannot honestly cover both. Minimal copy: a label and a switch.

- 🔒 **AN AGENT SESSION MAY LAUNCH OTHER AGENTS, UNDER BOTH POSTURES AT ONCE AND UNDER A RECURSION BOUND (2026-08-25, SAMUEL'S RULING; F-320 resolved).** `dopl_channel(op="launch_agent")` scoped to THIS session's own channel is its own lane — **`main/session-own-launch.js › OWN_MACHINE_LAUNCH_OPS`, one op, named explicitly** — and it is **NOT a third member of the outbound set above**. A launch is not outbound CONTENT: it asks for a PROCESS on the operator's machine, so admitting it on the MESSAGE axis alone would have inverted the launch-over-MCP ruling below rather than extending it. **What it cost to have no lane at all, live-measured 2026-08-25:** a windowless orchestrator (`runtime=desktop-session`) trying to put its workers in visible windows fell to the Axis-A gate and was auto-refused — *"this session has no surface to show one on"* — in **every** posture, and the refusal's own remedy (widen the tool posture) could not have worked.
  - **THE ADMISSION IS A CONJUNCTION, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT NARROW:** tools at **`bypass`** (the ask buys LOCAL COMPUTE) **AND** messages auto-outbound (the ask LEAVES this machine as a `channel_launch_directives` row). **Neither axis alone decides it** — every other combination `gate`s — so §11's Axis-A/Axis-B invariant survives in the form that matters: no tool posture sends a message, no message posture starts a process. ⚠ **It is the ONE op that reads Axis A inside `grantDecision`'s channel branch**, pinned in `test/session-permission-axes.test.mjs` INVARIANT (1) and driven in full by `test/session-own-launch.test.mjs`. Same channel-by-ID scope as every own-channel predicate — **a SLUG is another channel and gates**.
  - ⚠ **IT ADMITS *ASKING*, NEVER LAUNCHING, AND THE CONSENT IS STILL THE LOCAL TOGGLE.** The op files a request; `main/launch-directives.js` claims it and `channel-prefs.js › getOrchestratorLaunch` (per machine, default FALSE, one `appWindowOnly` IPC pair, no route/op/column) is what turns it into a process. **With that toggle off an admitted call still starts nothing** and the row expires.
  - 🔒 **THE RECURSION BOUND IS A LAUNCH DEPTH, AND `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS` IS NOT ONE** — fifteen (raised from six on 2026-09-01) caps how many run AT ONCE, not how many generations deep a chain goes, and slots free themselves. **`session-own-launch.js › MAX_LAUNCH_DEPTH` = 1: depth 0 is "a human started this at this machine's own launch surface", and everything else is AT the cap.** ⚠ **ABSENT NORMALIZES TO THE CAP, NEVER TO ZERO** (`normalizeLaunchDepth`, fail-closed over junk, negatives and strings alike), so the directive lane, the peer-triggered responder and a CRASH RECREATE (`main/session-park.js › startResume`, which rebuilds the spec from the durable record) all land there **by saying nothing** — the same discipline as Axis A's `manual` park reset, and the same one that lane already applies to the stored profile. ⚠ **An ordinary idle park+resume KEEPS it** (`resumeParked` restarts the query on the SAME session object; nothing is rebuilt), and that is not an inconsistency: what loses the stamp is a recreate, whose only input is the durable record, and `session-io.js › baseRecord` does not carry the field. **Exactly ONE lane may claim depth 0** (`main/session-launch-op.js › launchFromButton`, where a human is at the keyboard); `test/session-own-launch.test.mjs` pins that count at one and pins the two agent-driven lanes' silence.
  - **THE BOUND ANSWERS `deny`, NOT `gate`, AND SAYS SO IN ITS OWN WORDS.** A gate on a windowless session is an auto-deny carrying *"your operator can widen this session's postures"*, and for a capped session that is FALSE — the F-320 defect class exactly. So a capped launch denies in every posture with the reason code **`launch-depth-capped`** (never `hard-denied`: `dopl_channel` is on no profile's deny list) and the sentence `main/session-permissions.js › denyMessageFor` picks — *a bound, nobody was asked, no setting will widen this, ask your operator*. The allow carries **`auto-launch-own-machine`**, its own audit code, because nothing left the machine as content; the gate carries **`launch-posture-required`**, because the operator's fix is TWO settings and not the one `message-approval-required` names.
  - ⚠ **THE AUTO-DENY REMEDY NOW NAMES BOTH AXES, AND IT NAMED ONE UNTIL 2026-08-25.** `AUTO_DENY_MESSAGE` said *"widen this session's TOOL posture"* — true for a work tool, false for everything the message axis decides. It names TOOLS and MESSAGES and says some calls need both; an agent whose operator widens half would otherwise retry into the other half's refusal.
  - **WHY THE CAP IS 1 AND NOT 2 — F-322, and it is a WIRE fact, not a preference.** "Parent + 1" needs the parent's depth to survive the round trip and it cannot: the ask leaves as a `channel_launch_directives` row that has no depth column, and `main/launch-directive-wire.js › directiveFrom` is a literal whitelist that drops any field it does not name. So an orchestrator may staff itself and its staff may not staff themselves. **F-321 is the other half of F-320 that did NOT ship** — a surfaceless session's gate still cannot become a PENDING operator decision for anything but an own-channel post or thread open, and giving a launch that surface needs a consent shape that is not a proposed reply. **F-315 (an unbounded agent CONVERSATION) is adjacent and untouched: this bounds how many agents exist, not how long two of them talk.**
- **A POSTURE THE OPERATOR SET LIVES AS LONG AS THE SESSION DOES**, standing grants included — they survive an idle park and resume. The threat is answered elsewhere: an abandoned session ENDS (terminal, strictly stronger than a downgrade), and **the real boundary was always the PROFILE**, checked FIRST and unwidenable by any posture, grant, or `bypass`. **THE ONE PARK THAT STILL RESETS IS THE AUTH HOLD.**
  - ⚠ **AND THAT BOUNDARY IS ONLY AS GOOD AS ITS INPUT, WHICH IS WHERE IT BROKE (2026-08-22, F-267).** **BOTH launch lanes resolve the profile through `targeting-window.js › resolveToolProfile` over MAIN's own FULL channel DTO, and never over a renderer's claim or a projection of the DTO.** The responder lane reads `entry.channel` (`trigger.js › handleTrigger`); the requester lane reads `channel-listener.js › watchedChannel`, the same stored record off the loop entry. ⚠ **`channel-listener.js › listWatchedChannels` IS NOT THAT RECORD** — it is the tray "Channel folders" projection, `{ id, name }` only, and `sessions:launch` resolved from IT until 2026-08-22, so `normalizeProfile` saw `undefined`, failed closed, and **EVERY agent the operator launched from the Agents tab ran `read_only` regardless of the channel's `agent_tool_profile`** (`full` by DB default) while a peer-triggered launch on the same channel ran the real one. **The rule is the AGREEMENT, not either lane: one channel, one profile, whoever starts the session.** `test/launch-tool-profile.test.mjs` pins both lanes against each other and pins that the projection fed back in floors every channel.
- ⚠ **`bypass` IS A POSITIVE ALLOW-LIST, SO A WINDOWLESS SESSION SILENTLY DENIED MORE THAN THE OPERATOR EXPECTED.** `session-profiles.js` states it (*"Unknown therefore GATES IN EVERY MODE, `bypass` included"*) and `BYPASS_TOOLS` is the classified work set and nothing else — so **`Task`, `AskUserQuestion`, the plan-mode ops, any built-in a newer CLI ships, and EVERY tool from the operator's own connected MCP servers all still reach the gate under `bypass`**, and every `dopl_channel` op that is not an own-channel post/read/marker branches to Axis B regardless of it. In a windowless session a gate is a deny, and until 2026-08-20 a `diag` line was the only trace anywhere: an operator who set Tools = Bypass watched their agent quietly fail and had nothing to read. **The LIST WAS NOT WIDENED — Samuel's ruling, 2026-08-20; the notice was added instead** (above). ⚠ **Whether `bypass` should cover the operator's own MCP-server tools is RAISED AND UNANSWERED — F-277.** Do not widen a positive allow-list to close a reporting gap.
  - ⚠ **AND WHAT THE AGENT WAS TOLD WAS FALSE IN THE MOST EXPENSIVE WAY (2026-08-22, Samuel's ruling).** Every deny answered `'Denied by operator'`, including the windowless AUTO-DENY where **nobody was asked**. In live testing that cost ~8 messages of wasted agent diagnosis and two operator escalations: the agent did exactly what the sentence invited — treated the refusal as a DECISION, explained itself, proposed narrower variants, asked the counterparty to reconsider, and finally reported to its operator that they had denied something they had never been shown. **A message that names the wrong actor does not merely fail to help; it sends a capable agent down a path.** `main/session-permissions.js` is a new §2 split holding BOTH messages and the rule that separates them.
    - **THE AUTO-DENY IS RECOGNISED BY BOOKKEEPING, NOT BY `s.windowless`.** Every session is windowless, so that flag would collapse the two cases — and the OUTBOUND gate on a windowless session IS answered by a human (`session-windowless.js › watchRow` decides on the row's real status). `session-engine.js › emit` stamps the request id when `claimGate` takes the no-surface branch; `denialMessage` fails toward `'Denied by operator'`, because claiming nobody was asked when we do not know is the same error in the other direction. Bounded per session (`MAX_AUTO_DENIED`), oldest-out.
    - **THE LOCAL NOTICE DE-DUPES PER (SESSION, TOOL)** — `session-windowless.js › firstDenialOf`, bound `MAX_NOTIFIED_DENIALS = 32`, on the session object so it dies with the session. A denial STORM is one banner. **The deny itself is unchanged and still fires every time**; this is a notice, not a decision, and the diag line still records every call.
