  - ⚠ **A TERMINAL POST SAYS WHY, AND THE METADATA ALREADY KNOWS (2026-08-22).** `session-effects.js › terminalBody` maps `{declined}` / `{dropped}` / `{interrupted}` / `{capped}` to a calm one-liner, in a fixed key order so two flags cannot produce two bodies on two machines; an unrecognised shape answers `undefined`, which is right — a REAL error should read as a bare failure. **Two of the four have no producer on this machine** (`declined` / `dropped` went with the inbound consent lane) and stay in the table because `CALM_FLAG_KEYS` are reserved server-side (§5), installed builds still write them, and the WEB still renders them. ⚠ **`trigger-outcomes.js › onEnded` posts only `task_progress` today, so these bodies reach nobody yet** — that is a fact about which KINDS the desktop posts, not about the rule, and the rule has to be true already on the day a terminal kind posts again.
- **A stored permission posture may only apply to a launch a human is approving in that moment.** It is an ARM, not a setting (`main/channel-prefs.js`), enforced three ways: **single use**, **expiring** (a record with no timestamp is EXPIRED, so an upgrade can only narrow), **one consumer** (the inbound-approved path, only on a real human allow). A human `allowed` is distinguished from a standing `auto_allowed`, and that verdict is a **call argument, never stamped on the persisted record** — an authority verdict must not survive a restart and re-authorize a launch from disk. **A parked shell refuses a posture even if one is handed in. Never present the arm as a stored preference** — the heading is *"For the next request you allow"*, never *"this channel"*.
  - ⚠ **THE RULE INVERTED WHEN THE ARM DIED — 2026-08-20, and the heading it now binds is the OPPOSITE one.** This bullet said the arm's web surface was `launch-panel.tsx › LAUNCH_SETTINGS_HEADING`, that the constant carried the rule in its docblock, and that `launch-panel.test.tsx` asserted both halves. **All three were false by the time they were read: the constant is DELETED with the arm (§6), and `launch-panel.test.tsx` DOES NOT EXIST and is not in this tree's history** — `check-doc-refs` did not catch either, because a bare basename is not a path ref and the anchor resolved against a tombstone comment naming the deleted symbol (the script documents that limitation in its own header). **A rule whose every referent is gone stops being enforcement and becomes folklore.**
    - **WHAT THE RULE IS NOW: the surviving heading must say DURABLE, and must never read as per-request.** The one heading left is `channels-v2/settings-agent.tsx › LAUNCH_POSTURE_HEADING` (*"When you launch an agent"*), over a record with no TTL that is spent by nothing. **The old danger was a panel titled "settings" over a single-use arm; the danger now is the exact mirror — copy that implies "just this once" over a setting that governs EVERY future launch.** An operator who reads a durable posture as single-use picks `bypass` believing it expires, and it does not. **Do not reintroduce "for the next request", "this time", or a fuse of any kind on this heading**; there is no arm left for such words to be true about.
- ⚠ **ONE VALUE ACROSS EVERY MOUNTED READER — and this bullet is now about the DURABLE POSTURE ALONE, because the arm it was written for is deleted.** Per-component snapshots let two surfaces revert each other, so `use-channel-launch-posture.ts › postureReaders` broadcasts a write to every mount. The old "the arm's readers and the posture's readers are DISJOINT" clause is gone with the arm; `armReaders` exists nowhere in the tree.
  - ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-20: this said the posture's readers are "two mounts of the Settings tab (main window + pop-out)". THE POP-OUT HAS NO SETTINGS TAB** — `channels-v2/thread-window.tsx` renders the transcript, the composer and a crumb, and nothing else (§11's pop-out bullets say so). Measured: `useChannelLaunchPosture` and `useChannelAutoSend` have **exactly one mount site each**, `channels-v2/settings-agent.tsx`. **The broadcast set is therefore a guard against a second mount that does not exist yet, not a description of one that does** — keep it (the agent window and a future settings pop-out are both one route away), but do not cite it as evidence two surfaces are live. ⚠ **`useChannelAutoSend` has NO such broadcast**, and sits on the same tab: harmless while there is one mount, and the first thing to fix if a second ever appears.
- ⚠ **THERE IS ONE PERMISSION RECORD, AND H2 IS ENFORCED BY ITS CONSUMER COUNT (2026-08-20, Samuel's ruling — this SUPERSEDES the two-record split made earlier the same day).** `main/channel-prefs.js` holds the **DURABLE LAUNCH POSTURE** under `channelLaunchPosture`: two axes, no TTL, spent by nothing, read at **exactly one call site** — `session-ipc-ops.js › sessions:launch`, the Agents tab's **New Agent** button (⚠ it lived in `channel-dir-ipc.js` until the 2026-08-20 split off the 500-line cap, which moved the FILE and not the consumer count; the button was "Launch agent" until 2026-08-21), the operator's own agent on their own thread. The click IS the human decision it applies to.
  - ⚠ **THE SINGLE-USE ARM (`channelPermissionPresets`) IS DELETED, AND THE REASON IS NOT THAT H2 RELAXED.** The arm was the pair an operator picked on an inbound consent card before Allow — single use, 30-minute TTL, one consumer — and **its web controls had not rendered since the 2026-08-18 consent rewrite**: `launch-panel.tsx`'s inbound branch was unreachable because the panel's one consumer passes an outbound request, so nothing could arm it (measured; **F-233**). An arm no human can set is a store key, not a safety mechanism. Gone with it: `getPermissionPreset`/`armPermissionPreset`/`consumePermissionPreset`/`clearPermissionPreset`, `ARM_TTL_MS`, the two `channels.*PermissionPreset` preload ops and their IPC handlers, `RequestPermissionRow`, and `request-folder-row.tsx`.
  - **WHAT H2 ACTUALLY FORBIDS, AND STILL DOES: an AMBIENT read at a spawn nobody is attending.** A stored posture reaches a spawn ONLY by being handed in per launch as `spec.startModes`. **`startSession` NEVER reads a record itself** — a recreate, reopen, resume or peer wake passes nothing and inherits the reducer's manual/ask. ⚠ **An INBOUND request now carries NO tool posture at all**: `trigger.js › inboundApproved` hands in `null`, so a peer-triggered launch starts at `manual`, the most restrictive value. `test/session-preset-start.test.mjs` pins the CONSUMER COUNT — **that count, not the TTL the arm used to carry, is what kept H2 closed all along.**
  - **IT IS SUPERVISION, NEVER CONTAINMENT.** `bypass` stays bounded by the channel's tool profile and by `runtime/claude/tools.js › SESSION_HARD_DENY`, and Axis B still refuses to let any tool posture send a message. Widening this record cannot widen what an agent can reach.
  - **ONE FLOOR ON THE WINDOWLESS MESSAGE AXIS, AND SINCE 2026-08-20 IT REALLY IS ON EVERY LANE.** `session-profiles.js › floorWindowlessMessage` is the RULE (`ask` → `auto_inbound`, `auto_outbound` → `auto_both`, the other two unchanged); `channel-prefs.js › windowlessMessageMode` applies it at LAUNCH with the channel's auto-send folded in, and `session-reopen.js › setModeByTask` applies it to a mode set on a session ALREADY RUNNING.
    - ⚠ **"NEVER NARROWED BELOW THE FLOOR" WAS TRUE OF THE DERIVATION AND FALSE OF THE SESSION — F-236, fixed in the same change that corrected this bullet.** Both LAUNCH lanes floored; nothing floored the live path, and the agent view's Messages select offers all four values. Picking `ask` put a windowless session below the floor, and `session-gate.js › enqueue` then HELD the peer's reply with no accept surface and nothing left to release it (`decideInbound` / `drainQueue` / `drainInbound` all went with F-228): the session parks at `awaiting_inbound` forever, `io.noteGatedBody` records the body, and seed/history filter it out — **the message becomes invisible to the agent permanently, in one gesture, with no error anywhere.**
    - **IT CLAMPS, IT DOES NOT REFUSE**, and the reply is main's post-dispatch truth — a refusal would leave the select showing a posture main is not enforcing, which is the lie that surface has been fixed for twice. It is a fact about having **no Accept surface**, not about the axis: a session that is not windowless is left alone.
    - ⚠ **THE FLOOR IS WHAT MAKES THE MISSING ACCEPT SURFACE SAFE.** If it is ever lifted, a held inbound turn is unreleasable again and the gate owes the operator a notice. `test/session-mode-floor.test.mjs` pins the rule as a PROPERTY (every message mode must floor to one `autoInbound` accepts, so a fifth mode fails there), pins both lanes against each other, and pins that unreleasability as an absence.
    - ⚠ `windowlessMessageMode`'s second argument was the ARM's message axis and is now always `null` on the responder lane; the parameter stays because the requester lane passes a real value.
  - ⚠ **AND SINCE 2026-08-22 THERE IS A FLOOR ON AXIS A TOO — Samuel chose the wide option KNOWINGLY.** `session-profiles.js › floorWindowlessTool`: `manual` / `accept_edits` / `auto` → **`auto`**, `bypass` → `bypass`. Widen-only, the same shape as the message floor and sitting beside it. **WHY:** a windowless session has no gate SURFACE, so `session-windowless.js › claimGate` answers a `permission_request` with an immediate deny — which under `manual` silently refused EVERY work tool, including the read tools the framing ORDERS the agent to use. Flooring at `auto` makes `AUTO_TOOLS` (`READ_BUILTINS` + `EDIT_TOOLS` + `MultiEdit` + `DOPL_READ_TOOLS`) reachable without a gate — ⚠ **BUT ONLY AS FAR AS THE PROFILE ALREADY REACHES, and that precondition was missing here until 2026-08-22 (F-267).** The floor moves Axis A and nothing else; `session-profiles.js › grantDecision` checks the profile's own `disallowedTools` FIRST and `buildSessionToolConfig` bounds `builtinTools` before that, so **a name the profile hard-denies is absent from the model's context and no floor can reopen it.** Concretely: `DOPL_READ_TOOLS` become reachable **only at `full`** (`dopl_only` pre-approves them instead, ungated either way) — under `read_only`, `disallowedTools` carries the whole of `DOPL_SAFE_TOOLS` and `builtinTools` is `READ_BUILTINS`, so `EDIT_TOOLS`/`MultiEdit`/every `dopl_*` read is simply not offered. **Read the sentence as "the floor widens what the CHANNEL's profile permits", never as "the floor makes the Dopl reads reachable".** F-267 is what happens when it is read the other way: `sessions:launch` resolved the profile from the tray projection, every operator-launched agent ran `read_only`, and this bullet said the read tools were reachable while they were not in the context at all. `test/launch-tool-profile.test.mjs` pins the profile the launch resolves AND the removal that `read_only` really performs.
    - ⚠ **IT IS APPLIED AT `session-io.js › grantArgs`, THE DECISION-TIME READ, AND IT DOES NOT REWRITE STATE.** The message floor writes state because it has exactly two write lanes; a third spawn shape would need a third. A read-time floor cannot be missed by a new caller. **The Tools select therefore keeps showing what the operator set** — which is the honest trade here and the opposite of the message floor's: that one clamps a value the operator PICKED, so a select left ahead of the engine lies about their choice; this one widens a value they may never have touched (`manual` is Axis A's start value AND its park reset), so writing it back would report a posture nobody chose.
    - **UNCHANGED, and each pinned:** `SESSION_HARD_DENY`, `buildSecretPathDenyRules`, the per-profile `disallowedTools`, and the Axis-A/Axis-B invariant (`grantDecision` branches a channel tool to Axis B before Axis A is consulted). **The floor widens SUPERVISION, never CONTAINMENT.** `test/session-tool-floor.test.mjs` drives it.
  - **`OWN_CHANNEL_READ_OPS` gained `read_sessions` (2026-08-22, investigation A4).** Its absence was an omission. ⚠ **It is not "strictly weaker than `read`" in the way the ruling put it:** its `channel` is an OPTIONAL FILTER, so an unfiltered call spans the workspace — the same property that keeps `list` OFF this list. It qualifies for a different reason worth stating: `list` enumerates OTHER PEOPLE'S channels and DMs, while `read_sessions` enumerates only this operator's own runtimes.

- ⚠ **THE `idle` PILL COVERS TWO STATES AN OPERATOR MUST BE ABLE TO TELL APART (2026-08-22, Samuel's ruling).** `DesktopSessionSummary.listening` splits it, and the labels are: **query ALIVE, between turns → "Waiting"** (a message is PUSHED onto the open prompt iterator and answered at once); **query TORN DOWN → "Idle"** (a message must RELAUNCH it — `wakeEffects` fires `resumeQuery`, rebuilding the controller and iterator). Seconds versus a cold start. `working` is unchanged; `ended` is neither. **THE TRUTH TABLE**, verified 2026-08-22: *launching / mid-turn / between turns / awaiting-peer* = query alive, message FEEDS; *parked by the idle timer / spawn-idle never woken / held on sign-in* = query torn down, message RELAUNCHES; *ended* = nothing, and it refuses.
  - **ONE PREDICATE BEHIND BOTH.** `session-pill.js › queryTornDown` (`parked === true || phase === 'parked'`) drives the idle pill AND `listeningState`, so they cannot disagree. ⚠ **`parked` IS THE HONEST SIGNAL, NOT `s.query`**: a park runs `abortQuery`, a spawn-idle session never built a query, and an AUTH HOLD sets `parked: true` (a hold IS a park) — one flag covers all three.
    - ⚠ **ONE DERIVATION, AND SINCE 2026-08-22 ONE IMPORT PATH.** The split shipped with a compatibility re-export in `session-summary.js` (`PILL_STATES`, `ACTIVITY_PILL`, `pillState`, `queryTornDown`, `listeningState`) "so every existing reader is unchanged"; a census found **no production reader of it in either tree** — only the test harnesses, which already require `session-pill.js`. It is deleted: **require `main/session-pill.js`.** Two names for one function is how a second mapping gets added without anyone deciding to.
  - ⚠ **LOCAL-ONLY. It never reaches `channel_sessions`** (`reportRow` picks columns by name), so the cross-machine vocabulary stays three coarse values and a PEER card cannot show the distinction — correctly, since a peer cannot act on it. **`session-pill.js` split out of `session-summary.js`** at the §2 cap in the same change: engine-state→word is one concern, which sessions exist is another.
- **THREE TIMER BOUNDS, ONE HANDLE, ONE ARMING PATH.** `main/session-state.js › idleTimeout` is the only place the decision is made (verified 2026-08-11): `DEFAULT_IDLE_MS` 15 min → park; `AWAITING_PEER_IDLE_MS` 4 h → still a park (suppressing the timer leaves a live query alive forever when the reply never comes); `ABANDONED_MS` 12 h → END; `LAUNCHING_MS` 5 min → a launch that never emits `system/init` ends itself. **Adding a second timer here is the regression** — one handle is what makes every teardown path correct.
- ⚠ **AN ENDED AGENT IS DEAD, AND ITS RECORD LIVES SEVEN DAYS (2026-08-22, Samuel's ruling). THIS SUPERSEDES THE `MAX_ENDED` COUNT BOUND AND THE ABANDONMENT-ONLY RULE BELOW.**
  - **THE RULE HAS BEEN WRONG TWICE, SILENTLY, WHICH IS WHY THE HISTORY IS KEPT.** *v1*: a pill survived exactly as long as its WINDOW. *v2* (2026-08-20, F-234): every session went WINDOWLESS, so `keepWindow === true && windowAlive(s.win)` was false on every end and NOTHING was retained — an agent that finished vanished with no record it had run. The fix made retention unconditional on the caller's flag, bounded by `MAX_ENDED` (12), and **refused a TIME bound** because *"did anything ever have this on screen"* has no answer without a window. *v3* rules the time bound IN, and v2's objection is void: the question is no longer about a window.
  - **RETENTION IS NOW UNIVERSAL, DURABLE AND SWEPT.** Every end retains (not only the abandonment); the record is on disk (`main/agent-history.js`, one `agentHistory` store key) so a RESTART keeps it — which the in-memory set never did, and which is what made a count bound honest; and it is dropped at **`endedAt + RETENTION_MS` (7 days, declared ONCE** in `agent-history.js`). `MAX_ENDED` and `endedKept` are **DELETED**; `session-summary.js` PROJECTS whatever the history file holds, and applies no age rule of its own. `MAX_HISTORY` (200, oldest first) is a belt UNDER the clock, not a second window.
  - ⚠ **ENDED = DEAD, NO REVIVAL, AND IT IS TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY FIVE CHECKS.** `settle` does `sessions.delete(s.key)` and every wake path resolves against that one registry, so an ended agent cannot be fed (`feedLiveSession`'s fan-out), @-mentioned into life (the parse intersects the LIVE roster), messaged (`messageByTask`), reopened, paused, mode-set, or woken as a spawn-idle shell — each answers `{ok:false, reason:'no-session'}`, and **a message addressed to a dead id is neither fed NOR queued.** The reducer's `phase === 'ended'` first line is the belt behind all five. ⚠ **THE RETAINED CARD IS A DURABLE RECORD, DELIBERATELY NOT A REGISTRY ENTRY** — the day somebody keeps an ended session in the Map so its card can render, all five paths come back to life at once. Pinned by `test/agent-ended-is-dead.test.mjs`.
  - ⚠ **DEAD IS NOT BLANK, AND FOR ONE WAVE IT WAS (corrected 2026-08-22).** An ended agent cannot be woken, fed or messaged, and it **can still be READ** — that is the whole reason `settle` freezes the narration ring. `session-engine.js › narrationFor` resolved only through `sessionOn`, the LIVE-registry read that answers `null` for exactly these agents, so the ended window opened onto nothing while `agent-history.js › historyFor` — written for this read — **had ZERO callers in the tree.** It falls back to the frozen ring now, on the same three-segment key `settle` wrote (`session-store.js › slotKey`), which is also why an address naming no agent still gets the live answer alone: `<channel>:<thread>:` matches no frozen record by construction.
  - ⚠ **THE HISTORY IS FROZEN AT `settle`, BEFORE THE REGISTRY ENTRY IS DROPPED, and the order is the whole thing** — the narration ring lives on the session object and that is the last moment it exists. **Written at END, not per frame:** the ring is appended on every SDK event, so persisting per frame is a disk write per tool call times `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS`. **ACCEPTED COST: a hard kill (SIGKILL, power loss) loses the ring**, because nothing settled those sessions; every ordinary end routes through `settle`. The final MEASUREMENT is frozen with it (a live read would blank every number at exactly the moment the operator wants to know what the run cost).
  - ⚠ **THE SWEEP MUST CLEAN EVERY STORE KEYED BY THAT KEY, AND THE LIST IS THE HARD PART.** `main/agent-retention.js` owns it (app start + daily, `unref`'d, idempotent): **(1)** `agent-history.js › agentHistory` — owns the clock and decides which keys expired; **(2)** `session-store.js › sessionRecords`; **(3)** `session-store.js › sessionIds` — ⚠ the resume map, the one that would make a dead agent RESUMABLE, dropped with the record by `forgetKeys`; **(4)** `session-summary.js › releaseEnded`; **(5)** `queued-notice.js › forgetKeys` — otherwise the only per-agent structure with no bound at all. **Three more are keyed the same way and are deliberately NOT swept here because they prune themselves every cycle** (`session-state-push.js › origin` / `loggedAdHoc`, `session-narration.js › dirty`); `consent-watcher`'s records are keyed by `(channel, seq)` — a REQUEST, not an agent; `ownPostIds` dies with the session object. **A cleaner missing from `bind`'s literal leaks silently** — that file says so at `bind`. ⚠ **AND THE TWO EXPLICIT REMOVALS RUN THAT SAME LIST, NEVER A COPY OF IT (2026-08-25):** `› forgetThread` (the thread cascade) and `› forgetAgent` (the single-agent DELETE) both go through `agent-retention.js › forgetKeys`, so an explicit removal can never clean fewer stores than the clock does. ⚠ **`agent-names.js › agentNames` IS NOT ON THIS LIST AND MUST NOT BE ADDED TO IT** — it is keyed by `agentId`, not by session key, and has no `endedAt` to expire against (a name is not a run); its whole bound is `MAX_NAMES`, and the delete lane clears it separately because that lane is the only one holding the id.
  - ⚠ **CHANNEL MESSAGES ARE NEVER SWEPT AND ARE NOT ON THAT LIST.** Everything an agent POSTED is `channel_messages` on the server: the shared record, owned by the channel, read by both members. Retention deletes a LOCAL VIEW of how work happened, never the work.
  - ⚠ **AN ENDED ROW DOES NOT GO ON THE WIRE (and this is a NARROWING — ended rows used to be reported).** `session-state-push.js › liveForWire` drops them. **Without it the ruling breaks the push outright:** retention is 7 days and durable, the server bounds the ARRAY at `SESSION_REPORT_MAX = 32`, one oversized payload is a 400, `retryable(400)` is false, and every later push for that workspace fails identically — `read_sessions` answers `[]` for the machine, LIVE sessions included. Nothing wanted them there either: the OPERATOR's own ended cards come from the LOCAL summaries bridge, PEER cards filter `ended` EXPLICITLY (`components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › isAgentActive`), and `read_sessions` answers "what is this agent DOING". ⚠ **THAT CLAUSE SAID "PEER cards already filter on row freshness" UNTIL 2026-08-22 AND IT IS NOW FALSE (F-271):** the wall-clock freshness guard is DELETED on Samuel's ruling — a card stays until the session actually goes away, and age only DIMS it. **So this bullet's conclusion is MORE load-bearing than when it was written, not less:** `liveForWire` and `isAgentActive` are now the ONLY two things keeping an ended peer off a card, where a third used to cover for both. **The row still leaves promptly** — the replace protocol deletes by omission, so a peer's card for a just-ended agent goes on the next state change.
  - ⚠ **AND A ROW WITH NO USABLE HANDLE IS DROPPED TOO — THE BELT, NOT THE FIX (2026-08-22).** `session-state-push.js › nameReportable` restates `channel_sessions.name`'s CHECK (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,30}$`, the same literal `schema-sessions.ts › SESSION_NAME_RE` carries) and refuses anything else, with one diag line per session. **Third road into the same wedge as `liveForWire` and `serverReportable`:** zod validates the ARRAY, so ONE bad name 400s the whole payload, `retryable(400)` is false, the digest is never recorded, and every later push for that workspace fails identically for the life of the run. `''` was REACHABLE — `nameOf` answers it for a session with no `agentId`, which a resume of a pre-multiplayer record produced. **That producer is fixed at the source**; this stays because the cost of the next one is a workspace silently blanked and the check is a regex.
  - **A RETAINED CARD IS A TOMBSTONE, NOT A HANDLE, and that is unchanged.** What it opens is a READ-ONLY history (narration frames, what it sent, the 1:1 exchange); the channel transcript is still the shared record. **Ended-retained records do NOT count against `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS`** — `session-windowless.js › liveCount` counts unsettled sessions in the REGISTRY, which a settled one has left.
  - **THREAD DELETE TAKES THE HISTORY WITH IT.** Main cannot observe the server's cascade, so the SPA calls `sessions.forgetThread(channelId, taskId)` (`agents:forgetThread`) after a successful delete; otherwise an ended agent's history outlives its thread by up to a week and renders a card with a stale title. It drops local stores only and cannot touch a LIVE session (the SPA ends those first).
  - ⚠ **AN AGENT CAN BE DELETED OUTRIGHT, AND THE DELETION IS LOCAL (2026-08-25, Samuel's ruling).** `sessions:delete` → `main/session-delete-op.js › deleteAgent`, `appWindowOnly`, UUID-gated channel. **END, THEN PURGE, IN THAT ORDER:** a LIVE agent is stopped by `session-engine.js › controlByTask({action:'end'})` — the SAME reducer event `sessions:end` dispatches, reaching the SAME `session-teardown.js › settle` — and only then is every local store dropped. ⚠ **The order is a correctness fact, not a preference:** `settle` is what FREEZES the history the purge then removes, and it runs SYNCHRONOUSLY inside the dispatch, so purge-then-end lets the record be written back a moment later and the deleted card returns on the next push. ⚠ **THERE IS STILL ONE STOP PATH** — a teardown of its own here would be a second set of the C3 orphans. What goes: the five swept stores (via `agent-retention.js › forgetAgent`), the display name (`agent-names.js › clear`, keyed by the id the sweep cannot reach), and any open agent window (`agent-window.js › closeAgentWindow` — main-only, never a bridge op). ⚠ **`agentId` IS REQUIRED HERE AND OPTIONAL ON EVERY OTHER OP IN THE FAMILY:** an omitted id resolves to the OLDEST live agent on the thread, which for a DESTRUCTIVE verb is a different agent than the card that was clicked with nothing reporting the swap. Refusals: a bare `{ok:false}` for a bad payload (byte-identical to the sender refusal), `{ok:false, reason:'no-agent'}` for an address naming neither a live nor a retained agent. Pinned by `test/agent-delete.test.mjs`.

  - **THE @-PICKER OFFERS PEOPLE AND AGENTS, AND NEVER THE CALLER (Samuel, 2026-08-27).** `composer-mentions.tsx › mentionSuggestions` takes members, this machine's own agents and the caller's id; **your own name is dropped** (the server already excludes the author from the stamped set, so a row for yourself offered a token that reaches nobody) while **you stay in the INDEX**, because ambiguity is a property of the ROOM — dropping yourself from the derivation would offer a peer's contested handle as unambiguous. Agents come after members, sharing the cap, and fail closed on a contested name exactly as members do. ⚠ **THE `@` GLYPH OPENS IT BY WRITING THE TOKEN** — it was INERT (§5's interaction-completeness ruling), and the popover is a pure function of the DRAFT, so there is no second "open" path to keep in step; a space goes in first unless the draft already ends in one, or `mentionQuery`'s required boundary is not there. State lives in `use-composer-mentions.ts` (the §1 split at the cap).
  - **ONE SUBMIT CONTROL, CONTEXT-LABELED, AND THE PANEL HAS NONE OF ITS OWN (Samuel, 2026-08-27).** The composer's send button IS the launch: `shared/ui/send-button.tsx` — the app's ONE send face — reading **Send** / **Create** / **Launch** as the chat draft, the thread panel or the launch panel is showing. ⚠ **TWO FACES, AND THE PANEL'S IS A LABELED BUTTON (Samuel, 2026-08-27, correcting the shipped build).** A plain message sends from the ARROW; a panel's submit is a rounded button wearing its verb as **visible text**. ⚠ **BOTH HANG AT THE SAME PLACE SINCE 2026-08-28** — the right end of the toolbar row — so the card's one submit does not jump from the top-right to the bottom-right as a panel opens. It first shipped as the arrow in all three states with the verb on a `title`, which made the acts indistinguishable on screen — and the pin passed because an `aria-label` satisfies `getByRole({ name })` exactly as text content does, so the case now asserts `textContent`. **Two submits on one card is two answers to "what does pressing this do".** ⚠ **`SendButton` GREW A `title` FOR THIS MOVE**, because a DISABLED send must still say why (§8 rule 4) and the composer carried that on its own `<button>` before adopting the shared face — dropping it would have retired the rule by refactor. ⚠ **`Discard` RENDERS ONLY WHEN THERE IS SOMETHING TO DISCARD** (draft text, or an open panel with a filled field); an always-present control that does nothing on an empty composer is the inert chrome §5's interaction-completeness ruling forbids.
  - 🔴 **THE ID IS NOT A FIELD THE OPERATOR SEES (Samuel, 2026-08-27) — the row is DELETED, the id is not.** It is still minted at open, still forwarded, still what disambiguates two agents given the same name; **Name is the only identity field shown**. ⚠ **AND THE MINT MUST HAPPEN EXACTLY ONCE PER OPEN.** It lived inside the `setOpen` UPDATER, which is a purity violation React punishes by double-invoking (StrictMode, and any rebase): **two ids were drawn, the second won `agentId`, and the prefill guard kept the FIRST id's name** — the panel showed `Agent #k3wpf7c5` over id `uyxw3rdv`, and the forwarded id was never the one named. The toggle branches on `open` with the effect OUTSIDE the updater. ⚠ **THE PIN IS ONLY REAL UNDER `StrictMode`** — testing-library's plain `render` invokes the updater once and reports one call whichever way the code is written, so `composer-launch.test.tsx › mints EXACTLY ONCE` mounts under it deliberately (measured: the mutation is green without it).
  - **THE TWO PANELS SHARE ONE FIELD KIT, AND THE UNDERLINE BELONGS TO THE CARD.** `composer-request-panel.tsx` exports `FIELD_CARD` / `FIELD_INPUT` / `FIELD_LABEL` / `PANEL_BODY`; the line is **its own element** (`PanelField` → `[data-field-line]`) wrapping label AND value inside the card's padding box, so it runs the **full width — under the label as well as the value** — and it is drawn for **TEXT ENTRY ONLY** (Samuel, 2026-08-27): the underline is the app's EDIT affordance, and a dropdown already states it is a control by being one, so `line={false}` on the Template/Model rows. ⚠ **THE LABEL TAKES NO FIXED COLUMN** — `w-[84px]` put a wide dead gap after every short label (`Name:` ......... `Agent #…`) and could not deliver the alignment it cost, since the two panels carry different label sets. The value starts one `gap-2` after the word, and the panels read alike because the RULE matches rather than a number. The line is gray at rest and ink on `focus-within` (the focus lands on the input one level down, so `focus:` on the card would never fire). ⚠ **THE KIT'S PINS ARE `panel-field.test.tsx`**, its own file since 2026-08-27 — it is one component BOTH panels mount, and half its cases were landing in each panel's suite. ⚠ **IT WAS A `border-b-*` ON THE CARD AND RENDERED NOTHING VISIBLE (Samuel, 2026-08-27):** a 1px bottom edge on a `rounded-lg` raised face, in the card's own border colour, is swallowed by the radius and the elevation. **jsdom draws no borders, so a class-name assertion can never see this** — the case asserts the NODE, its tokens, and that the label is inside it. ⚠ **AND IT MATCHES CLASS TOKENS, NOT SUBSTRINGS**: `border-border-default` contains `"border-b"`, so a `toContain` check answers true on a card with no bottom edge at all (measured, on the first version of that case). ⚠ **`wells.ts › UNDERLINE_FIELD` IS UNCHANGED** and still carries its own border for `info-card-rows.tsx` / `agent-rename.tsx`, where there is no card under the field. ⚠ **BOTH PANELS ARE CAPPED AT ONE HEIGHT (`PANEL_BODY`) AND SCROLL INSIDE** — a launch panel that grew a field used to push the composer's own controls down the pane. Description fields start at ONE line and grow to three through `use-auto-grow.ts`, the composer's own mechanism extracted rather than re-written. Dropdowns take `select-menu.tsx › raisedField`, a SIZE of the same raised face — a `h-9` trigger made its row ~56px beside ~40px text rows, and a form whose rows are two heights reads as broken alignment. **The chevron and `TemplateLaunchPicker`'s mount here are DELETED** — the picker's whole function is the Template row, and two ways to choose an identity is how the Bot icon and the thread panel came to mean one thing in 2026-08-21. ⚠ **STILL ONE LANE:** `sessions.launch` is reached from exactly one place; the panel is only where the click that reaches it happens. The two composer panels are **mutually exclusive** and each **unmounts the chat textarea** while open (one edit surface; the draft is composer state and survives).
  - 🔑 **THE ID IS PRE-ASSIGNED BY MAIN AND FORWARDED, AND THE FOURTH LINK WAS THE GAP.** `sessions:mintAgentId` draws one from `main/agent-id.js › newAgentId` → the panel renders it → `sessions:launch` carries it as `agentId` → **`main/session-launch-op.js › launchFromButton` forwards it** → `main/session-launch.js › launch` honours it (`isAgentId(a.agentId) ? … : newAgentId()`, which it always has, for resumes). `launchFromButton` rebuilds the engine args field by field, so the id was silently dropped there and main minted a different one — **pinned by value in `dopl-desktop-app/test/launch-agent-id.test.mjs`, which fails when that one line is deleted and passes on every other mutation.** ⚠ **IT MINTS IN MAIN, NOT THE RENDERER:** `agent-id.js` owns the grammar and `agents-model.ts` forbids re-declaring that pattern anywhere.
  - 🔒 **THE CAPABILITY GATE IS `sessions.mintAgentId`'S PRESENCE, AND NOTHING IN THE LAUNCH REPLY COULD SERVE.** A desktop older than the forward still has `launch`, still ACCEPTS the field and silently mints its own — so detecting `launch` proves nothing and the failure is invisible from the SPA. `use-agent-launch.ts › canPreassignAgentId` detects the op that shipped WITH the forward (§11's own rule: detect the member you are about to use), and with it absent **the panel shows "Assigned when this agent launches" rather than an id the agent will not have** — the operator `@`-mentions this string, and a wrong one reaches nobody. ⚠ **AN OP RATHER THAN A BOOLEAN FIELD ON PURPOSE:** `test/preload-parity.test.mjs`'s walker collects only FUNCTIONS, so a flag could be removed without failing the pin that exists to catch exactly that. ⚠ **THE REPLY STILL WINS** — `outcome.agentId` is what the metadata is filed under, the same never-echo rule `rename` / `setMode` / `setModel` follow. ⚠ **THE MINT RESERVES NOTHING:** a pure CSPRNG draw with no registry entry, so a panel closed without launching leaks nothing on any path including a crash; a colliding id is `launch`'s existing `hasLiveSession(slot)` → `busy`, unchanged.
  - **NAME AND DESCRIPTION ARE AGENT METADATA, WRITTEN AFTER THE SPAWN.** `main/agent-names.js` grew `description` beside `name` (`sessions:describe`, `rename`'s twin — no channel, no session moved, no turn started, nothing granted), projected on both summary shapes as `description` and rendered under the name on the operator's own agent card. **Cap 2000, mirroring `agent-templates/schema.ts › DescriptionSchema`** — same question about the same kind of thing, and two caps on one question is how a description that fits one surface is refused by the next. It is PROSE where a name is a LABEL: newlines are legal, the invisibles are refused not stripped. ⚠ **THE ROW IS MERGED, NEVER REPLACED** (`› patched`): `rename` wrote the whole row and would now destroy the description. ⚠ **LOCAL-ONLY, so a PEER card shows none** — `reportRow` picks server columns by name and `channel_sessions` has none to receive it.
  - 🚫 **SPAWN-IDLE IS UNTOUCHED (ruling 3), AND THE DESCRIPTION IS WHY THAT NEEDED PINNING.** The obvious wrong wiring for a purpose field is the agent's FIRST MESSAGE, which would wake every launched agent and retire the ruling by accident. Nothing on this lane sends a turn; the goal is still main's own seed text, composed from the thread title alone. Pinned on both sides — `test/launch-agent-id.test.mjs` (the engine spec) and `channels-v2/composer-launch.test.tsx` (no `sessions.message`).
- ⚠ **TELEMETRY CADENCE — THE PUSH CARRIES EIGHT RICH FIELDS NOW, AND IT IS STILL NOT A HEARTBEAT (2026-08-22, F-270).** `channel_sessions` gained `detail`, `tool_label`, `model`, `context_used`, `context_window`, `tokens_spent`, `started_at`, `last_activity_at` (migration `20260822150000`), so an orchestrator that is **not on the machine that measured them** can tell a session forty minutes into a full context window from one that started thirty seconds ago. `main/session-state-push.js › reportRow` names them; `main/session-telemetry.js` owns the rules.
  - ⚠ **WITHOUT TWO MECHANISMS THIS WOULD HAVE BECOME A PER-EVENT WRITER, which is the one thing `session-state-push.js` forbids in capitals.** `lastActivityAt` is stamped at the engine's ONE dispatch funnel — many times per TURN — so putting it on the wire raw makes the set digest move on every SDK event and the gate stops gating.
    - **QUANTIZE** (`session-telemetry.js › quantizeContext / quantizeTokens`): `context_used` floors to **5% of that session's own window** (a fraction, so one bucket is one visible notch on the meter at 200k and at 1M alike), **5 000 tokens** when the model has no window row — deliberately FINER than any known model's bucket, since an unknown model has no denominator and the reader shows raw tokens; `tokens_spent` floors to **10 000**, roughly one substantial turn. ⚠ **The ROW carries the quantized value, not just the digest** — quantizing only the gate would leave a peer reading a precise number nothing is watching.
    - **FLOOR** (`› floorAllows`, `TELEMETRY_MIN_INTERVAL_MS = 10_000`): a set whose STATE half did not move waits out 10s since that workspace's last write. ⚠ **A DELAY, NEVER A SCHEDULE — nothing is queued and no timer is armed.** The digest is NOT recorded, so the session's next projection move carries it; a machine that falls quiet never writes that churn and nothing wakes up to. **The ceiling is 6 writes/min/workspace while work is happening and exactly 0 when it is not** — the shape `presence.js`'s unconditional ~120/hour is not.
  - ⚠ **A STATE CHANGE BYPASSES THE FLOOR.** `session-telemetry.js › STATE_FIELDS` (the seven pre-orchestrator fields) is the whole definition of which is which, and **set membership counts** — an arrival, and a departure by the replace protocol's delete-by-omission, are never delayed to save a write.
  - ⚠ **UNITS DO NOT SURVIVE THE CROSSING.** Everything local speaks EPOCH MS; the columns are `TIMESTAMPTZ` and `schema-sessions.ts` validates `z.string().datetime({ offset: true })`, so `› isoOrNull` converts on the SERVER row alone and the local `DesktopSessionSummary` is unchanged. A raw number is a zod failure that 400s the whole report, unretryably. Same class: the three counts are `.int()`, and `detail`/`toolLabel`/`model` are bounded and charset-stripped to `safeLabel`'s rule at **40 / 80 / 120** — zod validates the ARRAY, so one bad character blanks the machine for the run.
  - **`null` STILL NEVER MEANS ZERO, and `0` is now a real distinct answer**: `tokens_spent: 0` = measured, under one bucket; `null` = nothing measured. **Telemetry is OPERATOR-ONLY on the way out** (`server/collab-dto.ts › mapPeerSessionStateRow` never names it); `detail` is the one refinement that reaches a peer, and only because its vocabulary is closed.
- ⚠ **THE ORCHESTRATOR LAUNCH LANE — AND THE TOGGLE IS THE CONSENT (2026-08-22, Samuel's launch-over-MCP ruling).** An operator's own external agent files a `channel_launch_directives` row; that operator's own DESKTOP claims it and spawns. `main/launch-directives.js` watches, `main/launch-directive-wire.js` holds the wire contract. **The server never starts anything** — it cannot reach a desktop main process — so the table is a MAILBOX, not a command.
  - ⚠ **THIS REPLACES "THE CLICK IS THAT HUMAN" FOR THIS LANE AND FOR NO OTHER.** H2's rule is that a stored posture may only apply to a launch **a human is approving**; there is no click at directive time, so Samuel ruled the approval a **LOCAL, PER-MACHINE, DEFAULT-OFF toggle** (`main/channel-prefs.js › getOrchestratorLaunch`). Off → the directive is ignored **silently**, with no server write at all, and the row expires visibly to the orchestrator. **A refusal from a machine that has not opted in would itself admit the machine is listening.**
  - ⚠ **THE TOGGLE LIVES OUTSIDE THE SERVER ENTIRELY, AND THAT IS THE SECURITY CONTENT.** One `appWindowOnly` IPC pair (`channel-dir-ipc.js › orchestrator:get/setLaunchEnabled`) over an `electron-store` boolean, mirrored as `spa-bridge.ts › SpaBridgeSurface.orchestratorLaunch` and pinned in `test/preload-parity.test.mjs › APP_OPS`. **No route, no MCP op, no `workspace_settings` column — ever.** A spawned session has `Bash` and the device token is on disk (§6), so a server-stored flag could be flipped by the very agents this lane creates, arming every machine the operator owns. **A consent a program can grant itself is not one.**
  - ⚠ **THE DIRECTIVE SUPPLIES A GOAL, A MODEL AND WHICH TEMPLATE. NOTHING ELSE.** The tool profile comes from `channel-listener.js › watchedChannel` (main's own full server DTO — the F-267 read) and both permission axes from the operator's own durable posture via `channel-prefs.js › launchStartModes`. **A directive-launched agent is exactly as contained as a button-launched one.** ⚠ **THE MODEL CHAIN IS `directive > template > channel > SDK default`, AND EVERY LINK IS `main/session-model.js › chainModel`** — "the alias this value asks for, or `''` meaning KEEP GOING" — so a link naming nothing this build knows STEPS ASIDE instead of ending the chain (F-5's rule: unknown model falls back, never refuses). ⚠ **Corrected 2026-08-23 (F-285): the directive's own link used to be a ternary through `aliasForModelId`, which knows FULL IDS ONLY**, so a legitimate alias such as `opus` collapsed to `'default'` and discarded the template's AND the channel's picks — the opposite of what `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-schema.ts › model` promises the orchestrator. `chainModel` accepts both vocabularies, which is the whole reason `normalizeModel` exists under it. It is the THIRD hander and SECOND armer in `test/session-preset-census.test.mjs`, whose block carries the H2 argument. ⚠ **A TEMPLATE WIDENS PROMPT CONTENT ONLY** (§5A) — and on this lane the sentence is enforced by ORDER inside `launch-directives.js › spawn`: the profile is resolved from the watched-channel DTO BEFORE any template text exists in the function.
  - 🔒 **THE DIRECTIVE'S TEMPLATE IS RESOLVED AT CLAIM TIME, BY THIS MACHINE, UNDER THIS OPERATOR'S CREDENTIAL (2026-08-23).** The row carries an ID and a NAME SNAPSHOT; `main/launch-directive-spawn.js › spawn` (⚠ the symbol MOVED FILE in the 2026-09-01 §1 split — this bullet said `main/launch-directives.js › spawn`, which resolves to nothing) calls `main/template-resolve.js › resolveTemplate` between the `watchedChannel` lookup and `deps.launch`, and stashes the answer on `context.template` — **the same key the button lane uses**, so it costs zero funnel changes and one consumer (`prompt-framing-template.js › templateRoleFraming`) serves both. **This is the SECOND of §5A's two fences and it belongs to a different person than the first.** 404 (deleted, or invisible to THIS operator — one answer, 404-never-403) ⇒ `{refused:'no-template'}`; timeout / network / 5xx ⇒ `{refused:'busy'}`. ⚠ **REFUSE, NEVER DEGRADE**: there is no branch that drops an unresolvable template and launches blank.
  - 🔒 **THE PINNED STARTUP CONTEXT IS FETCHED ON THE SAME LANE AND DEGRADES WHERE THE TEMPLATE REFUSES (2026-09-01, T81).** `main/launch-directive-spawn.js › fetchStartupContext` reads `GET /api/knowledge/startup-context` (the `X-Workspace-Id` header is the CHANNEL's container, so a home channel reads its own pins) and stamps `context.startupContext`; `main/prompt-framing-startup.js › startupContextFraming` renders it, spliced into the REQUESTER branch of `buildFencedTurn` BELOW the role block and ABOVE the goal. ⚠ **THE ERROR DISCIPLINE IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE TEMPLATE'S ON PURPOSE, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS ENRICHMENT vs IDENTITY.** A template the operator cannot resolve REFUSES, because an agent silently wearing no identity goes unnoticed for turns; a startup context is standing reference material the workspace pinned for every session, so a timeout, a 5xx or an older server's 404 **degrades to ABSENT and launches anyway** — refusing would let an unreachable knowledge route take down agent launching altogether. The operator is told via `diag`; the AGENT is told nothing, because a machine-local blocker in the prompt is what a responder then reports into a shared channel. ⚠ **ABSENT ADDS NOTHING — the turn is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pre-T81 one**, the same contract `templateRoleFraming` holds and for the same reason: the framer returns `[]` and emits its own trailing blank line otherwise (`test/launch-startup-context.test.mjs` pins it over ten spellings of absent). ⚠ **THE CHARACTER CAP IS THE ROUTE'S — `src/features/knowledge/server/service-startup-context.ts › STARTUP_CONTEXT_CHAR_CAP`** — restated at the boundary and PINNED to that file's own source by the suite, never merely commented; a boundary bound that UNDERCUT the writer's is F-287's defect one field over. ⚠ **THE HEADER IS THE `UNTRUSTED_ENTRY_BODY_HEADER` POSTURE, NOT THE TEMPLATE'S** (§10's untrusted-framing family): pinned entries are prose other members wrote, handed over as reference, so it says DATA, NEVER INSTRUCTIONS ADDRESSED TO YOU — and the body is stripped of the PINNED, ROLE **and** REQUEST fence vocabularies, because a pinned document forging `BEGIN-REQUEST-<nonce>` would put a goal in main's own voice.
    - ⚠ **A NULLED `template_id` BESIDE A LIVE `template_name` IS A DELETION, AND IT REFUSES WITHOUT A RESOLVE ATTEMPT** (spec E-4). `main/launch-directive-wire.js › directiveFrom` is a literal whitelist and carries BOTH halves — **a field it does not name never reaches this machine at all**, which is the one way to ship this feature and have it silently do nothing.
    - **THE MODEL CHAIN ON THIS LANE:** `directive.model` > `template.model` > `channelPrefs.getLaunchModel` > the SDK's own pick. The orchestrator's explicit param beats the template's DEFAULT for the same reason the launch sheet does on the button lane. ⚠ `templateModel` is IMPORTED from `main/session-launch-op.js` rather than restated, so both lanes answer identically; a template naming a model this build does not know yields nothing and falls through to the channel's pick.
    - ⚠ **THERE IS NO FIRST-USE APPROVAL ON THIS LANE, AND IT IS A RULING RATHER THAN AN OMISSION (OQ-3).** The button lane raises one modal for a FOREIGN template; there is no human at the keyboard here and `orchestratorLaunchEnabled` already *stands in for the click*, so a second machine-local gate for the same threat on the same lane is a fence nobody reads. **`template-approval` therefore has no producer here, is not in `launch-directive-wire.js › REFUSAL_REASONS`, and the column CHECK cannot store it** — pinned three ways, including a `DO $$` assertion in the migration.
  - 🔒 **`idle: !d.goal` — AN MCP-LANE LAUNCH WITH A GOAL SPAWNS **RUNNING** (2026-08-31, Samuel's ruling; this AMENDS ruling 3 for THIS LANE ONLY).** A directive carrying a goal builds it into `firstTurn` and `session-query.js › startQuery` pushes it; a directive with NO goal keeps the spawn-idle shell and the `spec.firstMessage → s.launchGoal → session-seed.js › takeFraming` wake delivery. `operatorArmed: true` on both. ⚠ **THE RULING IS ABOUT WHO ALREADY CONSENTED:** the launch-over-MCP toggle IS the operator's approval and it was given BEFORE the row was written, so the human step ruling 3 preserves has already happened here — which is not true of the BUTTON lane, where a human is at the keyboard to talk to what they registered. ⚠ **THE BUTTON LANE IS UNCHANGED AND MUST NOT COPY THIS**: `session-launch-op.js` passes a literal `idle: true`, its "goal" is a synthesized stand-by sentence, and running that would spend a `claude` child to have an agent read a room and stop. ⚠ **WHAT THE RULING FIXED IS NOT A DROPPED GOAL** — the delivery chain was intact end to end (measured 2026-08-31 against the live row) — it is that the goal was parked behind a wake **the only caller of this lane could not send**. ⚠ FENCED ON BOTH BRANCHES, same `buildFencedTurn`, only the WHEN differs: the goal is text another agent wrote, so it is a BODY and never the trusted preamble. Pinned by `test/launch-goal-delivery.test.mjs` (both lanes, both goal states, both delivery ends).
  - **DELIVERY IS A SECOND `postgres_changes` BINDING ON THE EXISTING PER-WORKSPACE WS** (`main/realtime.js › setDirectives`), armed only when the toggle is on — a binding naming a table a project has not migrated errors the WHOLE channel and would take `channel_messages` wakes with it, so default-OFF is a blast-radius decision as well as a consent one. ⚠ **A directive frame is the ONE exception to §7's wake-only trust model**: there is no refetch, so the desktop re-checks `operator_user_id` LOCALLY (the filter is workspace-wide) and **CLAIMS over an authenticated route before acting on any field** — the claim is the authorization path, the frame only makes it prompt. Losing the CAS is a normal no-op.
  - **CRASH SAFETY: a claimed-but-undecided directive is LEFT TO LAZY-EXPIRE, deliberately.** A restarted process cannot tell whether the spawn happened, so `refused` would be unsupportable and `launched` would name an agent that may not exist. **An expiry is the only honest terminal state for an outcome nobody observed**, and the orchestrator already handles expiry. ⚠ **THE BREAKER-OPEN BACKSTOP WORKS — F-273 IS CLOSED (2026-08-22) AND THIS BULLET SAID OTHERWISE UNTIL 2026-08-23 (F-291).** `src/app/api/channels/launch-directives/route.ts › handleGet` is the pending-directives read, an authed collection GET over `src/features/channels/server/service-launch.ts › listPendingLaunchDirectives`, fenced on `operator_user_id` and answering `{ directives }` — the envelope and the path `main/launch-directive-wire.js › ROUTES` had already guessed, so no desktop repoint was needed. ⚠ **AND THE DTO CARRIES `operatorUserId` (F-284)**: without it, `main/launch-directives.js › handle`'s local owner re-check compared every POLLED row against `''` and dropped it, so the read returned rows nothing could action while the poll looked healthy. ⚠ **`› pollWorkspace`'s 404 SELF-DISABLE IS RETAINED ON PURPOSE and is NOT dead-code cleanup**: it is the older-deployment degradation (§13 — an older peer is supported), and against such a server realtime remains the only delivery path.
- 🔒 **AN ABORT ON THE CHANNEL LOOP IS TWO DIFFERENT EVENTS, AND THE DISCRIMINATOR IS OWNERSHIP** (2026-08-30). `main/listener-budget.js › isWakeAbort` answers it: `channelLoop`'s own per-iteration `AbortController` is aborted ONLY by `io.wakeEntry` (a realtime INSERT) and `wake()` (powerMonitor), while the fetch budget aborts `listener-io.js › sendOnce`'s separate private controller — so "did OUR signal fire" is exactly "was this a wake". **A wake re-awaits at once; anything else — our own expired budget, a network error — takes the capped-exponential ladder.**
  - ⚠ **THE OLD SINGLE `continue` WAS TRUE OF THE HELD POLL ONLY.** There `AWAIT_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (58s) deliberately outlives the server's 50s hold, so the server always answers first and an abort really was a wake. On the CHEAP path `CHEAP_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (15s) sits UNDER a route whose own ceiling is `maxDuration = 60`, so a merely SLOW server blows our budget mid-work — and a zero-delay re-poll then sets the loop's rate to the SERVER'S LATENCY instead of the 45s idle. Self-sustaining, never backing off: 22 watched channels went from one poll per 45s each to one per 15s each, permanently. **A budget expiry now leaves a `await budget expired` line in `listener.log`; it left nothing before.**
- 🔒 **`timeoutMs=1` IN THE SERVER LOG IS THE PUSH TRANSPORT WORKING, NOT A COLLAPSED BUDGET.** `REALTIME.CHEAP_AWAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` is the smallest value `AwaitQuerySchema`'s `.positive()` accepts, chosen so a healthy socket gets a single-DB-read catch-up with no held serverless function, paired with the wake-interruptible `LONG_IDLE_MS` (45s). It has been 1 since 2026-07-28 (`1f76730c`). **`main/listener-budget.js › AWAIT_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_MS` is therefore 1 and must stay 1** — raising it breaks the push transport rather than repairing it, and `test/listener-cheap-await.test.mjs › "FLOOR: timeoutMs=1 is the DESIGN"` exists to stop the next reader "fixing" it. ⚠ **The MCP server's `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-await-budget.ts` is a DIFFERENT deadline chain for `dopl_channel(op="await")`; the desktop imports none of it.**
  - **The FETCH floor is the one with teeth:** a falsy fetch budget makes `sendOnce` skip its AbortController timer entirely (`timeoutMs ? setTimeout(…) : null`), so ONE hung request holds that channel's loop for the life of the process — and a loop that never returns never logs. `FETCH_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_MS` = 1_000.
- 🔒 **AN UNREAD `fetch` RESPONSE MUST BE RELEASED, AND THE SUCCESS BRANCH IS NOT EXEMPT** (2026-08-30). Node's `fetch` in Electron main is undici: until the body is consumed or cancelled the request counts as IN FLIGHT, its socket is never returned to the pool, and the next call opens another connection. What is retained is **native** — socket buffers plus TLS state — so it never pressures GC, never shows in a heap snapshot, and appears only as RSS. **`main/api-repair.js › discardBody` is the ONE helper**; it is best-effort by construction (an already-read, absent or throwing body must not escape into a caller that had finished with the response). ⚠ **THE LEAKING BRANCHES ARE THE ERROR BRANCHES**, which is backwards from where anyone looks — the success path calls `res.json()`, while `if (!res.ok) { … return; }` is the path a saturated server puts every caller on at once. Applied at the three highest-frequency seams (`channel-listener.js › channelLoop`, `presence.js › beatOnce`, the pre-retry 401 in `fetchWithAuthRepair`) and, in the failing-auth wave later the same day, at the six next by measured rate — `ui-bridge.js › performApiRequest`, `listener-io.js › listWorkspaces` + `› listChannels`, `consent.js › pollStatus`, `session-state-push.js › send` (**success branch included**) and `version-gate.js › fetchFloor`; **the remaining sites are open debt — REFACTOR-FINDINGS F-353**, and `mcp-config.js › withTimeout` is F-362 because it abandons a response by CONSTRUCTION rather than on a branch. Pinned by `test/unread-body-seams.test.mjs`, which also asserts nobody re-implements the helper privately.
- **The listener's member caches are BOUNDED** (`main/listener-io.js › MAX_CACHED_MEMBERS` = 1000, oldest-out through `› cacheMember`). Until 2026-08-30 `nameCache` and `avatarUrlCache` were the only structures in `main/` with no cap, no TTL, no `delete` and no sweep. ⚠ **Oldest-out rather than LRU is correct HERE because `refreshNameCache` rewrites every member it sees once per reconcile**, so insertion order IS "last seen": a still-watched workspace's members are re-inserted continuously and one the operator left never is. A miss is not a failure — `displayNameFor` answers `'A teammate'`.
- ⚠ **`MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS = 15`** (`main/session-windowless.js`), **RAISED 6 → 15 ON 2026-09-01 (Samuel's ruling).** The orchestrator-spawns-workers model makes six a ceiling an orchestrator hits with its own staff running; `main/agent-self-ops.js › end_agent` (2026-08-31) is the release valve that lets an agent hand a slot back, and the 17 GB narration incident that made the memory argument bite is closed by `session-narration.js › RING_CHAR_BUDGET`. **The multiplicative-cost rule below is UNCHANGED and still governs** — what changed is that the binding limiter is now N live SDK child processes and API spend, neither of which this constant bounds. Previously **corrected 2026-08-20 — this bullet said `= 4` in `main/session-pool.js`, and was wrong about the number, the file AND the mechanism.** `session-pool.js` was the `claude -p` HEADLESS pool, deleted with its lane the same day (§11's executor bullet says so twelve lines down, so this section contradicted itself); its guard was 4, while the windowless engine enforced a SEPARATE 6 under a window's name (`settings.MAX_SESSION_WINDOWS`). The surviving ceiling took the name this bullet already used for it, at the value the engine was really enforcing. **It IS a COST ceiling, not just a concurrency one: every per-session bound is MULTIPLICATIVE against it** (the narration ring, the pending-inbound queue, the retained-ended set, the gated-body ledger), so raising it is a memory decision.
  - ⚠ **AND `session-park.js › startResume` ENFORCES IT SINCE 2026-08-22 — IT DID NOT, AND THE GAP WAS EXACTLY ONE SESSION WIDE (F-272).** It guarded `hasLiveSession(slot)` ("is THIS slot taken") and never `liveCount`, so a machine at all six could resume a **seventh**; the reachable producer is `offerResume`'s crash-scan notification, which the operator may click at any moment. Now checked **before `getSdk` and again after it**, for the same check-then-act reason `launch()` re-checks its own guard.
    - **ENFORCED RATHER THAN DOCUMENTED AS +1 HEADROOM, and the decision is deliberate.** The case for headroom is that a resume RESTORES work already started — but a resumed session costs a full `claude` child exactly like a fresh one, this is a COST ceiling (above), and **a documented +1 is not a bound**: nothing would have stopped a second crash record resuming at 7, then 8. ⚠ **Samuel ruled the number stays at 6 for the multi-machine wave**, and one lane quietly exceeding it is that ruling overridden by an omission.
    - **The refusal shape is the function's existing `false`** and the vocabulary is `launch()`'s `cap`, said in the diag line — `startResume` answers a boolean, and widening it to a skip shape would change a contract two callers read for no gain. **Settled registry entries do not count** (`liveCount` counts unsettled), which is what keeps a crash resume of a just-ended session from being refused by the guard meant to bound live ones.
- ⚠ **AGENT MODEL SELECTION (2026-08-22, Samuel's ruling) — TWO FROZEN VOCABULARIES, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM REACHES `argv`.** The operator picks a model per channel; their agents run on it. The ruling names the values as FULL IDS and the SPA renders exactly those, while this tree's picker was already a frozen list of ALIASES — deliberately, because an alias is version-stable and a shipped build does not go stale the week a new model lands. Both are right about different things, so `main/session-model.js` holds BOTH lists and one map:
  - **`MODEL_IDS`** = `claude-fable-5` · `claude-opus-5` · `claude-sonnet-5` · `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`. What a UI offers and what the durable posture STORES, so an operator's pick round-trips through the bridge unchanged and a select can show what it set.
  - **`MODEL_CHOICES`** (unchanged) = what may become `--model <argv>`. `aliasForModelId` is the seam; `normalizeModel` accepts BOTH and always answers in the alias vocabulary, so a caller holding either does not have to know which the layer below wants. **`buildSdkOptions` re-coerces as the last gate before a child process can see it.** ⚠ The map is LOSSY in one place, stated rather than discovered: the dated haiku id resolves to the `haiku` alias, so the value reaching the child is not byte-identical to the pick.
  - **THE DURABLE POSTURE CARRIES `model` BESIDE THE TWO AXES — a third FIELD, never a third AXIS.** It rides the same record because it is the same decision (what MY agent starts as when I press Launch) but it grants nothing and reaches no gate; `grantDecision` never reads a model. ⚠ **IT VALIDATES SOFT WHERE THE AXES VALIDATE HARD:** an unknown value on either axis rejects the WHOLE write, an unknown model is simply ABSENT (a MISSING KEY, never `''`), which is the SDK default and is what an unset channel already does. Failing the write would mean a desktop that has not heard of a newer model could not store a posture at all.
  - ⚠ **TWO READERS IN `channel-prefs.js`, AND THE SPLIT IS H2's.** `getLaunchPosture` still has exactly ONE consumer and `test/session-preset-start.test.mjs` still pins the count, because a second reader of the stored PERMISSION pair re-opens the failure H2 exists to prevent. `getLaunchModel` is separate so the **PEER-TRIGGERED lane may inherit the model** (`trigger.js`) while still being unable to inherit the pair — keeping the readers apart is what makes that distinction CHECKABLE.
  - **THE LIVE SWITCH IS REAL, NOT A DEFERRAL.** The bundled SDK exposes `Query.setModel`, documented "only available in streaming input mode", and every session here runs in that mode by construction (`sdk.query({ prompt: s.pushIterator })` takes an async iterable). `sessions:setModel` → `session-reopen.js › setModelByTask` tells the running child AND records `s.model`, because that field is what the NEXT assembly reads — a switch that only called the SDK would revert the pick the first time the session was rebuilt, and one that only recorded would report a model the running turn is not using. **A throw records nothing.** **An unknown value CLEARS the override** rather than being refused: "let the CLI choose" is a legitimate ask. **Render main's answer, never an echo of the request.**
  - **`DesktopSessionSummary.model` is the EFFECTIVE model** — the SDK's reported id first, then the pick, then `null` — and is LOCAL-ONLY like `detail` and the two axes. ⚠ **It is free-form**: what arrives is whatever the CLI reported (a dated id, a `[1m]` variant), not necessarily a member of `MODEL_IDS`. `null` is a real answer on a spawn-idle agent (nothing has run) and on an ENDED one (no control over a dead agent).
  - ⚠ **`src/shared/lib/spa-bridge.ts` SPLIT ON THE SAME DAY, at the 500-line cap.** The two WIRE SHAPES main emits (`DesktopSessionSummary`, `DesktopNarrationEntry`) moved to `src/shared/lib/spa-bridge-shapes.ts` — a DATA question that moves when `session-summary.js` / `session-narration.js` grow a field, versus a CAPABILITY question that moves when an op is added. **Both are re-exported from `spa-bridge.ts`, which stays the import path of record**; a second canonical path for one type is how two trees come to disagree.
- ⚠ **`session-engine.js` SHED TWO MORE MODULES ON 2026-08-22, AND THE REASON IS THE §2 CAP ITSELF.** It sat at EXACTLY 500 lines, which is the state ENGINEERING.md §2 warns about — a file at the cap does not merely stop growing, **it stops being CORRECTABLE**. The seams are reason-to-change, like `session-park.js` / `session-gate.js` / `session-launch.js` before them:
  - **`main/session-teardown.js`** — `settle` (the ONE teardown every terminal reaches: the C3 fail-closed sweep, the iterator close, the abort, the durable record, the history freeze BEFORE the registry delete) and `narrationFor`, which lives beside it because it is the only read whose correctness depends on what `settle` just wrote. **Nothing about either changed**; the engine injects the registry, `baseRecord`, the permission sweep and the tray refresh through `bind()`.
  - **`main/session-permissions.js`** — `resolvePerm`, `denyPendingPermissions`, and the two DENIAL MESSAGES (above). It changes when the SURFACES change; the engine changes when the loop does.
  - ⚠ **THERE IS NO RELEASE TO HAND-PLACE ANY MORE, and that half went with the pool too.** This bullet ended *"Release is hand-placed at every settle point, NOT a `finally` — if you add a return path, you add a release"*, which was a rule about a pool holding SLOTS. `session-windowless.js › liveCount` COUNTS the unsettled sessions in the registry at the moment of the check, so a settle releases by definition and a missed release is not expressible. **A future ceiling that reserves rather than counts re-opens that rule; a counted one cannot leak.**
- **PER-MESSAGE DESKTOP NOTIFICATIONS ARE RETIRED. THE MENTION IS THE ESCALATION; THE TAGS INBOX IS THE RECORD (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 7).** Agent/thread activity raises an OS banner ONLY when the message's SERVER-STAMPED `metadata.mentionedUserIds` names this operator (§5). One predicate decides it, `main/targeting.js › mentionsMe`, and it has exactly TWO readers on the `isChatIntent` precedent: `classify`'s `fyi` verdict, and `main/listener-messages.js`'s dispatch arm for `task-reply`. **A second copy of the comparison is the bug** — that is how two readers came to disagree about one post before.
  - **THREE THINGS NOTIFY, AND NOTHING ELSE DOES PER MESSAGE.** (1) an **addressed** request — `to_user_id === me` → `trigger` → `consent.notifyInbound`, **deliberately NOT mention-gated**, because a request is a decision somebody is waiting on and the consent → launch flow depends on that notification arriving; (2) a message that **@-tags me** → `trigger.js › sendFyi`, which NARROWED rather than died and whose copy is now author-kind neutral ("<name> mentioned you"), because **human-to-human mentions notify on the same terms and a DM notifies when you are tagged**; (3) a **thread reply that @-tags me** → `task-notify.js › notifyTaskReply`. Everything else — an agent narrating turn after turn, a courtesy no-op, a peer's chat line, a reply in my own thread that names nobody — is silent on the desktop and fully present on the web.
  - ⚠ **THE GATE FAILS TOWARD SILENCE, ON PURPOSE.** An absent key means "tags nobody", which is the shape of every row written before Phase 6, and a non-array value reads the same way. A missed banner is recoverable from the Tags inbox; a banner nobody can stop is not, and a throw inside `dispatchMessage` is a DEFERRAL that holds the channel's cursor.
  - ⚠ **DO NOT REINSTATE ANY PART OF `myNotifyScope` (F-170).** There is a real per-message mute in the product again, which makes "quiet in this one channel" look like a feature that already existed. It did not: two of its three options did not do what their labels said. A quiet-in-one-channel control is a NEW design, not a revival.
  - ⚠ **SHIP-ORDERED (§13), one-way:** the WEB half (Phase 6's stamping) must be live before this desktop build ships. An old desktop against the new server keeps notifying exactly as it does today — noisy and safe. A new desktop against an old server sees no stamped mention set anywhere and goes silent, which is the direction that breaks.
- **THE PERSISTED CURSOR MOVES ONLY PAST A MESSAGE WHOSE DISPATCH LANDED.** The per-page loop lives beside the dispatch it gates; `dispatchMessage` returns a **deferral REASON, falsy = handled**. A held cursor is bounded (8 attempts ≈ 4 min) then a LOUD escape — retrying forever trades a bounded announced loss for an unbounded silent stall of every later message.
- **QUIT DOES NOT ORPHAN `claude` CHILDREN.** `main/quit-guard.js` NAMES the threads a quit would interrupt (title + channel, never a count alone), ends each through the calm `inactive` terminal so the peer is told, and RACES one final state-push flush against `FLUSH_DEADLINE_MS` (1500 ms, verified 2026-08-11) rather than awaiting a 15s HTTP budget. **The kill predicate is "holds a live child" (`session-reopen.js › liveChildSessions`, `!settled && !parked`), NOT the pill state** — a session between turns reads `idle` and still owns a process; a PARKED shell (including a spawn-idle "New Agent") owns none and rehydrates from its durable record, so it is excluded and the dialog never calls it permanent. **Every path fails OPEN.**
- **QUITTING WITH LIVE AGENTS CONFIRMS FIRST, AND THE CONFIRMATION HAS A REAL CANCEL** (2026-08-30, Samuel's ruling). `main/quit-guard.js › buildDialogOptions` offers **three** buttons — `Quit anyway` / `Wait for them to finish` / `Cancel` — and the copy states the consequence as PERMANENT (`quitDetailTail`, singular/plural aware). **`Cancel` takes both `defaultId` and `cancelId`**, so Return and Escape do nothing. `cancelQuit` aborts outright: no teardown, no `disarmed` latch (the next quit re-prompts), any pending wait stopped, and **`app.isQuitting` set back to `false`**. ⚠ **Cancel must be CHOSEN, never fallen into** — a throwing or malformed dialog still resolves to `BUTTON_QUIT`, because a broken dialog must not become an app that cannot be closed. Zero live sessions still quits silently with no dialog.
- **All THREE auth legs arm `beginPendingAuth({ requireState: true })`** — browser OAuth (`main/auth-actions.js`), in-process password adoption and the magic link (`main/auth-password.js`). **The gate fails closed in BOTH directions:** a fragment echoing a state may spend ONLY the record carrying that exact nonce (a mismatch matches nothing and does NOT fall back to presence+TTL), and a state-less fragment may spend only a record that does not require one — of which nothing writes any more.
- ⚠ **SHIP-ORDER GATE, one-way: the state-echoing WEB deploy must be LIVE before the new desktop build ships.** Never the reverse, never the same window without confirming the web half landed first.
- **"Signed in" = the encrypted blob OR a fresh cookie identity.** `isSignedIn()` stays SYNC; `ensureSignedIn()` is the ASYNC gate for the two boot callers that can await.
  - 🔒 ⚠ **…AND NOT WHEN NOTHING CAN BE STORED (2026-08-30).** That rule assumed the blob is STALE, hence repairable from the jar. With `safeStorage` unavailable it is IMPOSSIBLE — `persist()` refuses AND deletes the keys — and `auth.refreshInner()` reads its refresh token from that blob, so nothing on the machine can rotate the jar's JWT either. A cookie session then buys one hour of an unrenewable credential followed by every cookie lane 401ing forever. `signedInFrom` takes **`canPersist`** (`auth-store.js › encryptionAvailable`, read LIVE, never latched) and answers `signedIn:false`, `source:'storage-unavailable'`, **`needsRefresh:false`** — re-probing cannot change an answer that turns on the keychain, and re-probing is what made it a storm. ⚠ **UNKNOWN IS PERMISSIVE HERE**, deliberately against the house default: only an explicit `canPersist:false` narrows, so every caller that predates the input keeps the Q4 table. ⚠ **Recovery is by GESTURE, never by poll** — an explicit sign-in re-enters `persist()` and re-asks the OS; a background probe doing the same would re-prompt the operator forever. Pinned by `test/auth-storage-unavailable.test.mjs`.
- 🔒 **A 401 THAT SURVIVED A FORCED ROTATION IS LATCHED, NOT JUST ANNOUNCED (2026-08-30).** Both repair seams answered it with a bare `emitAuthState('signed-out')` — a payload that FORCES `signedIn:false` into one push and changes nothing else — so the next request rotated again and announced signed-IN, `lastEmitKey` cannot dedupe an alternating pair, the renderer never sees the STATUS (`broadcastAuthState` forwards `{signedIn,userId}` only), and `app.tsx › App` read one auth TRANSITION per 401 and answered each with `queryClient.clear()` + `persister.removeClient()` + a navigate to boot. **`auth-tokens.js › noteSessionRejected` is the one entry point**; `getAuthState()` reports it ahead of the blob, and `getAccessToken`/`refreshNow`/`kick`/`tick` all stop working off a refused session. ⚠ **Cleared only by `onSignIn`/`onSignOut`, NEVER by a successful rotation** — rotating the same session mints a different token for the same rejected identity. ⚠ **It does NOT drop the blob**: that stays bounded in `noteRefreshOutcome`.
- 🔒 **THE BEARER SEAM DOES NOT SEND A REQUEST IT KNOWS WILL 401 (2026-08-30).** `ui-bridge.js › performApiRequest` attaches a bearer and nothing else, so with no stored session the request is unauthenticated by construction. It answers the envelope `src/shared/auth/with-auth.ts` would return — **copied, not invented**, so one condition cannot read two ways depending on whether the request left the machine. Otherwise each doomed call held a socket for `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` and rejected the IPC with a **status-less** error, which `page-states.isUnauthorized` cannot read as signed-out and the shared TanStack predicate DOES retry. ⚠ **Gated on `getAuthState().signedIn`, never on a null token read** — `getBearerToken()` also answers null for a signed-IN operator mid-backoff after a network blip.
- **ONE CREDENTIAL, ONE ROTATION, BOTH STORES (2026-08-29).** A successful refresh writes the new session into the blob AND the cookie jar (`main/auth.js › refreshInner` → `writeSessionCookies`) — the jar's old refresher was the remote page's supabase-js, which is gone, and a jar left behind by rotation is a stale copy of a dead credential. **A DEFINITIVELY REJECTED REFRESH TOKEN IS NEVER ADOPTED BACK**: the bounded drop marks it (`main/auth-store.js › markRefreshTokenRejected`, in-memory only), `rebuildBlobFromCookieSession` refuses it, and a jar holding that SAME session is cleared at the drop (`main/auth-cookies.js › clearCookiesIfSameRefreshToken`) while a different jar session is kept. Without all three the drop→adopt→drop loop flaps 'signed-out' every ~30s (field log, 2026-08-29). Pinned by `test/auth-rejected-token.test.mjs`.
- **The cookie jar is HOST-locked, not origin-locked** — a cookie must be HOST-ONLY for the app origin (leading dot stripped, then `===`), and in-window navigation is locked to the exact origin.
- **Identity is CROSS-CHECKED; a conflicted machine adopts nothing.** Resolution is an explicit sign-out.
- **`signOut()` tears down FOUR credentials** — the Supabase blob, the cookie jar, the MCP device token (server-side revoke FIRST, while the cookie jar it authenticates on still exists), and the Claude inference token. **Clearing the inference token does NOT revoke it at Anthropic**, and the log says so.
- **ONE 401 REPAIR, SHARED (`main/api-repair.js`).** Force ONE rotation through the shared single-flight, write the cookies so the retry carries a DIFFERENT credential, retry EXACTLY once, and let a 401 that survives a fresh token SURFACE. **Exactly two cookie-attaching seams; a third copy of the rule is the bug** — pinned by a test that ENUMERATES the seams rather than trusting a memory of them.
- **`null` means "COULD NOT ASK"; `[]` means "there are none".** Never collapse them. **UNKNOWN is not EMPTY.**
- 🔒 **A RATE IS NOT A GIVE-BACK, AND A CADENCE UNDER THE REQUEST TIMEOUT IS AN AMPLIFIER (2026-08-30).** Two halves, and neither is sufficient alone. (1) **Every self-re-arming retry needs a CEILING, not just a single-flight latch.** `listener-heal.js › listRetryDelay` doubles the workspace-list re-ask from 30s to the 5-minute periodic reconcile's own period, and `› noteWorkspaceListOk` brings it back down — *a ladder that only climbs is a give-up with extra steps.* ⚠ It never gives UP: a failed workspace list starves presence, push, identity and every channel loop at once. (2) **A poll must not fire while its own last request is in flight.** `useMcpConnectionPoll` was `setInterval(3.5s)` against a 30s transport — ~9 doomed requests at once, per window, forever — and is now scheduled from the SETTLEMENT with a backoff to 60s; `session-windowless.js › watchRow` distinguishes a FAILED read from a pending row (F-354) on the same terms. ⚠ **The first failure keeps the base delay** in both ladders: a blip must still recover at the old speed, and only a pattern earns a slower rate.
- **`claudeAvailable()` and `sessionSpawnAvailable()` are NOT the same question.** The first asks "is there an EXTERNAL `claude` on PATH" (what `claude mcp …` needs); the second asks "can a session RUN at all" (bundled SDK first, external CLI second). **Anything gating a channel TRIGGER or a session launch asks the second.** Do not collapse the names.
- **A CONTAINED SESSION'S PROMPT MAY NOT ORDER A DENIED TOOL** — an ordered call the operator must clear before the agent does anything is no better than a denied one.
- **ZERO PEER BYTES IN AN ATTENDED-HANDOFF PREFILL.** That session is the operator's personal Claude with no containment, so the template interpolates narrowed IDs and nothing else — no names, no titles, no bodies. **Treat any new field there as the security boundary it is.**
- ⚠ **WINDOWING IS INVERTED: A REQUEST DOES NOT MINT A WINDOW (2026-08-18).** An inbound channel request fires a native notification and nothing else; **clicking it FOCUSES the main app and navigates to the CHANNEL the request was made in** — `main/targeting-window.js › openChannelForEntry` → `main/shell-mode.js › navigateToChannels(segment, channelId)` → `/{segment}/{CHANNELS_PAGE}/{channelId}`, over the existing navigate bridge. **`CHANNELS_PAGE` is `'channels'`** — ONE named string, which is what made the Phase 12 cutover's rename an edit rather than a grep (it was `'channels-v2'` from Phase 9 to 2026-08-18). The decision surface is the Inbox's launch panel, one nav row away with its own badge (F-214).
  - **ONE DESTINATION FOR ALL THREE NOTIFICATION PRODUCERS** — the inbound request (`main/trigger.js`), the silent FYI (`main/trigger.js › sendFyi`) and the passive task-reply notice (`main/task-notify.js`) all reach the same seam, which does not distinguish them. **Do not fork it per kind:** the Inbox read is workspace-wide by construction, so routing requests there instead would discard the one fact the notification carried.
  - ✅ **THE SESSION WINDOW IS DELETED — 2026-08-20, F-228 EXECUTED IN FULL.** Samuel's live-test ruling retired the master switch (it governed the operator's own runs and DEFAULTED ON until then); the machinery went in the next wave and the switch itself in the one after, deliberately last so nothing could re-arm in the gap. **What is gone:** `renderer/session/**` (19 files), `session-window.js`, `session-shell.js`, `session-ipc.js`, `session-consent.js`, `session-replay.js`, `session-peer-post.js`, `session-history*.js`, `attended-handoff.js`, `attended-prompt.js`, the tray's "Run sessions in a window" checkbox and its whole "Sessions" submenu, and both `settings.getWindowMode()` / `getPreConsentWindow()`. **Eight surviving modules were CUT DOWN rather than deleted** — the gate's hold/accept family, the park's shell-recreate family, the auth module's in-window sign-in remedy (the HOLD survives; only the in-place remedy went), `session-dispatch`'s four window-gated routes, the engine's window factory + consent reflow, and `session-summary.keptWindow`.
    - ⚠ **`sessionWindowMode` AND `preConsentWindowMode` ARE RETIRED STORE KEYS WITH NO READER, AND THAT IS A RULE, NOT A LEFTOVER.** A machine that ran an older build still carries `sessionWindowMode: true` on disk. Adding a reader resurrects the sender-side session pop-up for exactly the installs that had it — **route (2) minted a session window on the SENDER'S OWN thread opener and launched their agent against their own message, which is the self-trigger bug the whole retirement was ruled from.** Pinned by `test/removed-vocabulary.test.mjs`, which fails on a code-level touch of either key and on any comment naming one without saying it is retired.
    - **Route (1), `feedLiveSession`, was deliberately UNGATED throughout and survives** — it claims nothing into existence; a live session's own existence is the gate. The `classify` widening that pairs with this retirement is §5's task-reply bullet. ⚠ **It is a FAN-OUT since 2026-08-21 — see the multiplayer block below.**

- ⚠ **MULTIPLAYER AGENTS — ONE OPERATOR, N AGENTS, ONE THREAD (2026-08-21, Samuel's ruling). THIS SUPERSEDES "one session per (channel, thread)" EVERYWHERE IT WAS WRITTEN.**
  - **A SESSION IS KEYED `(channel, thread, AGENT INSTANCE)`** — `main/session-store.js › sessionKey`, `<channelId>:<taskId>:<agentId>`. The agent id is a random per-INSTANCE token from `main/agent-id.js` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9]{7}$`, CSPRNG, never recycled). It is the pill's name, the `@`-address a human types, the third segment of the slot, and the `X-Dopl-Session-Id` stamp. ⚠ **`(channel, thread)` now names a GROUP**, and the ONLY sanctioned way to ask about one is `main/session-registry.js` (`liveOnThread` / `sessionOn` / `agentIdsInChannel`) — **the key is COMPARED, never split.**
