  - **THE CURATED STONE-NAME POOL IS DELETED IN BOTH TREES**, with its parity test: `main/agent-names.js`, `src/features/channels/server/agent-names.ts`, `agent-names.test.ts`, `agent-names-desktop-parity.test.ts`. **Do not re-file it as an unused-export loss.** A pool handle was RELEASED when its pill left and re-issued within one projection pass, so under multiplayer `@flint` in a transcript could name a different agent than the one it was typed at. `agent-id.js`'s charset is a deliberate SUBSET of `channel_sessions.name`'s CHECK, so no migration was needed.
  - ⚠ **EVERY SPAWN MINTS AN ID, AND `startResume` IS THE ONE THAT DOES NOT GO THROUGH `launch` (2026-08-22).** `session-launch.js › launch` is the funnel that guarantees no session exists without an address; `session-park.js › startResume` builds its own spec, and it passed `rec.agentId || null` straight through — but **every durable record written before 2026-08-21 has no `agentId`**, and those records are on shipped operators' disks. It mints one now (`isAgentId(rec.agentId) ? rec.agentId : newAgentId()`), uses it for BOTH the slot key and the spec, and writes it back onto the record so a second click of the interrupted-notice still finds the live slot instead of starting a second session on one `sdkSessionId`. **What `agentId: null` cost was not local:** `session-summary.js › nameOf` answers `''`, the push files `name: ""`, and the server 400s the WHOLE array — see the wire bullet above.
  - **THE SERVER'S `SESSION_KEY_RE` WAS WIDENED TO MATCH** (`src/features/channels/schema-sessions.ts`, one line by design — the desktop suite lifts the literal out by regex). **The two-segment form is still accepted**: an older desktop is a supported peer during a rollout (§13), and refusing its keys blanks its whole workspace's rows rather than degrading.
  - **THE FEED IS A FAN-OUT, AND THE COUNTERPARTY FENCE IS GONE.** `main/session-dispatch.js › feedLiveSession` feeds EVERY live agent on `(channelId, firstClassTaskId)` **except the one that wrote the message** — counterparty posts, the OPERATOR'S OWN posts and SIBLING agents' posts alike. ⚠ **This is a real widening, stated plainly: a third member posting into the thread now reaches my live agents as a turn.** What still holds: nothing there MINTS a session, the body is still fenced as DATA, and **`targeting.classify` is UNCHANGED** — the addressing law that decides whether a message may START an agent is untouched.
  - **SELF-FILTERING IS BY `client_msg_id`, NEVER BY AUTHOR.** Every agent posts under the operator's own account with `authorKind: 'agent'`, so authorship cannot tell three of my agents apart. `main/session-outbound-tag.js` injects `client_msg_id: agent-<agentId>-<n>` on an own-channel post — the SAME seam and the SAME rules as the forced thread tag (own-channel only, never an overwrite, never on a deny) — and the session records it in a bounded `ownPostIds` set. **An unstamped post is fed to everyone, which is a duplicate turn rather than a lost one.**
    - ⚠ **THE COUNTER IS PERSISTED, BECAUSE THE AGENT ID IS (2026-08-22).** `n` counts from `s.ownPostSeq`, which `startSession` initialised to 0 on every session object while `startResume` deliberately RE-USES the stored agent id — so a crash+resume re-minted `agent-<id>-1, 2, …`, ids the server already had rows for, and its idempotency short-circuit **silently discarded the resumed agent's replies**. It is whitelisted onto the durable record (`session-io.js › baseRecord`, `session-store.js › durableSessionRecord`) and rehydrated through `session-store.js › resumedPostSeq`, which **adds slack** — the record is written at spawn / init / park / settle, so it always lags the posts a crash hid, and resuming from a lower bound is the collision. ⚠ **The SERVER half of that same failure is a different bug and was fixed in the same wave** — idempotency there was channel-scoped, not author-scoped (§5).
  - **`@<agentId>` IS PARSED ON THE DESKTOP, AND MUST STAY THERE.** The server's `metadata.mentionedUserIds` resolves against the channel ROSTER and correctly answers "nobody" for an agent id — that fail-closed behaviour is right and may not be widened, because a caller-settable mention set is a notification-forgery primitive. The parse is intersected with THIS machine's live ids, and **it decides only FRAMING**: an addressed message and an unaddressed one are both DELIVERED to every sibling; the difference is what the turn SAYS about it (`main/session-seed.js › addressingLines`). ⚠ **It was "one line in the turn" until 2026-08-22 and is not any more:** the coordination protocol fired ZERO times in 40 real messages, so the UNADDRESSED branch (which said nothing at all) and a MULTI-ADDRESSEE branch were added, and the unaddressed default was flipped — a message naming nobody is **not automatically yours**, and an agent that takes it claims it in one short line first. The multi-addressee branch carries a DETERMINISTIC TIE-BREAK (the first id named acts) because "COORDINATE IN THE OPEN" alone had failed in production. ⚠ **`addressingLines(undefined)` still answers `[]`, and `null` is what gets the prose** — `undefined` means no caller ran the fan-out verdict, `null` means it looked and found nobody; every production path supplies `null` (`session-reducer.js › pushInboundEffect` normalizes).
  - **SPAWN IDLE — `sessions:launch` REGISTERS AND STARTS NOTHING.** It mints an agent id, returns `{ ok, agentId }`, and registers a PARKED session with prepared context and **no first SDK turn**; no `claude` child exists until the first message for that agent arrives, which then launches the query with the full framing plus that message. ⚠ **Not "build the query and hold the prompt"** — a held query is a live child holding this session's pre-approved `dopl_channel` access with nobody watching it, the orphan shape C3/C-8 exist to prevent. The abandonment timer is armed at the spawn, so an agent nobody messages ends on its own. **The RESPONDER lane still spawns WITH a first message** and mints an id like every other.
    - ⚠ **IT IS STILL A SPAWN, SO IT ANSWERS THE CREDENTIAL QUESTION LIKE EVERY OTHER LANE (corrected 2026-08-22).** `startSession`'s `parkedShell` early return used to sit IN FRONT of `session-auth.js › holdIfNoCredential`, so **New Agent on a signed-out machine answered `{sessionId, agentId}` — a success** — and registered an agent holding a slot against `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS` that could never start a query, while `{skipped:'auth-hold'}` and every refusal line behind it were unreachable on the one lane an operator reaches by CLICKING. The preflight runs first now. **Starting no query is not the same as needing no credential**: the whole point of registering is that a later message wakes it, and on a signed-out Mac that wake produces a dead session with a peer waiting on it. Pinned behaviourally against the shipping tail in `test/spawn-idle-auth-hold.test.mjs`.
    - ⚠ **THE WAKE RULE IS TIERED (2026-08-28, Samuel's ruling — this REPLACES the 2026-08-22 ruling stated in the next bullet, which is kept because a reversal must be readable).** A DORMANT agent — a spawn-idle shell that never started a `claude` child (`awaitingDirective`), or a session an idle park tore the query down for (`state.parked`) — now wakes on **three tiers**, and every one of them sits behind a **LOOP FENCE**. `main/session-wake-tiers.js` is the one statement of the rule; `main/session-dispatch.js › feedLiveSession` is the one place it routes; `main/session-triage.js` is the only thing that spends money.
      - **THE LOOP FENCE — `session-wake-tiers.js › wakeEligibility(m, operatorUserId)`, asked ONCE PER MESSAGE, and it FAILS CLOSED on every axis.** ⚠ **IT ANSWERS ONE OF THREE STRINGS, NOT A BOOLEAN, SINCE 2026-08-31** — `ELIGIBLE_ALL` (a HUMAN-authored `kind: 'message'` row with an author: every tier), `ELIGIBLE_MENTION` (**TIER 1 ONLY** — the same-account carve below), `ELIGIBLE_NONE` (everything else). The middle member is the whole design: "may wake" and "may wake WITHOUT BEING ADDRESSED" are two questions, and collapsing them is how a carve meant for tier 1 leaks into 2 and 3. **A legacy `eligible: true` at `tierFor` is REFUSED, never read as `ALL`.** ⚠ **THE 2026-08-28 REVERSAL STANDS FOR A PEER'S AGENT**: tiers 2 and 3 wake on traffic nobody addressed, so the blast radius would stop being one disclosed agent id and become whatever is said in the room, and two agents that can wake each other on unaddressed prose is a loop with no operator in it. ⚠ **AN AGENT-AUTHORED MESSAGE IS STILL FED TO A RUNNING SESSION** — that is ruling 4's fan-out, how two of my agents coordinate, and it is untouched. The fence is on WAKING a dormant one, the only place a turn is conjured out of nothing. A session is also never woken by its own post (`wroteIt`, by `client_msg_id`).
      - 🔒 **THE SAME-ACCOUNT CARVE — AN AGENT-AUTHORED MESSAGE UNDER THE OPERATOR'S OWN USER ID MAY @-WAKE THAT OPERATOR'S DORMANT AGENTS (2026-08-31, Samuel's ruling; SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-28 fence's agent-authored blanket for the OWN-ACCOUNT case only).** `wakeEligibility` compares `m.authorUserId` against **this machine's signed-in operator** (`feedLiveSession`'s `myUserId`) — never an identity off the row being judged — and answers `ELIGIBLE_MENTION`. ⚠ **WHY IT HAD TO EXIST:** `launch_agent` over MCP hands the caller an agent id, the id door is tier 1, tier 1 sat behind the fence, and every post an agent makes is agent-authored — so the product published an address its only holder could never spend (ENGINEERING 2026-08-31: five @-posts, nothing woken, nothing said). ⚠ **WHY IT IS SAFE, AND WHY IT IS EXACTLY THIS NARROW.** Both properties that make agent-agent wake a loop are preserved: **ADDRESSED ONLY** (tiers 2 and 3 stay shut to every agent-authored message on every account — `tierFor` returns `TIER_NONE` for `mention` after the `addressedMe` branch, which is where the carve stops), and **ONE ACCOUNT** (a PEER's agent is as dead as 2026-08-28 left it). ⚠ **RESIDUAL, STATED:** two of MY OWN agents can now @-wake each other — a loop the operator authored, on one account, needing an explicit id per hop, with `wroteIt` still stopping a session waking on its own post. ⚠ **FAILS CLOSED ON THE IDENTITY**: a blank or unknown operator id makes an agent-authored message `NONE`. ⚠ **OUTSIDE THE KILL SWITCH**, like tier 1 itself — the switch must revert to a KNOWN GOOD state and since 2026-08-31 that includes the carve. Pinned in `test/wake-tiers.test.mjs` (the tri-state and the tier ceiling) and `test/wake-tier-routing.test.mjs` (own-account wakes / peer never / own-account UNADDRESSED still starts nobody).
      - **TIER 1 — @-MENTION, AND IT IS TWO DOORS SINCE 2026-08-28 (Samuel's F-350 ruling: *"the parser learns names"*).** An `@<id>` / `@agent-<id>` **or the agent's SLUGGED CUSTOM NAME** (`@research-bot` for an agent renamed "Research Bot") wakes it, at any roster size, and SUPPRESSES tiers 2 and 3 for every agent it did not name ("somebody else's addressee is not a directive", preserved verbatim). Both doors are intersected with the ids live on the thread.
        - ⚠ **THIS BULLET SAID "the parse is unchanged" AND LISTED THE ID FORMS ONLY, WHICH WAS TRUE OF THE PARSER AND FALSE OF THE PRODUCT.** The renderer's picker INSERTS the slug in preference to the id (`components/channels-v2/composer-mentions.tsx › mentionSuggestions` over `lib/agent-mentions.ts › agentMentionHandle`) and the transcript TINTS it, so **every agent the operator had actually named got a blue token main ignored** — and since the launch panel makes a name the one required field, that was very nearly all of them. F-266's tint-says-tagged / stamp-says-nobody class, one namespace over.
        - **THE NAME DOOR IS `main/agent-handles.js`, AND THE RENAME STORE IS ITS ONLY POSSIBLE AUTHORITY.** A rename lives in `main/agent-names.js`, on this machine, keyed by an id minted on this machine — no server has it and no peer can. `session-dispatch.js › feedLiveSession` builds the index per message over THIS thread's live ids (`agentHandles.handleIndexFor(liveIds)`, structurally pinned) and hands it to `mentionedAgentIds`; a caller that passes none gets the id door alone, which is the pre-ruling behaviour.
        - ⚠ **IT WIDENS RECOGNITION AND NEVER LOOSENS THE REFUSAL.** An UNKNOWN slug wakes nobody; an UNNAMED agent claims no slug at all and keeps its id door; **a slug two of this machine's agents claim resolves to NEITHER** (rule 5's fail-closed, so registry iteration order cannot decide a wake) while the ID form still reaches each twin; a name lookup that THROWS costs recognition, never the route. The ID form is never withdrawn.
        - **THE SLUG RULE AND THE TOKEN STRIP ARE HAND COPIES of `lib/mentions.ts › mentionSlug` / `› mentionHandleOf`** — main cannot import TypeScript — so they are paired the way `deep-link-target.js` is paired with `routes.tsx`: one fixture table in `main/agent-handles.js` (`PARITY_NAMES` / `PARITY_TOKENS`), driven by `dopl-desktop-app/test/agent-handles.test.mjs` on one side and by `src/features/channels/lib/agent-handle-parity.test.ts` — which SLICES the desktop pure block and runs it — on the other. **Either tree changing the rule alone fails a suite.**
        - **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE TINT:** a tinted agent mention now also WAKES for named agents, so tint and routing finally agree. The residual is the one both trees share and now pin together: a trailing HTML tag followed by punctuation (`@x</b>.`) defeats the tag strip in BOTH implementations.
      - **TIER 1 HAS A THIRD DOOR SINCE 2026-08-31: AN ESCALATION ANSWER.** A human pressing an option on an escalation card posts an ordinary `kind: 'message'` row carrying reserved, server-stamped `metadata.escalationAnswer`; `main/session-dispatch.js › escalationAnswerAgentIds` reads the `agentId` off it, intersects with the ids live on the thread, and UNIONS it into the addressee list after the two body doors. The agent that ASKED is the one that must be told, and by then it is usually dormant.
        - 🔒 **IT IS A DOOR ONTO ADDRESSING, NOT A HOLE IN THE LOOP FENCE.** `wakeEligibility` is asked FIRST and of the MESSAGE, so an agent-authored row carrying the key still wakes nothing at all. Pinned as an explicit case.
        - ⚠ **IT IS STRICTLY LESS FORGEABLE THAN THE OTHER TWO, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR IT.** The id and slug doors read the BODY, which any member can type; this key is stripped from caller input unconditionally and re-stamped server-side only after the caller is proved to be a member that escalation ASKED, with the agent id DERIVED from the escalation's own post stamp (§5).
        - ⚠ **WHY THE ANSWER DOES NOT SIMPLY WRITE `@agent-<id>` IN THE BODY:** the raw agent id is never user-visible chrome (this section's own rule), and a PEER's machine cannot know the asking agent's local display name — so the body token is the only form available to them and it is the forbidden one.
        - ⚠ **IT DEGRADES, IT DOES NOT REFUSE.** An older main ignores the key; the answer is still an ordinary visible message, so `feedLiveSession` delivers it to every LIVE agent on the thread. Only a DORMANT one fails to wake there, and it wakes on the operator's next word.
      - **TIER 2 — THE SOLO ROOM.** Exactly ONE agent associated with the channel: **EVERY human message wakes it, guests included, with no @ and no model call.** This is what the ruling exists for — an agent id is minted on this machine and known to no server, so a guest arriving through a link could never learn the eight characters that would have woken the agent sitting in the room with them.
      - **TIER 3 — TRIAGE.** Two or more agents and no @: **one Haiku call per DORMANT candidate** (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, coerced to the `haiku` alias by `session-model.js › aliasForModelId`), answering **CLAIM or PASS and nothing else**. Only a claimant wakes. ⚠ **THE PARSE IS AN EQUALITY TEST, NOT A SEARCH** (`parseTriage`: trimmed, length-capped FIRST, `/^CLAIM\.?$/i`) — the prompt reads guest text, so everything that is not that one word is a PASS. ⚠ **TIE-BREAK: FIRST CLAIM WINS, AND "FIRST" MEANS SPAWN ORDER** (`firstClaim`, over the registry Map's insertion order), never first to answer — answer order is wall-clock noise and cannot be written down in a test. It is the same ordering the ambiguity fallback already resolves by.
      - **"ASSOCIATED WITH THE CHANNEL" IS THE REGISTRY: `session-registry.js › agentIdsInChannel`** — every UNSETTLED session this machine holds for that channel, thread-scoped and channel-level, live and parked and spawn-idle alike. `channel_agents` is a WRITE-DEAD legacy table (`supabase/migrations/20260807000000_drop_unbound_tables_from_realtime.sql`) and agent TEMPLATES are identities rather than channel bindings, so neither is an honest source. ⚠ **COUNTED CHANNEL-WIDE WHILE THE FEED STAYS THREAD-SCOPED** — the same asymmetry, for the same reason, that this function already serves the framing's sibling list. An empty or uncountable roster wakes NOBODY.
      - ⚠ **THE TIER GATE GOVERNS DORMANT SESSIONS ONLY. A RUNNING SESSION KEEPS RULING 4's FAN-OUT, UNTOUCHED** — every message in a thread still reaches every live agent on it, so tier 3 does not silence a running agent and buys no triage call for one. That is the SCOPE DECISION of this build, stated rather than discovered; the residual is **F-344**.
      - **THE VERDICT TRAVELS AS ONE BOOLEAN.** `session-dispatch.js › mayFeed(s, wake)` is the primary gate; `session-gate.js › feedInbound` is the BELT and reads `a.wake` rather than re-deriving the addressing rule — two spellings of one rule is how two readers come to disagree about one message. ⚠ **THE TWO ARE DELIBERATELY NOT THE SAME WIDTH**: the primary gate fences every DORMANT session, the belt fences `awaitingDirective` alone, because every other lane that resumes a parked session (the 1:1 `steer`, an inbound release, the post-sign-in resume) reaches it through paths that never set `wake`.
      - **COST, AS A CEILING.** Zero model calls on an @-message, zero in a solo room, zero for agent-authored or lifecycle traffic, zero for a running session. Otherwise at most one Haiku call per dormant candidate, capped at `session-triage.js › MAX_TRIAGE_PER_MESSAGE` (15 since 2026-09-01, raised with the concurrency ceiling — the cap TRUNCATES, so leaving it at 6 under a cap of 15 would have made a room's 7th+ dormant agent unwakeable) by TRUNCATION rather than refusal, ~1–2k input tokens and a one-token answer each, all issued concurrently and bounded by `TRIAGE_TIMEOUT_MS` (8s) — an abort reads as PASS. ⚠ **THE PASS IS AWAITED INSIDE `listener-messages.js › dispatchMessage`, so it HOLDS THAT CHANNEL'S CURSOR**, which is why it is time-bounded at all.
      - **THE FENCE AROUND THE CALL**: `mcpServers: {}` (no Dopl surface), a `canUseTool` that DENIES every tool, `maxTurns: 1`, `settingSources: []`, `permissionMode: 'default'`, the credential-path deny rules, and `cwd: os.tmpdir()` — never the channel folder. ⚠ **THE DENYING GATE IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT BELT**: the SDK has no "offer no tools" option (`options.tools = []` means NO BOUND), so the positive bound cannot express it. Auth is the SESSION path verbatim (`session-auth.js › withStoredCredential` + `runtime/claude/loader.js › buildScrubbedEnv`); a machine with no credential triages nothing.
      - **KILL SWITCH: `session-wake-tiers.js › WAKE_TIERS_ENABLED`, a main-side constant with NO settings surface** (Samuel: "no new UI"). `false` collapses tiers 2 and 3 and leaves the @-mention rule exactly as it shipped on 2026-08-22 — it reverts to a KNOWN GOOD state. ⚠ **The LOOP FENCE and TIER 1 are read BEFORE it**, so it can neither re-admit an agent-authored wake nor take tier 1 away.
      - **Gates:** `test/wake-tiers.test.mjs` (fence, tiers, parse, prompt, ring, tie-break), `test/wake-triage-call.test.mjs` (the call's fence, budget, timeout and failure modes — the model is mocked, never live), `test/wake-tier-routing.test.mjs` (the routing truth table), `test/session-dispatch.test.mjs` (the fan-out, unchanged), `test/spawn-idle-wake.test.mjs` (the belt).
    - ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-28 BY THE TIERED RULE ABOVE — KEPT VERBATIM BECAUSE A REVERSAL MUST BE READABLE. What still holds: the flag, the two gates, the drop-not-queue rule and the launch goal. What was REVERSED: "@-mention … FROM ANY AUTHOR" no longer includes a peer's AGENT, and an unaddressed message can now wake via tier 2 or tier 3.** ⚠ **AN UNWOKEN SPAWN-IDLE AGENT IS FED NOTHING (2026-08-22, Samuel's wake ruling).** Under the plain fan-out its first turn was whatever was said next in the thread, so an agent parked "ready" woke on a passing remark between two other people and spent its launch answering nobody. It now wakes on exactly TWO things: a **1:1 `sessions:message`** (the reducer's `steer`, which never passes through `main/session-dispatch.js`), or a thread / main-room message that **@-mentions its agent id — FROM ANY AUTHOR**, operator, peer or peer's agent. The third case is the ruling verbatim ("user tells peer: I'm going to wake another agent, you direct it") and is bounded by DISCLOSURE: an agent id is minted on this machine, known to no server, and the parse is intersected with the ids live on this thread.
      - **UNADDRESSED TRAFFIC IS DROPPED, NOT QUEUED.** Nothing is lost: the agent READS the thread on demand once directed, and that read costs no permission (`session-profiles.js › isOwnChannelRead` scopes by channel only, auto-allowed under the windowless message floor). Queueing would only defer the problem — a woken agent would open on a pile of context nobody addressed to it.
      - **THE FLAG IS `s.awaitingDirective`, ITS OWN FIELD, NOT AN OVERLOAD OF `freshFraming`.** That one is a ONE-SHOT marker about whether a TURN carries the full framing and is consumed by the first turn built; this is read on EVERY message and answers whether anything may reach the agent at all. `session-engine.js › dispatch` clears it at the ONE funnel, on `steer` or `inbound_arrived`, so both wake lanes mean the same thing. **Gate:** `session-dispatch.js › mayFeed` (with the held count on the diag line, because a deliberate drop and a routing failure look identical from outside). **Belt:** `session-gate.js › feedInbound`, because that is the entry point the engine exports.
      - ⚠ **THE OPERATOR'S LAUNCH GOAL WAS DEAD TEXT ON THIS LANE, and it is the same fix.** `sessions:launch` composes a goal and hands it in as `spec.firstMessage`; on every other lane that becomes the fenced body of the first turn, but here `startSession` sets `firstTurn = ''` and the wake path never pushes `s.firstTurn` (only `session-query.js › startQuery` does, and this lane never runs it). A woken agent opened on an EMPTY FENCE. `s.launchGoal` carries it to the wake turn, where `session-seed.js › takeFraming` fences it as that turn's request body — **inside the fence, because the goal interpolates a renderer-supplied thread title**, and a real channel transcript still wins over it.

  - **THERE IS NO `busy` REFUSAL ON A LAUNCH ANY MORE** — it WAS the one-agent-per-thread law. `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS` (15 since 2026-09-01, 6 before) is what still bounds the machine. ⚠ **`trigger.js` must keep HANDLING `skipped: 'busy'`**: the cap and the post-await re-check both land there, and deleting the handler leaves a peer waiting forever. **`no-counterparty` is no longer returned at all** — the counterparty fenced the feed and the thread replaced it.
  - **CHANNEL-LEVEL AGENTS (`taskId: null`).** A launch with no thread attaches the agent to the CHANNEL: key `<channelId>::<agentId>`, feed = the channel's **MAIN ROOM** (an untagged post resolves `firstClassTaskId(m) === ''`, so it reaches exactly the sessions whose own thread id is ''), delivery = a main-room post with no `thread` argument. **The scoping falls out of the fan-out; nothing is special-cased.** **`null` (channel-level) and `''` (a responder whose thread never became first-class) are different requests that land on the same scope**; both are accepted, and keeping them spelled apart is what lets a later reader tell them apart.
    - ⚠ **PUSH AND PULL ARE DIFFERENT, AND BOTH HALVES ARE THE RULING (2026-08-22, Samuel).** **PUSH is main-room only and stays that way:** a channel agent is never fed thread traffic, **including a thread message that @-mentions its agent id** — there is no cross-scope push, and `main/session-dispatch.js` is not to grow one. **PULL is the whole channel:** it may READ any thread on demand (`list_threads` / `get_thread` / `read` with `thread=` / `members`), which is what makes the SUPERVISOR case work — *"monitor the threads and the agents working in them"* is answered by reading on a cadence its operator sets, not by being fed.
    - ⚠ **THE PULL LANE WORKS BECAUSE OF AN ABSENCE, so treat it as load-bearing: `session-profiles.js › isOwnChannelRead` SCOPES BY CHANNEL ONLY and never looks at `thread`.** A thread-scoped read of the session's own channel is therefore the same own-channel read as the bare op and auto-allows under the windowless `auto_inbound` floor. **Do NOT "tighten" the read half by scoping the thread:** a channel agent has no thread of its own, so that would deny every supervisor read, and in a windowless session a gate is a DENY (there is no surface to answer it on). Pinned by `test/session-channel-read.test.mjs`'s channel-agent cases.
    - 🔒 **REPLY ROUTING — `prompt-framing-text.js › REPLY_ROUTING`, ON ALL FOUR `deliverySection` BRANCHES (2026-08-31, Samuel's ruling).** A session has TWO inbound lanes and only one is visible to anybody but the operator: the CHANNEL (posts) and the operator's private 1:1 composer (`sessions:message` → the reducer's `steer`), whose turns are rendered in the agent panel and are **on no wire at all**. The framing said where to DELIVER and never that the two have different AUDIENCES, so an agent woken through the panel answered in the panel — right for "what are you doing?", wrong for the channel work it was launched to do, and indistinguishable from an agent that did nothing when read from the room or over MCP. ⚠ **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS WHO IS WAITING, NOT WHICH LANE ASKED.** "Reply where you were asked" (the law block) is right for a question about the AGENT and wrong for the room's work. ⚠ **IT DOES NOT SAY "ECHO EVERYTHING"** — the panel keeps its job, and an agent mirroring every private exchange into the room is the running commentary the sparseness rule forbids. ⚠ **ALL FOUR BRANCHES, and that count is the rule**: the lane an answer leaves by is a property of neither the side nor which ids the launch knew, and the defect was found on a RESPONDER. Pinned by `test/prompt-reply-routing.test.mjs`, which asserts the spread count.
    - ⚠ **THE FRAMING MUST STATE BOTH HALVES OR THE CASE FAILS SILENTLY AND LOOKS LIKE A PERMISSION BUG.** `prompt-framing.js › channelScopeFraming` teaches the main-room scope, the loop brake, the four read ops, "monitoring means reading", and that **nobody in a thread can summon it**. It **interpolates the channel UUID into every read call it prints**, and that is a GATING fact rather than convenience: `isOwnChannelRead` compares against the ID, so a slug-addressed read is ANOTHER channel (the safe failure) and would be denied. Degrades to generic wording when the ids are absent, exactly as `deliverySection` does. Pinned by `test/prompt-channel-scope.test.mjs`.
    - **PULL WIDENS NOTHING ELSE.** A thread-scoped POST is still an outbound op under the OUTBOUND half alone, and `open` / `invite` / `set_thread_mode` / `list` stay gated in every posture with a `thread` argument exactly as without one. ⚠ **`create_thread` was on that list until 2026-08-24 and left it for the OUTBOUND half, not for the pull lane** (above) — a supervisor reading every thread in its channel under the `auto_inbound` floor still cannot open one.
  - **EVERY PER-AGENT OP TAKES AN OPTIONAL THIRD COORDINATE** — `pause` / `end` / `setMode` / `message` / `narration` / `reopen` / `openAgentWindow`, on the bridge and at the IPC boundary (F-239). **Optional, never required, and the fallback is the compatible one:** a named `agentId` resolves EXACTLY (a wrong id answers nothing — "pause my agent" must never pause a different one), an omitted one takes the OLDEST live agent on the thread, which is byte-for-byte what a caller got when a thread could hold only one. ONE statement of that rule: `main/session-reopen.js › resolveSession`.
  - **THE AGENT WINDOW IS ONE PER AGENT** (`agentWindowKey`, `?thread=&agent=`), not one per thread — before this, opening the second agent on a thread silently FRONTED the first one's window. A channel-level agent has no thread and is addressed by its id alone.
  - ⚠ **THE EXECUTOR IS THE WINDOWLESS SDK SESSION (2026-08-20, second half of the retirement).** ⚠ **The launcher is `trigger.js › launchResponderSession`, reached from the ask notification's Launch button (2026-08-22); `inboundApproved` was the consent-approved resolver and is deleted with the inbound lane (§6).** It launches `sessionEngine.launchResponderSession({ windowless: true, … })` — no `getWindowMode` read anywhere in it — so an approved request runs on the REAL engine: it registers in the session Map (the Agents tab, `sessions.pause`/`end`, the context/token metrics and the `channel_sessions` push all work), and `main/session-windowless.js` owns the shape: no window is ever minted, every emit no-ops on the null `win`, and a PENDING GATE bridges instead of painting a card — an own-channel post to a CONSENT ROW (`kind: 'outbound'`, seq-keyed to the latest inbound turn so the thread view's send box finds it) + a native notification whose SEND action decides and whose body click lands on the THREAD (`?thread=`, `main/shell-mode.js › navigateToChannels`); any other gated tool DENIES (a gate means "ask a human"; there is no surface) — ⚠ **and SAYS SO since 2026-08-20: `session-windowless.js › notifyDenied` fires a local `channel-post.notifyLocal` naming the tool and the channel, after the deny, alongside the diag line that used to be the only trace.** It is a NOTICE, not a decision surface — nothing there can reverse the deny and nothing posts into the channel. **The message axis is floored at `auto_inbound`** (the consent that admitted the THREAD is the human decision; a counterparty reply feeds as a turn), **and the OUT half is the channel's durable AUTO-SEND setting** (`main/channel-prefs.js › getAutoSend`, default OFF = ask-first, toggled on the Settings tab over the `channels.get/setAutoSend` bridge ops) — ON maps to `auto_both` and the reply posts itself. ⚠ **THE `claude -p` HEADLESS LANE IS DELETED (2026-08-20, Samuel's ruling) AND THERE IS NO SECOND EXECUTOR.** `trigger-headless.js` and `session-pool.js` are gone, `session-spawner.js` is a 46-line re-export facade over `claude-resolve` / `claude-runtime` / `tool-profiles`, and the `claudeSessions` store key has no reader. **Every engine skip is now a TERMINAL**: `cap` / `no-sdk` / `disabled` fire a local notification naming the reason (`trigger.js › skippedHint`), post `trigger-outcomes.js › CANNOT_RUN` to the peer as a courtesy, and settle the record — so `launchResponderSession` always returns true and a request is never left pending against a machine that will not run it. **The watcher's `await-outbound` PHASE went with the lane** (`toOutbound`, `outboundApproved`, and `isAwaiting`'s second arm), because a `claude -p` run handed back a reply STRING for the desktop to post on the agent's behalf. ⚠ **Approve-out itself did NOT go — it moved INTO the session:** `session-windowless.js › bridgeOutbound` raises the same `outbound` consent KIND, and the AGENT posts its own bytes when its held tool call is released.
  - ⚠ **MY OWN AGENTS MID-TURN SHOW ABOVE THE COMPOSER TOO, AND THEY ARE A SEPARATE LANE (2026-08-25, Samuel)** — `channels-v2/agent-activity.tsx › AgentActivityRows`, through `message-pane.tsx`'s `agentActivity` SLOT, rendered ABOVE the peer row on both hosts of the surface. **ONE ROW PER AGENT, STACKED, NAMED** (`agents-model.ts › agentDisplayName`, so a rename reads through) — never a count, because these are the operator's own agents and the name is the only thing that makes the row actionable.
    - **THE SCOPE IS THE COMPOSER'S, via `agents-model.ts › ownAgentsFor`** — the same one derivation the Agents tab's list and badge run (a second inline filter is the F-142 shape). Channel view shows every working agent in the channel; thread view narrows to the open thread. **Unlike the peer row it is NOT thread-only**, because a channel-level composer has a channel-level target.
    - 🔒 **NO FRESHNESS WINDOW HERE, AND THAT ASYMMETRY IS LOAD-BEARING.** The peer row guards on `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS` because a `channel_sessions` row outlives the process that wrote it. This one reads the LOCAL bridge feed, where **liveness is membership** — an ended session is gone from the feed, not stale in it — so a clock here would invent doubt about a fact the machine holds directly. **`null` sessions render NOTHING** (could-not-ask is not idle; §11's UNKNOWN-is-not-EMPTY).
    - **`state === "working"` IS the active-turn signal**, not an approximation of one: main leaves `working` when the turn settles, so the row clears itself with no timer. The finer word (`› agentDetailLabel`) stays on the CARDS — a caption beside the composer that re-words once per tool call is two vocabularies for one fact on one screen.
  - ⚠ **A PEER'S AGENT WORKING ON THE OPEN THREAD SHOWS ABOVE THE COMPOSER (2026-08-20)** — `src/features/channels/components/channels-v2/peer-activity.tsx › PeerActivityRow`, in the page's thread view and in the pop-out window alike (both take it through `message-pane.tsx`'s `peerActivity` SLOT, the same injection shape as `popOut`). **THREAD VIEW ONLY** — the row is about one exchange and the channel view has no thread to be about.
    - **IT SAYS "WORKING", NOT "THINKING", AND THAT IS THE DESIGN.** The finer `detail` signal is LOCAL to the machine running the agent (§11); the cross-machine wire is `channel_sessions`, whose vocabulary is three values. **Widening it was considered and REJECTED on cost:** a migration, the `z.enum`, the duplicated SDK type with its tracked `dist` (F-203), the MCP renderer's membership set and its description prose, and a one-way ship-order gate whose failure mode is a 400 that unretryably stops every later push for the workspace — to buy a distinction a PEER cannot act on.
    - **LIVENESS IS THE ROW'S OWN ARITHMETIC OVER `updatedAt`, AND IT FAILS TOWARD SILENCE.** `channel_sessions` rows outlive the process that wrote them (`main/session-state-push.js`'s own header says so and names sign-out as the uncovered case), so a crashed desktop leaves a row reading `working` forever — and an indicator that believes it is **strictly worse than no indicator**, because the reader waits for a reply from a machine that is gone. A row older than the window renders nothing; an absent or unparseable stamp reads as STALE. **The window is `PRESENCE_ONLINE_WINDOW_MS`, deliberately REUSED** rather than a second number, so the roster's dots and this row cannot disagree about whether a member's machine is alive. ⚠ **AND ON THE MCP SURFACE THE ROW IS NO LONGER THE ONLY WITNESS (2026-08-23, F-294).** The row's own arithmetic still decides whether a line may assert a present tense, but WHICH hedge it gets is now decided by the caller's `agent_presence` freshness, joined server-side — because an idle-but-alive agent crosses this window in ninety seconds and was being reported with the same words as a crash. ⚠ **THE PUSH CONTRACT IS UNCHANGED and the keepalive that would have changed it was refused** — `main/session-state-push.js`'s header carries the argument. §10 owns the rendering rule.
    - **The read is the one the Agents tab already polls** (`use-channel-agent-sessions.ts`; `channel_sessions` is unpublished, §7). ⚠ **The pop-out makes its OWN call of it** — a different React tree in a different `BrowserWindow` inherits nothing — on the SAME `use-agents-panel.ts › PEER_SESSIONS_POLL_MS`, exported for exactly that reason.
  - ⚠ **A MESSAGE BODY RENDERS AS MARKDOWN, AND THE BODY IS UNTRUSTED (2026-08-21, Samuel).** `components/channels-v2/message-markdown.tsx` — bold / italic / strikethrough, inline code, fenced blocks, links, lists, modest chat-sized headings, blockquotes and GFM tables. ⚠ **NO HTML EVER REACHES THE DOM AS MARKUP, and it is structural rather than sanitized:** the renderer runs **`marked.lexer`, never `marked.parse`**, and maps TOKENS to React elements, so no HTML string is ever produced and there is no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` on this path and no sanitizer to keep in step with an attacker. `html` tokens — block and inline — render as their own LITERAL TEXT. ⚠ **NO NEW DEPENDENCY: `marked` is already this tree's markdown engine** (`shared/editor/doc-editor.tsx`). ⚠ **LINKS ARE A POSITIVE ALLOW-LIST** — `http`/`https`/`mailto` only (`› safeHref`); `javascript:` and `data:` PARSE as valid URLs, so a try/catch is no check. A refused href loses the ANCHOR and keeps the WORDS, and **no `<img>` is ever rendered** — a remote image in a body is a read receipt and an IP disclosure for every member who scrolls past it. Anchors use the app's one external-link idiom (`shared/lib/open-external.ts`), because `window.open` is denied in the packaged renderer. ⚠ **THE MENTION TINT IS THE TEXT LEAF** (`› MentionText` — it was `transcript.tsx`'s private body renderer, splitting ONE line; it moved here unchanged in rule and became the leaf every text node runs through), which is what makes a handle tint inside a heading, a bullet or a bold run — and deliberately NOT inside a code span, where it is a quoted example. ⚠ **Blocks render as a FRAGMENT into the row's own column** (no wrapper, or own-message `items-end` anchoring re-parents), and **layout and type arrive as TWO class props** because `cn` cannot arbitrate between them: `tailwind-merge` groups this tree's `text-lead` SIZE with a `text-*` COLOUR and drops one (measured 2026-08-21).
  - ⚠ **EVERY SESSION OP NAMES THE AGENT INSTANCE (2026-08-22 — F-239 closed on this side).** `(channelId, taskId)` stopped identifying a session when multiplayer landed: it names a GROUP of one operator's agents on a thread, and main resolves a group to its **OLDEST LIVE member**. So `pause` / `end` / `setMode` / `message` / `reopen` / `openAgentWindow` each take `agentId` as a trailing coordinate, and `components/channels-v2/agents-controls.ts` passes it on all six — **read off the SESSION OBJECT the surface is showing, never re-derived from a key or a route.** ⚠ **THE FAILURE IT PREVENTS IS SILENT IN EVERY DIRECTION:** an op that drops the id does not error — it SUCCEEDS against a different agent, answers `{ok:true}`, and leaves the card the operator clicked visibly unchanged, which reads as a bug in the feed. ⚠ **OPTIONAL, AND ITS ABSENCE IS THE CORRECT DEGRADATION** rather than a refusal: an older main omits it from its summaries and also runs at most one agent per thread, so oldest-live IS the agent on screen. ⚠ **THE AGENT WINDOW'S ROUTE reads `?agent=`** (`apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/agent-window/index.tsx` → `components/channels-v2/agent-window.tsx`), ahead of main emitting it — absent, the window falls back to first-on-thread, which is the last piece of F-239 and lives on the desktop side.
  - ⚠ **THE AGENT'S WORK STREAM IS ONE COMPONENT, ON BOTH SURFACES (2026-08-22, Samuel).** `components/channels-v2/agent-stream.tsx` over the pure `› agent-stream-model.ts`. The slide-out panel showed a SENT-LANE ONLY — the one lane that says least about what an agent is doing, so an agent mid-tool-run read as an agent doing nothing — and now renders the window's stream whole. **The panel is that content at another width, not a different question**, which is why it is shared on the same rule as `agent-composer.tsx`. ⚠ **FOUR LANES, FOUR FACES, and the distinction is the product:** `sent` wears the BOX (below) because it is the only thing here the counterparty can see; `operator` is the VIEWER'S OWN TURN — right-aligned with their avatar and **no name, no email and no "You"** (Samuel, 2026-08-27: side is the signal, the label column went) — while `private` is the agent's answer: **left, plain, and carrying NOTHING but the text** — the quote bar and the "Agent" marker went the same day (2026-08-27, second pass), because ALIGNMENT already tells the two sides apart and a rule plus a noun on top of it restates the layout, exactly as the `says` label restated the lane. **Plain text is also the face that claims least**, which is what this lane needs: private traffic that looked like a channel post would let an operator believe their steer was read by the other party; `thinking` / `tool` / `note` are quiet log lines in `› agent-stream-log.tsx`. ⚠ **THE VIEWER'S FACE IS READ OFF THE TRANSCRIPT, NEVER A ROSTER** (`› view-model.ts › viewerPerson`): both hosts already hold `messages` and NEITHER holds a roster — the window's diet is messages + consent and stays that way — and the hydrated author fields are keyed on `authorUserId`. **Unresolved renders NO avatar, never a placeholder face.** ⚠ **A RUN OF CONSECUTIVE TOOL ACTIVITY IS ONE MUTED ROW** — "Used N tools" with a chevron, COLLAPSED BY DEFAULT, per-group state, nothing persisted (`› agent-stream-log.tsx › ToolRunGroup` over `› agent-stream-model.ts › groupStreamItems`). Every tool call used to render its own row of raw JSON, which buried the POST the operator opened the panel to read. **The count is tool USES, not frames** (a call and its result are one), **any non-tool lane BREAKS the run** so a said line is never swallowed, and expanding gives back exactly the rows that were there — a failed call still says `failed` on the row inside. ⚠ **AND THE AGENT'S OWN WORDS CARRY NO LABEL** (2026-08-27): a bold "says" sat in front of every `thinking` line — a speech verb attached to a machine, restating the lane. The text stands alone in `text-primary`; only the two rows that name something ELSE (the tool's name, `failed`) keep a label. ⚠ **BOUNDED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS:** log lines are collapsed by default (a tool result otherwise pushes the post off screen) and CLIPPED even when expanded, saying so when they clip (§9) — "show more" on a megabyte of JSON destroys the stream it was meant to explain. ⚠ **ONE POST IS ONE ROW:** a `sent` narration frame is the local ECHO of a transcript row, so the frames are dropped whenever real transcript rows are supplied — and KEPT when there are none, or a surface with narration and no messages read shows an agent that thinks and never speaks. ⚠ **FOUR BECAME FIVE ON 2026-08-31** — the private direct lane's `directed` / `directed-reply` wear their own box (`› agent-stream-directed.tsx`), and the MESSAGE faces render markdown through the transcript's renderer (`› agent-stream-prose.tsx`) while the log lane stays plain. Both are stated once, under the private direct lane above.
  - ⚠ **THE `kind` VOCABULARY IS MAIN'S, OPEN, AND READ THROUGH AN ALIAS TABLE (`agent-stream-lanes.ts › frameLane` — the §1 split of `agent-stream-model.ts` on 2026-08-31, which re-exports it and stays the import path of record; **the vocabulary and the stream's arithmetic move on different clocks**).** `session-narration.js › entryFor` owns it and the desktop is still growing it, so the two trees are routinely one release apart: **an unrecognised kind is the NORMAL case, not an error case.** It falls back to a plain `note` row that still carries the frame's own text — a stream that silently drops what it does not recognise is worse than one that renders it plainly, because the operator is reading this to find out what happened. ⚠ **AN EXPLICIT `lane` OUTRANKS `kind`**: `kind` describes the SHAPE of an event, `lane` says who can SEE it, and audience is the one fact this surface must never infer wrongly. Both are read defensively off the entry rather than declared on it — the wire is the DESKTOP's to widen. ⚠ **Today's five (`assistant`/`tool`/`result`/`post`/`status`) all have rows**, so a build that emits nothing new renders exactly what it rendered before.
  - ⚠ **THE v1 SENT BOX IS RECOVERED, NOT REINVENTED (`agent-stream.tsx › SentToChannelBox`, 2026-08-22).** Provenance — **paths that no longer resolve, and that is the point**: the `.outbound` / `.outbound__banner` / `__label` / `__tag` / `__body` rules in `renderer/session/session.css`, and their DOM factory `makeOutbound` in `renderer/session/session-render.js`. Both were deleted whole in **`db901c39`** ("desktop: wave 1 — delete the session window, whole"). **An ADAPTATION, not a byte-copy:** the geometry, the dark-CTA banner and the label wording are v1's; every colour is read from the current token set per docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md, because that window's `renderer/session/tokens.css` was its own private copy and is gone. **Kept** — full stream width (v1's own note: "a delivery RECORD, not a conversational turn"), the `surface-cta` banner that reads as a header rather than a bubble, `border-active` on `card-surface-subtle`, and a `pre-wrap` body that breaks anywhere. **Dropped, deliberately** — `.outbound-pending` and `.is-not-sent`, the whole DECISION half: v1 painted the box BEFORE the operator answered the outbound gate, and this surface only ever renders posts that already exist in the transcript, so a pending face would be a dead affordance of exactly the kind F-212 was earned on. ⚠ **The per-item "→ sent to X · date" meta line is DELETED** — its one useful fact is the banner now.
  - ⚠ **THE DIRECT 1:1 COMPOSER IS ON BOTH AGENT SURFACES (2026-08-22, Samuel).** `components/channels-v2/agent-composer.tsx` — **extracted from the agent window, never copied into the panel**: one send path, one refusal vocabulary, one feature detection for one op. The slide-out panel's footer note used to point AT the window ("open the agent to message it directly"), which is a surface sending the operator elsewhere to say one sentence to their own agent; **that note is DELETED outright (2026-08-22, minimal-copy ruling)** now that the panel renders the whole stream itself. ⚠ **THE BAR IS RAISED, NOT INSET, AND CARRIES NO HAIRLINES** — `.raised-tab` (THE app-wide raised face, docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md), resting at the send button's own 30px, floating on the panel with no rule above it. It was a concave `FIELD_WELL` in a hairline-topped block, which in this kit means "a field pressed into a panel" and read as chrome to be found rather than the surface's one action. ⚠ **THE SEND AFFORDANCE IS THE GLOBAL `shared/ui/send-button.tsx`**, the v1 session window's `.send-btn` face — already what the playground channels pane and the knowledge hero chat send with. A third hand-rolled send glyph in one product is how three of them come to disagree. ⚠ **WHAT IT IS NOT: a channel post.** Nothing typed there is written to the thread and the agent's reply is not posted either (`main/session-seed.js › frameOperatorTurn` tells the agent so). ⚠ **A PARKED OR IDLE AGENT IS A VALID ADDRESSEE** — spawn-idle means the FIRST message is what wakes it, so gating on `working` would make every freshly launched agent unreachable by construction.
  - ⚠ **AN ENDED AGENT IS DEAD, AND THE UI SAYS SO IN ONE PLACE PER SURFACE (2026-08-22, Samuel).** Ending is TERMINAL — no revival, every wake path refuses; the agent's own history is retained for a window and then swept, but **what it POSTED stays in the channel forever**, which is exactly why the marker is not optional: a reader keeps meeting a dead agent's messages, and an unmarked card is indistinguishable from a live agent they are waiting on. `components/channels-v2/agent-bits.tsx › AgentEndedPill` marks the Agents-tab card, the slide-out panel's header and the agent window's header, and **it REPLACES the liveness element rather than joining it** — "Ended" beside a dot also reading "Ended" is one fact said twice, and a redundant pair is what drifts. ⚠ **THE COMPOSER IS GONE, NOT DISABLED.** A disabled box reads as "not right now", a state that will pass, which is the opposite of what ended means; the surrounding surface stays fully READ-ONLY (work stream, Sent lane, retained history). ⚠ **AN IN-FLIGHT SEND STILL GETS ITS ANSWER** — if the agent ends between Send and main's verdict the input goes but the refusal line stays, because "the box vanished" is not an outcome. ⚠ **`state === "ended"` IS WHAT MARKS IT, NEVER `endedAt`** — that stamp is additive and absent on an older main and on any agent that ended before it shipped, so gating the pill on it would render every legacy ended agent as live. No retention countdown is shown: the sweep is main's schedule and there is nothing the operator can do about it.
  - ⚠ **LIVENESS IS ONE MAPPING, FOUR STATES (`components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › agentLiveness`, 2026-08-22).** `working` → `detail` ?? **Running**; `idle` + `listening: true` → **Waiting**; `idle` otherwise → **Idle**; `ended` → **Ended**, which outranks every other signal. ⚠ **THE SPLIT INSIDE `idle` IS THE POINT:** the wire has three values and lumped an agent ALIVE between turns together with one that is parked and must be woken — both read "Idle", so watching a live agent wait for a counterparty looked exactly like watching a stopped one. ⚠ **`listening` IS OPTIONAL AND ITS ABSENCE CHANGES NOTHING** — an older main omits it and every one of its idle agents reads "Idle", exactly the labels that build already produced; absent is "cannot say", not "not listening" (§11). ⚠ **`working` KEEPS THE WORD "Running"**: the state's meaning did not change and renaming it would be churn bought with nothing. ⚠ **EVERY SURFACE RUNS THIS ONE FUNCTION** — the Agents tab's cards, both agent headers, the thread Info tab's rows — and `agent-bits.tsx › AgentLiveness` renders the verdict it is handed and owns no rule.
  - ⚠ **THE COMPOSER'S BOT ICON IS "NEW AGENT"; A SEPARATE GLYPH OPENS THE THREAD PANEL (2026-08-21, Samuel).** One icon used to mean both, and they do not reach the same layer: `MessageSquarePlus` ("New thread") toggles the request panel — a WRITE, aimed at another member, unchanged in every other respect — while `Bot` spawns MY OWN agent over the bridge and **posts nothing** (the engine spawns it IDLE; no first message is sent). ⚠ **CONTEXT-SENSITIVE TARGET:** thread view launches onto the open exchange, channel view launches a **CHANNEL-LEVEL agent (`taskId: null`)**. **`null` is not `""`** — the empty string is already a real wire value meaning "a responder whose thread never became first-class" (`spa-bridge.ts › DesktopSessionSummary`), and a channel agent has no counterparty, which is **not** the `no-counterparty` refusal (that one is a THREAD whose other party could not be resolved, and still says so). ⚠ **IT IS A SECOND BUTTON, NEVER A SECOND LAUNCH PATH:** the page hands its own `use-agents-panel.ts` instance down as `› AgentLaunchControls` (a second mount would be a second peer poll of `channel_sessions`). Rendered only where `canLaunch` — the web tree and the pop-out thread window get no affordance rather than one that can only refuse. ⚠ **A THIRD GLYPH EXISTED FROM 2026-08-22 TO 2026-08-27 — a chevron beside `Bot` opening the TEMPLATE PICKER — AND IT IS RETIRED.** The Bot icon and that chevron are BOTH replaced by the composer's LAUNCH PANEL (§5A, `channels-v2/composer-launch-panel.tsx`), whose Template row is the picker's whole function; `composer.tsx` imports `TemplateApprovalDialog` and `ComposerLaunch` and no picker at all. **Recorded rather than deleted**, because the reason it went is the rule: three glyphs, three acts, and re-merging any two makes one unreachable — which is exactly what happened to the thread panel before 2026-08-21, and two ways to choose an identity is the same failure in the other direction.
  - ⚠ **THE WHOLE RIGHT COLUMN IS THREAD-SCOPED IN THREAD VIEW (2026-08-21, Samuel).** THREE things change when a thread is open and nothing else does: **THREADS leaves the tab row** (`› tabsFor` — it is the one control in this column that navigates away from what the centre pane is showing; it returns in channel view, and the sidebar's tree covers thread-to-thread movement meanwhile), and **INFO renders the THREAD's facts** — `components/channels-v2/thread-info-tab.tsx`: title, mode, creation date, the two PARTIES named by their side of the exchange (opener / addressee, presence by the roster's own client-side `isPresent` arithmetic), and the agents standing on the thread by ID and state. And **SETTINGS becomes the THREAD's** — `components/channels-v2/thread-settings-tab.tsx`, which is the thread's whole settable surface and is exactly two rows: the MODE (creator only, mirroring `service-tasks.ts › setTaskMode`) and **Delete thread**, a destructive row behind a `ConfirmDialog` (§5 owns the deletion rule; the host with the dialog and the writes is `› thread-manage.tsx`). ⚠ **`info-tab.tsx` AND `settings-tab.tsx` ARE UNTOUCHED AND STILL RENDER VERBATIM IN CHANNEL VIEW** — separate files, because they answer separate questions.
    - ⚠ **`info-panel.tsx` MAKES ONLY THE FIRST TWO OF THOSE CHOICES.** The Settings body arrives as a SLOT and the channel-vs-thread branch is `components/channels-v2/settings-slot.tsx`, at the page's MOUNT boundary. **That placement is load-bearing, not tidiness:** `channel-manage.tsx` opens `useTrustRules` (`/api/channels/trust`) plus two write-hook families on mount and §5 pins that none of them fire until the tab is opened, and hooks cannot sit behind an early return — so the branch has to be made by a component with no hooks of its own. **Keep `settings-slot.tsx` hook-free**; `settings-slot.test.tsx` stubs both hosts so "did it MOUNT" is what is asserted.
    - ⚠ **NO STATUS ROW ON EITHER TAB, and its absence is load-bearing:** a thread has no finished state (§5), so the channel Info tab's Status row has no thread counterpart to fill and the Settings tab offers no close, no reopen and no "mark done". `thread-settings-tab.test.tsx` refuses that vocabulary outright.
    - ⚠ **THE SELECTION CANNOT GO DEAD** — opening a thread while Threads is the lit tab falls back to Info during render, never in an effect, so no frame paints a `SegmentedControl` whose `value` matches no option.
  - ⚠ **THE AGENTS TAB IS CONTEXT-SENSITIVE AND SHOWS PEERS (2026-08-20, third round).** Channel view lists the whole channel's agents; thread view lists that thread's alone plus a **New Agent** button (`sessions.launch` bridge op → `main/session-ipc-ops.js › sessions:launch` — start MY OWN agent on a first-class thread, windowless requester-side, the click IS the consent, posture main's; there is NEVER an auto-spawn). ⚠ **EVERY CLICK SPAWNS A NEW AGENT (2026-08-21, multiplayer agents — the button was "Launch agent" and the op resolved to at most one session per `(channel, thread)` until then).** The UI caps nothing and may not start to: the button disarms only while one launch is in flight (a double-submit guard) and when the bridge op is absent. **`(channel, thread)` therefore addresses a THREAD, not an agent** — `components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › agentKey` keys on the agent's own `agentId` and falls back to the pair only on a main that emits none, and what renders on every card, header and window title is `› agentDisplayName` rather than a handle (the stone-name pool is deleted). ⚠ **THIS BULLET SAID `› agentDisplayId` — THE RAW ID — UNTIL 2026-08-27**, and it was the doc half of the bug §11's *"the raw agent id is never user-visible"* rule fixed: it stated the id AS the rendered thing while three surfaces were shipping exactly that. `agentDisplayId` is `agentDisplayName`'s internal fallback and has **no legitimate component caller**; the sweep `channels-v2/agent-id-visibility.test.ts` now fails any that appears. **Peer cards are the server's state projection** — `GET /api/channels/[channelId]/sessions` over `session-state-service.ts › listChannelSessions`, fenced by `loadVisibleChannel` (⚠ which ADMITS a public channel's non-member readers — §5's channel-visible rule; the read runs as service role, so the member SELECT policy `20260820200000` is the PostgREST/realtime belt, not this path's fence) — state/name/thread only, never openable, no transcript or tokens exist in the table to leak. Every card wears its owner's avatar; own agents sort first. `channel_sessions` stays UNPUBLISHED (§7), so the tab POLLS while mounted (`use-channel-agent-sessions.ts`). ⚠ **`read_sessions` (MCP) is still caller-scoped** — the widening is the member READ, not the MCP op.
  - ⚠ **THE TWO LAUNCH SURFACES NOW OPEN A TEMPLATE PICKER, AND THE ONE-CLICK BLANK LAUNCH IS UNCHANGED ON BOTH (2026-08-22, Samuel's agent-templates ruling — it OVERRIDES `docs/AGENT-TEMPLATES-SPEC.md` §3a's OQ-4 recommendation, which proposed a popover in front of the click with `Blank agent` focused and Enter to launch).** The standing channels-v2 ruling is *one lane, one-click launch*: the Agents tab's **New Agent** face and the composer's **Bot** icon still spawn a BLANK agent on the first click, with a payload carrying **neither** `templateId` nor `overrides` — absent, never `null`, so the blank lane reaches main byte-identically to a build that has never heard of templates. The picker opens from a **DISTINCT ADJACENT ZONE** with its own accessible name (`"Launch from template"`, never a second control named "New Agent"): a **split button** on the Agents tab (hairline divider, 32px chevron). ⚠ **THIS SAID "and a 24px chevron beside the Bot icon in the composer" UNTIL 2026-08-30 — THE COMPOSER'S MOUNT WAS RETIRED ON 2026-08-27** by the launch panel (§5A). The split button on the Agents tab is the ONE mount; `grep -rn TemplateLaunchPicker src apps` is the check.
    - **ONE COMPONENT, ONE LIVE MOUNT SINCE 2026-08-27.** `features/agent-templates/components/template-picker.tsx › TemplateLaunchPicker` over the existing `› useAgentTemplates` read, mounted by `channels-v2/agents-tab.tsx` and nothing else. The hook lives inside the popover's body, so a picker that is never opened costs no request.
      - ⚠ **THIS BULLET READ "MOUNTED BY BOTH, AND MOUNTING IT TWICE IS FINE" UNTIL 2026-08-30, AND ITS ARGUMENT WAS LOAD-BEARING FOR A STATE THAT NO LONGER EXISTS.** The reasoning — *a react-query read on a stable key shares one fetch and one cache entry across mounts, unlike `useAgentsPanel`'s `PEER_SESSIONS_POLL_MS`, where two mounts are two answers to "how fresh is fresh enough"* — **is still correct and is kept for the next component that needs it**; what was wrong is the premise that there are two mounts. Do not read this as permission to re-add the composer's.
    - **ROW CLICK = LAUNCH WITH THE TEMPLATE'S DEFAULTS, in one click. ROW CHEVRON = the launch sheet** (`› launch-sheet.tsx`), where **model AND fields** are overridable (Samuel, resolving the spec's OQ-2 against its model-only recommendation) and instructions are **read-only, collapsed, verbatim** — an editable instructions box at launch is a second authoring surface for the durable thing. Overrides are **EPHEMERAL**: nothing is written back, and `› lib/launch-overrides.ts › overridesFor` answers `undefined` when the sheet changed nothing, so an untouched sheet and a row click put the same object on the wire.
    - **THE FOREIGN-AUTHORSHIP MARKER IS A SECURITY SIGNAL, NOT DECORATION.** Every row the caller did not create carries `by <member>` on its face **and in its `aria-label`** (`› authorMarker`); an author the channel roster cannot name still reads `by another member`, because dropping the marker would turn UNKNOWN into MINE. `AgentTemplate.createdBy` is already on the list DTO, so this costs no server change.
    - **FIRST USE OF A FOREIGN TEMPLATE IS APPROVED BY A HUMAN, ONCE PER MACHINE.** `sessions.launch` refuses with the wire word **`template-approval`** carrying `{ template: { name, instructions } }`; the SPA shows the instructions verbatim with `[Cancel] [Run as this]` (`› template-approval.tsx`) and on confirm calls `sessions.approveTemplate(templateId)` and relaunches. ⚠ **`template-approval` IS NOT AN ERROR LINE** — `use-agents-panel.ts › launchAgent` deliberately leaves `launchError` alone for that one word and RETURNS the outcome, because a red "could not start the agent" under a modal that is asking permission reports a question as a failure. ⚠ **The approval is stored in the desktop's `electron-store`, beside `orchestratorLaunchEnabled`, and is NEVER server-reachable** — same argument as that toggle's: a spawned session runs with `Bash` and the operator's device token is on disk, so a server-writable version lets a credential-holding agent pre-approve itself across the fleet. **The renderer caches no approval of its own** either, for the same reason.
    - **`no-template` JOINS THE REFUSAL VOCABULARY** (`use-agents-panel.ts › LAUNCH_REFUSALS`): *"That template is gone — reload the list"*. ⚠ The endpoint cannot tell DELETED from INVISIBLE (404-never-403), so neither does the copy.
    - ⚠ **THE SPA SENDS AN ID, NEVER A SNAPSHOT.** `agents-controls.ts › launchAgentOnThread` puts `templateId` on the payload and **main resolves the content itself** at spawn under the operator's own credential. A renderer-supplied `{name, instructions}` is renderer-authored text landing in a prompt, which main cannot tell from a fabrication — the F-267 class of mistake with PROMPT TEXT as the forged input. The picker's cached name is a LABEL; main's resolve is the PROMPT.
  - ⚠ **CONSENT'S DECISION SURFACES AFTER THE SAME DAY'S THIRD ROUND: INLINE ONLY.** The arrival pop-up (shipped that morning) is DELETED. ⚠ **THE "PASSIVE LIST" CLAUSE THAT STOOD HERE WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED (2026-08-20)** — it said the Inbox pane had "no decision buttons, no consent write". the Inbox pane's `InboxRow` rendered **Decline / Launch agent** (Cancel / Send on an outbound row) wired to the same CAS'd consent mutation as every other surface. ⚠ **THAT PANE IS ITSELF DELETED AS OF 2026-08-25 (§6)** — everything from here to the end of this bullet is the state on 2026-08-20 and is kept as the narrative of how the decision surfaces moved, not as a description of this tree. It was demoted to a passive list that morning and **the decision was restored the same day**, because the seq→thread join cannot place every row — an untagged trigger, an aged-out page, a deliberately seq-less outbound draft — and **a row no surface can decide is a hung agent**. The Inbox is the DURABLE decision home of last resort. What it still does NOT carry is launch settings. **THREE surfaces decide, and they are equals:** the Inbox row, the transcript thread card, and the thread view's awaiting strip (**"Launch agent" / "Decline"**, `thread-consent.tsx` + `transcript.tsx`), in the pop-out thread window too (it takes the channel-scoped consent read for exactly this); an outbound draft decides in the thread view's SEND BOX (`thread-consent.tsx › ThreadSendBox` — the old Inbox `LaunchPanel` in outbound mode, now that panel's ONE consumer) or from the notification's Send action.
  - ⚠ **THE AGENT WINDOW SHIPPED (2026-08-20, F-212's closure) — A THIRD APP WINDOW, AND THE SESSION WINDOW IS STILL RETIRED.** `main/agent-window.js`, landing on `/{segment}/agent-window/{channelId}?thread={taskId}` (`AGENT_WINDOW_PAGE`, a HAND COPY of `apps/desktop-ui/src/routes.tsx › AGENT_WINDOW_PATH`; `test/agent-window.test.mjs` reads the SPA export and fails on drift). It shows ONE of the operator's own agents: its live work, what it sent, and a 1:1 composer.
    - **IT IS THE POP-OUT'S SHAPE AND INHERITS ITS RULING, NOT A NEW ARGUMENT.** `spa-window.js › spaWebPreferences` + `› policeNavigation` shared VERBATIM, `appWindows.register(win)` in MAIN at creation, one window per **AGENT** (`(channel, thread, agent)` since 2026-08-21 — the pair fronted the wrong window once a thread could hold several), `MAX_AGENT_WINDOWS` (**4**, verified 2026-08-20), one `{ ok: false }` refusal shape, version floor honoured, and in **NEITHER** `WORKSPACE_PAGES` nor `deep-link-target.js › ROOT_ROUTES` — a `dopl://` link resolves it as an unknown page and opens the workspace HOME. **`main/app-windows.js › register` now has THREE call sites** (`shell-mode.js`, `popout-window.js`, `agent-window.js`); `test/app-windows.test.mjs` asserts that structurally, so a fourth is reviewed rather than absorbed.
    - ⚠ **"OPEN AGENT" ALWAYS OPENS IT, AND THE PRECEDING REFUSAL IS DELETED.** A live WINDOWLESS session used to fall through `reopenByTask` into `recreateParkedShell`, which answered `{ ok: true }` having opened nothing; the fix in between answered `{ ok: false, reason: 'windowless' }`, worded as *"this agent runs without a window"*. **Samuel rejected that wording: a window is a VIEW, not a runtime property** — whether main minted a `BrowserWindow` for a spawn is an implementation detail of the spawn shape and no answer to "show me my agent". Both `sessions.openAgentWindow` and the older `sessions.reopen` now reach this window; `canOpenAgentWindow()` accepts EITHER op, so a main predating the new one still works.
    - ⚠ **THE WORK LANE IS AUDIENCES, NOT STYLINGS, AND `lane` OUTRANKS `kind` (2026-08-22).** Kinds: `thinking` (the model's reasoning, addressed to nobody — the UI collapses it) · `assistant` (narrating a PUBLIC turn) · `operator` / `private` (the 1:1 lane, which nobody else can ever see) · `tool` + `result` (a call, its RAW name, a bounded input summary, and how it came back) · `post` (⚠ **this one LEFT THE MACHINE**) · `status`. **Collapsing any pair makes the view claim something was shared when it was not, or the reverse.**
      - ⚠ **`lane` (`'operator' | 'private' | 'channel'`) IS THE FACT AND THE READER MUST PREFER IT.** Audience is not something a renderer should infer from a word: a kind can be renamed, aliased or added, and `lane` cannot drift — so no future rename can leak a private reply into a public-looking face, nor dress a real channel post as private. It is **ABSENT on the narration kinds** (`thinking` / `assistant` / `tool` / `result` / `status`), deliberately — they went nowhere and have no audience to be wrong about. **Absent is not `'channel'`.**
      - Declared ONCE, in `spa-bridge.ts › DesktopNarrationEntry`; the SPA's `AgentNarrationEntry` is an ALIAS onto it since a hand-written second copy silently disagreed the moment main's vocabulary grew. **An unknown kind renders as a plain note carrying its own text — never dropped** — because a newer main can emit a member this build does not list. **A `post` frame is a LOCAL ECHO**: keep emitting it, and dedupe against the real transcript row once that exists.
      - **BOUNDED MAIN-SIDE SO THE IPC FRAME STAYS SMALL, NOT BECAUSE THOSE ARE DISPLAY LENGTHS — AND THE BOUND IS THREE NUMBERS, BECAUSE THEY ARE THREE KINDS OF STRING:** a CAPTION at `TEXT_CAP` (300 — the tool result summary and the status lines), a tool name at 40, an input summary already capped at 140 by `session-io.js › summarizeInput`, a `post` at `POST_CAP` (1000 — a MESSAGE, but one the TRANSCRIPT is the record of and the UI dedupes against it), and **the agent's own PROSE at `PROSE_CAP`** — `assistant` / `thinking` / the operator's 1:1 `rawText`, plus both directed kinds. **The UI truncates further.** ⚠ `inputFull` still NEVER enters a ring entry.
      - ⚠ **THE FOUR CAPS AND THE TWO SHAPERS LIVE IN `main/narration-text.js` SINCE 2026-08-31** (§2 split out of `session-narration.js` at the §1 cap; re-exported, so no caller moved). **RE-DERIVE THE NUMBERS FROM THAT FILE — this section deliberately no longer restates `PROSE_CAP`,** which is what let it stand at "2000" while the constant was 8000.
      - 🔒 ⚠ **PROSE KEEPS ITS NEWLINES AND A CAPTION DOES NOT (Samuel's 2026-08-31 ruling; F-376b RESOLVED).** `› line` flattens `/\s+/g` — correct for a LABEL on one row, where the full value is elsewhere. `› prose` must NOT, and did until this date: every heading, bullet, numbered step and fenced code block an agent wrote was **deleted, not truncated**, on the one class of frame this ring is the only copy of, and the SPA's markdown renderer never saw a newline to draw. **THE RULE: prose is bounded by CHARACTERS and NEVER by SHAPE** — a cut loses the tail and admits it (`truncated`), a flatten loses the meaning of everything that arrives. ⚠ What `prose` still normalizes: CR/CRLF → `\n`, blank-line runs → one, trailing spaces, and horizontal runs INSIDE a line — **leading indentation survives**, because a nested bullet and a code body are made of it. All of it can only SHORTEN, so `RING_CHAR_BUDGET`'s arithmetic is unchanged.
      - ⚠ **`PROSE_CAP` IS 2000 BECAUSE THAT IS `channels-v2/agent-stream-log.tsx › EXPANDED_CHARS`, AND THE PAIRING IS THE POINT (Samuel, live review 2026-08-27).** Prose rode `TEXT_CAP` until then, so the string reaching the SPA was **already 300 chars, mid-word, with no marker** — and the work stream's "Show more", which raises a DISPLAY clamp from 140 to 2000, expanded onto a string that had been cut long before it arrived. **The reader pressed the control meant to undo the truncation and the text stayed cut** ("…or I'll pi"). Two truncations, one of them silent, and the silent one upstream of the control. With the two numbers equal, **the renderer's clip is the only truncation an operator can meet, and that one SAYS it clipped (§9)**. Raise either and raise both; **never re-introduce a cut below what the UI will show.**
      - ⚠ **WHY PROSE AND NOT THE OTHER TWO.** A tool result is a caption ABOUT a payload (and `inputFull` is the thing that must never ride), so widening `TEXT_CAP` turns the ring into a file cache. A `post` has a second copy — the channel transcript — so a clipped echo costs nothing. **The agent's prose has no second copy anywhere: this ring is the only place it ever exists**, which is exactly why a silent cut there destroys the only text there is. ⚠ **THE COST, STATED:** `flush()` sends the WHOLE ring per dirty session, so the per-session worst case rises from 200 × 300 to 200 × 2000 chars, times `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS` (**15** since 2026-09-01; it was 6 when this arithmetic was written on 2026-08-27, and `session-narration.js`'s own header said 4 until then). That worst case needs all 200 entries to be maximal prose, which a working agent cannot produce: tool/result frames are the bulk of any ring and stay at `TEXT_CAP`. **If it ever needs tightening, tighten `NARRATION_MAX` or send a delta — not the cut.**
    - **THE WORK LANE IS A SECOND BRIDGE CHANNEL, NOT A WIDER `sessions.summaries`.** `main/session-narration.js` keeps a bounded per-session ring (`NARRATION_MAX`, ⚠ multiplicative against `MAX_CONCURRENT_SESSIONS` like every per-session bound) fed from the engine's ONE dispatch funnel, and fans out over the app-window registry on its own coalesced channel.
      - 🔒 ⚠ **THE RING IS BOUNDED TWICE SINCE 2026-08-30 — BY ENTRIES AND BY CHARACTERS — AND THE SECOND BOUND EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIRST ONE WAS NOT A BOUND ON ANYTHING THAT MATTERED.** `flush()` sends the WHOLE ring per dirty session, `sendToWindows` clones that payload into EVERY live window's message pipe (up to nine: the SPA + `popout-window.js › MAX_POPOUTS` + `agent-window.js › MAX_AGENT_WINDOWS`), and `note()` marks a session dirty on EVERY SDK event — so the feed re-serializes at up to `1000 / PUSH_COALESCE_MS` = 5 Hz per session per window. `webContents.send` structure-clones into a Mojo pipe, i.e. **native memory: no GC pressure, nothing in a heap snapshot, and no backpressure from a renderer slow to drain.** `PROSE_CAP` rising 300 → 2000 on 2026-08-27 took the per-flush ceiling from 60k chars to 400k, and that is what turned a cost into the 17 GB dev-RSS incident. **`main/session-narration.js › RING_CHAR_BUDGET` (60_000 = `NARRATION_MAX` × `TEXT_CAP`) restores the pre-2026-08-27 ceiling without undoing `PROSE_CAP`: a long line still arrives WHOLE — never cut mid-word, never cut silently — it costs more of the ring, so what pays is the OLDEST ENTRIES.** ⚠ **Do not answer a future over-run by lowering `PROSE_CAP`** — that reintroduces the silent cut Samuel's 2026-08-27 ruling deleted. Lower the budget, or send a delta. `test/session-narration-budget.test.mjs` pins all of it, and derives 60_000 rather than restating it. ⚠ **IT NAMED THE "BOUND:" CASES INSIDE `test/session-narration.test.mjs` UNTIL 2026-08-31** — they moved to their own file when that one crossed the §1 cap, on the seam of SUBJECT: what a FRAME SAYS vs what a FLUSH COSTS.
      - ⚠ **THE STANDING RULE THIS GENERALIZES: a per-session bound stated without the FAN-OUT and the RATE is not a bound.** `PROSE_CAP`'s own note did the size arithmetic correctly and still missed the incident, because ×9 windows × 5 Hz is a rate and nothing in that file bounded one. **Every `sendToWindows` feed owes an answer to "how many bytes per second, to how many windows".** `session-summary.js › flush` has one (a digest gate — it fires only when the projection MOVES); narration has none by construction (a digest over an append-only ring changes on every append), which is exactly why its bound has to be on the payload. **The summaries feed could not carry it:** it is digest-gated and `session-state-push.js` subscribes to that gate to decide when to write the server, so per-event narration there would cost a server write per tool call or be swallowed by the digest. ⚠ **`inputFull` NEVER ENTERS A RING ENTRY** — it is unbounded by construction (`session-io.js › sdkRenderEvents` builds it for the old expandable card) and this feed crosses to a renderer; `test/session-narration.test.mjs` asserts both the entry shape and that the module never reads the field.
    - ⚠ **A LIVE SESSION'S PERMISSION POSTURE CAN BE MOVED FROM THE AGENT VIEW, AND IT APPLIES FROM THE VERY NEXT GATE DECISION — NOT THE NEXT LAUNCH (Samuel, 2026-08-20).** `sessions:setMode` → `main/session-reopen.js › setModeByTask`, one axis per call, dispatching the reducer's own `set_tool_mode` / `set_message_mode`.
      - **"IMMEDIATE" IS A PROPERTY OF THE ENGINE, NOT A PROMISE THE UI MAKES.** `main/session-io.js › grantArgs` reads BOTH axes off `s.state` at CALL time, so moving the reducer's state IS the change — there is no cache to bust and nothing to invalidate. **Dispatch the reducer event; never assign `s.state` directly** — the reducer coerces fail-closed (`coerceMode`) and emits the `modes` echo, and a second writer to the same field is how two readers come to disagree about one posture.
      - ⚠ **IT WIDENS SUPERVISION, NEVER CONTAINMENT.** The two axes decide whether the OPERATOR IS ASKED. The **PROFILE** decides what is reachable at all, is checked FIRST, and no posture can widen it: `SESSION_HARD_DENY` is unconditional and `bypass` is a **POSITIVE allow-list**, so an unclassified tool gates in every mode, `bypass` included. The worst a forged call achieves is to stop asking about tools this operator's own channel profile ALREADY PERMITS — **the same authority `channels:setLaunchPosture` hands them at spawn**, exercised on a session already running.
      - ⚠ **IT IS NOT THE DURABLE LAUNCH POSTURE, AND THE TWO ARE STILL TWO RECORDS — BUT THEY ARE NOW WIRED, ONE WAY (2026-08-25, Samuel: "permission settings must apply to running sessions").** `channel-prefs.js`'s record governs the NEXT spawn; this op moves ONE live session and **writes nothing**. **Collapsing them is still forbidden** — it would make a per-session decision permanent, or a permanent one per-session. What changed is narrower and is a FAN-OUT rather than a merge: `channel-dir-ipc.js › applyPostureToLive` runs after a SUCCESSFUL `channels:setLaunchPosture` and calls **this op**, once per axis, for every live session in that channel.
        - **The defect it closes.** The pair was read at spawn and never again, so an operator who opened a channel to Bypass / `auto_both` while agents were already in it watched the pre-change cohort go on gating every post while the Settings tab displayed the new pair. **The room ignored the setting.** Reproduced from one `listener.log`: two cohorts four minutes apart in one channel, `tool=manual msg=auto_inbound` beside `tool=bypass msg=auto_both`.
        - **The direction is load-bearing and only one way exists.** Durable → live, at the moment the operator changes the setting. There is **no live → durable** edge: moving one agent from the agent view still writes nothing, which is what keeps a per-session decision from becoming permanent.
        - ⚠ **IT ADDS NO AUTHORITY AND MUST REUSE THIS OP RATHER THAN WRITING THE AXES.** Same sender gate, same operator, same machine — and `setModeByTask` is where the windowless message FLOOR (F-236) and the reducer's fail-closed coercion live. **Address per AGENT, never per (channel, thread)**: `resolveSession` takes the OLDEST agent on a thread when none is named, so a thread-addressed fan-out would move one agent and skip its siblings. **Best-effort by construction** — the durable write has already landed, so a settled session, a missing engine or a throw is diagnosed and skipped, never propagated. `test/channel-posture-live.test.mjs` pins all of it; the count rides back as an additive `applied` field on the existing `{ok, preset}` reply.
      - **NOTHING PENDING IS RE-DECIDED, AND IN THIS SHAPE THERE IS NOTHING PENDING TO RE-DECIDE.** The reducer's branch has refused a blanket drain since v2.9 (`pendingPermissions` holds requestIds only, so it cannot tell a queued `Bash` from a queued `op=open`, and draining would let the TOOL axis answer a MESSAGE operation). ⚠ In the windowless shape the question is moot: `session-windowless.js › claimGate` **denies a gated tool immediately** and bridges an outbound post to a server-decided consent row, so nothing is ever held locally. `drainInbound` went with the v1 purge for the matching reason — a windowless session's message axis is floored at `auto_inbound`, so the inbound queue never holds either.
      - **THE UI SHOWS MAIN'S VALUE, NEVER ITS OWN ASK.** `toolMode` / `messageMode` ride the summaries wire read-only (⚠ the REDUCER's state, not the channel's stored posture — different facts), `null` on an ended session. The op answers main's post-dispatch pair, and `components/channels-v2/agent-posture.tsx` does **not** stamp optimistically: three things move a posture without this control touching it (the auth hold resetting both axes, a resume, a change made in another window).
    - ⚠ **A 1:1 MESSAGE STARTS A *PRIVATE TURN*, AND THE GATE IS THE ENFORCEMENT — THE PROMPT IS NOT (2026-08-22, Samuel's ruling).** Two layers, both required, and only the second is a mechanism.
      - **FRAMING** (`main/session-seed.js › frameOperatorTurn`): the turn carries an explicit contract — their message was not posted anywhere, **your answer is the FINAL TEXT of this turn** and is shown only in their agent view, **do not post to the channel to answer**, a post they explicitly ask for **will be HELD for their approval**, and **reading is unrestricted**. ⚠ It promises a HOLD rather than an impossibility, because a deliberate operator-approved post IS possible and telling an agent it cannot do something it can do produces a refusal the operator has to argue with.
      - **GATE** (`main/session-private.js`): while the private turn is the active turn, AXIS B's **OUTBOUND widening is withdrawn** — `auto_both` reads as `auto_inbound`, `auto_outbound` as `ask` (`session-profiles.js › privateTurnMessageMode`, the deliberate inverse of `floorWindowlessMessage` and sitting beside it).
