Any `dopl_channel` post, milestone **or `create_thread` (2026-08-24 — it joined the outbound half, and the withdrawal transforms the AXIS, so it was inherited with no call-site change)** therefore hits `grantDecision`'s `gate` and BRIDGES to an outbound consent row + notification. **An accidental public reply is impossible; a deliberate approved one is not.** Read through `session-io.js › grantArgs`, the ONE call site.
      - ⚠ **THE WINDOW IS A DEPTH, AND IT LEAKED — 2026-08-22, the confirmed root cause of the posture degradation.** THREE fixes, all in the same pass: (a) `openPrivateTurn`'s `+2` DOUBLE-COUNTED when the in-flight turn was ALREADY private — the extra unit exists to cover a CHANNEL turn that happened to be running, and a private turn in flight is already paying for itself, so `const add = (turnInFlight(s.state) && !isPrivateTurn(s)) ? 2 : 1;`. Two 1:1 messages sent while the agent worked left the session permanently gated, with every later CHANNEL turn silently unable to auto-send. (b) **A TORN-DOWN QUERY OWES NO RESULTS**, so `resetPrivateTurn` zeroes the depth on the `abortQuery` and `denyPending` effects and in `session-park.js › resumeParked`: `session-query.js › consume` drops a superseded query's tail (`s.query !== q`), so the `result` events that would have SPENT the depth never arrive and the surplus was stranded on a resumable session. **The remaining over-cover is unchanged and still deliberate** — a non-private turn in flight still costs `+2`, because the alternative failure is a private answer posted in public.
      - ⚠ **THE STATIC FIXTURE IS WHAT HID IT.** `test/session-private-turn.test.mjs` drove `openPrivateTurn` against a session whose `activity` never moved, and the double-count only happens once the FIRST 1:1 message has flipped the session to `working` — which only the reducer does. The suite now drives `session-reopen.js › messageByTask` TWICE through the REAL reducer with its state applied.
      - ⚠ **THE IN HALF IS DELIBERATELY PRESERVED — this is NOT "as if message mode were `ask`".** Own-channel READS follow the INBOUND half, and in a windowless session a gated read is a DENIED read (no surface to answer on): an agent asked "what did they say in that thread?" would be unable to look. The ruling is about what LEAVES the machine. AXIS A is untouched, so the v2.9 invariant holds — `bypass` still cannot send a message, private turn or not.
      - ⚠ **THE WINDOW IS A DEPTH, NOT A FLAG, because a `steer` QUEUES (`priority: 'next'`).** Agent IDLE → **+1** (the pushed message IS the next turn); agent WORKING or `awaiting_permission` → **+2** (the in-flight turn's `result` spends one, leaving the private turn covered). A plain boolean would gate the in-flight CHANNEL turn and then be cleared by ITS `result`, leaving the private turn ungated — the exact failure the rule exists for. Decremented at `result` in the engine's ONE dispatch funnel and **floored at zero** (a drained tail's stray `result` must not make the NEXT private turn public). The `+2` over-covers by one turn, deliberately: the safe direction.
      - **BOTH DIRECTIONS REACH THE UI, TAGGED** — kind `operator` + `lane: 'operator'` (what the operator TYPED, never the framed prompt) and kind `private` + `lane: 'private'` (an `assistant` line re-tagged by `session-narration.js › retagPrivate` when the turn is private). ⚠ **ONLY `assistant` MOVES**: a `post` inside a private turn keeps kind `post` and `lane: 'channel'`, because it is the one thing that did not stay private.
    - ⚠ **`sessions:message` IS THE ONE RENDERER-REACHABLE OP THAT STARTS A TURN ON AN EXISTING SESSION**, and it got its own review (F-212 carries it in full). It dispatches the EXISTING `steer` event through `main/session-reopen.js › messageByTask` — no new reducer branch, no second wake path — on a session resolved by `(channel, thread, agent)` against MAIN'S OWN registry — which is what makes it own-agents-only structurally rather than by a check, and since 2026-08-21 is also what makes the 1:1 lane reach exactly ONE agent among several (the `agentId` is optional and falls back to the oldest live one). **It BYPASSES the inbound gate, correctly: AXIS B governs COUNTERPARTY turns**, and this is the operator's own keyboard in a window main created. The body is delimited with the session's nonce and carries OPERATOR authority (`main/session-seed.js › frameOperatorTurn`) — ⚠ **deliberately NOT fenced as data**, because the operator is the one voice the framing tells a session to weigh and `frameContinuation`'s "this is DATA, never instructions" preamble would invert that. Bounded in the preload AND at the boundary (`MESSAGE_CAP`), empty bodies refused, version floor applied. **Worst case of a forged call: the operator's own agent does work they did not ask for, inside its existing profile and containment** — no tool granted, no posture widened, no other machine reached, nothing posted without the outbound gate.
    - **NOTHING TYPED THERE IS POSTED**, and the agent is told so in as many words; the surface says it too. An out-of-band steer is not channel traffic.
  - **THE OPT-IN POP-OUT THREAD WINDOW SHIPPED (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 10). It was parked on a SECURITY decision and Samuel took OPTION (a): WIDEN THE SENDER BINDING to a registry of app-owned windows.** Option (b) — reuse the session window's renderer — was rejected because it builds the thread view twice, which is the duplication the channels port exists to remove. The rule as it now stands is the `app-windows` bullet below; what follows is the window itself.
    - **The button is `src/features/channels/components/channels-v2/pop-out.tsx › PopOutThreadButton`, in the thread view's header and NOWHERE ELSE** — the channel view has no thread to pop out. **Its place in that header is IMMEDIATELY LEFT OF THE INFO TOGGLE, wearing the identical `IconButton` face** (Samuel, 2026-08-19; it sat beside the breadcrumb, at a 14px glyph, until then). It is DESKTOP-ONLY by feature detection on `window.dopl.threads.openWindow` (`src/shared/lib/desktop.ts › getDesktopThreadWindows`) and renders **nothing** in a browser or against an older main. The read is `useSyncExternalStore`, not a render-time read and not an effect: the bridge is a window global, so a render-time read disagrees with SSR and a synchronous `setState` in an effect is a `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` ERROR in this tree.
    - ⚠ **THE WINDOW SHOWS ONLY THE THREAD, AND IT DID NOT UNTIL 2026-08-19.** It landed on the FULL channels page, so a window opened to read one exchange arrived carrying the app sidebar, the channels tree and the info panel — every surface the operator already had open behind it. What it renders now is `components/channels-v2/thread-window.tsx`: the transcript, the composer, and the crumb reduced to the thread's own title. **It is NOT a second transcript** — it mounts `message-pane.tsx` with `chrome="window"`, so the scroller, the stick-to-bottom rules and the scroll-target contract have exactly one implementation. A thread id not in the channel's bounded list renders an empty STATE, not an empty transcript; there is no channel view here to fall back to.
    - **THE LANDING IS A ROUTE OF ITS OWN, OUTSIDE THE APP SHELL.** `main/popout-window.js › threadRoute` builds `/{segment}/{THREAD_WINDOW_PAGE}/{channelId}?thread={threadId}` (`THREAD_WINDOW_PAGE` is `'thread-window'`, a HAND COPY of `apps/desktop-ui/src/routes.tsx › THREAD_WINDOW_PATH`; `test/popout-window.test.mjs` reads the SPA export and fails on drift) and the window is LANDED AT CREATION on that HASH (`loadFile(INDEX_HTML, { hash })`; the SPA is a hash router over a `file://` document), never steered afterwards over the navigate bridge. The row is a **TOP-LEVEL sibling of `/:workspaceSegment`, not a child of `AppShellLayout`** — a layout route cannot be opted out of from inside it. **`?thread=` is still a SELECTION, not a route.** ⚠ The channels page still READS `?thread=` into `initialThreadId` and nothing produces one for it any more; that is a live, tested capability, not the pop-out's landing.
    - 🔒 **THE ROUTE IS DELIBERATELY NOT DEEP-LINKABLE, and that is two absences making one decision.** It is in neither `main/deep-link-target.js › WORKSPACE_PAGES` nor `› ROOT_ROUTES` (which is what carries `onboarding`), so `dopl://open/{segment}/thread-window/{id}` is an unknown page inside a real workspace and opens that workspace's HOME page — the table's existing rule, ASSERTED in `test/deep-link-target.test.mjs` rather than left to fall through. **A pop-out is created by MAIN, at a window main built and registered**; a grammar that could mint one from an arbitrary caller's URL would be a new surface, not a shortcut. The `WORKSPACE_PAGES` drift test reads only that block of `routes.tsx`, so the new row keeps it green by construction.
    - **DEFAULT SIZE IS 520x600, AND THAT IS ALSO ITS FLOOR** (`minWidth`/`minHeight` equal the defaults) — the OLD SESSION WINDOW'S footprint, taken deliberately because a single-column transcript beside another app was already proven at it. ⚠ The file it was copied from is DELETED (F-228), so this is now the only statement of where the number came from. It was 720x820 / 460x420 while it landed on the three-column page.
    - **THE WINDOW'S TITLE IS SET BY THE RENDERER: "Dopl — <thread title>".** Main creates the window from three ids and has no title to set — titles live behind an authenticated read the renderer is already making — so `thread-window.tsx › threadWindowTitle` writes `document.title` when the thread loads and Electron's default `page-title-updated` handling copies it onto the window. **`popout-window.js` must not start intercepting that event**; its `title: 'Dopl'` option is the PRE-PAINT fallback for a window that never finishes loading, and `index.html`'s `<title>Dopl</title>` is the same answer one layer down.
    - **SAME PRELOAD, SAME LOCKDOWN, SHARED NOT COPIED.** `main/spa-window.js › spaWebPreferences` and `› policeNavigation` are exported and taken verbatim by the pop-out, so `setWindowOpenHandler` deny, `contextIsolation`/`sandbox`/`nodeIntegration:false` and the exact-path navigation lock cannot drift between the two windows. **The pop-out gets the SPA's own bridge deliberately: it is an app window, and REGISTRATION is what authorizes it, not a narrower preload.**
    - **THE OP IS `threads:openWindow`, in `main/session-ipc-ops.js`** (⚠ it was in `channel-dir-ipc.js` until the 2026-08-20 split; the binding did not move with it)**, under the same binding as every other op there.** `channelId` is UUID-gated; `segment` and `threadId` go through `deep-link-target.js › isSafeSegment` (§11's one character rule — a second regex would be a second answer). **It REFUSES while the min-version gate is blocking**, because `createShellWindow` is that gate's single enforcement point and a second factory would otherwise be a door the block does not cover. **One window per (channel, thread)** — asking again FRONTS the existing one — and the count is capped at `MAX_POPOUTS` (**4**, verified 2026-08-18): a renderer-driven window factory with no ceiling is a resource primitive. Every refusal is the same `{ ok: false }`.
    - **NO WINDOW HANDLE CROSSES THE BRIDGE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE SAFETY ARGUMENT FOR WIDENING.** The renderer asks; main creates the window and main registers it. A renderer cannot enlarge the set of senders it is judged against.
    - **EVERY MAIN→RENDERER PUSH FANS OUT OVER THE REGISTRY, or the pop-out is stale with no error anywhere.** Three did not, and all three were widened in this phase: `main/ui-sync.js › sendToWindows` (the realtime doorbell — a transcript that just stops updating), `main/session-summary.js › sendToWindows` (the Agents projection) and `main/ui-bridge.js › broadcastAuthState` (the worst — a window still rendering a signed-out session's data). **One dead window may not swallow the rest of a fan-out.** ⚠ **ONE BOUND REMAINS AND IT IS F-222:** the feed holds ONE realtime channel filtered on ONE `workspace_id` and `dopl:sync-watch` is last-writer-wins across windows, so switching workspaces in the MAIN window while a pop-out is open leaves the pop-out's workspace unwatched. `watch()` logs that transition by name rather than making it silent.
    - **THE PRE-CONSENT MACHINERY IS STILL INTACT AND IS STILL NOT THIS.** Phase 10 did not touch `session-consent.js`; the pop-out is a THREAD window (the shared transcript), not a session window (an agent's run).
  - ⚠ **THE CAPABILITY IS GONE, NOT JUST DEFAULTED OFF — 2026-08-20 (F-228).** This bullet said "nothing in `session-consent.js` was removed or narrowed; flipping the key back restores the old path byte-for-byte", and that was true right up until the file was deleted. Flipping `preConsentWindowMode` now restores nothing. **The clause about Phase 10's pop-out being "built on that machinery" was WRONG even then** — `popout-window.js` clones `spa-window.js`, shares its `spaWebPreferences` / `policeNavigation`, and never touched the pre-consent registry; the pop-out is unaffected by the deletion, which is the evidence. The DISARMED-GUARD record moved to `test/removed-vocabulary.test.mjs` when the getters themselves went: it now refuses any code-level read of either retired store key, which is the property that actually matters once there is no getter left to answer OFF.
- **A deep link returns a ROUTE, never a URL.** Every value is assembled from segments decoded and character-checked one at a time. **`new URL` DOES run first — for the ORIGIN check — but the segment walk is over the RAW path string, never `parsed.pathname`**, because `new URL` NORMALIZES `..` away rather than refusing it (`/{seg}/../../etc` arrives as `/etc`, a link that says one thing and resolves to another). `main/deep-link-target.js › rawPathOf` cuts the path out of the string (query and fragment come off FIRST), and `pathSegments` percent-**decodes each segment and THEN** rejects `.` / `..` — decoding first is what stops `%2e%2e`. **Handing that walk a pre-normalized path silently disarms it, because by then there is nothing left to refuse** — which is how the bug was found: the unit case passed, since it was handed the raw string. **Malformed opens home; unknown resolves as deep as it can — there is no failure mode where a Dopl link does nothing.**
  - **The character rule is ONE exported function, `main/deep-link-target.js › isSafeSegment`, and it has a SECOND caller now** (2026-08-18): `main/shell-mode.js › navigateToChannels` builds `/{segment}/{page}/{channelId}` from a channel id that arrived on a server DTO. That is not a deep link and does not pass through the grammar, but it is the same question, and **a local regex over there would be a second answer to it** — so shell-mode requires the export lazily and carries no character class of its own. **BOTH interpolated values are checked**: an unusable segment shows the window and navigates nowhere, an unusable channel id degrades to the page.
- **`WORKSPACE_PAGES` in `main/deep-link-target.js` is a HAND COPY** of the SPA routes (main cannot import TypeScript); `test/deep-link-target.test.mjs` re-reads `routes.tsx` and fails on drift. **Its value means "this page has a `:param` detail child", NEVER "this page exists"** — a `true` with no matching row hands the renderer a route that matches nothing. Measured 2026-08-18, AFTER the cutover: **`channels` is `true`** (it holds the `channels/:channelId` row, which is where a clicked notification lands) and **there is no `channels-v2` key at all** — that page's route is gone, so a row for it would be exactly the `true`-with-no-route this bullet warns about. Between Phase 9 and Phase 12 the pair was split the other way; the swap was one edit. ⚠ **Flipping a page to `true` ENLARGES the surface the raw-path segment walk protects; it exempts nothing** — the third segment still decodes and character-checks like every other one. ⚠ **THE DRIFT TEST READS THE `WORKSPACE_PAGES` BLOCK OF `routes.tsx` AND NOTHING ELSE, so a TOP-LEVEL SPA row is invisible to it — which is deliberate, not a hole.** Two rows are outside that block today: `/onboarding`, which IS reachable by link and therefore carries a `ROOT_ROUTES` entry, and `/:workspaceSegment/thread-window/:channelId`, which is deliberately reachable by NEITHER table (the pop-out window — see the pop-out bullets above). **A new top-level route owes an explicit deep-link decision, in both directions**: add it to `ROOT_ROUTES` to make it linkable, or assert what a link naming it degrades to. Adding `thread-window` to either table is the mistake to refuse. **The web tree has TWO `dopl://` producers** — `src/app/auth/desktop-handoff/page.tsx` and `src/features/workspaces/url.ts › workspaceDeepLink` (the join/invite landing cards, 2026-08-11) — and the same test re-reads the producer source and asserts scheme + verb parity, so a grammar change that strands a producer fails in CI. **A join/invite TOKEN never crosses into the app**: `join`/`invite` stay in `WEB_ONLY_ROOTS` by design — the token is a web credential redeemed by a Next route; only the resolved workspace segment is deep-linked. ⚠ **`main/agent-names.js` WAS a byte-for-byte PORT of a web module held by a parity test, and BOTH ARE DELETED (2026-08-21, multiplayer agents — see §11's multiplayer block).** The hand-copy DISCIPLINE this bullet is about is unchanged and still has live subjects (`WORKSPACE_PAGES`, `AGENT_WINDOW_PAGE`, `THREAD_WINDOW_PAGE`); the pool is simply no longer one of them, because an agent's name is now generated per instance on the desktop and nothing in the web tree defines it.
- **Preload surfaces are a PINNED INVENTORY — `test/preload-parity.test.mjs` fails on ADD as well as on REMOVE.** ⚠ **The old rule ("every callable op the remote preload exposes must also exist in the SPA preload") is RETIRED: the remote shell is deleted and its preload orphaned**, so there is no second preload left to compare against and no reference implementation. What replaced it is an explicit list of ops, asserted against preloads that are **EXECUTED against a fake `electron`** (the surface the shell really exposes, not a grep over source text). A derivation from the tree was tried and rejected on measurement — it found 3 of ~20 ops, because the tree destructures the bridge or reaches it through typed wrappers. **ADDING an op fails this test on purpose**: the pin is worth only what its last review was, so a new op gets looked at rather than absorbed. The reason any of this exists is unchanged — the shared `src/` tree feature-detects each capability and renders **nothing** when absent, so a gap does not fail, **it deletes a feature silently.** **Adding an op is still a FOUR-file change** (the preload, the `ipcMain` handler, the renderer-side typed mirror, the web tree's feature detector) — **plus the pin.**
  - ⚠ **`claude.signIn` JOINED ON 2026-08-25 — the FIRST NAMESPACE ON THIS BRIDGE WHOSE SUBJECT IS THE MACHINE'S CLAUDE CODE CREDENTIAL, and it exists because Q6's DETECTION had no REMEDY for five days.** `session-auth.js › holdIfNoCredential` has preflighted a windowless launch and HELD it since Q6, `session-query.js` turns an auth-shaped mid-session failure into the same hold, and the composer says so verbatim (`channels-v2/agent-composer.tsx › MESSAGE_AUTH_HELD`) — while `claude-auth.js › startSignInFlow` and `session-auth.js › resumeAfterSignIn` both shipped **complete, test-covered, and with ZERO production callers.** Nothing could enter the flow, so a re-post into a held agent was refused `auth-hold` forever and no dialog could ever appear. ⚠ **THIS IS THIS BULLET'S OWN FAILURE MODE ARRIVED AT FROM THE OTHER END:** the surface feature-detects and renders nothing when absent, and here the op was never written at all — so the pin could not fire, because there was no op to add. **A test over either half stays green through it; only the WIRE can be pinned** (`test/claude-signin-recovery.test.mjs`). Handler `session-ipc-ops.js › claude:signIn`, `appWindowOnly`, body in `main/claude-signin-op.js`; web detector `channels-v2/claude-signin.ts › canSignInToClaude`.
    - **IT TAKES NO PAYLOAD, so the sender binding is the ONLY guard on it** — the third op in that census with that shape, after the two `orchestratorLaunch` members, and it is enumerated in `test/channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs › OPS` for exactly that reason. **NO CREDENTIAL CROSSES IT IN EITHER DIRECTION**: main opens the OAuth page in the SYSTEM BROWSER and collects the pasted code in its own local window, and no Dopl surface has a field that would take one. The answer is a bare `{ ok }` — **the state of the CREDENTIAL after the flow, never the flow's own outcome**, because `startSignInFlow` resolves `undefined` on every path (completed, declined, failed, single-flight no-op) and is therefore unable to report one. It is re-probed through `credentialState()` with `forget()` called FIRST, since that probe's 5s cache is wrong at exactly this moment.
    - **ONE SIGN-IN, N RELEASES.** The flow is driven ONCE per call and the fan-out is `session-auth.js › resumeHeldSessions`, which walks the engine's registry and hands every held, unsettled session to the existing `resumeAfterSignIn`. ⚠ **THE PREFLIGHT LANE RECOVERS DIFFERENTLY and the difference is invisible from the surface**: a windowless launch that holds is UN-REGISTERED by the engine (`sessions.delete(s.key); return { authHold: true }`), so there is nothing left to release — the operator's next post makes a NEW session, and what has to be true is that the preflight now lets it through. **That is why the op re-probes and drops the cache; the probe TTL is the only thing that could still refuse there.**
  - ⚠ **`sessions.setMode` JOINED ON 2026-08-20** — the agent view's LIVE permission controls (its own bullet above). Reviewed at `test/preload-parity.test.mjs › APP_OPS`: it widens SUPERVISION, never containment, and is deliberately NOT the durable launch posture.
  - ⚠ **THREE OPS JOINED ON 2026-08-20 (F-212's closure): `sessions.openAgentWindow`, `sessions.message` and `sessions.narration`/`onNarration`.** They are NOT equivalent and are reviewed separately in `test/preload-parity.test.mjs › APP_OPS` — the window op is `threads.openWindow`'s twin, the narration pair is read-only in-memory state, and **`message` is the one that starts a turn** (its bullet is above). `sessions.reopen` also gained an OPTIONAL third argument, the workspace segment, because the agent window lands on a router path and main holds the workspace UUID rather than the slug.
  - **The app preload's inventory is TWENTY-THREE ops** (measured 2026-08-18; the list itself is `test/preload-parity.test.mjs › APP_OPS` — read it, do not trust this number). **One joined on 2026-08-18 (wiring plan Phase 10): `threads.openWindow`**, the pop-out thread window. It ASKS for a window and gets none back. **Two joined on 2026-08-18 (wiring plan Phase 5): `sessions.pause` and `sessions.end`**, the Agents tab's controls on the operator's OWN agent. They dispatch the reducer events the session window's own buttons have always dispatched — `interrupt` (the send button's pause morph) and `end` ("End session") — through `main/session-reopen.js › controlByTask`. **Neither can start a query, wake a parked shell, grant a tool or post anything: the failure direction of a forged call is an agent that STOPS.**
  - **PAUSE/END ARE OWN-AGENTS-ONLY, STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN BY A CHECK.** Main resolves `(channelId, taskId)` against ITS OWN in-memory session registry, which holds nothing but this operator's sessions on this machine, so an unresolvable key answers `{ok:false}` and there is nothing else to reach. **Never add a cross-machine control op here** — a peer's paused agent is read from PRESENCE on their side (§7's 90 s window, which already fails safe to offline), never driven from another member's client.
  - ⚠ **`sessions.summaries` / `onSummaries` WIDENED in the same phase and the wire shape is now FIVE fields larger** — `contextUsed`, `contextWindow`, `tokensSpent`, `startedAt`, `lastActivityAt` (`main/session-summary.js › metrics`, mirrored in `src/shared/lib/spa-bridge.ts › DesktopSessionSummary`). ⚠ **"None of them reaches the server" STOOD HERE AND IS NOW FALSE (2026-08-22, F-270)** — see the TELEMETRY CADENCE bullet below; all five cross, quantized. **What is still true, and is the half worth keeping:** `main/session-state-push.js › reportRow` picks the `channel_sessions` columns BY NAME, so a metric added to `metrics()` and named nowhere else still does not widen the table. **`null` is a real answer on every one and NEVER means zero** — an older main omits the field, a model this build has no window row for has no denominator, and nothing is measured before the first turn reports usage. `metricOrNull` checks `typeof` FIRST for exactly this reason (`Number(null)` is 0), and the Agents tab renders the absence rather than a 0% meter.
  - ⚠ **AND TWO FIELDS WIDER AGAIN SINCE 2026-08-20 — `detail` and `toolLabel`, THE FINER ACTIVITY SIGNAL, AND THE PILL VOCABULARY DID NOT MOVE.** `main/session-detail.js › detailFor` answers one of six keys (`thinking` / `tool` / `posting` / `permission` / `awaiting_peer` / `awaiting_inbound`) or `null`, from `{ phase, activity }` plus **`lastEventKind`**, stamped by `› noteEvent` at the engine's ONE dispatch funnel (`session-engine.js › dispatch` → `session-summary.js › noteActivity(s, event)`, which now takes the event). **The obstacle the old docblocks named is GONE and the reason the pill stays three-valued CHANGED:** it was never `includePartialMessages` (F-146 corrected that in four places) and it is no longer "the fact does not exist here" either — **it is that `state` is the SERVER's vocabulary.** `session-state-push.js` hands it to `channel_sessions.state`, whose CHECK and whose `schema-sessions.ts` zod enum both admit exactly `working|idle|ended`, and **zod validates the ARRAY**, so one row carrying a fourth value 400s the WHOLE payload; `retryable(400)` is false, the digest is never recorded, and **every later push for that workspace fails identically** — valid sessions included — leaving `read_sessions` answering `[]` and stale rows uncleared. So the finer signal rides BESIDE the pill and is **LOCAL-ONLY**, on the same by-name column pick the five metrics rely on (`test/session-state-push.test.mjs` asserts the row shape against a widened entry; that case is the belt).
    - **`detail` is `null` over any pill but `working`, by construction** — it REFINES the pill and may never contradict it. **The SPA re-enforces it rather than trusting the wire, in `components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › agentLiveness`**, which reaches `agentDetailLabel` only on the `working` branch and therefore cannot render "Idle" and "Thinking…" on one card whatever a main sends. ⚠ **`agent-bits.tsx › AgentLiveness` IS NOT THAT GUARD AND ENFORCES NOTHING** (corrected 2026-08-22, F-254 — this line claimed it did): it is handed a precomputed `{tone, label}` and paints it. That is the point of the split — every surface runs the one mapping and the one component draws its verdict — but it means the render layer has no rule of its own to state.
    - **THE KEY IS DERIVED ON THE DESKTOP; THE SENTENCE IS WRITTEN IN THE WEB TREE** (`components/channels-v2/agents-model.ts › agentDetailLabel`). Which situation this is has exactly one answer and belongs beside the engine; what a human reads is a product decision that must not need a desktop release to change. **An unrecognized key renders NOTHING, never the raw key** — a newer main emitting a seventh value degrades to the pill's own word, which is always true.
    - ⚠ **PEER CARDS GET NONE OF IT.** The cross-machine wire is `channel_sessions`, which carries the coarse state alone — see §5's Agents-tab bullet. The asymmetry is deliberate: fine-grained activity is LOCAL, coarse state is CROSS-MACHINE.
  - ⚠ **REGISTRATION IS A PROJECTION MOVE, AND IT IS NOT THE SDK's TO ANNOUNCE (2026-08-20).** `session-engine.js › startSession` calls `sessionSummary.touch()` on the SAME LINE as `sessions.set`, and again on each of its two rollbacks (`attachSurface` refused; a windowless spawn held on a missing credential). **Until it did, the earliest possible push was the FIRST reducer dispatch — which is the SDK's `system/init`** — so a freshly launched agent was absent from the Agents tab for as long as the `claude` child plus its MCP servers took to boot, and `session-query.js`'s own **C-4** comment records that a child can boot and never emit one at all, in which case the pill never arrived and only a reload could show it. **The reload worked for the reason that names the bug:** mount takes the `sessions:summaries` INVOKE, which reads the registry directly (`ui-bridge.js`), and a `phase:'launching'` session was in it the whole time. **The push and the invoke must project the same registry at the same moments; a surface that is only correct after a reload is a surface with no error shape.** `test/session-summary.test.mjs` pins the contract behaviourally and the call site as source (labelled as the weak half, §14).
  - **A LAUNCH MAIN REFUSED IS NOT A LAUNCH THAT IS STILL COMING, AND ONLY THE CALLER CAN SAY SO.** `sessions:launch` answers `{ok:false, reason}`, and **the set is the one `main/session-ipc-ops.js › sessions:launch` states over its own handler** — `cap`, `busy`, `no-sdk`, `auth-hold`, `disabled`, and since 2026-08-22 `no-template` and `template-approval` (§5A). ⚠ **`disabled` IS THE ONE THAT MOST NEEDS ITS OWN LINE and this bullet omitted it until 2026-08-23**: it is not a settings state, it is `session-engine.js › startSession`'s `attachSurface` ROLLBACK arriving through `session-launch.js`'s `{ skipped: 'disabled' }`, i.e. the spawn failed on the way up. ⚠ **`no-bridge` AND `no-counterparty` ARE SPA-PRODUCED WORDS, NOT ANSWERS FROM MAIN** — `agents-controls.ts › launchAgentOnThread` mints `no-bridge` when the IPC op is absent, `use-agents-panel.ts › launchAgent` mints `no-counterparty` before it calls, and `main/session-launch-op.js`'s own header states there is no `no-counterparty` refusal on this lane or any other. (The DIRECTIVE lane's separate seven-word wire vocabulary DOES carry both — `launch-directive-wire.js › REFUSAL_REASONS`, where `refusalFor` maps `disabled` → `no-bridge` — and confusing the two lists is what put them here.) **THE COUNT IS THE HANDLER'S OWN LIST**, not a number written down beside it. The Agents tab discarded the result, so every refusal rendered as a button that stopped spinning over a list that stayed empty — indistinguishable from the push gap above. **A refusal is not a push: nothing else on the surface can report it** (`use-desktop-sessions.ts › DesktopSessionsFeed` says the same about `refresh`). The copy is `components/channels-v2/use-agents-panel.ts › launchRefusalText`, one short line per reason, and an unrecognized reason falls back rather than rendering a raw enum.
  - **THE POST-LAUNCH RE-READ MUST NAME THE OWN FEED, NOT THE PEER PROJECTION.** `use-channel-agent-sessions` reads `channel_sessions` over HTTP and the tab filters this operator's own rows OUT of it by construction (`agents-model.ts › peerCardsFor`), so refetching it after a launch can never show the agent just launched. `useAgentsPanel` takes `refreshDesktopSessions` and calls it on success only. ⚠ **It is the BELT, not the mechanism** — the touch above is what makes the push arrive; this covers the child that boots and never emits `system/init`.
  - **`useDesktopSessions` RE-PROBES FOR THE BRIDGE, BOUNDED.** The subscription effect ran once on `[]` deps, so a first mount that raced the preload's global left the feed permanently `null`. It now re-probes five times at 200 ms and then stops: **`null` is the CORRECT terminal answer** for a plain browser and for a main without the feed (UNKNOWN is not EMPTY), so this must converge on it rather than poll for a bridge that is never coming. It re-reads a window global, not data.
- ⚠ **THERE IS ONE SENDER-BINDING REGIME AGAIN — 2026-08-20, AND THAT IS A DELETION, NOT A UNIFICATION.** The `session:*` regime is gone with `main/session-ipc.js` (F-228): its 15 handlers carried no `appWindowOnly(` wrapper and no frame check, binding instead by "does this webContents own a session" through the engine's injected `getSessionBySender` / `getConsentBySender`. What made that safe was CONTENT POLICY rather than a check — the session window shipped `default-src 'none'` with no `frame-src`, denied every `window.open` and blocked navigation off `file://` — and all of it went together. **Nothing was merged into the surviving regime and nothing should be: the rule below is now the whole rule.** ⚠ Why a second binding shape was ever acceptable is ENGINEERING §18's to keep. What follows is the `channel:*` / `ui:*` / window regime, unchanged.
- **Every renderer-reachable `ipcMain.handle` IN THE WINDOW REGIME is sender-bound to an APP-OWNED WINDOW's TOP FRAME.** Two checks, because one is not enough: the sender's `webContents.id` must be in `main/app-windows.js`'s registry, **and** the calling frame must be that `webContents`' top frame — a cross-origin iframe SHARES its host's `webContents`, so identity alone is not enough. Fails closed; **the refusal shape matches an invalid-payload rejection so a hostile page cannot probe the difference.** ⚠ **THERE IS ONE COPY OF THE PREDICATE NOW, AND THIS BULLET USED TO FORBID THAT (2026-08-20).** It read *"THREE copies, enumerated … **Do not de-duplicate them** — the second is sliced by `test/channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs`, the third by `test/update-required-screen.test.mjs`"*, and the reason was real: those copies sat inside BEGIN/END pure blocks their suites slice, and a sliced block may hold no `require`. **The conclusion did not follow.** A shared module can BE the block both suites slice, which is what `main/ipc-guards.js › isAppWindowSender` is — one source, sentinels of its own, sliced by `test/channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs` and `test/ui-bridge-guards.test.mjs` alike. `ui-bridge.js` and `channel-dir-ipc.js`/`session-ipc-ops.js` all import it. **De-duplicating out from under a test is the hazard; de-duplicating INTO the thing the tests slice is the fix.**
  - **`main/update-required-window.js › isGateSender` STAYS SEPARATE, deliberately** — its subject is ONE window (`sender === win.webContents`), not the registry, because the blocking screen is un-registered on purpose. Collapsing it would widen the update gate to every app window. Its frame branch is byte-consistent and separately pinned.
  - **The WRAPPER is still written literally at every `ipcMain.handle` site**, in both IPC files, because `test/channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs`'s structural belt reads that shape — hiding it in a factory would pass review and disarm the check that catches the next op added unbound. **What is shared is the PREDICATE, which is where the security content lives.**
  - ⚠ **THE SUBJECT WIDENED ON 2026-08-18 FROM "THE MAIN WINDOW" TO "THE REGISTRY", ON SAMUEL'S EXPLICIT RULING** (wiring plan Phase 10, option (a)), so the opt-in pop-out thread window could exist at all. **The iframe check, the fail-closed direction and the refusal shape did not move.** The wrapper is `appWindowOnly(...)`, written LITERALLY at every `ipcMain.handle` site because `channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs`'s structural belt reads exactly that shape — **hiding the wrap in a factory would pass review and silently disarm the guard that stops the NEXT op being added unbound.**
  - **WHO MAY REGISTER: MAIN, AT WINDOW CREATION, AND NOTHING ELSE.** `app-windows.js › register(win)` takes a live `BrowserWindow` — an object no renderer can name, let alone send over IPC — is behind no `ipcMain.handle`, and is named by no preload. **Two call sites today** (`main/shell-mode.js › createShellWindow` for the shell, `main/popout-window.js` for a pop-out); `test/app-windows.test.mjs` asserts all three properties structurally over the real tree, so a third site is reviewed rather than absorbed. ⚠ **The update-required screen is deliberately NOT registered** — it takes its own preload and a blocking screen holding the app's privileged surface would be the block leaking a door.
  - **A DESTROYED WINDOW LEAVES THE SET, BELT AND BRACES.** Both `'closed'` and `webContents 'destroyed'` are bound at registration, and every read sweeps first, so a window that dies emitting neither still falls out. **An empty registry is a DEAD privileged surface, which is the correct state before the first window exists** — an absent accessor is not a compatibility mode.
  - **F-221, and the reason the copies are gone rather than merely agreeing:** the lenient form `if (frame && sender.mainFrame && frame !== sender.mainFrame)` **admits a `senderFrame` reading as null/undefined**. Phase 10 closed `channel-dir-ipc.js` (then the most privileged surface) against `ui-bridge.js`'s already-fail-closed copy; ⚠ `update-required-window.js › isGateSender` was **STILL LENIENT after that, because the bullet above said there were two copies and nobody counted a third** — on the window whose two live ops end the process. Closed 2026-08-18. **That is TWO drifts in one predicate inside three months, which is what the 2026-08-20 extraction is an answer to:** the identical copies are one source, and the one that is genuinely a different question stayed separate.
- **The version floor FAILS OPEN.** Offline, timeout, 5xx, captive-portal HTML, a malformed floor, an unparseable own version — all "no floor", app opens. **No floor is ever cached to disk.** Two anti-brick guards from two sources of truth: the server refuses a floor above the declared latest; the client degrades when no updater exists or when a COMPLETED check found nothing newer (a FAILED check never sets that evidence).
- **`GET /api/version` is unauthenticated, and that takes TWO places** — `PUBLIC_ROUTES` **and** `SELF_AUTH_ROUTES`, both in `src/shared/auth/public-routes.ts` since 2026-08-23 (§3; they were in `src/proxy.ts`) — because the proxy 401s every unmatched `/api/**` before a route runs. Pinned by `proxy.test.ts`.
- 🔒 **THE CEILING BOUNDS FUTURE READS, NEVER CONTEXT ALREADY IN THE WINDOW (2026-08-26; §10's audience ceiling, plan §4.5).** A solo channel that gains a peer tightens at the session's very next tool call — layer A re-reads the roster from the database on every foundational lookup, so nothing NEW is reachable. **It cannot un-read.** A session that was running SOLO a second ago is still holding whatever it already read, in a room a stranger has just walked into, and no fence anywhere can retract that. **Every claim about the ceiling must be scoped to FUTURE reads**; a sentence saying a container "is protected" once a peer joins is false about the session already running in it.
  - ✅ **THE CONSEQUENCE IS RULING 5, AND IT IS BUILT: A CONTAINER THAT GAINS A PEER ENDS ITS LIVE SESSIONS AND TELLS THE OPERATOR** (`main/session-park-on-claim.js`, called once per `channel-listener.js` reconcile pass). **It ENDS rather than parks** — the ruling permitted either — because `settle` IS the one stop path (a parallel stop is a second set of the orphaned-child bugs it exists to prevent), because ending returns the container credential so the NEXT session in that room mints a locked one instead of waiting for a resume, and because **a parked session is resumable BY A PEER'S MESSAGE**, which is precisely the thing that changed.
  - ⚠ **THE SIGNAL IS A POLL AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN GLOSSED. Roster changes do not reach the desktop MAIN process at all.** `channel-listener.js` subscribes only `channel_messages` and `channel_launch_directives`; `ui-sync.js` forwards a table NAME to the RENDERER, for the ONE workspace the UI is currently viewing. What DOES reach main, for every workspace, every pass, is `GET /api/workspaces` — which carries `memberCount` since 2026-08-26 (§10). **The cost is up to one reconcile interval of latency, and it is acceptable because this is NOT the fence:** layer A has already tightened at the session's next tool call.
  - 🔒 **THE FIRST PASS RECORDS AND NEVER FIRES.** Containers already shared at app launch are memoised silently — there are no sessions to end yet, and firing would make a security notice indistinguishable from a routine boot. **That rule is also what makes the inverted stale-field default safe:** an absent `memberCount` counts as SHARED (§10), so an older server reports every container as shared on pass one and fires on none of them. A peer LEAVING drops the container from the set (§4A's departure-is-removal), so a re-join fires again — one notice per arrival. Pinned by `test/session-park-on-claim.test.mjs`.
- 🔒 **A SESSION SPAWNED INTO A SHARED CONTAINER RUNS ON A CONTAINER-LOCKED CREDENTIAL, NOT THE DEVICE TOKEN (2026-08-26, §10's layer B1).** `main/session-credential.js` mints one at spawn and `main/runtime/claude/loader.js › buildMcpServers` takes it as a THIRD argument that REPLACES the bearer; `''` falls back to the device token, so every unlocked session is byte-for-byte unchanged. **This is the layer that covers Bash**: whatever the agent shells out to inherits the same credential and can name no other workspace.
  - ⚠ **TWO MINT SITES, AND IT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT** — `session-query.js › startQuery` and `session-park.js › startResumedConsumer`, the two places a query STARTS. A woken SPAWN-IDLE shell never passes through the first (`startSession` returns before `startQuery`, and `wakeEffects` fires `resumeQuery`), so one site would leave a whole spawn shape unlocked. The call is idempotent per session. **Pinned as a PAIR by source scan** (`test/session-audience-ceiling.test.mjs`), because deleting either is otherwise silent.
  - ⚠ **RELEASED AT `settle`, DELIBERATELY NOT AT PARK.** `resumeParked` re-enters `buildSdkOptions` on the SAME object, so a released credential would come back as a session that 401s on its first tool call with nothing to say why. The 24h TTL is the backstop for a `settle` that never runs. The release is NOT awaited — `settle` is best-effort by construction and a network call must never hold a teardown open.
  - ⚠ **THE MINT FAILS OPEN AND LOGS.** A credential endpoint that is briefly down must not stop the operator's agent starting; the session then runs as TRIPWIRES-ONLY, with layer A (server-side) unaffected. **A fence that is silently absent is worse than one that is loudly absent**, which is why every failure path emits a `diag` line naming what was lost.
- ⚠ **`grantDecision` HAS A STEP 1.5: THE AUDIENCE BELT (§10's layer B2).** `main/session-audience.js › containerOnlyDenies` — a session whose `audience` is `container-only` denies a dopl call naming a workspace other than its container. **AHEAD of the channel branch AND of `preApproved`**, because a pre-approved tool is SHADOWED by the SDK's `allowedTools` and never reaches `canUseTool` at all, so a later check would never fire on `dopl_search`. **AFTER the hard deny**, so a hard-denied name keeps its own explanation. It **FAILS OPEN** on anything it cannot read — no audience, a non-dopl tool, an absent `workspace` arg (which means the session default, i.e. the container) — because it is a tripwire and a belt that guessed would break the daily path.
  - **The audience is STAMPED AT SPAWN by `session-credential.js`, BEFORE the mint**, and read by the ONE argument builder `main/session-io.js › grantArgs`. The order is load-bearing twice: stamping after a successful mint would disarm the belt on exactly the runs where the credential could not be minted, and reading the audience at a call site instead of in `grantArgs` would let `postWillGate`'s PREDICTION and the real gate disagree about the same call.
  - **`GATE_REASONS` gained `container-audience`**, its own code for the same reason `launch-depth-capped` has one: the tool is on no deny list, so `hard-denied` would send the operator to a setting that has nothing to do with it. ⚠ **`deny` now has THREE causes and the explainer asks them in `grantDecision`'S OWN ORDER** — a first draft asked the audience question first and narrated a hard-denied admin tool as `container-audience`, which is a true fact about the wrong refusal.
  - ⚠ **`session-profiles.js` WAS AT THE 500-LINE CAP, SO THIS WAS A SPLIT (§1), NOT A CRAM.** `session-audience.js` is new, and the EXPLAINER wiring moved into `session-gate-reason.js` behind `makeGrantDetail(grantDecision, predicates)` — the seam that file's own comment already named (making a verdict and explaining one change on different clocks), in the shape that keeps the two modules acyclic.
- **Never log token values.** Every silent decision is one line to `userData/listener.log` via `diag()`.


#### 11.1 The agent-efficiency wave (2026-09-01) — what an orchestrator may ask for, and what it is told

- 🔒 **`dopl_channel(op="await")` IS DENIED ON EVERY SESSION THIS MACHINE RUNS, AHEAD OF EVERY GRANT AND BOTH AXES.** `session-profiles.js › isAwaitOp` is asked inside `grantDecision`'s CHANNEL branch immediately after `postFieldsOk`, so no standing grant, no posture and no operator click can open it. **It is not a permission question**: a desktop-run session is woken by the MESSAGE ITSELF (`session-dispatch.js › feedLiveSession` delivers an addressed post as a TURN), so a hold buys nothing an ended turn does not already get and costs a long-poll plus the whole held context on the far side of it. A GATE would be worse than either — on a windowless session it becomes a notification a human must answer for a call that could not have helped. ⚠ **THE OP STAYS A MEMBER OF `OWN_CHANNEL_READ_OPS`**, and the two statements are not in tension: membership answers "what KIND of call is this" (it is what makes a cross-channel await say *another channel* instead of *unknown op*), the deny answers "may THIS session make it". ⚠ The refusal carries its own reason code `await-desktop-session` and its own SHORT sentence (`session-permissions.js › AWAIT_DENY_MESSAGE`) — short because the whole ticket is that the call wastes tokens, and it NAMES WHAT REPLACES IT, or the agent reaches for the poll loop the refusal was steering it away from. ⚠ `denyMessageFor` is a `Map` and not an object literal: `reason` is a plain string, and an object lookup answers `constructor` with a FUNCTION, which is truthy and would be handed to an agent as its refusal message.
- **A WINDOWLESS DENIAL IS COUNTED AND ANNOUNCED ONCE (T25).** `session-windowless.js › noteDenied` keeps `deniedCalls` + `lastDeniedTool` on the session object, written at the two denials that bridge DECIDES — the platform-without-notifications fallback and a prompt that expired unanswered. ⚠ **A `deny` VERDICT FROM `grantDecision` IS NOT COUNTED AND MUST NOT BE DESCRIBED AS IF IT WERE**: it never reaches `claimGate`, because `session-gate-bridge.js › gateCall` answers it settled. The FIRST denial per session writes exactly ONE `task_progress` line (`denied <tool> (tool mode <mode>); further denials counted`) — one per SESSION because this lands in the shared transcript every member reads, so a per-denial post would be `notifyDenied`'s banner storm one turn worse with a peer's listener paying a decision per row. The MODE is on the line because the mode is the REMEDY (a narrow posture is widenable; an unclassified tool is not). `task_progress`, never `message`, so it wakes nobody.
- **THE PROJECTION CARRIES A HEALTH HALF (T50 / T51 / T83): `turns`, `tokensDelta`, `stale`, `deniedCalls`, `lastDeniedTool`, `lastWakeSeq`, `lastWakeAt`.** `main/session-health.js` is the derivation and it starts no counter — four writers stamp the facts, each at the one site that knows it, pinned by `test/session-health.test.mjs`. ⚠ **IT IS CARRIED INTO THE PROJECTION THROUGH `session-metrics.js › metrics`, WHICH IS A MEASUREMENT AND NOT A PREFERENCE**: `session-summary.js` stands at exactly the 500-line §1 cap, so a require line and a spread line there would be a lint error. ⚠ **`stale` IS THREE CONDITIONS AND ALL THREE ARE REQUIRED** — WORKING, quiet past `STALE_QUIET_MS` (ten minutes), and still SPENDING. Quiet-and-working alone describes every agent doing a long piece of work, and a flag that fires on those is one everybody learns to ignore, which is strictly worse than no flag. An unmeasured spend WITHHOLDS the claim rather than asserting one. ⚠ **"SINCE LAST REPORT" MEANS SINCE THE LAST CHANNEL POST**, deliberately: the telemetry push is CHURN floored at ten seconds (`session-telemetry.js`), so a delta measured against it says nothing an orchestrator can act on, while the last post IS the last thing that orchestrator saw. ⚠ `stale` rides the CHURN half of the wire row and is absent from `session-telemetry.js › STATE_FIELDS` — it is derived from a WALL CLOCK, and putting it in the state half would let it flip a set past the cadence floor on a timer, which is the "digest gate stops gating" defect that module exists to prevent. ⚠ **THE SERVER HALF OF THIS CHAIN IS WRITTEN — F-409 CLOSED 2026-09-02.** This bullet said it was NOT, on a branch that supplies all six pieces: the migration (`20260909120000_channel_sessions_health.sql`), the zod fields on `schema-sessions.ts › SessionStateEntrySchema`, `collab-dto.ts`'s upsert / operator-only column list / `mapOwnSessionStateRow`, `ChannelSessionHealth` in both type trees, and the render in `channel-session-health.ts`. ⚠ **WHAT IS STILL OWED IS THE APPLY, WHICH IS A MEASUREMENT AND NOT A CLAIM (§12)** — until that migration replays, the columns do not exist and `zod` keeps stripping the seven at the route, which is the safe direction: dropped rather than 400-ing the whole push, so nothing regresses and nothing renders yet.
- **A WAKE IS ACKNOWLEDGED ON A FIELD, NEVER ON A POST (T50).** `session-gate.js › enqueue` stamps `lastWakeSeq` / `lastWakeAt` when the wake VERDICT is true — at DELIVERY, past the queue-overflow return, because a wake recorded above that line claims a turn for a message the queue rejected. ⚠ **IT READS THE VERDICT, NEVER THE BODY**: `a.wake` is the tier decision already made for THIS message and THIS agent, and the LOOP FENCE lives inside that decision, so a body-reading stamp would credit an agent-authored post that woke nothing. ⚠ **`typeof` FIRST, WHERE `lastInboundSeq` BESIDE IT COERCES**, and the asymmetry is deliberate: `Number(null)` is 0, harmless for a consent-row de-dupe hint and a LIE for a field rendered as `woke on #0`.
- 🔒 **THE DIRECTIVE MAILBOX CARRIES FOUR KINDS, AND THE CONSENT SET IS DATA.** `launch-directive-wire.js › KINDS` is `launch | end | rename | set_agent_mode`, and `KINDS_NEEDING_LAUNCH_CONSENT` is `launch` + `set_agent_mode`. ⚠ **`set_agent_mode` SITS ON THE GATED SIDE WHERE ITS TWO NON-LAUNCH SIBLINGS DO NOT**: a STOP verb and a DISPLAY verb widen nothing, and Axis A at `bypass` PRE-APPROVES work tools on hardware this operator pays for — which is the LOCAL COMPUTE the toggle exists to gate. The set is a LIST rather than a `||` chain so a fifth kind cannot be admitted past whichever reader nobody updated.
- 🔒 **A DIRECTIVE MAY ASK FOR A POSTURE AND IT MAY NEVER WIDEN ONE (T24), ON BOTH LANES, THROUGH ONE RULE.** `main/launch-posture.js › narrowTo` / `resolvePosture` / `resolveChain` are required — never copied — by `launch-directive-spawn.js › spawn` and `directive-agent-ops.js › setAgentMode`. The ceiling is the operator's OWN durable channel pair (`channel-prefs.js › getLaunchPosture`, which never answers null, so an unset or unreadable record IS `manual`/`ask` and a store failure NARROWS). ⚠ **T24's OWN CARVE-OUT — "unless the caller is the operator's own account" — WAS REFUSED, AND THE REASON IS MEASURABLE.** Every caller on this lane IS that account: a spawned session calls the MCP server with the OPERATOR'S credential, which is why `operator_user_id` never refused a launched agent and could not have. The exception is the whole set, and taking it would hand every agent on the machine the power to launch children wider than its operator's room allows. ⚠ **CLAMP, THEN FLOOR, IN THAT ORDER** — flooring first would let a clamped `ask` come back out as `auto_inbound` and read as though the ceiling had allowed it. ⚠ **A CHAIN REQUEST THE CHANNEL FORBIDS REFUSES UP FRONT RATHER THAN CLAMPING**: a clamped posture still produces a working agent under more supervision, where a clamped chain produces one that hits a bound it was told it did not have, mid-run, and `channelAgentChain` is a SPAWN-TIME stamp nobody can flip for a session already started. ⚠ **`narrowTo` ASKS MEMBERSHIP BEFORE COMPARING INDEXES** — an unknown request also indexes to -1 and `-1 > n` is FALSE, so a bare comparison fails OPEN on the one axis that must not.
  - 🔒 **`chain` IS A TRUE TRI-STATE AND ALL THREE VALUES ARE HONOURED (fixed 2026-09-01).** `true` ASKS IT ON and is refused where the channel forbids it; **`false` ASKS IT OFF, is ALWAYS granted, and WINS over a channel set to ON** — it only ever narrows, so there is nothing for the operator's setting to protect and NARROWING IS NEVER REFUSED; `null` did not ask and inherits the channel setting. ⚠ **TWO DEFECTS HID EACH OTHER AND EACH ALONE MADE `chain: false` UNHONOURABLE**: `launch-directive-wire.js › directiveFrom` narrowed the column as `r.chain === true || r.chain === 'true' ? true : null`, so a stored `false` arrived as "did not ask", and `resolveChain`'s `false` arm fell through to `allowed === true`. **A suite that drives ONE half cannot close this** — `dopl-desktop-app/test/launch-chain.test.mjs` drives a RAW ROW through `directiveFrom` into `resolveChain` and on into the spawn spec, which is the only shape that goes red on either revert (3 reverts, 4 failures each, 0 vacuous). Every copy on the lane said `false` did nothing and every one is corrected in the same change; the column type did not move.
  - 🔒 **THE CLAMP IS NOW A WIRE FIELD AS WELL AS A DIAG — F-410 CLOSED (2026-09-01).** The decide echoes the APPLIED pair and chain (§10's T24 bullet), so a caller can read back what landed. It is reported on EVERY launch, not only a clamped one: otherwise silence would mean both "an older desktop said nothing" and "a current one agreed with you", and the render has one word for it.
- ⚠ **`spawn` LEFT `main/launch-directives.js` ON 2026-09-01** for `main/launch-directive-spawn.js`, at the 500-line cap F-388 measured that file sitting exactly on. The seam: the WATCHER decides whether to act on a row (arming, the owner re-check, the per-kind consent gate, the CAS, the dedupe ledger, the backstop poll); the SPAWN decides what a session is built out of. **The §6 THREAT MODEL stayed with the watcher; the field-by-field containment statement went with the code it governs**, and `test/session-preset-census.test.mjs`'s posture census now names `launch-directive-spawn.js`.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §18 (v1.1–v1.9, F-119/F-125/F-138/F-140/F-177, the reliability round, H1–H3).

## 12. Migrations

- **REPLAY IS THE GATE.** `supabase db reset` → exit 0 is the check that means something. Local config is `supabase/config.toml` (present, verified 2026-08-11), ports remapped off the defaults so a second stack can run beside it.
- **MIGRATION-LIST-IS-CLAIMS DOCTRINE.** `supabase migration list` compares history ROWS to FILENAMES and **structurally cannot notice that the files do not build a database** — it reported "in sync / 0 pending" through months of a tree that died on its first file. **Treat "in sync" as evidence about the history table only.**
- 🔒 **THE HISTORY VERSION IS NOT THE FILENAME — MATCH ON THE *NAME* (measured 2026-08-24, F-304).** Every migration in the recent wave applied under a RE-STAMPED version: `20260822150000_channel_sessions_telemetry` → `20260823091543`, `20260822170000_overview_time_range_indexes` → `20260823091749`, `20260823150000_home_link_channels` → `20260823205007`, `20260823160000_default_workspace_kind_guard` → `20260823205026`. **Order is preserved, so nothing is broken** — but roughly ninety doc and code sites cite a migration BY FILENAME, and the one command an agent runs to check them (`supabase migration list` / MCP `list_migrations`) prints VERSIONS. **So "is `20260823150000` applied?" returns a list not containing that string, and the honest reading of that is "no" — which is exactly the wrong answer this file gave for a day.** ⚠ **Do NOT mass-rename the files to match:** the filenames are the anchors every reference (and `check-doc-refs.mjs`) resolves against. Re-derive with the command; join on the NAME.
- **`20260907120000_channel_launch_directives_kind.sql` — THE DIRECTIVE **KIND** (2026-09-01, Samuel's external end/rename ruling).** ⚠ **WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED** — this directory's standing gate; replay is OWED and recorded rather than glossed (Docker is unavailable on this machine, so `supabase db reset` cannot start, and the applied state was measured instead by `supabase migration list --linked` **joined on the NAME** plus a live `information_schema.columns` read of `channel_launch_directives`). **ADDITIVE ONLY, and it edits no applied file.** Adds `kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'launch'` + its three-word CHECK, `target_agent_id` (the INPUT an end/rename names — ⚠ **never merge it with `agent_id`, which is the OUTPUT a launch produced**), and `target_name` (the rename's new display name, bounded at 60 = `main/agent-names.js › MAX_NAME`, ⚠ **where `''` is LEGAL and means CLEAR**). It also widens the status CHECK with `done` and the refusal CHECK to nine words, and rewrites the terminal-shape CHECK to be KIND-AWARE.
  - ⚠ **`DEFAULT 'launch'` + `NOT NULL` IS WHAT MADE IT A NO-BACKFILL CHANGE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** — every existing row IS a launch, and an older SERVER build mid-rollout that inserts without naming a kind writes what it meant (§13: an older peer is supported).
  - ⚠ **A TYPED `target_name` COLUMN, NOT A `payload JSONB`, AND THE REASON IS THE DESKTOP.** `main/launch-directive-wire.js › directiveFrom` is a LITERAL WHITELIST, and that whitelist is the only thing stopping a widened table from influencing that machine by accident; a JSON blob passes it as one opaque key and then carries whatever it likes into main. A named column with a CHECK is refusable AT REST.
  - ⚠ **AND A TENTH WORD, `no-chain`, JOINED ON 2026-09-02 (review fix).** A directive that asked to CHAIN in a channel where the operator has not enabled it answered `no-bridge` — the SAME word this machine sends when it is not watching that channel at all, and the two are OPPOSITE instructions: one says go elsewhere, the other says the channel is right and ONE NAMED SETTING (`main/launch-posture.js › CHAIN_SETTING` = `channelAgentChain`) is off. Producer: `main/launch-directive-spawn.js › spawn`; CHECK: `20260910120000_channel_launch_directives_posture.sql` §3A, which re-creates the constraint WHOLE and RAISEs if any earlier word or the `template-approval` negative pin went missing.
  - ⚠ **THE REFUSAL CHECK AND ITS PRODUCER LAND IN THE SAME WAVE** — `no-session` and `bad-name` are produced by `main/directive-agent-ops.js`, which ships with this file. That is the 2026-08-22 sequencing lesson APPLIED: `no-template` spent a day in four TypeScript files while the CHECK was still at six, and a `decide` carrying it would have passed zod, passed the route and been refused AT REST. ⚠ **The NEGATIVE PIN on `template-approval` is carried forward and the `DO $$` block still RAISEs on it** — this file REWRITES that constraint whole, which is exactly the change that drops a word by accident, so the block also RAISEs if `no-template` went missing.
  - ⚠ **IT NEVER TOUCHES THE REPLICA IDENTITY OR THE POLICY SET, and asserts both survived rather than assuming.** Losing the replica-identity index stops every UPDATE frame reaching the desktop; a non-SELECT policy appearing on a table whose ENTIRE authorization story is "no write policy exists" would be the quietest possible regression. Pinned from the desktop side too, in `test/launch-directive-agent-ops.test.mjs`.
- **`20260903120000_channel_agent_directions.sql` — THE PRIVATE DIRECT LANE's mailbox (2026-08-31).** ⚠ **WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED** — this directory's standing gate; replay is OWED and recorded rather than glossed. Cloned from `20260822160000_channel_launch_directives.sql` with three differences that are each rulings: `agent_id` is **NOT NULL** (a direction with no addressee is a broadcast into somebody's private lane), the TTL is longer (a direction is answered by a TURN, not a process start), and it carries a **`reply`** column. 🔒 The owner-only SELECT matters more here than on the launch mailbox because `reply` holds a PRIVATE TURN's answer — the closing `DO $$` asserts the policy still scopes on `operator_user_id`, not merely that one policy exists.
- **`20260904090000_direction_sender_agent.sql` — `sender_agent_id` on that mailbox (F-376a, 2026-08-31).** ⚠ **WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED**, and it is OWED behind the one above rather than instead of it. What WAS done: applied via MCP `apply_migration` with an in-migration `DO $$` block that RAISEs unless the column exists, is NULLABLE, and carries both CHECKs; then the catalog read back (`information_schema.columns`, `pg_get_constraintdef` for both constraints, `convalidated` true on each); then **five behavioural probes inside a ROLLED-BACK transaction, each reported as a PASS row rather than as the absence of an error** — a container mint stores the kind; a kind with no lock is `23514`; an unknown kind is `23514`; the locked-but-kindless legacy shape is still legal (and reads as shared); an ordinary unlocked token is untouched. **Replay remains owed.**

Dropping the column under live container credentials reverts them to the pre-ruling refusal: nothing widens, and the symptom is an operator's agent 404ing on their own granted knowledge base with nothing in a log saying why. ⚠ **Roll the APP code back with it**, or `issueContainerToken` 42703s on every mint and no session in a shared container gets a credential at all.
- ⚠️ **THE MEMBER-CAP DROP IS WRITTEN AND — AT WRITE TIME — NOT APPLIED** (2026-08-26, §4A; name `link_container_multi_member`, file `20260830120000_link_container_multi_member.sql`; F-304's re-stamp will apply, so **join on the NAME** and never on that prefix). It `DROP`s the trigger `workspace_members_enforce_link_cap` and then the function `enforce_link_container_member_cap()`, and rewrites the `workspaces.kind` comment ("ONE OR TWO members" → one or more). **Deploy state is a measurement:** `supabase migration list` (or MCP `list_migrations`), then the header's own VERIFICATION block. ⚠ **The filename is `20260830` although the file was written on 2026-08-26** — the four migrations committed that day already took `20260826`–`20260829`, and this directory's convention is MONOTONIC ORDER, one slot per file.
  - 🔒 **WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT TOUCH, and the `DO $$` block asserts one of them:** `channel_links_one_open_per_workspace` survives. With no member cap, **single-use-one-at-a-time IS the surviving shape of an invitation** — drop that index and two tabs pressing "Add person" hand out two live tokens for a room nothing counts. `channel_links.workspace_id`, `channel_links_workspace_idx` and the legacy unbound claim branch all stand.
  - ⚠ **NOT A REALTIME CHANGE.** No SELECT policy, replica identity or column grant is touched; `workspace_members` is unchanged apart from losing a BEFORE trigger, and `channel_links` is untouched (and still unpublished, §7).
  - ⚠ **ROLLBACK IS PROSE IN THE HEADER AND ITS TRAP RUNS THE OTHER WAY.** Re-creating the trigger does NOT shrink containers that have since grown — it fires on INSERT and on status UPDATE only, and it excludes `NEW.user_id` — so an over-full container keeps its roster while the next re-activation of any member of it is refused. Reduce the rosters first, or accept a cap that binds writes and not what is already there.
  - ⚠ **F-306 IS CLOSED BY THIS FILE, BY DELETION** — `LINK_CONTAINER_FULL` no longer exists in the SQL, in `mintContainerLink`, or as a message-prefix match in `service-claim-bound.ts`. **F-307 WAS OPENED AND CLOSED THE SAME DAY**: this drop made `listContainerPeers`' un-ordered first-row-wins pick live, and the follow-on wave (Samuel's ruling) turned it into an ORDERED LIST — see §4A's roster bullet. ⚠ **This migration has no client contract of its own**; the `peers` field is a pure application change and needs no schema.
- ⚠️ **THE PINNED-STARTUP-CONTEXT MIGRATION IS WRITTEN AND — AT WRITE TIME — NOT APPLIED** (2026-09-01, §10's T81 bullet; name `knowledge_pinned_startup_context`, file `20260908120000_knowledge_pinned_startup_context.sql`; F-304's re-stamp will apply, so **join on the NAME** and never on that prefix). It adds `knowledge_bases.pinned` and `knowledge_entries.pinned`, both `BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`, each with a `COMMENT ON COLUMN`. **Deploy state is a measurement:** `supabase migration list` (or MCP `list_migrations`), then the header's own VERIFICATION block.
  - **NO NEW RLS POLICY, AND THAT IS THE POINT RATHER THAN AN OMISSION.** The existing member-select policies on both tables already gate on the BASE's visibility, which is exactly what "RLS matching the base's visibility" asks for; the closing `DO $$` asserts that no policy, no grant and no replica identity was touched, so the absence is stated rather than trusted.
  - ⚠ **IT IS A REALTIME-ADJACENT CHANGE AND THE FILE SAYS SO:** `knowledge_bases` is in the `supabase_realtime` publication, so a new column rides the CDC payloads. Accepted here because it is a boolean curation flag with no PII, visible only on rows the SELECT policy already admits — but a future column on that table is a §7 read before it is written.
  - **NO INDEX AND NO BACKFILL, DELIBERATELY** (this section's own rules): nothing filters on a two-valued column that is not a leading term, and `DEFAULT FALSE` IS the backfill — a statement, not an absence.
  - ⚠ **REPLAY WAS NOT RUN, and that is recorded rather than glossed** — Docker is unavailable on this machine, so `supabase db reset` cannot start. **Replay remains owed, and so is the apply.**
  - ⚠ **NEW FILE. ROLLBACK IS PROSE IN THE HEADER, WITH NO ORDERING TRAP AND ONE DATA LOSS:** dropping the columns silently un-pins everything, so every agent session launched afterwards starts with no context and nothing anywhere says why. Announce it, or leave the columns.
- ⚠️ **THE AGENT-EFFICIENCY WAVE ADDED TWO MORE, BOTH WRITTEN AND — AT WRITE TIME — NOT APPLIED** (2026-09-01; names `channel_sessions_health` and `channel_launch_directives_posture`, files `20260909120000_channel_sessions_health.sql` and `20260910120000_channel_launch_directives_posture.sql`; F-304's re-stamp will apply, so **join on the NAME**). **Deploy state is a measurement:** `supabase migration list` (or MCP `list_migrations`), then each header's own VERIFICATION block.
  - `…_health` adds the SEVEN operator-only session columns (§10). 🔒 **It adds NO column GRANT and ASSERTS that absence** — the `favorited_at` precedent, because a column added by `ALTER TABLE` inherits nothing from `20260822150000`'s per-column list and an absence is what silently stops being true. ⚠ **No DEFAULT and no NOT NULL, asserted too:** six of the seven are counts, and a `DEFAULT 0` on `denied_calls` would turn "unknown" into "nothing was refused". ⚠ `channel_sessions` IS published, so a new column rides the CDC frames; the file states that and touches no policy, replica identity or publication membership.
  - `…_posture` widens the `kind` CHECK to a fourth value and adds the two request pairs plus the `applied_*` echo. ⚠ **BOTH CHECKS ARE DROPPED AND RE-CREATED WHOLE** — `20260907120000`'s rule, because a CHECK cannot be ALTERed and a partially-rewritten one is how a clause quietly disappears. The new terminal-shape clauses each close a way a row can be incoherent at rest: a re-posture must ask for at least ONE axis, the `target_*` pair appears only on that kind, and `start_*` / `chain` only on a launch.
  - ⚠ **REPLAY WAS NOT RUN FOR EITHER, and that is recorded rather than glossed** — Docker is unavailable on this machine, so `supabase db reset` cannot start. **Replay AND the apply are both owed.**
  - ⚠ **NEW FILES, never an edit to an applied one. ROLLBACK is prose in each header.** The health drop silently un-pins every health signal (an orchestrator loses the denial ledger with no error anywhere); the posture drop must come with the app code, or a `set_agent_mode` insert 23514s against the narrowed CHECK.
- ⚠️ **`channel_pings` IS WRITTEN AND — AT WRITE TIME — NOT APPLIED** (2026-09-01; name `channel_pings`, file `20260907130000_channel_pings.sql`). **Deploy state is a measurement:** `supabase migration list` (or MCP `list_migrations`), then the header's own VERIFICATION block; join on the NAME, never the prefix.
  - 🔒 **RLS IS BOTH FENCES AT ONCE, and neither alone is the rule.** `is_channel_member(channel_id)` AND a party clause (`recipient_user_id = auth.uid() OR sender_user_id = auth.uid()`). Membership alone would publish every ping to the whole room — the one property the table exists NOT to have; the party clause alone would let a removed member keep reading pings about a channel they left. ⚠ **AND THE POLICY CARRIES `deleted_at IS NULL` ON THE CHANNEL (2026-09-02)** — a soft-deleted channel is NOT-FOUND to every other read and a membership row outlives the tombstone, so without it a ping would outlive the room. The closing `DO $$` asserts the party half, and asserts that no non-SELECT policy and no `authenticated`/`anon` INSERT exists: **every write goes through the service on the admin client**, where the sender and the two self-scoped recipients are stamped.
  - ⚠ **IT IS A REALTIME CHANGE** (§7): INSERT-only publication, `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX (workspace_id, id)`. No UPDATE and no DELETE exist, so a doorbell for either would be a cost with no event. ⚠ **NOT in `ui-sync-core.js › SYNC_TABLES`** — the SPA panel refetches on mount and after its own write; the desktop MAIN process is the only realtime consumer.
  - ⚠ **`seq` IS `GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY`, `channel_messages.seq`'s idiom, and it is a SEPARATE CURSOR SPACE.** Table-global and GAPPY for any one reader (rows they cannot see still consume it), which is legal — a cursor is an ordering, never a count.
  - ⚠ **IT IS THE ELEVENTH FK INTO `channels` AND IT CASCADES**, like the ten before it; `schema-sql.test.ts` is the guard, and re-blessing its count without checking the cascade is the failure that guard exists to catch.
- **Count is a measurement:** `ls supabase/migrations/*.sql | wc -l` → **165 on 2026-08-19** (159 on 2026-08-11, itself 157 → 158 → 159 across that one day). Re-run it; do not quote it.
- **Whether a migration is APPLIED anywhere is deploy state, not tree state.** The tree holds the file; only the database holds the answer.
- **An index exists only if a named statement uses it — AND AN FK CASCADE COUNTS AS ONE.** Cover every FK column as a LEADING column (what the `unindexed_foreign_keys` advisor checks); drop a partial index with no query behind it.
- **`updated_at` is stamped by a TRIGGER, not by the writer**, wherever a read orders by it — an upsert that forgets the column freezes the ordering with no error and no empty result.
- **A migration touching a published table's SELECT policy, replica identity, or column grants is a REALTIME change.** See §7 before writing it.
- **Do not tax the hot write path to answer a question a cold reader asks once a day.**

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §7 (Migrations: the recovered baseline).
