## 13. Release pipeline

- **`npm run release` in `dopl-desktop-app/` is a WRAPPER SCRIPT, not `electron-builder --publish always`.** It is `bash scripts/release.sh` (verified 2026-08-11). ⚠ ENGINEERING.md §18's "Commands" block still documents the raw invocation — **the script is the supported way to ship.**
  - **The POST-UPLOAD ASSERTION IS THE ENTIRE POINT.** `electron-builder --publish always` reports success on a partial upload; two consecutive releases each lost a DIFFERENT artifact silently. The script re-reads what GitHub actually has and refuses to call it a release until every expected name is there and the public feed URL serves the same bytes as `dist/`.
  - It re-hashes after stapling: the afterSign hook staples the `.app`, THEN `latest-mac.yml` is generated, and only THEN is the DMG stapled — which changes the DMG's bytes.
  - Flags: `--dry-run` (build + staple + re-hash, publish nothing), `--skip-build` (resume from an existing `dist/` — the repair path both real incidents needed). **No secret is ever echoed** — credentials are probed for PRESENCE only.
- **The GitHub release TAG must be `v<version>`** or electron-updater will not match the feed.
- **`renderer/app/` must exist before a release** — build output of `apps/desktop-ui` (`npm run build:ui` at the repo root), gitignored.
- **The auto-updater never force-restarts.** It installs on normal quit or an explicit tray action — a background listener must not yank an active spawned session.
- ⚠ **UPDATE DISCOVERY IS THE INTERVAL *PLUS* A FOCUS CHECK, AND THE FOCUS CHECK IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS (2026-08-22, Samuel's ruling).** The steady interval is **30 minutes** (`update-policy.js › DEFAULT_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS`, measured 2026-08-22 — **it was 4h**, and a release published while the app was open went unnoticed for most of a day; the operator's workaround was to QUIT AND REOPEN the app to force the boot check, which is the feature failing at its one job). `DOPL_UPDATE_CHECK_MS` still overrides it, still clamped to **[60s, 24h]** — those two did NOT move.
  - **THE SIGNAL SET IS APP-LEVEL AND IT IS EXACTLY TWO:** `browser-window-focus` (covers the SPA shell, the pop-out, the agent window and the update screen at once, and it is what fires on the TRAY path — how a tray-resident app is usually reached) and `activate` (the macOS bring-forward: a dock click, or a cmd-tab into a run with no focused window yet). ⚠ **Per-window `win.on('focus')` was refused**: same event, plus a registration to keep in sync with `app-windows.js`, and the F-228 sweep is the record of what a missed one costs. ⚠ **`powerMonitor` resume/unlock is deliberately NOT in the set** — `wake.js` owns that seam for the listener, a machine waking is not the operator looking, and the interval covers it within 30 minutes.
  - **THE GAP IS THE RATE LIMIT: `FOCUS_CHECK_MIN_GAP_MS` = 10 minutes**, pure and tested (`update-policy.js › shouldCheckOnFocus`). Cmd-tabbing fires focus constantly, so without it "check when the operator looks" is "check tens of times a minute". ⚠ **It is SHARED with the interval — one `lastCheckAt`, stamped WHEN A REQUEST IS ISSUED, not when one succeeds.** An errored check still cost a request, so a success-only stamp would turn an offline machine into one request per window focus. Edge answers: never-checked → yes; clock moved backward → yes once (the stamp is rewritten); unreadable clock → **no**, the only branch that fails closed.
  - ⚠ **A FOCUS CHECK IS NOT A MANUAL ONE.** It passes no `{manual:true}`, so every no-op path stays silent — a staged update is left alone (**no re-check and no restart prompt**, unlike a manual check, which goes straight to the prompt because an explicit ask deserves the action), a download in flight says nothing, and a failure is swallowed by the global handler. The operator did not ask a question, so nothing answers one.
  - **NOTHING ELSE MOVED**: not the never-auto-restart rule, not the live-session prompt suppression, not the clamp. **RATE-LIMIT SANITY, measured 2026-08-22:** 30-min polling is 48 requests/day and focus checks add at most one per 10-minute gap (~144/day worst case) against a public GitHub Releases asset — far clear of the 60/hr unauthenticated API quota.
  - **THE MIN-VERSION FLOOR RIDES THE INTERVAL AND GAINED ONLY FRESHNESS** (`min-version.js § Cadence`, re-verified 2026-08-22): a raised floor is now noticed in half an hour rather than half a day. ⚠ **The floor read is NOT hung off the focus signal** — it is a *second* server (`/api/version`), and a request per cmd-tab is a cost nobody asked for. If the two are ever coupled, couple them at the interval, where the gap already bounds them.
- **The download URL is RESOLVED, never guessed.** electron-builder stamps the version into the artifact name, so `/releases/latest/download/:asset` is evergreen in the RELEASE, not in the asset NAME; `GET /download` reads the dmg's name out of `latest-mac.yml`. **Every failure still lands on a github.com URL** — a Download button may not show anybody a stack trace.
- **`latest` is DERIVED, not declared** (`shared/version/latest-release.ts` reads the electron-updater channel file — a release ASSET, outside the `api.github.com` 60/hr-per-IP quota, and the same fact the client reads). **Nothing is bumped by hand at release time.**
- **The floor rides CODE with an env override read per request** (`shared/version/desktop-floor.ts`). `DEFAULT_MIN_VERSION` and `DEFAULT_DECLARED_LATEST` are bumped in the same release commit, and a same-commit test pins floor ≤ latest so the clamp can never refuse its own release. **Malformed = no floor**, the fail-open direction. **Do not set `DOPL_DESKTOP_LATEST_VERSION` from `dopl-desktop-app/package.json`** — that file is a claim about what should ship and runs AHEAD of what is published; a latest ahead of reality permits a floor nothing can install.
- **THE SHIP-ORDER GATE IS WEB-BEFORE-DESKTOP.** Any desktop build requiring a server behaviour ships only after the web deploy carrying it is LIVE. The `requireState` auth gate (§11) is the worked example; the notify-scope column drop is the pattern: code deploy → raise the desktop floor → then the destructive migration.
  - ⚠ **A FORCED UPGRADE IS A DEPLOY STEP, NOT A CODE CHANGE — AND THE REPO CANNOT ARM ONE AHEAD OF A RELEASE.** Worked example, open right now: `/api/home/relationships` was DELETED on 2026-08-24 (§9), so every desktop build predating `/api/home/channels` renders an empty, erroring home page. Samuel ruled force-update. **`dopl-desktop-app/main/version-gate.js` is the imperative shell and holds NO minimum** — it fetches `/api/version` and obeys whatever floor comes back. The floor is `shared/version/desktop-floor.ts › DEFAULT_MIN_VERSION`, overridden per-environment by `DOPL_DESKTOP_MIN_VERSION`.
  - 🔒 **THE ANTI-BRICK CLAMP REFUSES A FLOOR ABOVE `latest`, WHICH MAKES "BUMP IT NOW" A NO-OP RATHER THAN AN ERROR.** `› resolveDesktopFloor` serves **no floor at all** (`rejected: "above-latest"`) when the configured floor exceeds the newest PUBLISHED build, and `latest` is derived from the release feed. **So committing a floor for an unreleased version does not gate anybody — it silently disarms the gate**, which is worse than leaving it alone because it looks done. `desktop-floor.test.ts` pins `DEFAULT_MIN_VERSION ≤ DEFAULT_DECLARED_LATEST` in the same commit for exactly this reason, and §13's standing rule forbids sourcing `latest` from `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` — that file is a claim about what SHOULD ship.
  - **THE ORDER, therefore:** publish the release carrying the new client → confirm the release feed serves it → THEN raise the floor, either by `DOPL_DESKTOP_MIN_VERSION` on Vercel (no deploy, instant, reversible) or by bumping `DEFAULT_MIN_VERSION` **and** `DEFAULT_DECLARED_LATEST` together in the release commit. **Measured 2026-08-25: `DEFAULT_MIN_VERSION` = `1.10.0`, `DEFAULT_DECLARED_LATEST` = `1.10.2`, `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` = `1.19.0`** — the code defaults are nine minor versions stale, so if the release feed is ever unreachable the cold-start clamp would refuse any floor above `1.10.2`. Re-measure before acting; do not quote.
  - ⚠ **A NEW SPA ROUTE IS NOT THAT GATE, AND SAYING SO IS THE POINT.** The SPA is BUILT INTO the app bundle (`renderer/app/`, from `npm run build:ui` — see the `renderer/app/ must exist` bullet above), so main and the route it navigates to ship as **one artifact**: `channels/:channelId` and the desktop `navigateToChannels(segment, channelId)` that pushes it (§11, 2026-08-18 — the path was `channels-v2/:channelId` until the Phase 12 cutover renamed both ends in one change, which is the same argument again) cannot be out of order with each other, and there is no web deploy in the path. **The gate that applies is the RELEASE gate — build the SPA before packaging** — not the server one. **The web-before-desktop gate applies when the desktop needs a SERVER behaviour**, which a router path is not; do not invoke it for renderer-only changes, and do not skip it because this case did.
- **Deployed values are measurements.** Whether `CRON_SECRET` is set, which migrations are applied, what `DOPL_DESKTOP_MIN_VERSION` holds — **verify by observing the deployment**, e.g. `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://www.usedopl.com/api/cron/oauth-cleanup` (401 = gated and live, 503 = secret unset; the apex 307-redirects to `www`, so check without `-L` or you learn nothing). Never record the answer here.
- **THREE crons, wired in `vercel.json`, gated by `requireCronSecret`** (re-measured **2026-08-30**, `ls src/app/api/cron/` and `jq '.crons[].path' vercel.json`): `oauth-cleanup`, `reconcile-seats`, **`playground-reaper`**. ⚠ **This said TWO until 2026-08-30 and had said so since 2026-08-18** — the reaper landed with the playground tree that F-208 recorded as untracked, and nothing re-ran the measurement the bullet itself prescribes.
  - ⚠ **`stale-threads` WAS THE THIRD AND IS DELETED (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 4).** Its entire body was a close PROPOSAL — a `task_progress` carrying `closeProposed` on any thread idle 14 days — and there is no close to propose (§5). The route, `repository-tasks.ts › listStaleOpenThreads` and the caller of the `channel_tasks_stale` RPC went together. **The RPC survives in the database with no caller; dropping it is F-207.**
  - ⚠ **THE `vercel.json` ENTRY DID NOT GO WITH THEM, AND THIS BULLET SAID IT HAD.** Phase 4 claimed the schedule was removed; the file was never touched, so `vercel.json` carried `/api/cron/stale-threads` at `0 7 * * *` for the whole interval and **every deploy re-registered a cron whose only possible answer was 404 — daily, indefinitely, not "until the next deploy"**. The entry was deleted 2026-08-18 in the wave-2 fix pass. **The lesson is the file, not the cron: a claim that a config entry was removed is checkable in one grep, and nobody ran it.** Whether the deployed schedule is actually gone is a MEASUREMENT (`vercel crons ls`, or the project's Cron Jobs tab) — the repo can only hold the claim.
  - ✅ **THAT DISAGREEMENT IS RESOLVED, AND §10 WAS RIGHT (2026-08-30, F-208 closed).** This bullet said *"§10's playground bullet names a THIRD cron, `app/api/cron/playground-reaper`, and no such directory or `vercel.json` entry exists in this tree"* (measured 2026-08-18). **The measurement was honest and the cause was that the playground was UNTRACKED WORK in the main checkout** — it has since landed: `src/app/api/cron/playground-reaper/route.ts` is tracked and `vercel.json` carries its schedule. §10 was correct-but-early, which was one of the two readings F-208 named.
  - 🔴 **SO THE COUNT IN THIS SECTION IS THREE, NOT TWO** — `oauth-cleanup`, `reconcile-seats` and `playground-reaper`. ⚠ `playground-reaper` has been running unobserved since 2026-08-19 and is named in no other doc; **F-133's job set is wrong for the same reason.** Re-derive rather than quoting: `grep -A2 '"crons"' vercel.json`.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §18 (Commands, updater, F-125, F-193), §9.3.

## 14. Testing gates

**Definition of green** — **FIVE suites, TWO lints, TWO typechecks, and SIX non-suite gates** (`check-doc-refs`, `size-check`, `check-knowledge-type-drift`, `check-role-drift`, `check-css-token-drift`, **`check-session-health-drift`**) — fifteen rows, and the table below is all of them. ⚠ **SIX SINCE 2026-09-02**, and that gate landed WITH its row here and in CLAUDE.md in the same change — which is the remedy the three warnings below have been asking for. Re-derive rather than quote: `grep -n 'run:' .github/workflows/ci.yml`.

⚠ **AND THIS LINE HAS NOW BEEN WRONG A THIRD TIME, IN THE SAME DIRECTION, FOR THE SAME REASON (corrected 2026-09-01).** It said FOUR and omitted `scripts/check-css-token-drift.ts` — the `type-drift` job's THIRD step since `522f53df` (2026-08-31, the seven-drift-gates wave), i.e. the change that ADDED a gate told no doc about it, exactly as guest-role M0 did with `check-role-drift.ts`. **Twice is a coincidence; three times is the process.** The bullet below already says re-derive rather than quote, and the re-derivation is one command — it was simply not run. Run it now, before you trust this paragraph: `grep -n 'run:' .github/workflows/ci.yml`.

⚠ **THIS LINE HAS NOW BEEN WRONG TWICE IN ONE DAY, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, AND BOTH ERRORS CAME FROM COUNTING A PROSE LIST INSTEAD OF THE WORKFLOW.** It said "FOUR" and omitted `check-role-drift.ts` — a CI step since guest-role M0 (commit `080b7b48`, the `type-drift` job's second step) that no doc listed. The correction then said **FIVE** non-suite gates over a table of FOUR, because the five it enumerated included `npm run typecheck -w @dopl/desktop-ui` — **which is one of the TWO typechecks, counted twice**. CLAUDE.md's tail carried the same double count and is corrected in the same change. The forgettable-gate LIST and the non-suite-gate COUNT are different questions: the desktop typecheck belongs on the first and not in the second. **Re-derive, never quote: `grep -n 'run:' .github/workflows/ci.yml`.**

| Command | Scope |
|---|---|
| `npm test` (root) | `vitest run` over `src/` |
| `npm test -w @dopl/client` | `packages/dopl-client` |
| `npm test -w @dopl/mcp-server` | `packages/mcp-server` (incl. MCP parity) |
| `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` | `apps/desktop-ui` |
| `cd dopl-desktop-app && npm test` | `node --test 'test/**/*.mjs'` |
| `npx eslint` (root) | the root tree |
| `cd dopl-desktop-app && npm run lint` | the desktop tree — its OWN config, where the 500-cap and `no-redeclare` live |
| `npm run typecheck` | `tsc --noEmit` — **`src/` + `scripts/` + the Next tree ONLY.** Root `tsconfig.json` EXCLUDES `apps` and `packages` |
| **`npm run typecheck -w @dopl/desktop-ui`** | the SPA — **outside the root program**, and `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` (vitest) does not typecheck. Also the only compile-time cover the unrouted retirement leftovers have |
| **`node scripts/check-doc-refs.mjs`** | doc anchors: files exist, `F-NNN` resolve, `path › symbol` anchors resolve, **and since 2026-08-26 a PLAIN BACKTICKED PATH is existence-checked too (class (e), a RATCHET — see below)** — **and, since 2026-08-18 (F-224), `.md` targets are checked like any other extension and `F-NNN` ids are checked in the SOURCE trees too**, not only in `docs/`. **NOT covered by `npm run lint`** — `globalIgnores` excludes `scripts/**`. ⚠ **It catches a DANGLING id, never a MIS-CITED one** — a comment naming the wrong LIVE finding passes |
| **the `size-check` job** | the 500-line cap, **over `packages/` only** — an inline `find`/`awk` in ci.yml, not a script and not eslint |
| **`npx tsx scripts/check-knowledge-type-drift.ts`** | knowledge types, server vs SDK |
| **`npx tsx scripts/check-role-drift.ts`** | the workspace ROLE SET across server, SDK and MCP — **the second step of the `type-drift` job, and it was missing from this table and from CLAUDE.md until 2026-08-26** though it has been in CI since guest-role M0 (`080b7b48`). It guards the role SET, not `ROLE_RANK`'s numbers (§12) |
| **`npx tsx scripts/check-session-health-drift.ts`** | the SEVEN session-health fields, across FOUR hand-mirrors — `src/features/channels/types-sessions.ts › ChannelSessionHealth` (the reference), the HEALTH block of `schema-sessions.ts › SessionStateEntrySchema`, the SDK's `session-health-types.ts`, its committed `dist/` — plus `20260909120000_channel_sessions_health.sql`'s own `ADD COLUMN` set. ⚠ **WHY A GATE AND NOT A COMMENT: every field is `optional` AND `nullable` by design** (an older desktop must not 400 its whole push, §11/§13), so a field added on one side and not another COMPILES on both, passes every suite, and simply never arrives. Added 2026-09-02, **with this row** |
| **`npx tsx scripts/check-css-token-drift.ts`** | the DESIGN TOKENS, `src/app/globals.css` vs `apps/desktop-ui/src/styles/tokens.css` — the SPA's stylesheet is a second copy of the web's with no shared module, which is the same hand-mirror class as the three rows above it. **The `type-drift` job's THIRD step since `522f53df` (2026-08-31), and it was missing from this table and from CLAUDE.md until 2026-09-01** — the third time in this table's life that a gate shipped without a doc row. Measured 2026-09-01: 217 tokens identical, 3 documented deviations (`--font-mono`, `--font-playfair`, `--shell-rail-w`) |

`npm run test:all` chains the first four **and nothing else** — it is not the definition of green. **CI is `.github/workflows/ci.yml`**, five jobs (`build-test` on ubuntu+windows, `desktop`, `typecheck-lint`, `size-check`, `type-drift` — whose steps are FOUR since 2026-09-02), and runs every row above — **path filters are DELETED on purpose**, because a filter over a repo-wide job would let a `src/` PR skip the `src/` suite.
- ⚠ **"RUNS EVERY ROW" IS NOT "RUNS ONLY THESE ROWS", and reading it that way is how a gate stayed invisible.** `build-test` also runs `npm run build -w @dopl/client` and `npm run build -w @dopl/mcp-server` ahead of the suites — **preconditions, not gates**, which is why they are not rows; but the same reading is what let `check-role-drift.ts` sit in CI, unlisted, for the whole guest-role wave. **The workflow is the authority and this table is a claim about it** (§'s own doctrine): re-derive with `grep -n 'run:' .github/workflows/ci.yml` before trusting a row count.

- **THE TWO LINT STEPS ARE ENFORCED DIFFERENTLY, AND THIS BULLET SAID NEITHER WAS (corrected 2026-08-18).** Re-read `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, do not quote a line number:
  - **ROOT lint — `--max-warnings 0` IS SET.** The `typecheck-lint` job runs `npm run lint -- --max-warnings 0`, with a comment saying the two known warnings it used to allow were fixed in the launch wave so the allowance had become a hole. **A new WARNING in the root tree fails CI.**
  - **DESKTOP lint — no flag.** The `desktop` job runs a bare `npm run lint` in `dopl-desktop-app/`, so **only a new ERROR fails there** (`no-redeclare` and the 500-cap are both `error`, which is why the gap has been survivable).
  - Both trees measured a TRUE ZERO — 0 errors, 0 warnings, both exiting 0 (2026-08-11). **The root zero is now held by CI; the desktop zero is held by nobody.** Enforcing the desktop half is a one-flag change to ci.yml. ⚠ **The prior wording ("`--max-warnings` is deliberately unset on both lint steps") also survived verbatim in `CLAUDE.md`'s Definition-of-green tail and was corrected there in the same change.**
- 🔒 **`check-doc-refs` NOW CHECKS THE CITATION FORM THE DOCS ARE TOLD TO WRITE, AND IT IS A RATCHET (class (e), 2026-08-26).** `FILE_REF_RE` required a `:LINE` and `SYMBOL_ANCHOR_RE` required a `›`, so **a plain backticked path was never existence-checked — i.e. the gate enforced existence only on the form CLAUDE.md's standing rule 2 FORBIDS.** Class (e) fires only when a whole inline-code span IS a path (no globs, no sentences, no `:NNN`, no `›`), which keeps prose invisible to it, and it un-aliases `@/` first. ⚠ **IT SHIPS WITH A 457-KEY BASELINE** (`scripts/doc-refs-plain-path-baseline.json`, 613 raw references measured 2026-08-26) because repointing them is not one wave's work and a gate that lands red gets switched off: a NEW dead plain path FAILS, an old one is recorded debt. **The baseline only shrinks** — stale entries are printed with a delete instruction and never fail, a missing file is a hard exit 2, and there is deliberately no regenerate flag. **A baselined ref is unchecked debt, not a passing citation. F-337.**
- **THE PRIVATE DIRECT LANE'S SUITES ARE FOUR, AND THE SPLIT IS THE POINT** (2026-08-31): `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-directions.test.ts` (the two ops, the refusal sentences, the operator-argument ABSENCE), `src/features/channels/server/service-directions.test.ts` (the server fences — the stamp, membership-not-readability, the offline pre-check, the malformed-id 404, lazy expiry), `dopl-desktop-app/test/agent-directions.test.mjs` (the watcher, the consent, the wire contract, the capture, and the zero-width fence forgery of F-370), and `dopl-desktop-app/test/session-direction-lane.test.mjs` (the framing ruling AT THE CALL SITE, the cross-account fence, the no-fallback refusal). ⚠ **The framing pin is in the LAST of those and not the third**, because asserting that the two framers differ is not the same assertion as the caller reaching for the right one — a mutation that swapped the call site left every framer-level case green.
- **THE ESCALATION CARD'S SUITES ARE SEVEN, ACROSS FOUR TREES, AND THE SPLIT IS THE POINT** (2026-08-31): `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-escalate.test.ts` (the op, its refusals, the degrade contract), `src/features/channels/escalation-body-parity.test.ts` (the two trees' renders, driven over ONE shared fixture table), `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata-escalation.test.ts` (both reserved keys, the answerer fence, the derived wake id), `components/channels-v2/escalation-card.test.tsx` (the transcript pipeline), `› escalation-agent-stream.test.tsx` (the SECOND row pipeline), and `dopl-desktop-app/test/escalation-answer-wake.test.mjs` (the wake door AND the outbound lane, including the loop fence as an ABSENCE). ⚠ **The two render suites exist because there are two row pipelines** — two suites each agreeing with themselves is not the two ends agreeing.
- **A bug-fix PR must include a test that would have caught it.** Tests live next to code (`pipeline.ts` → `pipeline.test.ts`); no `__tests__/` tree.
- **MUTATION-VERIFY CULTURE: an assertion is not verified until reverting the code turns it red.** State the count when you claim it ("N reverts, N failures, 0 vacuous"). A suite that stays green against the bug it names is the defect, not the evidence.
- **A PIN ON A SYMBOL IS NOT A PIN.** Asserting a constant is *referenced* stays green while its VALUE is rewritten out from under the thing it must match. Pin the value, on both sides of any join with no shared module.
- **A REGEX OVER SOURCE TEXT IS NOT A BEHAVIOURAL ASSERTION.** It goes red on a refactor that changed nothing and green on a path that does the wrong thing. Drive the real function.
- **Assert the object that CROSSES the process boundary**, not the function that computes it (§11).
- **A comment asserting where a filter lives is not evidence the filter exists.** When a comment says a constraint is enforced somewhere else, open that somewhere else.
- **A MIXED TEST FILE WHOSE FEATURE IS DELETED IS REWRITTEN DOWN TO WHAT SURVIVES, NOT REMOVED** — deleting one whole has twice taken an unrelated live guard with it.
- **THE BOUND IPC SENDERS ARE ENUMERATED, NOT REMEMBERED** (2026-08-18, wiring plan Phase 10). Widening a fail-closed security guard is only as good as the list of who it now admits, so `dopl-desktop-app/test/channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs` **binds TWO windows (a shell and a pop-out) and drives EVERY op from both**, plus from a third unregistered window, from an iframe inside a bound one, and against an unbound surface — with the refusal VALUE pinned identical in every case, so the "a hostile page learns nothing" claim is asserted rather than asserted-about. Its structural belt still fails on any `ipcMain.handle` in that file not visibly wrapped, and now also fails if the retired `mainOnly(` name survives anywhere in it. `test/app-windows.test.mjs` holds the other half — that nothing renderer-reachable can enlarge the set — **by construction, over the real tree** (no IPC handler reaches `register`, no preload names it, and the call-site list is asserted whole). ⚠ **And `test/shell-mode.test.mjs` DRIVES the one line the whole surface rests on** — that `createShellWindow` binds the shell it just built, and that the update-required screen is deliberately not bound. An unregistered shell has its entire privileged IPC surface refused, silently, and nothing else in the suite would notice.
- **Executable authorities to prefer over prose:** `src/shared/auth/write-gate-coverage.test.ts` (the three auth gate sets), `dopl-desktop-app/test/ui-sync-tables.test.mjs` + `ui-sync-replica-identity.test.mjs` (publication + replica identity), `packages/mcp-server/src/strict-args.test.ts` (strict tool args), `test/removed-vocabulary.test.mjs` (no dead module, no dead require, every surviving mention annotated as history), `test/eslint-config-coverage.test.mjs`, `test/preload-parity.test.mjs`, `test/deep-link-target.test.mjs`, `test/prompt-profile-drift.test.mjs`, `test/channel-ipc-sender.test.mjs` + `test/app-windows.test.mjs` (the sender binding and its registry).
- **`dopl-desktop-app/test/live/` is a live-contract tier and is NOT in the default suite** — its files are `.js` deliberately, so a credential-less `npm test` (which globs `.mjs`) can never fail on it.
- **`no-redeclare` is on at `error` in the desktop config.** A duplicate declaration wins silently, and `max-lines` cannot catch it — a duplicate makes a file BIGGER.

Why/history: ENGINEERING.md §13, §0 (Known debt), §18 (F-146).
